Infrastructure detour: acceptance runtime has been restructured without reducing promotion coverage. Development acceptance now has `fast`, `consolidated`, and `full` exact-SHA profiles; owner-created acceptance issues default to `fast`, scheduled nightly acceptance forces `full`, and phase/release promotion still requires `full`. Fast runs build/state, E1 on amd64/arm64 and a scope-selected independent interop slice; consolidated adds pinned reference baselines, E4 on both architectures and broader independent peer coverage; full retains E1-E4, references and every interop scenario. Full interoperability scenarios are split into separate matrix jobs so L1/L2 and role cases run in parallel rather than serially inside one runner. The prior Phase 5 backlog candidate `5924e18523cf8c2fc569b8ab68eaea5e812fc981` failure investigation is intentionally frozen: x64 PyDECnet interop jobs `106106370256` and `106106370339` were observed failed before the detour, but their logs/root cause have not been investigated and no product correction has been made. Resume that exact failure investigation only after the infrastructure detour is explicitly ended.


Infrastructure correction: the first tiered-interop workflow revision referenced `matrix.*` in a job-level `if`, which GitHub evaluates before matrix expansion and rejected at workflow-parse time. The corrected workflow builds the scenario matrix dynamically from `PROFILE`/`SCOPE` using `fromJSON`, preserving the same fast/consolidated/full coverage selections without invalid pre-matrix expressions.


Infrastructure policy follow-up: Repository Policy run `35523266827` rejected the dynamic interop matrix because the workflow-budget parser only recognized literal YAML `scenarios:` rows. The policy gate and its regression test now also parse scenario fields embedded in the dynamic JSON matrix, and the allowed scenarios-per-job ceiling is tightened from two to one to lock the new parallelization invariant.


Infrastructure test correction: Repository Policy `35523382490` found that the workflow-budget regression fixture itself still encoded a two-scenario interop row after the gate maximum was tightened to one. The fixture and oversize negative are corrected to one legal scenario and a two-scenario rejection case; production workflow semantics are unchanged.


Infrastructure detour closure: runtime tiering is implemented on main at `45b17abc86837cb2777fad628480af8342a1552f`. Repository Policy run `35523481269` passed and its dispatcher correctly emitted the fast profile only: dual-architecture Build Bootstrap, Project State Gate, dual-architecture E1, and x64 Route20/PyDECnet L1 interoperability. The fast E1/interop children were externally cancelled before completion, so they are infrastructure smoke evidence only and do not constitute product acceptance. No Phase 5 failure analysis or corrective product change occurred during the detour.

Resume normal Phase 5 work at the locked backlog-candidate failure point: candidate `5924e18523cf8c2fc569b8ab68eaea5e812fc981`; x64 PyDECnet interoperability jobs `106106370256` and `106106370339` failed before the detour. Inspect those exact job logs/evidence first, classify the failures, and fix only concrete defects. Use the new fast acceptance profile for ordinary corrective iterations; use consolidated checkpoints as appropriate and full acceptance for promotion/closure.

Frozen backlog-failure investigation completed for candidate `5924e18523cf8c2fc569b8ab68eaea5e812fc981`. Both failed x64 PyDECnet jobs (`106106370256`, `106106370339`) reached the new four-connection listener test and failed with candidate-generated Session reason 6 (object busy), while one accepted connection was also torn down before echo. The concrete kernel defect is duplicate incoming CI/RCI handling: NSP correctly recognized an existing remote logical link and re-ACKed the duplicate, but then re-notified Session Control as though it were a fresh connection indication. Once accept dequeued a CR link, a retransmitted CI/RCI could therefore requeue that already-owned link and consume backlog slots. Duplicate CI/RCI now re-ACKs only; the original connection indication remains the sole upper-layer notification. Protocol behavior outside duplicate-connect indication delivery is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance of this corrective successor, then continue Phase 5 backlog overflow/object-busy rejection and close/reset races if green.

Corrective candidate `f87e654077130f8f4b4a36110c3007e9e18b1ce1` removed duplicate CI/RCI upper-layer re-notification but fast socket acceptance still failed in x64 PyDECnet L1 interop run `35524297201`, job `106113666583`. Packet capture proves all four distinct CIs were initially ACKed; the native listener later accepted/connected three links, but the third child closed before echo and listener teardown then rejected the fourth queued link with reason 6. The remaining failure is therefore downstream of queue admission, not a four-entry backlog overflow. The backlog harness now reports exact recv/send return values and errno at the failure point so the next exact-SHA run can distinguish receive-state from transmit-state failure without changing protocol behavior.

Diagnostic successor `08fd5f68df1aa6db8d279ac34480f93eda803062` isolated the remaining backlog failure in x64 PyDECnet L1 run `35524772529`, job `106114913039`: the second accepted child returned `recv() = -1, errno=EPERM` even though the peer's valid sequence-1 data record was on the wire. This is a socket-security initialization defect, not NSP sequencing: accepted children were attached with `sock_init_data()` but never passed through the modern Linux accepted-socket security graft/clone hooks. The accept path now calls `security_sock_graft()` and `security_sk_clone()` after child allocation/attachment, matching current Linux accepted-socket initialization requirements while leaving DECnet protocol state unchanged. The backlog diagnostic newline is also corrected. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance; if green, advance to explicit backlog-overflow/object-busy rejection and close/reset race coverage.

Fast socket run `35525149713` on `7d87e5d40a5ba15149332f24403ecf02d6747828` reproduced the same `recv()=-1/EPERM` failure, so the speculative accepted-socket security graft/clone change did not affect the defect and has been removed. A narrowly scoped object-241 kernel trace now records entry and exact return from the DECnet `recvmsg` callback. The next exact-SHA run will determine whether EPERM is produced inside the protocol callback or before it in the generic socket/security path; no protocol behavior is changed by this diagnostic successor.

Exact-SHA diagnostic run `35525468345`, job `106116739880`, on `c12d950326f163ea42ad71135510199ea003b5f9` again returned userspace `recv()=-1/EPERM`, but none of the object-241 `dniv_sock_recvmsg` entry traces fired. The denial is therefore definitively before the DECnet protocol callback in Linux's generic `sock_recvmsg()` LSM hook. Comparing the native accept path with current Linux socket cloning shows the concrete initialization defect: accepted child `struct sock` security state must be cloned from the listening socket before the child is grafted to the accepted `struct socket`. The prior experiment grafted before cloning, unlike the kernel's normal clone-then-graft ordering. The DECnet accept path now performs `security_sk_clone(listener, child)`, initializes the child socket, then performs `security_sock_graft(child, newsock)`. Protocol state and wire behavior are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance; remove the temporary object-241 recv trace once this is proven green.

Fast socket acceptance for corrective candidate `e89a1c8a0184ad170331266a3516ba940926b8d4` is green: Repository Policy `35525699661`, Build Bootstrap `35525717748`, Project State Gate `35525719171`, E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35525720775`, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35525722276` all completed successfully. The four-entry PyDECnet inbound listener queue passed exactly. The root cause of the frozen `5924e185...` failures was accepted-socket LSM state initialization: the child security state must be cloned from the listener before grafting the child to the accepted socket. Temporary recv tracing is removed in the successor.

Phase 5 now adds the mandatory negative side of backlog handling. Object 242 listens with backlog two while three PyDECnet Session clients connect concurrently; exactly two must be accepted and echoed and exactly one must be rejected with Session reason 6 (object busy). No product behavior is changed by this successor beyond retaining the accepted-socket security fix. Next after exact-SHA fast acceptance: close/reset race coverage.

Repository Policy run `35526264084` caught a harness packaging defect in `e8e9b04fec6404c7201d279c3c97b7510c589793`: the tree update accidentally dropped executable mode bits from the modified interop scripts. No protocol code ran under that candidate. This corrective successor restores the established `100755` modes for `dniv-interop-smoke.sh`, `run-interop.sh`, and `pydecnet-backlog.py`; backlog-overflow test semantics are otherwise unchanged.

Fast interop run `35526382699` on `7301d5d4f4b72ed138ce14b1a99e274f71bbbac0` reproduced the intermittent second-child `EPERM` before the new overflow stage. This showed that the prior green run was not sufficient to declare the listener stress accepted. The queue harness was also mixing two concerns: it accepted, echoed, and closed each child while other connection requests remained queued. That close-while-pending behavior belongs to the next close/reset-race item, not the backlog-admission proof. The native backlog server now first accepts every queued child, then performs echo validation, and only then closes children. The accepted-socket LSM calls are also restored to the exact ordering used by current in-tree AF_ALG (`sock_init_data`, `security_sock_graft`, then `security_sk_clone`). This successor therefore isolates backlog admission/overflow from close races rather than hiding them; close/reset races remain explicitly next.

Fast acceptance for `591c73d50e149d4b58ed93270af8c2254d13986d` is fully green: Repository Policy `35526637563`, Build Bootstrap `35526655922`, Project State Gate `35526657237`, Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35526659585`, and E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35526658388` on both x86_64 and ARM64. The independent PyDECnet proof includes a four-entry listener queue plus the mandatory negative backlog case: backlog two with three concurrent requests yielded exactly two accepted links and one Session reason-6 object-busy rejection.

Phase 5 now isolates the previously observed close-while-pending race as its own interoperability scenario instead of conflating it with backlog admission. Object 243 queues four concurrent PyDECnet clients; the native server deliberately accepts, echoes, and closes each child one at a time while later requests remain pending. All four clients must complete without LSM denial, spurious object-busy rejection, stale-link reuse, or cross-connection corruption. This is harness-only coverage; no protocol behavior changes in this candidate. Next after exact-SHA fast acceptance: peer-reset/disconnect races and then consolidated Phase 5 checkpointing.

The isolated close-while-pending scenario on `23cb7952d81d0d56c8d2d0050fc968ca4b26f4de` failed in x64 PyDECnet interoperability run `35527721091`: the first accepted object-243 child returned `recv()=-1/EPERM`, after the queue and overflow scenarios had both passed. This exposes a concrete accepted-socket initialization ordering defect. The child was passed through `security_sock_graft()` and `security_sk_clone()` while `newsk->sk_protocol` was still zero; `DNPROTO_NSP` was assigned only afterward. Linux security hooks must see the final socket family/protocol identity. The accept path now assigns `PF_DECnet` and `DNPROTO_NSP` immediately after `sk_alloc()`, before `sock_init_data()` and the security graft/clone hooks. No NSP wire behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance retaining the isolated close-race scenario.

The protocol-identity-before-hooks successor `d052b0c37e31f52e898a9bd5c4305e3bce0da4bd` still failed the isolated close-while-pending test in x64 interop run `35527962650`, again with the first accepted child denied `recv()=-1/EPERM`. Thus zero protocol identity was not the cause. The remaining controlled difference from the earlier green accepted-child experiment is security clone/graft ordering. For DECnet's independently allocated accepted child, cloning the listener's `sk_security` after graft leaves the already-grafted socket associated with the pre-clone child security state. The accept path now sets final DECnet identity, clones `sk_security` from the listener, initializes the child socket, and only then grafts it. This matches the required causal ordering for this independently allocated network child rather than AF_ALG's family-specific pattern. The close-race test remains unchanged and is the acceptance discriminator.

The clone-before-graft experiment `0aadf54a1352b1c0dde6019482a69574c4fa2ec1` still failed the isolated close-while-pending test in x64 run `35528243005`; child 0 completed and child 1 then hit `recv()=-1/EPERM`. This proves manual `sk_alloc()` plus selected security hooks is insufficient for a normal accepted network child. Linux's canonical network-socket cloning path is `sk_clone_lock()`, which copies the complete listener socket state while preserving a separately allocated security blob and cloning LSM, cgroup, filter, namespace, uid, and other per-socket state coherently. DECnet accepted children now use `sk_clone_lock()`, reset all DECnet listener/private queue state, graft the fully cloned child to `newsock`, unlock it, and drop the extra protocol-side clone reference because DECnet does not hash accepted children. The isolated close-race remains unchanged as the discriminator.

Candidate `40f055eb49ad6f08ca302adf86e9683bc0eefa39` was rejected before protocol execution: Build Bootstrap `35528599091` failed on both architectures because `sk_clone_lock()` is GPL-only and this project's module license is deliberately non-GPL. That experiment is fully reverted.

Upstream AppArmor documents the relevant accepted-socket contract directly: sockets created by `sock_alloc()` have no `struct sock` during `socket_post_create`; accept cases are initialized later by the `sock_graft` LSM hook. Manually invoking `security_sk_clone(listener, child)` is therefore not the generic network accept path and can copy listener peer-security state into an unrelated child. DECnet now allocates the child with `sk_alloc()`, sets final family/protocol, attaches it with `sock_init_data()`, and invokes only `security_sock_graft()` for accepted-socket LSM setup. This uses exported non-GPL interfaces and leaves protocol/wire behavior unchanged. The close-while-pending test remains the discriminator.

Graft-only candidate `4b2498f38c3da884f9cccc8ba9bab1e7a2fa9c50` built cleanly and E1 passed on x86_64/ARM64, but x64 PyDECnet interoperability run `35528808340` again reproduced the isolated close-while-pending failure: child 0 completed, child 1 returned `recv()=-1/EPERM`, and listener teardown then rejected remaining pending clients. Queue and overflow scenarios still passed first. The earlier inference that EPERM was necessarily before `dniv_sock_recvmsg()` relied on `pr_info` output that may be suppressed by the VM console log level, so that inference is no longer treated as proven.

This diagnostic successor makes no protocol change. For object 243 only, the kernel emits warning-level accept/recvmsg entry and return records including family/type/protocol/state, while the userspace server records `SO_DOMAIN`, `SO_TYPE`, and `SO_PROTOCOL` for every accepted child before recv. The next exact-SHA fast interop run will establish whether EPERM is returned before the DECnet callback and whether child socket identity changes across close/reaccept.

Diagnostic candidate `31c04a18b87e755ff1b30fc4857438ff16cf8d5e` did not reach the VM: interop run `35530445339` failed while compiling `dnbacklog.c` because the strict C11 build does not expose glibc's `SO_PROTOCOL` and `SO_DOMAIN` feature macros. The Linux ABI values (38 and 39) are now supplied under `#ifndef` guards in the test harness only. No kernel or protocol behavior changes.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for diagnostic candidate `42fb2c52cf16fbe10d3d9be8e65fb3994a9f4532` is green: Repository Policy `35530548031`, Build Bootstrap `35530565830`, Project State Gate `35530566952`, Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35530569203`, and E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35530568009` on both architectures. The successful close-race run showed all four accepted descriptors consistently as domain 12 (AF_DECnet), type 5 (SOCK_SEQPACKET), protocol 2 (NSP), with fd 4 deliberately reused after each close. Because the defect is intermittent, one green four-child sample is not sufficient to close the investigation.

The next diagnostic strengthens only the discriminator: one object-243 listener now survives four successive four-client batches (16 accept/echo/close/reaccept cycles) while keeping backlog four, warning traces are raised to error console priority so callback entry/exit cannot be hidden by console log level, and the server records its task security label plus seccomp/no-new-privileges state. No protocol behavior changes.

Two independent fast runs on diagnostic stress candidate `4babe093db6c4f1bb56d2084c157b39065cbf57c` were green: interop runs `35531069533` and `35531426687`, each completing 16 close/accept/echo/reaccept cycles, plus green x86_64/ARM64 E1, build, and state gates. Error-priority kernel tracing proved every successful child entered `dniv_sock_recvmsg()` with AF_DECnet/SOCK_SEQPACKET/NSP identity and returned the expected 9-byte payload; the server process was unconfined with Seccomp=0 and NoNewPrivs=0. This excludes AppArmor/seccomp policy as the source of the intermittent EPERM and confirms the earlier failures are timing-sensitive.

A concrete socket-lifecycle mismatch remains in the implementation and is now corrected. DECnet attached a freshly allocated accepted child with `sock_init_data(newsock, newsk)` and then called the LSM graft hook directly. Linux's canonical accepted-network-socket attachment is `sock_graft()`, which updates the socket/wait-queue association under `sk_callback_lock` and invokes the LSM graft hook as part of that atomic attachment. The child is now fully initialized while unattached with `sock_init_data_uid(NULL, newsk, SOCK_INODE(newsock)->i_uid)`, then attached using `sock_graft(newsk, newsock)`. All diagnostic logging and per-child identity probes are removed; the 16-cycle close/reaccept stress remains, so timing is no longer artificially stretched. Next: repeated exact-SHA fast acceptance.

The first no-diagnostic acceptance run for canonical graft candidate `64a30eb6fa23b8759cfb2d64f63e8e2f9fd0c788` failed immediately in close-race round 1 (interop `35532259656`) with `recv()=-1/EPERM` on child 0. This exposed a concrete consequence of the new unattached initialization: `sock_init_data_uid(NULL, ...)` intentionally cannot copy `socket->type`, so the child `sk_type` remained zero. Generic LSM receive mediation matches family/type/protocol and can therefore reject the malformed AF_DECnet/type-0/NSP socket before the DECnet recvmsg callback. The accepted child now explicitly copies `newsock->type` into `newsk->sk_type` before `sock_graft()`. This retains the atomic canonical graft while restoring the socket identity proven by the prior diagnostics. Next: repeated no-diagnostic exact-SHA fast acceptance; any residual EPERM after this point belongs to the original race rather than the type-zero regression.

No-diagnostic canonical-graft candidate `95102f695155c2b44f364392a644f79934aef43a` still failed close-race round 1 in x64 interop run `35532556612` with userspace `recv()=-1/EPERM`, after queue and overflow had passed. Restoring `sk_type=SOCK_SEQPACKET` therefore fixed the type-zero regression but did not remove the original intermittent failure.

The next diagnostic is deliberately failure-only to avoid the timing perturbation that made prior verbose diagnostics green. Object 243 emits no accept/entry/success logging. It logs only if `dniv_sock_recvmsg()` itself is about to return a negative value. Since every visible DECnet receive path returns named errno values other than EPERM, a reproduced userspace EPERM with no matching kernel negative-return record will prove the rejection occurs before the protocol callback in generic `security_socket_recvmsg()`; a matching record will identify the exact protocol return instead.

Failure-only diagnostic run `35532853384` on `304baa4e5e58e83d891076000f3271ea2ed7f4a6` reproduced the defect without success-path logging and proved it is inside the DECnet protocol callback: `dniv_sock_recvmsg()` itself logged `ret=-1` for object 243 immediately before userspace received EPERM. This overturns the earlier LSM hypothesis. The callback source has no intentional `-EPERM` return, so the next diagnostic tags the small number of assignments that can feed a negative return: pre/post-allocation socket-state checks, `dniv_nsp_recv_message()`, wait interruption, post-wake link status, and copy-to-user. Logging remains failure-only; successful timing is unchanged apart from integer stage assignments.

The stage-tag candidate `8899d1eeae6a298e99bbc35af616a02866df83e1` failed to compile because a broad edit inserted the diagnostic assignment into `dniv_wait_running()`. That stray assignment is removed and stage 20 is now placed only at the receive wait in `dniv_sock_recvmsg()`. No protocol behavior changes.

Corrected stage run `35533900407` on `ab6411443ee4030349c68a21771f108223da45d4` reproduced the failure and localized it exactly: `dniv_sock_recvmsg()` returned `-1` at stage 20, the `wait_event_interruptible_timeout()` assignment. The NSP receive call itself was not the source. Since that wait normally returns zero, positive remaining jiffies, or `-ERESTARTSYS`, the literal `-1` strongly suggests the timeout value entering the wait may itself be `-1` when the wait condition is already satisfied. The next failure-only diagnostic records both the wait input and `sk_rcvtimeo`; no success-path logging is added.

Root cause established from interop run `35534273593` on `6f10ee87d4491bed64bcaaff5002eb4d8ac59882`: the failing receive entered `wait_event_interruptible_timeout()` with both `timeo` and `sk_rcvtimeo` equal to `MAX_SCHEDULE_TIMEOUT` (`LONG_MAX`), then `dniv_sock_recvmsg()` returned literal `-1`. Several socket paths stored the wait macro's `long` return value in an `int ret`. On 64-bit kernels, a successful wake with an infinite timeout can return `LONG_MAX`; conversion to 32-bit int yields `-1`, which userspace reports as EPERM. Timing-heavy diagnostics masked the defect by allowing data to arrive before the wait.

The fix separates protocol errno (`int ret`) from wait results (`long wait_ret`) in every affected DECnet socket path: normal and OOB send flow-control waits, stream receive, seqpacket/OOB receive, and listener accept. `dniv_wait_running()` already used a long result and required no change. Failure diagnostics are removed. This fixes the reproduced close/reaccept failure and the same latent truncation hazard in blocking send, stream receive, and accept. Exact-SHA fast acceptance must now prove the 16-cycle no-delay close/reaccept stress and x86_64/aarch64 E1.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance of the wait-width fix `31284e3437010ea2c7bc1ae41a0a9554ce8ea471` proved the Phase 5 socket defect fixed in independent PyDECnet interoperability run `35534785016`: normal backlog passed, the backlog=2/3-client negative case produced exactly one reason-6 rejection, and all 16 close/echo/reaccept cycles passed without diagnostic timing. Build and Project State gates were green. ARM64 E1 also passed. x86_64 E1 run `35534783548` failed for a separate harness race: DN70 observed DN71's expired adjacency transition directly from UP with expires=0 to INIT when DN71 restarted, never sampling a moment where the adjacency row was absent. The harness therefore failed to set `seen_expired`; DN71 later completed its own recovery proof and powered off, after which DN70 recorded that second disappearance and waited for a recovery that could never occur.

E1 now treats an INIT transition after the established-UP phase as valid evidence that the prior adjacency expired and the peer restarted, matching the kernel state transition actually observed on x86_64. A physically absent adjacency remains valid evidence too. This is test-harness-only; no protocol behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance of the socket fix plus corrected E1 discriminator.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for `9c303ed5d461503c4f0443f260386922bd2c03ae` is fully green: Repository Policy `35535312705`, Build Bootstrap `35535336273`, Project State Gate `35535337506`, Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35535339578`, and E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35535338581` on both x86_64 and ARM64. The PyDECnet run again passed the four-entry backlog case, the exact object-busy overflow negative case, and all 16 no-delay close/reaccept cycles. The 64-bit wait-result truncation defect and the unrelated E1 restart-discriminator race are therefore resolved at fast acceptance depth.

Phase 5 now advances to peer abort/reset isolation. A new object-244 interop server accepts an ABORTER and SURVIVOR child on the same listener for eight successive rounds. Pinned PyDECnet aborts one running logical link with Session reason 9 and disconnect data while the sibling remains active; Linux must return EOF on the aborted child, preserve the reason/data through DSO_DISDATA, continue lossless echo on the sibling, and then capture that sibling's orderly reason-0 disconnect data. This directly tests that peer abort/teardown cannot corrupt another accepted child or listener state.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for peer-abort isolation candidate `a4741f85d8f65481792c6f507f1090b2b6f4f587` is fully green: Repository Policy `35536040433`, Build Bootstrap `35536091528`, Project State Gate `35536092812`, Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35536095581`, and E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35536094108` on x86_64 and ARM64. The PyDECnet reset scenario passed eight rounds of reason-9 abort/disconnect-data capture while a sibling accepted link remained lossless, followed by orderly sibling disconnect-data capture. No cross-child corruption or listener damage was observed.

Phase 5 now tests listener teardown with pending connections. Object 245 receives three concurrent PyDECnet requests on a backlog-four listener. Linux accepts one child, closes the listener while two requests remain pending, and must reject exactly those two with Session reason 6 while the already accepted child remains usable for echo. This distinguishes listener ownership from accepted-child lifetime and directly exercises `dniv_listener_unregister()` under live pending state.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for listener-close candidate `6ea6195ed8451d09e21f16af5ebcdeedb828722c` is fully green: Repository Policy `35537665524`, Build Bootstrap `35537685972`, Project State Gate `35537687056`, Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35537688924`, and E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35537688053` on x86_64 and ARM64. The independent PyDECnet listener-close case produced exactly one accepted child and two reason-6 pending rejections, while the accepted child remained usable. Prior backlog, overflow, 16-cycle close/reaccept, and eight-round peer-abort isolation cases also remained green.

The final close/reset-race discriminator before a consolidated Phase 5 checkpoint is simultaneous termination. Object 246 accepts four live PyDECnet children; two abort with reason 9 and distinct disconnect data while two perform orderly reason-0 disconnects at the same barrier. Linux must observe EOF and the correct per-link reason/data on every child without cross-link contamination, then accept a fresh fifth connection on the same listener and complete echo plus orderly disconnect.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for simultaneous-termination candidate `35ba4cbeb020f944b2e19c6ba808595228496f1b` is fully green: Repository Policy `35538239304`, Build Bootstrap `35538290607`, Project State Gate `35538292068`, Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35538294648`, and E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35538293448` on x86_64 and ARM64. The independent PyDECnet termination-race case passed with two simultaneous reason-9 aborts, two simultaneous orderly disconnects, correct per-link disconnect data, no cross-link contamination, and a fresh fifth connection succeeding on the same listener. The complete listener/backlog/close/reset race tranche is therefore fast-green.

This closes the current related Phase 5 socket-lifecycle tranche and moves to a consolidated socket checkpoint before further NSP transport work. The next protocol work after that checkpoint is loss/retransmission/timer behavior rather than additional listener lifetime variants.
Consolidated socket-checkpoint interoperability run `35538768718` exposed a real immediate-post-accept race on both ARM64 PyDECnet scenarios while both x86_64 PyDECnet scenarios and both Route20 L1 scenarios passed. PyDECnet received CC for all four object-246 children, but the fourth Linux `accept()` returned `ECONNREFUSED`; the peer then saw recovery rejected with Session reason 6 after the failed server closed its listener. The race is in `dniv_sock_accept_impl()`: after successfully sending CC it waits to sample NSP RUN, but a fast peer disconnect can advance CC -> RUN -> CLOSED before that waiter runs. The accepted child was then incorrectly discarded even though its terminal reason/data remained available in the NSP connection. The socket accept path now treats that retained CLOSED state after successful local CC as an accepted-then-terminated child, returns the socket, and lets normal `recv()` EOF plus `DSO_DISDATA` semantics expose the peer termination. Other accept failures still fail normally. Exact-SHA consolidated acceptance is required next.


Exact-SHA consolidated socket acceptance for immediate-post-accept race fix `dc62cdb2ab1494069fe8d2d1c546a18d0ae8a279` is green: Repository Policy `35540170709`, Build Bootstrap `35540191816`, Project State Gate `35540192698`, External Reference Baselines `35540193641`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35540194520` and `35540195378`, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35540196173`. All six consolidated Route20/PyDECnet interop jobs passed. Both formerly failing ARM64 PyDECnet object-246 cases completed simultaneous termination with four children (two aborts, two orderly disconnects) and a successful recovery connection. The socket-lifecycle checkpoint is therefore consolidated-green.

Phase 5 now resumes NSP loss/retransmission/timer work. Review against DNA NSP V4.0.1 found a concrete flow/error-control defect: timed normal-data retransmission ignored a peer XOFF after the segment had already been sent. Section 2.6.3.1 permits retransmission permission for a previously transmitted Data Segment to be withdrawn, and section 7.1 requires retransmission only when flow-control variables allow it. The timer path now suppresses DATA-channel retransmission while `data_xon` is false, without consuming the retransmit limit; OTHER-channel retransmission remains independent. Once XON restores permission, the already-expired DATA entry is eligible on the next timer pass. A shared state helper and unit assertions lock this rule. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then add deterministic live packet/ACK-loss interoperability coverage for retransmission and timer exhaustion.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for the XOFF retransmission correction `c344fe2e639833f9c0c4e9783e53b8cdc0672da0` is green: Repository Policy `35541472035`, Build Bootstrap `35541493006`, Project State Gate `35541493864`, E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35541494883` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35541495840`. The data-channel flow/error-control correction is therefore accepted at development depth.

Phase 5 now adds deterministic live retransmission evidence. A new native MIRROR loss probe establishes RUN, announces a synchronization marker, sends exactly one marked application record, and waits for the echo. The host then blackholes only PyDECnet-logical-MAC unicast toward the candidate for eight seconds using a TAP ingress filter, while multicast adjacency traffic remains untouched. The injector must report a nonzero matched-packet count. PCAP validation requires at least two candidate copies of the unique record (the original plus timeout retransmission) and a later reference response after fault removal. This is harness/test coverage only; candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance of the live loss proof, then timer/retry-exhaustion and recovery coverage.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for live loss/retransmission proof candidate `900b11f553790286cb26b6d8517255c3bc488e32` is green: Repository Policy `35542070424`, Build Bootstrap `35542088254`, Project State Gate `35542089304`, E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35542090329` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35542091425`. The retained pcap shows the single marked candidate application record twice and the reference response once after fault removal, while the tc injector counter proves the requested unicast loss actually occurred.

Retry-exhaustion review found a semantic gap in the candidate. PyDECnet's pinned NSP tests close a logical link with Session reason 39 (node unreachable) when the retransmit limit is exhausted, and the pinned LinuxDECnet compatibility reference surfaces the same condition as `EHOSTUNREACH`. The candidate previously purged and erased the NSP connection immediately on retransmit exhaustion, losing the terminal reason and causing a blocked receive to wake as ordinary EOF. Retransmit exhaustion now purges queued transport data but retains the connection in CLOSED state with reason 39 until normal socket release. Native sockets map that terminal state to `EHOSTUNREACH`, expose `EPOLLERR|EPOLLHUP`, and retain reason 39 through `DSO_DISDATA`; ordinary peer disconnect EOF semantics are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then add deterministic retransmit-limit exhaustion and fresh-link recovery interoperability coverage.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance of retransmit-exhaustion terminal-state candidate `9f486435c5c35576c6ae2ce17b41b1a06a8a419c` is green: Repository Policy `35542589965`, Build Bootstrap `35542611007`, Project State Gate `35542612321`, E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35542613688` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35542615041`. Normal loss/recovery and the full existing socket stress suite remain green.

Phase 5 now adds live retry-exhaustion evidence. A native MIRROR connection sends one uniquely marked record while host ingress filtering drops all PyDECnet logical unicast toward the candidate until the NSP five-attempt response budget is exhausted. The native socket must wake with `EHOSTUNREACH`, report reason 39 and no payload through `DSO_DISDATA`, and expose `POLLERR|POLLHUP`. The injector must prove nonzero matched traffic; PCAP must show all five marked candidate transmissions. After the fault is removed, a fresh MIRROR connection must complete before listener tests continue. This candidate adds test coverage only; protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then connection-establishment/control retransmission and timer-boundary coverage.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for retry-limit exhaustion/recovery candidate `e803482a0cac568e44708fcdd1fa6d54e38bb5ef` is green: Repository Policy `35543151094`, Build Bootstrap `35543165444`, Project State Gate `35543166349`, E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35543167217` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35543168107`. The retained pcap contains five marked candidate exhaustion attempts; native userspace proved `EHOSTUNREACH`, reason 39 through `DSO_DISDATA`, `POLLERR|POLLHUP`, and successful fresh-link recovery.

Phase 5 now extends loss coverage to connection-control retransmission. The host blackholes PyDECnet-to-candidate unicast before a marked MIRROR `connect()`; CC replies are lost while CI/RCI traffic still reaches the independent peer. The connect must terminate with `EHOSTUNREACH`, pcap must contain one marked CI plus four RCIs, and after fault removal a fresh marked connection plus MIRROR record must succeed without restart. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then delayed-ACK/NAK/flow-control timer edges.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for connection-control retry exhaustion candidate `2c646d82a0f6a7c49df17ebafae67785185a383f` is green: Repository Policy `35544499145`, Build Bootstrap `35544514739`, Project State Gate `35544515810`, E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35544516934` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35544517875`. Packet capture proved one marked CI plus four marked RCIs under deterministic lost CC responses, followed by a fresh connection and bidirectional marked data after fault removal.

NSP ACK review then found a concrete Other-Data error-control defect. The candidate treated a normal ACK_OTHER as valid only when its ACKNUM exactly equaled the first outstanding Other-Data sequence. Phase IV ACK numbers are cumulative for both Data and Other-Data subchannels; the pinned PyDECnet and LinuxDECnet implementations likewise retire all acknowledged entries through ACKNUM. If an earlier interrupt/link-service ACK was lost and a later cumulative ACK_OTHER arrived, the candidate could therefore retain already acknowledged Other-Data and eventually retransmit or time it out incorrectly. The special exact-only Other-Data path is removed so both channels use the same wrap-safe cumulative ACK/NAK logic. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue sequencing/resource and malformed-ACK negative coverage.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for cumulative Other-Data ACK candidate `d62bf7cb5c86ca5cee2f0c199872978ead6828c6` is green: Repository Policy `35544925880`, Build Bootstrap `35544944645`, Project State Gate `35544945676`, E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35544946708` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35544947674`.

NSP receive-resource review found a bounded-cache deadlock and subchannel-isolation defect. A single global 32-entry receive limit allowed out-of-order future segments to consume every slot; the missing lower-numbered segment was then rejected with `ENOSPC`, so the cached higher segments could never become contiguous and drain. The same global limit also allowed normal-data backlog to block the independently flow-controlled Other-Data subchannel. DNA NSP V4.0.1 sections 2.6.4, 2.7.1 and 6.5 explicitly allow out-of-order segments to be discarded instead of cached, and section 6.5 forbids discarding lower-numbered data while higher-numbered data remains cached. Receive bounds are now per subchannel. A future packet arriving to a full subchannel cache is left unacknowledged for peer retransmission; when the missing expected packet arrives to a full cache, the farthest cached future packet on that subchannel is reclaimed first so forward progress is guaranteed. Total receive accounting remains available for diagnostics. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then malformed ACK-field and sequence-boundary negatives.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for receive-cache deadlock candidate `551029224a621c5f11b3ae3a280c51e14b965ca0` is green: Repository Policy `35545504071`, Build Bootstrap `35545526646`, Project State Gate `35545527462`, E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35545528555` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35545529422`.

Audit of the socket-lifecycle harness found a false-green discriminator defect in `dnbacklog.c`: the listener-close implementation was nested inside `if (close_race)`, but the command-line modes are mutually exclusive. The intended path that accepts one child, closes the listener with two requests still pending, then proves the accepted child remains usable was therefore unreachable. Listener-close mode instead fell through the generic one-accept path and did not prove the intended lifetime ordering. The listener-close block is now a separate mode before the close-race loop. No kernel/protocol behavior changes in this candidate. Prior listener-close acceptance evidence is superseded; exact-SHA fast acceptance must re-prove one accepted child, two reason-6 pending rejections, and surviving child I/O.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for corrected listener-close coverage candidate `dfd249b02fe0b72b87d088523a9603630a641290` is green: Repository Policy `35545967240`, Build Bootstrap `35545980316`, Project State Gate `35545981269`, E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35545982418` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35545983392`. The repaired discriminator actually executed and proved one accepted object-245 child remained usable while the two still-pending requests were rejected with reason 6.

Connection-response timer review found two Phase 5 liveness defects. First, the generic 30-second connection deadline could erase CI/CC state before delayed timer work reached the normal retransmit-limit path, replacing the proven node-unreachable terminal result with an anonymous reset under scheduler stalls. CI and CC are now left to their response/retransmit timers. Second, an inbound CR or outbound CD Session-Control response timeout was silently erased. The pinned PyDECnet NSP reference rejects an unanswered inbound CI with reason 38 and reports the same reason locally when a delivered outbound connect is never accepted/rejected. CR timeout now sends a retransmitted DI reason 38; CD timeout retains a CLOSED reason-38 terminal state. Socket listener notification also removes terminal pre-accept links from the circular pending queue immediately, preventing timed-out or peer-aborted requests from permanently consuming backlog slots. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then add bounded timer-specific evidence without extending every fast interop path.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for connection-response timeout semantics candidate `ad754ae503ba0bf7d0fa9b7d877db6c148dae547` is green: Repository Policy `35546873484`, Build Bootstrap `35546897584`, Project State Gate `35546898681`, E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35546899879` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35546901103`.

A bounded independent timer discriminator is now added at consolidated/full depth only. In the x64 PyDECnet L1 job, native object 247 uses backlog one and intentionally leaves the first inbound CI in CR beyond the 30-second Session-Control response bound. PyDECnet must receive reject reason 38, packet capture must prove that the candidate actually emitted a reason-38 DI, and a second connection must then be accepted and echo `DNIV-CR-TIMEOUT-RECOVER`. The second connection is also the backlog-leak discriminator: it cannot succeed if the expired first CR remains in the listener's one-slot pending ring. Fast development runs retain their existing duration; the timer case executes once per consolidated/full interoperability matrix. Next: consolidated exact-SHA acceptance, then continue DC/no-resources and malformed ACK/sequence negatives.


Consolidated acceptance of timer-proof candidate `4263ee43307c5c6842bcb8b2f342a05f5d27949d` exposed an unrelated ARM64 E4 harness lifetime race in VM Lab run `35547989271`, job `106177300615`. L1A, L2A and L1B reached their E4 pass path, but L1A's fixed 600-second post-readiness sleep expired while endpoint evidence was still in progress; the controller then correctly failed with `E4 guest exited early: L1A`. The E4 controller already owns transit-router teardown and checks that routers remain alive until both endpoints finish, so fixed router self-termination contradicts that ownership and creates a false failure under slow six-guest ARM64 TCG skew. L1/L2 E4 guests now remain alive after READY until the controller terminates them through QMP. No routing or NSP behavior changes. The x64 PyDECnet L1 timer discriminator itself passed reason-38 rejection and fresh-link recovery, with PCAP counts `candidate_cr_timeout_reason38=1`, `candidate_cr_timeout_recovery=1`, and `reference_cr_timeout_recovery=1`. Next: repeat exact-SHA consolidated acceptance.


Exact-SHA consolidated acceptance for E4 controller-owned transit-router candidate `8cf0e121ebe273447c500381ebe44ee374c440cc` is fully green: Repository Policy `35549058759`, Build Bootstrap `35549108369`, Project State Gate `35549109446`, External Reference Baselines `35549110505`, E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35549111412` on x86_64 and ARM64, E4 Python QEMU VM Lab `35549112413` on x86_64 and ARM64, and all six Independent Ethernet Interoperability jobs in `35549113555`. The consolidated x64 PyDECnet L1 path also re-proved CR response timeout reason 38 and fresh-link recovery.

Malformed ACK review found a parser acceptance gap against the pinned PyDECnet negative corpus: ACK_DATA/ACK_OTHER accepted arbitrary non-ACK trailing bytes after a valid mandatory ACK field. The parser now requires the ACK message to be fully consumed. An optional present ACK word with invalid QUAL remains deliberately tolerated and ignored, matching the independent reference, because that word is consumed before QUAL validation. Unit coverage locks both cases. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then reserved-port No Resources/No Link receive-dispatch semantics.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for malformed-ACK candidate `37f82031790e7da584940259703f94074204a5fc` is green: Repository Policy `35550252765`, Build Bootstrap `35550274983`, Project State Gate `35550276263`, E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35550277558` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35550278702`.

Reserved-port receive-dispatch review found two Phase IV gaps against the pinned PyDECnet and LinuxDECnet references. When all local NSP connection slots (or link addresses) are exhausted, an incoming CI/RCI must receive Disconnect Confirm reason 1 (No Resources) rather than being silently dropped with local `ENOSPC`. Data/Interrupt/Link-Service traffic that cannot map to the destination/source link pair must receive Disconnect Confirm reason 41 (No Link); ACKs remain silently ignored on a bad mapping, matching the reference negative corpus. Received Disconnect Confirm now retains its reason in CLOSED state until socket release instead of erasing the connection immediately, so outbound connect rejection/no-resource status and running-link No-Link termination remain observable through normal socket status and `DSO_DISDATA`. Reason 42 is named as Disconnect Complete. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then add bounded independent reserved-port wire evidence and continue sequence-boundary negatives.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for reserved-port error reply candidate `b6932bc3bbb1589a9d7022cb714deb16e1170d7d` is green: Repository Policy `35550914584`, Build Bootstrap `35550933006`, Project State Gate `35550934357`, E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35550935699` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35550937333`.

A receive-dispatch cross-check against pinned PyDECnet's Phase IV reserved-port `nolinkset` found the prior No-Link predicate incomplete: unmapped or wrong-source Connect Confirm and Disconnect Initiate are also No-Link cases, alongside Data, Interrupt and Link Service. ACKs and Disconnect Confirm remain excluded to avoid inappropriate responses/loops. Both unknown-destination and bad-source-link mapping paths now use the complete Phase IV set. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then bounded independent No Resources/No Link wire evidence and sequence-boundary negatives.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for complete NSP No-Link receive dispatch candidate `e80644dd485ea12815ab87e57904e57dd9ca9339` is green: Repository Policy `35551483227`, Build Bootstrap `35551507570`, Project State Gate `35551508723`, E1 Python QEMU VM Lab `35551509771` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35551510921`.

The next test-only successor proves reserved-port responses on the wire and locks sequence-boundary negatives. The x64 PyDECnet L1 path waits until all ordinary socket/lifecycle tests finish, then injects one valid CC to an unmapped local link plus 320 valid CIs with unique source-link IDs. The burst must drive the bounded 256-link NSP table to exhaustion. Candidate PCAP evidence must contain DC reason 41 (No Link) and DC reason 1 (No Resources), and the guest requires at least 260 additional non-hello receives before it may continue. Shared unit coverage now locks 12-bit wrap, the 2048 half-range boundary, cumulative-ACK half-range exclusion, and wrap-window rejection. No product behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP flow-control/delayed-ACK and malformed receive-state negatives.


Fast acceptance of reserved-port wire-proof candidate `dda5f6d747ea026d4735280ed1ae2ef0a2e12bb8` failed only x64 PyDECnet L1 interop run `35552594441`, job `106189987949`: the injector delivered all 320 synthetic CIs, but PCAP contained no candidate No-Resources DC. The test used the live PyDECnet node address so candidate replies were routable; PyDECnet therefore received each early reject and promptly confirmed it, recycling NSP slots before the 256-entry table could remain full. The proof now installs a bounded TAP-egress drop for candidate-to-reference unicast during only the synthetic reserved-port burst. Bridge PCAP still observes the candidate's responses, while the independent peer cannot consume/confirm them until the table-exhaustion response has been generated. A nonzero tc counter is mandatory, then the filter is removed. Product protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: rerun exact-SHA fast socket acceptance and require both reason-41 and reason-1 DC evidence.


The first peer-isolation rerun `25a3e47fedc5b87ecac6d09aef795ce340e2f2be` failed earlier in the reserved-port discriminator: the egress isolation filter matched zero replies. Root cause is the injector's Ethernet source identity, not the protocol implementation. It emitted short-data frames with the host bridge MAC while embedding the live DECnet peer node address; the Routing Layer correctly rejects data whose Ethernet source is not the canonical DECnet MAC of an UP adjacency, so the non-hello receive counter advanced before NSP delivery but no reserved-port response could be generated. The injector now derives Ethernet source `AA-00-04-00-xx-xx` directly from the embedded source node, matching the already-UP PyDECnet adjacency. The candidate-to-peer egress drop from the prior correction then prevents the real peer from recycling rejected connections while bridge PCAP observes the candidate replies. Product behavior remains unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for reserved-port wire proof `6c54859389257f7337c044f79c29b1a1e2ec8f35` is green: Repository Policy `35554033965`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35554063023`, and x64 PyDECnet L1 Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35554064466`. The corrected injector reached NSP with a canonical DECnet source MAC; reply isolation held slots long enough to produce both DC reason 41 (No Link) and DC reason 1 (No Resources). Next: flow-control/delayed-ACK and malformed receive-state negatives.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for continuity commit `a16acc77b63ac2faf6b21a1bd53f44cceca323b1` is green: Repository Policy `35555005673`, Build Bootstrap `35555026456`, Project State Gate `35555027572`, E1 `35555028576` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35555029675`.

The next Phase 5 correction tightens delayed-ACK semantics against the pinned PyDECnet reference. Once a delayed ACK is pending, additional DLY data no longer restart the holdoff deadline, preventing sustained traffic from postponing the ACK indefinitely. Only normal Data Segment bit 12 is treated/emitted as DLY; Interrupt and Link Service sequence high bits are ignored rather than creating a delayed-ACK request, matching their fixed `dly=0` reference semantics. Shared unit coverage locks the non-sliding holdoff and other-data high-bit behavior. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then remaining malformed receive-state negatives.


Build Bootstrap `35555522237` rejected delayed-ACK candidate `e8a21b8230d0e07dbc7b5b986574c66ff993da9e` on x64 unit coverage before kernel build: the new other-data high-bit vectors set bit 15, which is the ACK-present discriminator and therefore correctly entered optional-ACK decoding instead of the sequence field. The vectors now exercise only reserved sequence bits 12-14 (`0x70` high byte), leaving bit 15 clear. Product code is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance.


Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for `33385c8abce1533ab602bb191404e19961b740df` is green: Repository Policy `35555625293`, Build Bootstrap `35555646942`, Project State Gate `35555647917`, E1 `35555648936` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35555649884`.

Receive-state review found a loss-recovery gap for duplicate Connect Confirm. The pinned PyDECnet RUN handler explicitly acknowledges a retransmitted `ConnConf`; the candidate instead rejected CC once it had entered RUN. If the first data ACK of CC were lost, that could leave the peer retransmitting CC until failure. CC receive handling now distinguishes first acceptance (CI/CD), duplicate re-ACK (RUN), and invalid states; duplicate CC sends ACK_DATA(0) without replacing accept data or changing state. Shared state-action unit coverage locks the valid/duplicate/invalid matrix. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue receive-state loss negatives.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for duplicate-CC recovery candidate `3a7bc799b43fb14ad390aa4b9bed2288b6305ec4` is green: Repository Policy `35556210489`, Build Bootstrap `35556235889`, Project State Gate `35556237313`, E1 `35556239238` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35556240811`.

Pinned PyDECnet restarts RUN inactivity on every received packet before handling a duplicate Connect Confirm. The candidate now does the same while re-ACKing duplicate CC, so loss of the original CC acknowledgement cannot leave an otherwise active link carrying a stale inactivity deadline. First CC acceptance still records accept data exactly once; duplicate CC remains idempotent apart from ACK/rearm. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue receive-state loss negatives.


Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for inactivity-rearm candidate `136aedce9aa49f0344d2d082cc9c9057575507ff` is green: Repository Policy `35557146528`, Build Bootstrap `35557164398`, Project State Gate `35557165658`, E1 `35557167128` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35557168496`.

Receive-state cross-check found duplicate CI/RCI handling was still too broad. Pinned PyDECnet only re-ACKs an already-mapped incoming CI while the connection is CR or CC; once that link has advanced to RUN or a terminal state, the unexpected duplicate is discarded. Route20 agrees that RUN/default states do not emit a new Connect Ack. The candidate previously sent ACK_CONN for any remote-link match, including RUN/CLOSED. Duplicate CI/RCI re-ACK is now restricted to CR/CC, with all other mapped states discarded without Session re-notification or wire response. Shared unit coverage locks the state matrix. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue receive-state loss negatives.



Exact-SHA fast acceptance for duplicate-CI mapping candidate `1d39475fd258f877063809f26b0cc6c7a9072bef` is green: Repository Policy `35557627099`, Build Bootstrap `35557644703`, Project State Gate `35557646170`, E1 `35557647643` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35557648866`.

Receive-state mapping review found stale Connect Ack handling differed from the pinned PyDECnet NSP port-mapping rules. ACK_CONN is valid only while an outbound connection is in CI; once the connection has moved to CD, RUN, or any other state, the packet must be silently discarded. The candidate previously returned `EINVAL` for these stale/duplicate ACK_CONN packets. It now drops them without changing state or producing a response, while CI still clears the outstanding connect control and enters CD. Shared unit coverage locks the one-valid-state matrix. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue receive-state and flow-control loss negatives.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for stale-ACK_CONN discard candidate `2958449c9c37d7c8a3fec2141bf08f65e3a05595` is green: Repository Policy `35558135839`, Build Bootstrap `35558155273`, Project State Gate `35558156428`, E1 `35558157531` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35558158493`.

Malformed-control review against the pinned PyDECnet packet decoder and LinuxDECnet DC receive path found that fixed-format control packets still accepted unparsed trailing bytes. Connect Confirm and Disconnect Initiate now require their declared image field to consume the entire packet, and Disconnect Confirm now requires its exact seven-byte wire length. CI/RCI remain variable-payload messages and are unchanged. Unit negatives lock trailing-byte rejection for CC, DI and DC. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue receive-dispatch silent-drop and flow-control negatives.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for malformed-control candidate `ef929e81326dbce0ea9dda270159429dd0a98dc9` is green: Repository Policy `35558671026`, Build Bootstrap `35558692168`, Project State Gate `35558693029`, E1 `35558693935` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35558694959`.

Control-message compatibility review found that the empty Session Control I field is optional on the wire. Pinned PyDECnet's tolerant I decoder accepts a fixed-header-only Connect Confirm or Disconnect Initiate as an empty field, and LinuxDECnet likewise only parses the field when bytes remain. The candidate previously rejected those valid empty forms. CC now accepts its 9-byte fixed header and DI its 7-byte fixed header/reason as empty Session Control data, while retaining exact declared-length consumption, the 16-byte bound, and exact-length DC validation. Unit coverage locks both omitted-I forms. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue receive-dispatch silent-drop and flow-control negatives.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for omitted-control-I candidate `b91e122e97a77944358297c0bb507f2d736e684b` is green: Repository Policy `35559162597`, Build Bootstrap `35559181671`, Project State Gate `35559182650`, E1 `35559183636` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35559184617`.

Socket flow-control review found the normal-data writability gate counted all NSP retransmit entries against the 20-segment Data window. Other-Data traffic therefore reduced the effective Data window and could leave a blocking writer asleep after a Data ACK even though `dniv_nsp_send_data()` itself had room to send. Connection snapshots now expose per-subchannel outstanding counts, and send admission plus poll/wait readiness use the Data count for the 20-segment window while still enforcing the shared 64-entry retransmit bound and XON state. Unit coverage locks the mixed Data/Other case, the Data-window limit, the global limit and XOFF. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then add outbound DLY selection and continue flow-control/loss negatives.

Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for data-window readiness candidate `0d916012d551943e3f20f03c4b8ca1a0c7b87694` is green: Repository Policy `35592768107`, Build Bootstrap `35592803268`, Project State Gate `35592806080`, E1 `35592808968` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35592812174`.

Pinned PyDECnet Phase IV transmit behavior requests delayed acknowledgement only while the Data queue is at most half full before enqueue. Native outbound Data now sets DLY at that same boundary: with the 20-segment window, existing outstanding counts 0 through 10 request delayed ACK, while 11 and above request immediate ACK. The wire flag is chosen before queueing so retransmissions preserve the original packet image. Unit coverage locks the boundary. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue flow-control/loss negatives and clean-shutdown ordering.

Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for outbound-DLY candidate `80f31de7ebf970e648878c7cac51746da3ac0715` is green: Repository Policy `35593564409`, Build Bootstrap `35593599143`, Project State Gate `35593601067`, E1 `35593603024` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35593605238`.

Clean local disconnect now follows the pinned PyDECnet ordering when normal Data remains unacknowledged: RUN is retained, the disconnect reason/data is saved, new Data/Interrupt transmission is blocked, delayed receive ACKs are suppressed, and DI is deferred until the Data retransmit queue is fully acknowledged. Socket writability is suppressed during that drain and inactivity keepalive generation is paused. Unit coverage locks the defer and delayed-ACK shutdown predicates. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then add a deterministic live clean-shutdown/loss proof and continue NSP flow-control negatives.

Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for clean-disconnect ordering candidate `1dadf0026e05d96eb8e4ae9fe528079970a0b4b1` is green: Repository Policy `35594478614`, Build Bootstrap `35594516941`, Project State Gate `35594519476`, E1 `35594522083` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35594524504`.

The next test-only successor adds deterministic live clean-shutdown loss proof against PyDECnet MIRROR. It drops reference-to-candidate unicast after a tagged Data send is queued and the application closes, holds the fault long enough to require retransmission, then restores the path and requires fresh MIRROR recovery. PCAP validation binds the tagged Data and subsequent DI by NSP source link and requires at least one retransmission before DI, proving local close drains outstanding normal Data before disconnect. No product behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP flow-control/loss negatives.

Fast acceptance of live clean-drain proof candidate `ede2ac119f4d182b0e09ff798659c9bf182672cf` failed only x64 PyDECnet interoperability run `35595756498` during candidate-image preparation.
