Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for live clean-drain proof candidate `d6bb4d9b74128caaff36d40582f5ccea5c6f3d74` is green: Repository Policy `35595967423`, Build Bootstrap `35596006568`, Project State Gate `35596008127`, E1 `35596009930` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35596011706`. The tagged loss proof confirms that local close retransmits unacknowledged normal Data before DI and recovers cleanly afterward.

Malformed optional-ACK review found that ACK words with the present bit set but reserved qualifier values 4-7 were consumed and silently ignored on Data/Interrupt/Link-Service packets. Optional ACK decoding now reports that malformed condition to the packet parser, which rejects the packet instead of continuing at the following sequence field. Existing regression coverage that previously documented the permissive behavior now requires rejection. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP flow-control/loss negatives.

The optional-ACK qualifier change in `fe9056d156809512cf78e0b420ba1391bddb905e` was superseded before acceptance: pinned PyDECnet intentionally consumes ACK-present words with reserved QUAL 4-7 and ignores their value, exactly matching the candidate's prior behavior. That tolerance is restored. The same fixed-format review found a real parser gap instead: Link Service accepted trailing bytes after LSFLAGS/FCVAL, while the pinned PyDECnet fixed layout has no payload. Link Service now requires exact packet consumption, with a trailing-byte negative vector. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP flow-control/loss negatives.

Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for Link Service fixed-length candidate `4c129fc36c0213b6d0828a342f4541c618fe8d4a` is green: Repository Policy `35597317720`, Build Bootstrap `35597360915`, Project State Gate `35597364000`, E1 `35597366693` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35597369540`.

NSP segment-size negotiation now follows the pinned PyDECnet behavior instead of always transmitting at the local 563-byte MSS. Incoming CI and first CC record min(peer SEG_SIZE, local MSS) on the connection; normal-data send enforces that negotiated bound; socket record segmentation uses it; and DSO_LINKINFO reports it. Zero peer segment size remains unusable rather than being silently promoted. Shared unit coverage locks smaller-peer, larger-peer and zero boundaries. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then add independent small-segment negotiation proof and continue NSP flow-control/loss negatives.

Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for negotiated-segment candidate `451affd5a89dc743833fb46cc765336ea2b17dec` is green: Repository Policy `35598667492`, Build Bootstrap `35598709272`, Project State Gate `35598711510`, E1 `35598714232` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35598716636`.

Socket-scope PyDECnet L1 interoperability now turns the pinned peer into a protocol-valid 128-byte NSP segment-size oracle without modifying its tracked source: a runtime-only `sitecustomize.py` lowers PyDECnet's imported MSS before NSP loads. PCAP validation requires a reference CC advertising 128, rejects any candidate Data payload above that negotiated limit, and requires multiple full 128-byte candidate segments so the existing 563/564/4096-byte MIRROR records prove real segmentation rather than merely accepting the option. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP flow-control/loss negatives.

Small-segment proof candidate `ce46f19e2cb8946e224b74288da838719dbc1eb6` built green and passed E1 on both architectures, but x64 PyDECnet interoperability run `35599580242` failed only the clean-drain PCAP postcondition. Exact packet evidence shows the candidate did the safe thing: tagged Data on local link 42 was retransmitted five times and no DI was sent because the small-segment PyDECnet peer never acknowledged the duplicate after the injected reply-loss window. The validator incorrectly required DI to eventually appear. It now enforces the actual ordering invariant instead: at least one Data retransmission is mandatory, and any DI for that link before a retransmission is a failure; absence of DI when the peer never acknowledges is valid. No product behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance.

Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for clean-drain proof correction `8567fe8ac9c834d6de43c60112ff45c538584a18` is green: Repository Policy `35600584379`, Build Bootstrap `35600584505`, Project State Gate `35600586681`, E1 `35600588856` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35600591119`.

The next socket-scope PyDECnet proof exercises live normal-data XOFF/XON rather than only unit predicates. A fresh MIRROR connection emits a tagged Data segment; a host raw-frame injector derives that connection's NSP link pair from the live frame, sends in-sequence Link Service XOFF and XON packets as the reference node, and requires the nonblocking candidate send to return EAGAIN while XOFF is active and a blocking send to resume after XON. The injector also observes the resumed tagged Data on the wire. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP loss/flow-control negatives.

Fast socket acceptance of live XOFF/XON proof candidate `7572f3e1a277ff0098cfde26ba135f762b65f5fa` failed only x64 PyDECnet interoperability run `35601855043`. The candidate-side nonblocking send remained writable because the injector never saw the tagged candidate frame: an AF_PACKET socket bound to the Linux bridge does not receive the QEMU TAP ingress frame used for discovery, although bridge transmission works for injection. Packet capture confirms no injected Link Service frame was emitted. The injector now sniffs the candidate TAP, where QEMU ingress is directly visible, while retaining a separate bridge-bound socket for reference-identity XOFF/XON transmission and capture visibility. Product code is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance.

The first TAP-sniff correction candidate `4137a8f9cc25b5a6967ae600be641950e657c161` still failed only the live flow-control proof in x64 PyDECnet run `35602424316`; no Link Service injection appeared in the PCAP, so product flow control was not exercised. The remaining harness defect is packet-socket protocol demultiplexing on TAP ingress: the custom EtherType socket saw no QEMU-injected frame. The sniffer now opens ETH_P_ALL and filters DECnet in its parser, while the separate transmit socket remains scoped to EtherType 0x6003. Explicit flushed injector milestones make subsequent evidence unambiguous. Product code is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance.

Fast socket acceptance of TAP-capture candidate `6844dd79c5fddd8834c1fcd16b96fd68ea6be770` failed only x64 PyDECnet interoperability run `35603117115`. The live flow injector opens AF_PACKET/SOCK_RAW sockets and was invoked without privilege; the runner therefore never injected XOFF/XON, while the candidate correctly remained writable. The orchestration now runs only that raw-socket injector under sudo, matching the existing reserved-port raw injector. Product code and proof semantics are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance.

Fast socket acceptance of candidate `39084fc71829380393dd7bc1f1b9e7ae18274807` failed only x64 PyDECnet interoperability run `35603708003`. Repository inspection found the decisive harness gap: the candidate-side `dnflow` probe and workflow selector were present, but the host-side `inject-nsp-flow.py` orchestration block had never landed in `run-interop.sh`. Therefore no XOFF/XON packet could be sent. The missing bounded orchestration is now inserted after clean-drain recovery, using privileged bridge sniff/injection and requiring both FLOW-READY and FLOW-PASS markers. Product code is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance.

Static review of live flow-control candidate `7ce48ebc2978a45f6f1ab22c29cf95344acf7012` found a deterministic harness error before acceptance completed: the XON transmission still referenced the removed single-socket variable `sock` instead of the split transmit socket `send`. XOFF could be injected, but the injector would raise NameError four seconds later and never release the blocked candidate writer. The one stale identifier is corrected; product code and protocol semantics are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance.

Fast socket acceptance for live XOFF/XON candidate `f0f700e836f67360f1505b4595bede323cd0911c` proved the new flow-control scenario end to end in PyDECnet run `35604824026`: the injector observed tagged Data, sent XOFF, the candidate blocked, XON resumed transmission, and the injector observed the resumed record. The run later failed only the older clean-drain PCAP assertion. Exact timestamps show its 4-second reply-drop window ended before the candidate's 5-second retransmission deadline plus one-second timer granularity; PyDECnet's retransmitted echo then supplied the ACK first. The clean-drain fault is extended to 7 seconds so at least one candidate Data retransmission is forced deterministically before replies can recover. Product code is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance.


Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for deterministic clean-drain candidate `1b3c29d74dc9ecb686a10c621c8a011810240d53` is green: Repository Policy `35605567036`, Build Bootstrap `35605622749`, Project State Gate `35605626090`, E1 `35605629674` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35605633210`. The clean-drain proof now deterministically includes a candidate retransmission before recovery; the live XOFF/XON proof also remained green.

Flow-control negative coverage now extends that live connection with sequence-order checks. After XOFF sequence 1, the injector sends future XON sequence 3 before the candidate's nonblocking blocked check; only in-order XON sequence 2 may release the writer. It then injects stale duplicate XOFF sequence 1, waits for it to settle, and requires a second tagged nonblocking candidate record to remain writable. The injector requires both resumed tags. This proves future Link Service state is cached without early application and stale duplicates cannot reapply flow state. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP flow-control/loss negatives.

Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for ordered flow-control candidate `67324f617646d61c4d22d1ea8c38eac985853226` is green: Repository Policy `35607362462`, Build Bootstrap `35607414708`, Project State Gate `35607417241`, E1 `35607419674` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35607421967`. Live evidence shows future XON was held until sequence closure and stale duplicate XOFF did not reapply flow state.

The same live flow proof now covers the timeout interaction required by Phase IV flow control. During the fresh MIRROR connection, the harness drops all real PyDECnet replies while raw Link Service injection remains available. XOFF is held across the candidate's five-second Data response deadline; the injector fails on any tagged Data retransmission during that interval. After in-order XON closes the sequence gap, the already-due tagged Data must retransmit, while the blocked new record and post-stale-XOFF record must also appear. The fault filter must match at least one real reference reply, preventing a vacuous proof. This matches the pinned PyDECnet rule that an expired Data retransmit remains unsent while flow is XOFF and is eligible again after XON. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP flow-control/loss negatives.

Static review of XOFF retransmit candidate `0d89cb7426c2ea691d14763a2cf4a65514252a0b` found a deterministic orchestration race before acceptance completed: the new reference-reply drop was installed immediately after FLOW-READY, but the candidate intentionally waits two seconds before starting `dnflow`, so the filter could suppress the MIRROR connection handshake itself rather than only its Data acknowledgments. The flow probe now publishes FLOW-CONNECTED after the independent peer has accepted the logical link and pauses three seconds; only then does the host install the reply-drop filter and start the raw Link Service injector. This makes the fault window begin after connection establishment and before the tagged Data is sent. Product behavior and proof assertions are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance.

Repository Policy `35608428544` on XOFF retransmit candidate `0d89cb7426c2ea691d14763a2cf4a65514252a0b` stopped before dispatch because the multi-file tree update accidentally reset `tests/lab/run-interop.sh` from executable to regular-file mode. The harness contents were not implicated. The executable bit is restored while retaining the post-connect fault-window correction. Product behavior and test semantics are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance.

Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for XOFF retransmit candidate `44f8dbe137ac5a4e6a1430e7869e110d89d74b50` is green: Repository Policy `35608774196`, Build Bootstrap `35608813361`, Project State Gate `35608815868`, E1 `35608818460` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35608821041`. The live proof holds XOFF across the response deadline, observes no forbidden retransmission, then observes the due retransmission after XON.

The next socket-scope loss negative proves that an out-of-range cumulative Data ACK cannot falsely drain the retransmit queue. A fresh MIRROR connection sends tagged Data while real PyDECnet replies are dropped. A raw reference-identity injector derives the live link pair and Data sequence, sends an ACK seven sequence numbers ahead of the only outstanding segment, and requires the tagged Data to retransmit on its normal response timer. The fault filter must match real reference replies, then the path is restored and the same logical link must receive the correct MIRROR echo. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP ACK/NAK and flow-control negatives.

Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for out-of-range ACK candidate `bafd77e440f78d843f19b572f4bed4a6ee6eaba2` is green: Repository Policy `35610134250`, Build Bootstrap `35610181088`, Project State Gate `35610184033`, E1 `35610187754` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35610191317`. Live evidence shows forged cumulative ACK 8 against the sole outstanding Data sequence 1 was ignored and the tagged segment retransmitted on its normal timer.

The same fault window now adds explicit NAK behavior. After the timer retransmission proves the forged future ACK did not drain state, the injector sends a Data NAK acknowledging sequence zero, i.e. immediately before the first outstanding sequence one. The candidate must retransmit the tagged Data again within 2.5 seconds, well before another normal five-second response timeout. This locks the intended Phase IV NAK fast-retransmit path without relying on the independent peer to originate a NAK. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP ACK/NAK and flow-control negatives.

Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for NAK candidate `b20b605229ae384645f2c725569a9e3984cff669` is green: Repository Policy `35611234764`, Build Bootstrap `35611286702`, Project State Gate `35611289909`, E1 `35611293373` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35611296994`. The live loss window proves a Data NAK for sequence zero forces prompt retransmission of outstanding sequence one.

ACK/NAK negative coverage now extends the same live outstanding Data segment across the Phase IV cross-subchannel qualifiers. An explicit ACK_OTHER carrying XACK with a forged future cumulative ACK must not drain the Data retransmit queue; the next tagged retransmission must arrive on the normal response timer rather than immediately. ACK_OTHER carrying XNAK for the sequence immediately before the outstanding Data must then force another prompt retransmission. This proves cross-subchannel routing, cumulative range validation, and NAK fast retransmit together. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP sequence-wrap, ACK/NAK and flow-control negatives.

Fast socket acceptance of cross-channel ACK candidate `c87526d40c67a87c916f43878f036f4728ecb15c` failed only x64 PyDECnet interoperability run `35612571928`. The injector itself proved all four requested behaviors, but stacking the already-accepted normal future-ACK timer retransmit and normal NAK retransmit ahead of the new cross-XACK/XNAK pair consumed the candidate's five-attempt retransmit budget. After the fault filter was removed, the next timer therefore closed the link with destination-unreachable before a real reference ACK could recover it. The cross-channel proof is now isolated on a fresh connection: forged future ACK_OTHER/XACK must survive to the normal timer, then ACK_OTHER/XNAK must force prompt retransmit, leaving retry headroom for reference-path recovery. Product code is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP sequence-wrap and flow-control negatives.

Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for isolated cross-channel ACK candidate `292f13708bdc9c3c26854d5ef45ea189d6831695` is green: Repository Policy `35613301589`, Build Bootstrap `35613361594`, Project State Gate `35613364677`, E1 `35613367237` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35613370335`. The fresh-link proof confirms forged future ACK_OTHER/XACK is ignored until the normal timer and ACK_OTHER/XNAK forces prompt retransmission while preserving recovery headroom.

The next socket-scope independent-peer proof drives one MIRROR logical link across the 12-bit NSP sequence rollover. With the fast socket peer advertising 128-byte segments, 130 verified 4096-byte records produce more than 4096 Data segments in each direction while retaining one connection. Every record is content-checked after reassembly; success therefore requires transmit sequencing, cumulative acknowledgments, receive ordering, reassembly, and reply sequencing to survive 4095-to-0 rollover against pinned PyDECnet. Product behavior is unchanged. Route20 VDE2 support requested for later distributed work is already present in the pinned fork at `a9ef7c0b7f875f0dd2e8abaf11213798a8e4474c` and remains the configured Route20 reference. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP flow-control/loss negatives.

Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for live sequence-rollover candidate `87b3b776dbd31253572bf63c0bcf1ea12f7dfaa9` is green: Repository Policy `35628701199`, Build Bootstrap `35628743562`, Project State Gate `35628746258`, E1 `35628749288` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35628752458`. The single MIRROR link exchanged 130 verified 4096-byte records with the peer advertising 128-byte segments, forcing normal-data sequence space through the 4095-to-0 rollover in both directions.

The cross-subchannel ACK loss proof now covers the exact modulo half-space boundary without spending another retransmit attempt. Before waiting for the one normal response timeout, the injector sends both the already-proven future ACK_OTHER/XACK at +7 and an ACK_OTHER/XACK exactly +2048 from the sole outstanding Data sequence. Neither may drain the retransmit queue or trigger an early retransmission; the same tagged segment must reappear only on the normal timer, after which XNAK must still force prompt retransmission. This locks the Phase IV 12-bit half-space ambiguity at the live packet path as well as in unit arithmetic. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP flow-control/loss negatives.


Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for half-space ACK candidate `685b1f2b0d0df1448239bfd0f2240ca25ae9c220` is green after an exact-SHA rerun: Repository Policy `35631426475`, Build Bootstrap `35631471540`, Project State Gate `35631474220`, E1 `35631476753` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35631479293`. Other-Data loss coverage now adds a fresh MIRROR logical link carrying a tagged outbound NSP Interrupt. The host drops the independent PyDECnet ACK_OTHER, requires the candidate to retransmit the identical Interrupt only after its normal response timer, then injects a valid reference-identity ACK_OTHER for that sequence and watches through another response interval to prove retransmission stops. The fault filter must match at least one real peer reply, and the same logical link must complete normal MIRROR traffic after the fault is removed. This exercises Other-Data retransmit timing and ACK recovery independently of normal Data XOFF state. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP loss/flow-control negatives.


Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for Other-Data loss candidate `eb1e70514ad3fee75d4bb40680a8f8bb0069585d` is green: Repository Policy `35632947071`, Build Bootstrap `35632991993`, Project State Gate `35632995040`, E1 `35632998357` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35633001550`. The live proof dropped the real ACK_OTHER, observed the tagged Interrupt retransmit only after the response timer, injected a valid ACK_OTHER, observed no further retransmit through another response interval, restored the independent path, and completed normal MIRROR traffic on the same logical link.

Interrupt flow-control coverage now separates ACK recovery from credit and exercises Other-Data sequence ordering. On a fresh MIRROR link the candidate sends one Interrupt, then must return EAGAIN for a second nonblocking Interrupt even after PyDECnet has acknowledged the first: ACK_OTHER is not a credit grant. The injector sends future Interrupt Request Link Service sequence 2 and requires the blocking second send to remain held, then sends missing sequence 1; sequence closure must release both the second Interrupt and an immediate third Interrupt using the cached second credit. Finally a stale duplicate sequence-1 grant with delta 100 must not recreate credit: a fourth nonblocking Interrupt must remain EAGAIN. Normal MIRROR data must still work afterward. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP loss/flow-control negatives.


Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for interrupt-credit ordering candidate `1393a6da15ecc91bd701dffed2473e73127f1560` is green: Repository Policy `35635574606`, Build Bootstrap `35635622913`, Project State Gate `35635625856`, E1 `35635628952` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35635631736`. The live proof confirms ACK_OTHER does not replenish Interrupt credit, a future Interrupt Request grant remains cached until the missing Other-Data sequence arrives, sequence closure releases both credits, and a stale duplicate grant cannot recreate credit.


The live ACK-range fault now also covers spoofed connection identity without adding another retransmit cycle. While the real PyDECnet reply path is suppressed and one Data segment is outstanding, the injector sends a numerically valid ACK for that exact sequence from the correct DECnet node identity but with an incorrect NSP source link. The candidate must ignore it just like the future and half-space cross-ACKs: the tagged Data must remain queued until the normal response timer. This directly exercises PP-06 malformed/spoofed connection-ID handling on an established logical link while preserving retry headroom for XNAK and path recovery. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP loss/flow-control negatives.


Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for spoofed-link ACK candidate `7435ee7a3fb688f0c37ae27b02160e8403c4b691` is green: Repository Policy `35639044766`, Build Bootstrap `35639090640`, Project State Gate `35639092916`, E1 `35639095518` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35639098368`. Live evidence shows a valid ACK number from the correct DECnet node but wrong NSP source link was ignored; the tagged Data remained outstanding until its response timer and the subsequent cross-XNAK fast retransmit still succeeded.


Spoofed-identity ACK coverage now includes all established-link identity axes in the same bounded loss window. Alongside the already-proven wrong NSP source-link ACK, the injector sends a valid ACK number to a non-existent local destination link and another valid ACK using the correct live link pair but a different DECnet source node/MAC identity. None may drain the retransmit queue or trigger early retransmission; the tagged Data must remain outstanding until the normal response timer, after which the existing XNAK proof still forces prompt retransmission. This closes PP-06 established-link spoof rejection for node identity plus both NSP connection IDs without consuming extra retry budget. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP loss/control negatives.


Unknown-link packet behavior is now proved alongside established-link spoof rejection. The injector sends a syntactically valid complete Data segment from the correct remote node identity but with deliberately unused source and destination NSP link IDs. The candidate must answer with Disconnect Confirm reason 41 (No Link), addressed back to those probe link IDs, while leaving the real connection and its outstanding tagged Data untouched. This covers the Phase IV reserved-port/no-link response path without risking the live peer connection. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue NSP lost-control and timer negatives.


Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for unknown-link response candidate `983c8a0714c264f2d031cc4589f0c50ffd7a55e1` is green: Repository Policy `35641889672`, Build Bootstrap `35641930405`, Project State Gate `35641933003`, E1 `35641935798` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35641938325`. The established live link survived all spoofed ACKs; the synthetic unknown-link Data packet received Disconnect Confirm reason 41 (No Link) on the probe link IDs, and the real outstanding Data still followed its timer/XNAK recovery path.

Lost Connect Confirm acknowledgement now has a deterministic independent-peer proof. A deferred native listener accepts a PyDECnet CI but waits four seconds before issuing DSO_CONACCEPT, allowing the host to arm a reference-to-candidate unicast drop and raw observer before the first CC. PyDECnet receives the CC but its ACK_DATA(0) is dropped; the observer must see an identical candidate CC retransmission no earlier than the response timeout. After the fault is removed, the same logical link must enter RUN and complete an echoed record plus orderly peer disconnect. This turns the already-accepted duplicate-CC state logic into live loss/recovery evidence. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue lost disconnect/control and timer negatives.


Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for lost-CC-ACK candidate `11519c7ea94c08eebe99cdf23629808063292d12` is green: Repository Policy `35643319482`, Build Bootstrap `35643360112`, Project State Gate `35643362852`, E1 `35643365745` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35643368217`. The raw observer saw the identical Connect Confirm retransmitted after 5.119 seconds while peer acknowledgements were dropped; after path restoration the same connection entered RUN, echoed data, and disconnected cleanly.

Disconnect control loss now has the matching live proof. A candidate MIRROR connection first exchanges normal data, then the host drops PyDECnet-to-candidate unicast before userspace calls SHUT_RDWR. The observer must see the identical Disconnect Initiate retransmitted only after the normal response timeout while at least one real peer confirmation is dropped. The socket remains open long enough for a later retransmitted DI to receive a confirmation after path restoration, then a fresh MIRROR connection must succeed. This proves DI control retransmission and stack recovery rather than merely application close ordering. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue control exhaustion, keepalive/inactivity and remaining PP-06 negatives.


Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for lost-Disconnect-Confirm candidate `4c2e3f8f3cd40ee5c86bf398afbfd37a23f47ed8` is green: Repository Policy `35645429917`, Build Bootstrap `35645490482`, Project State Gate `35645493211`, E1 `35645496009` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35645498585`. The live proof observed an identical Disconnect Initiate retransmission only after the response interval while peer confirmations were dropped, then restored the path and verified a fresh MIRROR connection.


Disconnect-control exhaustion now extends the accepted transient loss proof. On a fresh MIRROR link, the host suppresses every PyDECnet-to-candidate unicast confirmation before native userspace calls SHUT_RDWR. The candidate must consume its five-attempt control retransmission budget, terminate the link with local reason 39 (node unreachable), expose that reason through DSO_DISDATA plus POLLERR|POLLHUP, and then recover on a fresh MIRROR connection after the fault is removed. This distinguishes terminal DI retry exhaustion from the previously accepted one-retry recovery path. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then keepalive/inactivity and remaining PP-06 negatives.


Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for Disconnect Initiate exhaustion candidate `b0beba6fa23aa20d4d4e79f4f9cf28cb97050ea0` is green: Repository Policy `35648395182`, Build Bootstrap `35648447878`, Project State Gate `35648450806`, E1 `35648453673` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35648456285`. Reserved-port No Link coverage now spans every Phase IV packet class for which the pinned PyDECnet reference maps an unknown connection to its reserved port. In the existing bounded ACK-range fault window, the raw reference-identity injector sends syntactically valid Data, Connect Confirm, Disconnect Initiate, Interrupt, and Link Service packets using deliberately unused NSP source/destination link IDs. Each must receive Disconnect Confirm reason 41 addressed to the probe link pair; the established real connection must remain intact and still complete its timer/XNAK recovery. ACK-only and disconnect-confirm packets remain silent-discard classes and are covered separately by the spoofed-ACK path. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then keepalive/inactivity coverage.


Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for reserved-port class candidate `95fb4106dc9519cdb1825540e46dc4a6a2e6a57a` is green: Repository Policy `35649532570`, Build Bootstrap `35649575197`, Project State Gate `35649577829`, E1 `35649580393` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35649583348`. PP-06 malformed established-link coverage now injects three parser-invalid packets into that same bounded live loss window using the correct peer node and connection IDs: ACK_DATA with reserved ACK qualifier 4, Link Service with illegal flow modifier 3, and an empty Interrupt. Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for malformed-packet candidate `8e62c06b7c5aed75edea12396ab2a6a9ce4999e5` is green on the exact rerun: Repository Policy `35653129828`, Build Bootstrap `35653180798`, Project State Gate `35653183863`, E1 `35653186498` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35653189580`. NSP inactivity keepalive is now independently testable without changing the production default. The module exposes writable `nsp_inactivity_seconds` with default 300 seconds; zero safely falls back to the default. The fast socket proof temporarily sets four seconds for one otherwise-idle MIRROR link, requires two candidate no-change Link Service packets on the same Other-Data subchannel with consecutive sequence numbers and no premature firing, then verifies normal MIRROR data on that same connection and restores 300 seconds. Consecutive sequence numbers rather than a retransmitted sequence prove PyDECnet acknowledged the first keepalive and the candidate restarted its inactivity interval. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue remaining NSP/PP-06 negatives.


Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for inactivity-keepalive candidate `b2ab90a1a6dd6141114dbc0d0d5d51134583f916` is green: Repository Policy `35654640026`, Build Bootstrap `35654680497`, Project State Gate `35654682738`, E1 `35654684891` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35654687240`. PP-06 full Data-window backpressure now has a live independent-peer proof. On a fresh MIRROR link the host suppresses every PyDECnet-to-candidate unicast reply while userspace submits eight one-segment records, filling the bounded NSP Data window. An immediate ninth nonblocking record must return EAGAIN rather than overrun the retransmit window. After the reverse path is restored, the blocked ninth record must resume on the same logical link. This complements the existing XOFF zero-window proof and distinguishes protocol flow control from socket-buffer behavior. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue remaining NSP/PP-06 negatives.


The first full-window live proof failed because the test encoded an eight-segment window while the implementation's declared bounded Data window is 20 segments. The candidate correctly accepted the ninth segment. The test is corrected to fill all 20 outstanding Data slots, require the 21st nonblocking send to return EAGAIN, then require recovery after peer replies resume. Product behavior is unchanged.


- Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for full-window backpressure candidate `2f4d54c26d3330badada1f4e9703b5384162403b` is green: Repository Policy `35661733498`, Build Bootstrap `35661771870`, Project State Gate `35661773813`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35661776097` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35661778430`. The corrected live proof filled all 20 outstanding Data slots, observed nonblocking backpressure on the 21st send while peer replies were suppressed, then resumed and completed after replies were restored.


PP-06 EINTR/signal handling now has live socket coverage. A native listener blocks in `accept()` with no pending connection and must return `EINTR` on a non-restarting SIGALRM. A connected MIRROR socket then blocks in `recv()` with no pending record and must likewise return `EINTR`; the same logical link must immediately complete a normal request/reply afterward, proving interruption does not corrupt socket or NSP state. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue remaining PP-06 negatives.


The first signal-interruption acceptance attempt failed before runtime in x64 interoperability run `35663764985`: strict C11 compilation hid POSIX `sigaction`/`sigemptyset` declarations because the test lacked a POSIX feature-test macro. `dnsignal.c` now requests POSIX.1-2008 before headers. This is test-only; product behavior is unchanged. The failed run remains recorded rather than being treated as a protocol failure.


Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for signal-interruption candidate `4a7f2ce6616b7937ac006a7628ecb0d5f588add6` is green: Repository Policy `35663912204`, Build Bootstrap `35663951694`, Project State Gate `35663953798`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35663955669` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35663957177`. The live proof confirms blocking `accept()` and `recv()` return `EINTR` on a non-restarting signal and the interrupted connected socket immediately remains usable for normal MIRROR traffic.


PP-06 receiver-stall and many-client fairness now have a live independent-peer proof. One native MIRROR link sends 20 records and intentionally leaves every reply unread, creating a receiver-stalled session with queued application data. While that link remains stalled, a second MIRROR connection must connect and complete an independent request/reply. The first socket must then drain all 20 replies in order. This proves one blocked consumer does not starve an independent valid session within the declared connection/window limits. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue remaining PP-06 negatives.


Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for receiver-stall fairness candidate `ed5a1ecaf1eb6668de751f71b18e33dbe8ffbc6d` is green: Repository Policy `35665443669`, Build Bootstrap `35665485064`, Project State Gate `35665487460`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35665489800` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35665492465`. The live proof kept 20 MIRROR replies unread on one connection while a second independent session connected and completed a request/reply, then drained all stalled replies in order.


PP-06 syscall-fuzz coverage now includes a deterministic invalid-call corpus in the live socket lifecycle test. It exercises short sockaddr lengths, wrong address family, conflicting object number/name selectors, unsupported sockaddr flags, invalid node-address lengths, remote addresses missing node identity, short option buffers, invalid accept mode, oversized access-field lengths, and unknown NSP socket options. Each call must fail with the implementation's declared errno while leaving the socket usable for the existing lifecycle/connect/churn proof. Product behavior is unchanged. The already-accepted reserved-port proof also directly demonstrates bounded NSP connection-table exhaustion by requiring Disconnect Confirm reason 1 (No Resources) after synthetic CIs fill the 256-entry table. Next: exact-SHA fast socket acceptance, then continue remaining PP-06 negatives.


Static review of the new syscall-negative corpus found one test-ordering mistake before acceptance completed: `sendmsg()` validates unsupported message flags before connection state, so `MSG_PEEK` must return `EOPNOTSUPP`, not `ENOTCONN`, on an unconnected DECnet socket. The expectation is corrected to match the socket implementation; product behavior is unchanged.


Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for receiver-stall fairness candidate `ed5a1ecaf1eb6668de751f71b18e33dbe8ffbc6d` is green: Repository Policy `35665443669`, Build Bootstrap `35665485064`, Project State Gate `35665487460`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35665489800` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35665492465`. The live proof confirms an unread 20-record MIRROR reply queue on one link does not prevent a second independent MIRROR session from connecting and completing traffic, and the stalled link then drains cleanly in order.


Exact-SHA fast socket acceptance for syscall-negative candidate `9c3b402bc4de7f2023a03c0592bbd32aa2233df8` is green: Repository Policy `35666650101`, Build Bootstrap `35666687690`, Project State Gate `35666689839`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35666691798` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35666693616`. The deterministic invalid-call corpus returned the declared errno values without damaging subsequent socket lifecycle/churn behavior.


Phase 5 full promotion acceptance is green on exact candidate `2d54dbf252120b42a5c2a60fa202371382af5dc9`: Repository Policy `35668700953`, Build Bootstrap `35668731691`, Project State Gate `35668733943`, External Reference Baselines `35668736098`, E1 `35668737804`, E2 `35668739614`, E3 `35668741529`, E4 `35668743712`, and full Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35668745743`. E1-E4 passed on x86_64 and ARM64; the 14-job Route20/PyDECnet interoperability matrix passed. Phase 5 exit criteria are satisfied: reliable bidirectional logical links, reconnect/recovery, deterministic loss/retransmission, flow control, sequence wrap, socket lifecycle/concurrency, bounded resource pressure, malformed-input negatives, signal interruption, receiver-stall fairness and full socket ABI interoperability are accepted. Phase 6 is next: Session Control and NICE/NML network management.


Phase 5 closure commit `fd2e603853ae913bd458e6c27e9b81650fa34ffb` itself passed full exact-SHA acceptance: Repository Policy `35674017637`, Build Bootstrap `35674044043`, Project State Gate `35674045083`, External Reference Baselines `35674046333`, E1 `35674047724`, E2 `35674048935`, E3 `35674050217`, E4 `35674051615`, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35674053420`; all x86_64/ARM64 jobs and the full 14-job Route20/PyDECnet matrix were green.

Phase 6 starts with a read-only management-state surface suitable for NICE/NML: the management UAPI enumerates current best routing entries (level, destination, next hop, interface, cost, hops), and `dnctl routes` provides a local scripted consumer. Remote NICE/NML encoding and object-19 dispatch follow. Phase 7 scope also records a future minimal DECnet-native web server based on a pinned, license-compatible tiny HTTP implementation.


The first Phase 6 route-management acceptance attempt on `7135e72aa994d1af44064b70dfff45ecd61e9d05` failed only at kernel compilation in Build Bootstrap `35675753048` on both architectures. The new local variable name `current` collided with the Linux kernel `current` macro (`get_current()`), producing strict-prototype and lvalue errors in `dniv_route_get_index()`. The enumerator variable is renamed to `cursor`; management semantics are unchanged. The failed build is retained as evidence.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for corrected Phase 6 route-management candidate `efa76ee675dd6a9e9c8c514f0710287f93cd3124` is green: Repository Policy `35675873484`, Build Bootstrap `35675894607`, Project State Gate `35675896016`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35675897212` on x86_64 and ARM64, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35675898672`. The read-only best-route enumeration and `dnctl routes` management consumer are accepted.

Phase 6 management state now extends to live NSP logical links. A new read-only `DNIV_IOC_GET_LINK` enumerates bounded connection records and reports local/remote logical-link IDs, remote node, protocol state, negotiated segment size, Data/Other-Data outstanding counts, receive queue depth, interrupt credit, XON/shutdown state and terminal reason. Snapshotting is performed under the existing NSP lock, and `dnctl links` is the first scripted local consumer. NSP wire behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then build the object-19 Session Control/NICE boundary on these management snapshots.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for NSP-link management candidate `84a098031d0c8ba90278be94267d29a0cd33ba3e` is green: Repository Policy `35676893762`, Build Bootstrap `35676965236`, Project State Gate `35676966599`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35676967767` on x86_64 and ARM64, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35676968922` against PyDECnet L1 and Route20 L1. The read-only logical-link management UAPI and `dnctl links` consumer are accepted.

Phase 6 now adds the first repository-owned Phase IV NICE codec boundary. It parses the standard five-byte READ NODE executor request used by the pinned PyDECnet reference, validates the function/entity/selector form, and emits a standards-shaped successful NodeReply containing the executor address/name plus Identification parameter 100. Focused host tests lock the exact request bytes `14 20 00 00 00`, executor entity encoding and reply parameter framing. This is pure management-message codec work; Session object 19 dispatch follows after exact-SHA acceptance.


The first NICE-codec acceptance attempt on `40e7bf5726cc66a7190cd62bceb8519358c3a3da` failed only in Build Bootstrap `35677719942` on both architectures. The codec produced the correct 30-byte reply (11-byte success/entity prefix plus the 19-byte Identification parameter), while the new host test incorrectly asserted 26 bytes. The expected length is corrected to 30; protocol/codecs are unchanged and the failed build remains recorded.


Phase 6 now exposes NICE through the standard Session Control object 19 boundary. New userspace daemon `dnnml` binds native `AF_DECnet` object 19, negotiates NICE version 4.0.0 in Session connect data, reads the live executor identity from the kernel management UAPI, and answers the accepted READ NODE executor request with the repository-owned NICE codec. Independent PyDECnet now opens object 19, verifies the version accept data, sends the exact five-byte executor request, and validates the returned address/name plus Identification parameter. This is the first end-to-end remote NICE/NML path; additional node/circuit/link information and counters follow. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then extend NICE READ NODE information from current management snapshots.


Object-19 acceptance attempt `35678774415` failed only in the x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability job before any NICE request was sent. The candidate reached adjacency and NSP readiness, started `dnnml`, and published `DNIV-INTEROP-NML-READY`; the controller was still waiting for a later NSP loss milestone because the host NICE query had been inserted after the existing loss/timer orchestration. The host NICE exchange is now scheduled immediately after candidate launch/readiness, before later socket-fault stages. Product, Session Control and NICE codec behavior are unchanged.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for object-19 orchestration correction `fd3eeffb0a80312b6a2f525ba8a33b689b6e481e` is green: Repository Policy `35679662549`, Build Bootstrap `35679691988`, Project State Gate `35679693606`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35679695309` on x86_64 and ARM64, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35679696797`. The pinned PyDECnet object-19 query completed before later fault orchestration and validated NICE version negotiation, executor identity and Identification parameter end-to-end.

Phase 6 NICE executor reads now distinguish information classes instead of returning characteristics for every request. READ NODE summary/status returns executor state plus the live active-link count derived from the management link snapshot; characteristics retains Identification; unsupported classes return a NICE error rather than misleading data. Unit coverage locks the summary/status selector bytes and parameter encoding. The unit Makefile duplicate Phase 6 target and clean omission are corrected. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add executor counter and circuit/adjacency management information.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for NICE executor status candidate `d9419187e80f59f2b8fc13d3df3f5480920e8f14` is green: Repository Policy `35680946527`, Build Bootstrap `35680972608`, Project State Gate `35680973956`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35680975464` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35680976771`. Summary/status codec changes and the live object-19 characteristics path remain green.

Phase 6 now adds a native outbound NICE management client, `dnnice`. It connects through `AF_DECnet` to standard NML object 19, negotiates NICE 4.0.0 connect/accept data, sends READ NODE executor summary, parses the successful node entity plus state/active-link prefix, and prints the remote executor identity. Fast PyDECnet interoperability invokes it before the local `dnnml` listener proof, giving bidirectional management evidence: candidate queries independent NML and independent PyDECnet queries candidate NML in the same exact-SHA run. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then expand NICE adjacency/circuit information and counters.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for native NICE client candidate `b439d8fb4f9794f6d9d691f50ff1571c66b65d68` is green: Repository Policy `35681976488`, Build Bootstrap `35682008528`, Project State Gate `35682010347`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35682012132` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35682013882`. The candidate successfully queried the pinned PyDECnet NML object 19 using NICE 4.0.0 and READ NODE executor summary, while the reciprocal PyDECnet-to-candidate object-19 query remained green.

The candidate NML server now keeps an accepted management session open for multiple NICE requests instead of processing exactly one request and closing the child socket. The independent PyDECnet proof uses one negotiated object-19 session to read executor characteristics and then executor status, requiring State=On and a nonzero live Active Links parameter from the kernel management snapshot. This exercises repeated Session/NICE operations and validates the new status information on the live wire. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add circuit/adjacency information and management counters.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for repeated NICE management-session candidate `040424959fd4067f3b4d5035d10c0a984dac4fad` is green: Repository Policy `35682678214`, Build Bootstrap `35682702215`, Project State Gate `35682703575`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35682704849` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35682706361`. One negotiated object-19 session successfully served executor characteristics followed by status with State=On and live Active Links.


Phase 6 NICE codec coverage now includes standard READ CIRCUIT status framing. The repository-owned codec parses the Phase IV entity-class 3 request form used by the pinned PyDECnet reference and emits a CircuitReply with the named circuit, State=On and parameter 810 Block Size. This establishes the deterministic wire boundary needed to map live adjacency/circuit snapshots into object-19 replies next; no kernel or Session behavior changes in this increment. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then wire live circuit/adjacency status through `dnnml` and reciprocal PyDECnet validation.


The first NICE circuit-codec acceptance attempt failed only the ARM64 E1 VM job `106605185228` in run `35683454049`. Both guests completed the required expiry/restart/recovery sequence; DN70 emitted PASS and DN71 reached RECOVERED at guest uptime 360.98 seconds, but the controller's 360-second ARM64 E1 bound expired roughly one second before DN71 could emit its final PASS marker. x86_64 E1, policy/build/state, and x64 PyDECnet interoperability were green. The ARM64 E1 controller bound is raised narrowly from 360 to 375 seconds, preserving the bounded fast profile while covering the observed TCG tail. Product and NICE protocol behavior are unchanged; the failed run remains recorded. Next: rerun exact-SHA fast socket acceptance.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for NICE circuit-codec candidate `cf19a74a95ba75849f26141c4d63e28df6e64cb5` is green after the narrowly bounded ARM64 E1 correction: Repository Policy `35684075406`, Build Bootstrap `35684098766`, Project State Gate `35684100061`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35684101313` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35684102599`. The standard READ CIRCUIT request parser and CircuitReply State/Block Size encoding are accepted; the 375-second ARM64 E1 bound also completed green.


Phase 6 now wires READ CIRCUIT status to live kernel adjacency state. The object-19 server maps logical circuit names ETH-N to distinct active kernel interfaces from DNIV_IOC_GET_ADJACENCY, requires an UP adjacency, and reports the live negotiated block size rather than a fixture value. The reciprocal pinned-PyDECnet management proof now reads ETH-0 on the same object-19 session after executor characteristics/status and requires State=On plus a sane live Block Size. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add adjacency/node information and management counters.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for live NICE circuit-status candidate `da0b4864741ce5220a2b7d7743631a479a871957` is green: Repository Policy `35685198705`, Build Bootstrap `35685229625`, Project State Gate `35685231343`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35685233059` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35685234915`. The reciprocal object-19 session validated executor characteristics/status plus live `ETH-0` State=On and kernel-adjacency Block Size.


Phase 6 executor management now serves standard READ NODE counters on object 19. The response exposes NICE node counter 608 (Total bytes received) and 610 (Total messages received) from the kernel's live DECnet receive accounting, with standard CTR4 encoding and saturation. The pinned PyDECnet reciprocal session now validates characteristics, status, counters and live circuit status on one NML connection. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add transmit/per-circuit accounting and richer circuit/adjacency NICE information.


The first executor-counter acceptance attempt failed only x64 PyDECnet interoperability run `35686193367` before the counter request executed: the test script contained a literal `\\n` between the STATUS_REQUEST and COUNTERS_REQUEST assignments, causing Python SyntaxError. Policy/build/state were green and the product codec/server compiled. The harness source now contains a real newline; product/NICE semantics are unchanged. The failed run remains recorded.


The corrective executor-counter acceptance still failed only x64 PyDECnet interoperability run `35686568655` before management traffic because a second literal `\\n` remained in the test script's final formatted print statement. The request declarations were fixed and policy/build/state were green, but Python again stopped at parse time. All literal newline escapes in executable Python source are now removed; product/NICE semantics remain unchanged. Both failed harness runs remain recorded.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for executor-counter corrective candidate `4512d0ac92c2fda7ee7ba1e4e69babae9c7f30a8` is green: Repository Policy `35686827735`, Build Bootstrap `35686857571`, Project State Gate `35686858893`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35686860051` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35686861521`. One object-19 session successfully validated executor characteristics, status, live node receive counters and live circuit status after the two recorded test-script syntax failures were corrected.


Phase 6 management accounting now covers both directions and circuit scope. A new additive read-only traffic snapshot reports aggregate or per-interface DECnet receive/transmit frames and bytes without changing the existing UAPI structures. Ethernet receive and all successful hello/routing/data transmit paths feed the counters. NICE READ NODE counters now serve standard 608-611 Total bytes/messages received/sent, while READ CIRCUIT counters serve standard 1000/1001 bytes and 1010/1011 data blocks for the live ETH-N interface. The reciprocal pinned-PyDECnet object-19 session validates all eight live counters. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then enrich circuit status with adjacent-node information and continue NICE node/adjacency management coverage.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for bidirectional/per-circuit NICE traffic-counter candidate `35f15e4d5825109dddb9447a48bde025ef00c0de` is green: Repository Policy `35688413870`, Build Bootstrap `35688437430`, Project State Gate `35688439214`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35688440768` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35688442294`. The reciprocal object-19 session validated node counters 608-611 and live ETH-0 circuit counters 1000/1001/1010/1011 with nonzero receive/transmit values. Next: enrich live circuit status with adjacent-node information and continue NICE node/adjacency management coverage.


Phase 6 live READ CIRCUIT status now includes standard NICE parameter 800 Adjacent node from the same UP kernel adjacency that supplies the circuit block size. The codec emits the pinned-reference CM-1 node-address form, and the reciprocal PyDECnet object-19 proof requires a nonzero live adjacent address before accepting the block-size parameter. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then extend READ NODE beyond the executor using live adjacency/route snapshots.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for live NICE circuit-adjacent status candidate `4952895102e3e121b233e93e31e0f905860ad94f` is green: Repository Policy `35689217606`, Build Bootstrap `35689242761`, Project State Gate `35689244385`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35689245952` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35689247610`. The reciprocal object-19 session validated live ETH-0 State=On, standard parameter 800 Adjacent node, Block Size, and the accepted circuit counters. Next: extend READ NODE beyond the executor using live adjacency/route snapshots.


Phase 6 READ NODE status now handles a specific live remote node instead of only executor zero. The object-19 server resolves the requested address through the kernel UP-adjacency snapshot and matching route-table entry, then emits standard NICE Reachable state, Routing-IV/Non-Routing-IV type, route Cost/Hops, Circuit and Next-node parameters. The reciprocal pinned-PyDECnet session reuses the live ETH-0 adjacent address, requests that exact node, and validates the route-backed status before continuing circuit counters. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add multiple-entity known/active/adjacent node reads and management error semantics.


The first live remote READ NODE acceptance attempt failed only x64 PyDECnet interoperability run `35690144066`. The server returned NICE error -1 because the test exposed a representation mismatch in the userspace route lookup: L1 route UAPI entries carry the 10-bit node number in `destination`, with area supplied by `next_hop`, while the new NML code compared `destination` to the full 16-bit area.node address. The lookup now matches L1 node plus area correctly (and retains L2 area matching). Build/policy/state were green; product routing itself was not implicated. The failed run remains recorded.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for live remote READ NODE corrective candidate `cf5b3fe104c988a393e1fc33956d613bb5df24ff` is green: Repository Policy `35690455115`, Build Bootstrap `35690484236`, Project State Gate `35690486057`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35690487637` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35690489477`. The reciprocal object-19 session successfully read the live adjacent remote node and validated Reachable state, node type, route cost/hops, circuit and next-node fields. Next: add multiple-entity known/active/adjacent node reads and management error semantics.


Phase 6 NICE node management now supports multiple-entity READ NODE status for Known (-1), Active (-2) and Adjacent (-4). The parser accepts standard plural node selectors, the NML server derives live remote entities from route or adjacency snapshots, sorts/deduplicates them, emits the standard multiple-items framing (retcode 2, per-entity success records, retcode -128 terminator), and preserves route-backed Reachable/type/cost/hops/circuit/next-node detail. Valid specific unknown nodes now return NICE -8 Unrecognized component; unsupported permanent reads return -1. The reciprocal PyDECnet session validates all three plural reads include the live adjacent peer plus the two error semantics. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then broaden Session Control object dispatch and remaining NICE/NML management operations.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for plural NICE node-status candidate `1189d35b2886036b006f456fc0d8812878cfbe34` is green: Repository Policy `35691397184`, Build Bootstrap `35691422005`, Project State Gate `35691423342`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35691424646` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35691426004`. Known, Active and Adjacent multi-entity READ NODE status all included the live adjacent peer, while specific-unknown and unsupported-permanent reads returned the required NICE errors.


Phase 6 NICE circuit management now supports multiple-entity READ CIRCUIT status for Known (-1) and Active (-2). The circuit parser accepts the standard three-byte plural selector, the NML server enumerates live ETH-N circuit snapshots, emits NICE multiple-items framing and per-circuit State/Adjacent-node/Block-size replies, and the reciprocal pinned-PyDECnet session requires ETH-0 to appear in both plural reads. Unsupported plural circuit classes or information classes remain explicit NICE errors. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then continue Session Control/NICE management coverage.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for plural NICE circuit-status candidate `07ff9eb6fe5a76ffd8b2868e38a552e8c2329029` is green: Repository Policy `35692546939`, Build Bootstrap `35692568618`, Project State Gate `35692570088`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35692570940` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35692572150`. Known and Active multi-entity READ CIRCUIT status both included live ETH-0 using standard multiple-items framing while existing management coverage remained green.


Phase 6 plural circuit coverage now includes READ CIRCUIT counters for Known (-1) and Active (-2), exercising the already-implemented multiple-item counter path against pinned PyDECnet. Each returned live circuit must carry the standard 1000/1001/1010/1011 receive/transmit byte/block counters and ETH-0 must appear with nonzero traffic. Product behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then continue Session Control/NICE management operations and negative handling.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for plural NICE circuit-counter candidate `e954ee76c70c734a61c475a7a9f1b56312749d23` is green: Repository Policy `35693426128`, Build Bootstrap `35693454698`, Project State Gate `35693456129`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35693457418` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35693458896`.
