Phase 6 PP-07 negative coverage now injects malformed NICE requests over an established object-19 session: wrong function, truncated node request, unsupported entity class and inconsistent circuit-name length. Each must return NICE -1 without closing or corrupting the management session, after which a valid executor status read must still succeed with State=On. Product behavior is unchanged; this locks malformed-management rejection and session recovery against the independent peer path. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then daemon/session restart and concurrent-management coverage.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for malformed NICE session-recovery candidate `7d34c5a016b8e79909625d8ee8c165b562e5f962` is green: Repository Policy `35694162276`, Build Bootstrap `35694195705`, Project State Gate `35694197206`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35694198772` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35694200328`. Malformed NICE requests returned -1 without terminating the object-19 session, and a subsequent valid executor status read succeeded.


Phase 6 daemon/session robustness now adds concurrent and restart coverage for object 19. The NML daemon can fork independent management-session workers; normal daemon mode reaps children automatically and bounded `--sessions N` mode waits for every child and propagates failure. The live pinned-PyDECnet proof holds three simultaneous NICE sessions open, requires valid executor status from all three, then restarts the daemon and reruns the full NICE management suite on a fresh session. This covers PP-07 many-client isolation plus daemon/session restart without weakening the existing malformed-request recovery proof. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then continue Session Control/NICE management operations.


Full interoperability run `35713822257` against `d95dc1131ed0c8985bfc25399155b871ea2f59a9` exposed management-coverage gaps outside the earlier x64 L1 fast slice: direct UP adjacencies without a matching route entry could return NICE -8, Known/Active plural node reads could omit a live cross-area adjacency, and one ARM64 restart attempt hit a transient object-19 registration race. The NML snapshot now treats every UP adjacency as a live node, uses route metrics when available with a direct-adjacency cost/hop fallback when they are not, and gives bounded NML lifecycle probes an additional guest second before publishing readiness. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then re-run the affected PyDECnet interoperability roles before continuing Phase 6.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for the live-node coverage corrective candidate `28b7503965fe8a72723911a9f886db20ed6dc4e6` is green: Repository Policy `35721733232`, Build Bootstrap `35721773410`, Project State Gate `35721775148`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35721777173` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35721779005`. Direct-adjacency fallback, plural live-node coverage and the bounded NML restart allowance all passed the fast gate.

Phase 6 local/remote management usability now extends `dnnice` from a fixed executor summary query to explicit executor summary, status, characteristics and counters queries over object 19. The command validates NML version negotiation and decodes standard identification plus node traffic counters for interactive use. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add specific remote-node and circuit query modes to the same client.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for the executor-query client candidate `3943fcd0a20cd552ac2953a25b10ff2028c593c3` is green: Repository Policy `35722657962`, Build Bootstrap `35722715333`, Project State Gate `35722717424`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35722719983` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35722722871`. The expanded `dnnice` builds on both architectures while the existing independent summary query remains interoperable.

The independent PyDECnet path now exercises every implemented interactive executor query from `dnnice`: summary, status, characteristics and counters. The restart-side bounded NML listener allowance is also made consistent with the already-proven two-second initial/concurrent listener allowance; the previous one-second restart sleep was a residual harness inconsistency. Product protocol semantics are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add specific remote-node and circuit query modes to `dnnice`.


Fast interoperability run `35723583203` for `3ab65e446ed08b49df587eefd28c2bb93db1c73d` proved adjacency, NSP, summary and status, then exposed a client-only NICE decoding defect: PyDECnet characteristics replies contain multiple standard parameters and `dnnice` incorrectly required Identification (100) to be the first and only decoded field. PyDECnet's pinned source confirms response parameters are sorted by parameter number, so State (0) precedes Identification and Management Version (101) plus Software Identification (126) may follow. `dnnice` now walks the full typed NICE parameter stream, extracts Identification wherever it occurs, and validates the remainder rather than assuming a one-field reply. Product routing/NSP/NML semantics are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance and continue Phase 6 remote-node/circuit client coverage.


The first characteristics-decoder correction still failed exact-SHA PyDECnet interoperability in run `35725003342`. The remaining defect was CM-n handling: NICE coded-multiple type 0xC1..0xDF encodes a count of nested typed values, not a byte-length prefix. PyDECnet's pinned `CM1.decode` confirms this. The generic `dnnice` value walker now recursively consumes exactly that many nested NICE values, allowing Management Version (CM-3) and other compound parameters to coexist with Identification. This remains a client decoder correction only. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance.


Fast interoperability run `35726017085` for `df4f954b1526be186ee9b420b50a4fa063fd525f` still reached the PyDECnet characteristics query but rejected its typed parameter stream. The remaining client-only decoder gap was the standard H-n/O-n numeric NICE families (type codes 0x21..0x2f and 0x31..0x3f); the generic value walker had handled DU/DS, coded, ASCII/hex-image and CM-n values but not those fixed-width numeric encodings. The walker now consumes H-n/O-n using the standard low-nibble width while retaining CM-n recursion. Product routing/NSP/NML semantics remain unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add specific remote-node and circuit query modes to `dnnice`.


Fast acceptance for `fa20b88c3f8ee268f4049825d8b06d84dbf6dee2` was green in Repository Policy `35727180595`, Build Bootstrap `35727229528`, Project State Gate `35727232423`, and Python QEMU VM Lab `35727234890` on x86_64 and ARM64, but x64 PyDECnet interoperability `35727237104` still failed only the remote characteristics decode after summary/status succeeded. H-n/O-n support was therefore not the remaining cause. The client now emits the bounded malformed characteristics reply bytes on this error path so the next exact-peer run can identify the exact unsupported encoding rather than guessing. Next: run the x64 PyDECnet fast slice, fix the concrete decoded gap, and immediately re-run exact-SHA fast acceptance.


Diagnostic fast interoperability run `35728633258` on `9897cbf93660018f22d4507af337fc9be7c9aaac` exposed the exact remaining characteristics mismatch. PyDECnet's successful NICE reply used detail `0x0000` and an empty counted message before the node entity, while `dnnice`'s characteristics/counter entity helper incorrectly required candidate-server-specific detail `0xffff` and hard-coded the entity at byte 4. The typed parameter stream itself decoded correctly. `dnnice` now parses the standard NICE success header generically—retcode, two-byte detail, counted message—then locates the node entity, accepting both independent-peer and candidate-server forms. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance; once green, add specific remote-node and circuit query modes.


Fast interoperability run `35729804786` for `759387e564dad30d6009753974dbf13030263574` passed PyDECnet summary, status and characteristics, then exposed the next client-only NICE compatibility gap in executor counters. The preserved LAN capture shows PyDECnet returns the standard full counter set in ascending parameter order and uses the NICE counter type nibble to encode 1-, 2- or 4-byte counters (and mapped variants); parameters 608-611 therefore are not the first fields. `dnnice` now walks the full counter stream, skips unrelated counters using their encoded widths, handles mapped-counter bitmap prefixes, and extracts 608-611 wherever present. Product routing/NSP/NML semantics remain unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add specific remote-node and circuit query modes to `dnnice`.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for the full NICE counter-stream decoder candidate `0bd1ccfa0beb6f70f1ddd9117f6e6a6a14714d10` is green: Repository Policy `35734916006`, Build Bootstrap `35734963015`, Project State Gate `35734965815`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35734968787` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35734971678`. All implemented executor queries now interoperate with the pinned independent peer. Phase 6 management usability now adds explicit remote-node and circuit query modes to `dnnice`: a management target can request a specific node summary/status or a named circuit status/counters, with generic typed NICE decoding for route state and circuit state plus standard counter streams. The independent PyDECnet path now exercises the candidate asking the peer about the candidate node and ETH-0 status/counters. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then extend interactive multiple-entity node/circuit reads or additional Session Control object dispatch.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for remote-node/circuit client candidate `bbe875eb1ab09e996d3793090e0cb90e97da7942` is green: Repository Policy `35736469661`, Build Bootstrap `35736527756`, Project State Gate `35736530459`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35736532797` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35736536010`. The independent peer successfully answered a specific candidate-node status query plus ETH-0 status and counters. Interactive `dnnice` now also supports standard NICE multiple-item reads for known/active/adjacent nodes and known/active circuits, including circuit counters; it consumes the retcode-2 item stream through the -128 terminator and decodes each entity with the same typed parsers used for specific reads. The independent path requires the candidate to appear in PyDECnet's adjacent-node result and ETH-0 to appear in active circuit status/counters. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then broaden Session Control object dispatch beyond NML/object 19.


Fast interoperability run `35737901590` for `6bc001e2e69784ce1c37d26baa7c7aeb59ed2b5f` passed every specific executor/node/circuit query and then exposed a standards-compatible independent-peer behavior in plural reads: when a plural selector resolves to exactly one entity, pinned PyDECnet collapses the response to one ordinary retcode-1 item instead of sending retcode 2 plus an -128 terminator; it uses multiple-item framing only when two or more replies exist. `dnnice` now accepts both legal response shapes while the candidate NML server's existing multi-item framing remains unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then broaden Session Control dispatch with standard MIRROR object 25.


Fast interoperability run `35739261965` for `1684c621764cb71a9641be519be510dd57bdb80d` passed all specific executor/node/circuit reads and then failed only the plural adjacent-node client query. Preserved LAN packet evidence shows pinned PyDECnet returned a valid single-item adjacent-node reply containing node type, cost, hops and circuit but no State parameter; adjacency membership itself supplies the active/reachable context. `dnnice` now accepts a remote-node item when it contains any recognized standard status field, and prints State only when present, while still rejecting an empty/malformed item. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then broaden Session Control dispatch with standard MIRROR object 25.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for sparse adjacent-node status candidate `279cabd631f34674fa7f1d3b862dc4bafed33bd1` is green: Repository Policy `35740654536`, Build Bootstrap `35740711394`, Project State Gate `35740715296`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35740719041` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet L1 interoperability `35740722243`. Phase 6 Session Control dispatch now extends beyond NML/object 19 with a native standard MIRROR object 25 daemon. `dnmirror` advertises the standard two-byte maximum-length accept data, serves loop command records with success status/payload reflection, rejects non-loop function codes with failure status, and supports bounded one-session operation for acceptance plus normal sequential service. The pinned PyDECnet peer now connects inbound to candidate object 25 and validates small, 1 KiB, and negative MIRROR records. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add object-name dispatch/registration and continue Phase 6 Session Control coverage.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for native MIRROR object 25 candidate `a494979e410cbedbf4adc4f13fa5a4cb5d4d1c2d` is green: Repository Policy `35742145930`, Build Bootstrap `35742206543`, Project State Gate `35742209944`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35742213010` on x86_64 and ARM64, and x64 PyDECnet interoperability `35742216169`. Phase 6 Session Control now also proves object-name registration and dispatch: `dnmirror --name` binds the standard `MIRROR` object name through the native AF_DECnet socket ABI, and the pinned PyDECnet peer connects by name and runs the same positive, 1 KiB and negative MIRROR record checks. Numeric object 25 behavior is retained unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then continue Session Control coverage toward access control/task dispatch and Phase 6 closure.


Acceptance attempt `35743974462` for `904da60b5e83bef6322bfad98e5d673a3059f8cd` stopped in Repository Policy before protocol execution because the Git-data commit accidentally reset executable modes on three interoperability scripts. This packaging-only defect is corrected by restoring `100755` on `dniv-interop-smoke.sh`, `pydecnet-mirror.py`, and `run-interop.sh`; Session Control and MIRROR protocol content is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then continue Phase 6.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for object-name Session Control candidate `7b70af547096e2d581621064c31724f79cf7e6ce` is green: Repository Policy `35744157479`, Build Bootstrap `35744209681`, Project State Gate `35744212187`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35744214743` on x86_64 and ARM64, and fast independent interoperability `35744217004`. Phase 6 Session Control access-data coverage now exercises deferred object acceptance through the native socket ABI. `dnmirror --require-access` reads inbound DSO_CONACCESS requester/password/account fields before accepting; matching credentials are accepted with normal MIRROR accept data, while a bad password is rejected with standard Session Control reason 34. The pinned PyDECnet peer proves both paths against the named MIRROR object. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add generic task/object listener coverage and drive Phase 6 toward consolidated/full closure.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for Session Control access-data candidate `a5582b75dc624c62e97137520d1e7b1fba0a11a2` is green: Repository Policy `35745502566`, Build Bootstrap `35745567580`, Project State Gate `35745570952`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35745574148` on x86_64 and ARM64, and fast independent interoperability `35745577593`. Valid and invalid access-data paths both passed against pinned PyDECnet. Phase 6 now adds a generic native object listener, `dnobject`, which registers either an arbitrary object name or object number through AF_DECnet and serves record-oriented sessions. Independent coverage registers the nonstandard named task `DNIVTASK`; PyDECnet connects by that name with connect data and exchanges multiple records, proving dispatch is not limited to hard-coded standard objects. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then run consolidated/full Phase 6 interoperability and close remaining management/session gaps found there.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for generic named object candidate `687c0611c61cbcc9263ef4b0fec955c11928fbd2` is green: Repository Policy `35746895127`, Build Bootstrap `35746964625`, Project State Gate `35746967922`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35746971078` on x86_64 and ARM64, and fast independent interoperability `35746974168`. Consolidated interoperability `35747985975` then exposed one architecture/timing-sensitive deferred-accept race in ARM64 PyDECnet endnode coverage: the peer received the Session Control accept successfully and `pydecnet-access` reported pass, but disconnected immediately enough that DSO_CONACCEPT sampled CLOSED instead of RUN and incorrectly returned ECONNREFUSED to the accepting daemon. The deferred-accept socket path now mirrors the already-proven immediate-accept behavior: after a successful CC send, CLOSED observed while waiting for RUN is retained as an accepted socket so userspace sees orderly EOF rather than a false accept failure. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then rerun consolidated Phase 6 acceptance.


Full Phase 6 promotion run `35752114459` on `7bf27c5aa90998c74914289a3a1d34d3576ff100` exposed a concrete NICE client compatibility gap in the router-to-PyDECnet-endnode role. The preserved x64 LAN capture shows the endnode returned a valid specific-node status record for 31.70 containing only standard Active links (parameter 600, DU2) and Delay (601, DU2). This is legal: PyDECnet's NSP layer contributed those fields while its endnode routing layer had no additional route-state fields for that instant. `dnnice` now recognizes and prints Active links and Delay as sufficient standard remote-node status content instead of rejecting the record as malformed. The same full run also produced one ARM64 router-endnode reverse-NML connect reject reason 39 after NML readiness; that remains separately observable and will be re-evaluated after this concrete decoder correction rather than conflated with the x64 failure. Next: exact-SHA acceptance, then repeat the full Phase 6 promotion matrix and address only any reproducible remaining failure.


Full Phase 6 promotion run `35754816703` on `6bccad0cb5f9b0cf42dbb5a46e3d1bbae10d5afe` confirmed the sparse endnode specific-node status correction: both router-to-endnode roles passed that query. The x86_64 PyDECnet router-endnode role then exposed a separate client-only plural-read framing gap. Pinned PyDECnet returned the standard retcode-2 multiple-items header as `02 00 00 00` (retcode, two-byte detail, empty counted message), while `dnnice` only accepted the candidate server's compact one-byte `02` form. `dnnice` now accepts either bounded form before decoding the item stream; candidate NML server and kernel protocol behavior are unchanged. The same run reproduced the ARM64 reverse-NML reason-39 failure. Packet evidence shows the candidate had object 19 listening, received the PyDECnet CI, and replied with Connect Ack plus Connect Confirm; PyDECnet then immediately returned NSP reason 41 (No Link), despite its API reporting Session reason 39 (Destination unreachable). Treat that as a separate reference-side state/routing investigation and do not alter candidate protocol behavior without concrete contrary evidence. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance of the plural-header correction, then isolate the ARM64 PyDECnet router-endnode state transition before repeating full Phase 6 promotion.


Follow-up review of the same preserved x86_64 PyDECnet plural-read exchange found that the standard end-of-multiple-items control record is likewise sent in full NICE reply-header form as `80 00 00 00`, not only the candidate server's compact one-byte `80`. The plural decoder now applies the same bounded full-or-compact framing rule to both the retcode-2 opener and retcode--128 terminator. This is client-only compatibility handling; candidate NML/kernel behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then isolate the reproduced ARM64 PyDECnet router-endnode reason-39/No-Link transition.


The reproduced ARM64 router-to-PyDECnet-endnode reverse-NML reason-39 failure is now concretely isolated to the independent reference. In pinned PyDECnet endnode routing, a send through an existing previous-hop cache entry physically transmitted the CI but returned `None`; endnode `routing.send(..., rqr=True)` interpreted that false result as send failure and immediately returned the same CI to local NSP. PyDECnet therefore closed its outbound NML link as destination-unreachable (Session reason 39) even though the candidate received the CI and correctly returned CA/CC; the later valid CC then found no PyDECnet connection and elicited internal NSP No Link reason 41. Fork commit `8d93c2a546317c67aba0adf9433f5f3efdf1f85c` corrects the cached-send return to `True` and adds a regression assertion. The live PyDECnet pin is advanced to that exact fork commit; candidate routing/NSP/Session behavior is unchanged. Fast acceptance of `3defe1bfa232f4aafd94d5e63bbee62fbf6c5ad3` was green in Repository Policy `35762127955`, Build Bootstrap `35762175317`, Project State Gate `35762178140`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35762181236`, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35762184295`. Next: exact-SHA acceptance with the corrected PyDECnet live pin, then repeat full Phase 6 promotion.


Full Phase 6 promotion run `35764358719` on `53582b86110f64ae152da6c702af86faa47b685a` confirmed the corrected PyDECnet cached-send behavior on ARM64 router-to-endnode reverse NML, but the x86_64 router-to-endnode role exposed a separate test-ordering race before Session Control coverage. The candidate had already formed its endnode adjacency and successfully completed specific executor/node/circuit NICE reads against PyDECnet, while the peer's first plural `adjacent nodes` query still returned an empty legal result because the faster x86_64 candidate reached management probing before the PyDECnet endnode had processed the candidate router hello. The same role is green on ARM64, and PyDECnet's endnode circuit records an adjacency only after receiving that router hello. The interoperability harness now bounds reciprocal peer-adjacency convergence for up to 10 seconds before requiring the candidate node in the plural adjacent-node result; malformed/query failures remain errors. Candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then repeat full Phase 6 promotion.


Phase 6 promotion is complete on exact protocol candidate `c11045a70e7deb59870fff5f8bc22213e0df2bc0`. Full acceptance triggered by issue #334 is green: Repository Policy `35767265536`, Build Bootstrap `35767310354`, Project State Gate `35767313766`, External Reference Baselines `35767317083`, Python QEMU VM Lab runs `35767320599`, `35767324333`, `35767327216`, and `35767330590` on x86_64/ARM64, plus Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35767334168` with all 14 Route20/PyDECnet jobs green, including both router-to-PyDECnet-endnode roles. Phase 6 exit criteria are satisfied. Phase 7 is now active; start with the standard DECnet/Linux command environment and preserve exact-SHA acceptance discipline.


Phase 7 userspace work has started with a native `ncp` command. The initial read-only subset provides one-shot and interactive `NCP>` operation, `show`/`list` for executor, specific/plural nodes and circuits, and `tell AREA.NODE` remote targeting. It translates those standard management commands onto the already-accepted native NICE client path without importing GPL LinuxDECnet code; the pinned LinuxDECnet NCP manual is behavior/reference material only. The build runs parser self-tests, candidate images install `/usr/local/sbin/ncp`, and PyDECnet interoperability now proves `ncp tell <peer> show executor status`. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then extend NCP management coverage and begin standard remote-login tooling.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for the initial native NCP frontend `4db157edddcc5dc821419e8c770a312be681c736` is green: Repository Policy `35771187931`, Build Bootstrap `35771238334`, Project State Gate `35771241038`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35771243516` on x86_64/ARM64, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35771246167`. NCP management coverage now adds the standard local administrative forms `set executor name NAME` and `zero executor`, mapped onto the native kernel identity/counter controls via `dnctl`; remote `tell` remains read-only until matching NICE modify/zero server operations exist. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then begin the CTERM/Foundation remote-login client without importing LinuxDECnet GPL source.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for NCP local management candidate `64e01554433444cb24468dcaa51797a1252126f7` is green: Repository Policy `35774136980`, Build Bootstrap `35774183702`, Project State Gate `35774186645`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35774189937`, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35774193323`. Phase 7 remote-login work now begins with a clean native `dnlogin` CTERM/Foundation boundary. The first increment connects to standard CTERM object 42 over AF_DECnet, validates the Foundation bind, emits the Linux bind-accept identity, validates the peer CTERM initiate and returns the client initiate/capability record; `--probe AREA.NODE` exercises that bounded handshake while parser/codec self-tests run in every userspace build. No LinuxDECnet GPL source is imported; its pinned manuals/source and the pinned PyDECnet remote-terminal notes are behavior/protocol references only. Interactive terminal semantics remain next.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for the initial CTERM/Foundation probe candidate `f5d88aa235608e15ca667d624256f99f68992d7e` is green: Repository Policy `35776943205`, Build Bootstrap `35777004186`, Project State Gate `35777006116`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35777008401` on x86_64/ARM64, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35777010829`. The login handshake is now exercised over a real independent PyDECnet NSP/Session path: a bounded PyDECnet object-42 server accepts the candidate connection, sends Foundation BIND and CTERM INITIATE records, and independently validates the candidate bind-accept identity plus CTERM capability reply. The candidate retries only the short object-registration race and requires the full handshake before continuing. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add interactive CTERM WRITE/START-READ handling and terminal mode.


Fast acceptance of CTERM/PyDECnet proof candidate `7936aa4b6efab331ea7304aaf68a71393f2f3731` passed Repository Policy `35778423274`, Build Bootstrap `35778471238`, Project State Gate `35778474091`, and Python QEMU VM Lab `35778476856`, but Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35778480144` exposed a harness-ordering defect before protocol execution. The candidate correctly emitted `DNIV-INTEROP-CTERM-READY` before starting its NML listener sequence, while the host controller waited for `DNIV-INTEROP-NML-READY` before launching the PyDECnet object-42 server. The candidate therefore exhausted its bounded registration retries with Connection refused. This follow-up launches the host CTERM server at the candidate CTERM-ready marker before NML orchestration; candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add interactive CTERM WRITE/START-READ handling and terminal mode.


Fast acceptance of harness-order candidate `936c117de4dacb70b9a9a3f2c92856cb8fbcdd41` passed Repository Policy `35780439395`, Build Bootstrap `35780479542`, and Project State Gate `35780482617`; x64 PyDECnet interoperability job `106924475317` in run `35780488366` then exited before the candidate reached CTERM readiness. Moving CTERM ahead of NML made it the first candidate marker, but its inherited 30-second post-convergence wait was too short for candidate boot; the prior first-marker path correctly used the architecture-bounded `timeout_seconds`. This follow-up uses that existing bound only for the initial CTERM-ready wait. Candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then interactive CTERM WRITE/START-READ handling and terminal mode.
\n\nExact-SHA fast acceptance for the corrected CTERM harness candidate `3b1a8d969f71afa6168147e94f7010f36b4edadb` is green: Repository Policy `35781043116`, Build Bootstrap `35781150597`, Project State Gate `35781152926`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35781155832` on x86_64/ARM64, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35781158744`. Phase 7 remote login now advances beyond the handshake: native `dnlogin AREA.NODE` enters a terminal session, decodes Foundation COMMON DATA carrying CTERM WRITE and START READ, writes host output, collects terminal input under raw terminal mode, and returns CTERM READ DATA with terminator position. The independent PyDECnet object-42 harness now serves a second live session after the probe, sends WRITE/START-READ, validates the candidate's `phase7<CR>` READ DATA response, then sends final output and disconnects cleanly. `--probe` and `--selftest` remain available. LinuxDECnet remains reference-only; no GPL source is imported. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then extend CTERM control coverage (write-complete, OOB/unread/check-input/characteristics) and access-data login options.\n\n\nBuild Bootstrap `35788574378` for interactive CTERM candidate `e53d0796aa4f1eb9dc02e1762946c6f788fd4db7` failed identically on x86_64 and ARM64 before protocol execution because fortified libc marks `write(2)` as warn-unused-result and `dnlogin` discarded the return value in its optional local-echo path; `-Werror` correctly rejected that. The follow-up checks the exact byte count for both CRLF and single-byte echo writes. CTERM wire behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then continue Phase 7 CTERM control and login-option coverage.\n\n\nFast acceptance for CTERM build-fix candidate `d8906804fa8bff24edd67f289a0a40c267a6d42e` passed Repository Policy `35788736038`, Build Bootstrap `35788770130`, and Project State Gate `35788772741`; x64 PyDECnet interoperability job `106951967136` in run `35788776755` then reached the new interactive CTERM exchange and exposed a harness quoting defect. The smoke script fed the literal two-character sequence `\\r` instead of carriage return, so the candidate correctly returned eight ordinary input bytes with terminator position 8; the independent harness expected a seven-byte `phase7<CR>` record with terminator position 6. The shell escape is corrected, along with the same over-escaped diagnostic newlines. Candidate CTERM behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then continue Phase 7 CTERM control and login-option coverage.\n\n\nFast acceptance for shell-escape candidate `9aa40686bd0b6e7a7422eeb231ca0582f97ed4f7` again reached live CTERM but x64 PyDECnet interoperability job `106953520596` in run `35789249607` showed the smoke file still contained two literal backslashes before `r`; the prior Git-data rewrite had escaped the replacement itself. Byte-level verification now enforces exactly one backslash character before `r` in the shell source (so `printf` emits carriage return) and likewise one before `n` in diagnostics. Candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then continue Phase 7 CTERM control and login-option coverage.\n

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for interactive CTERM candidate `b0c7c355365c6315200544f61c3a679e3c03a8ca` is green: Repository Policy `35789632001`, Build Bootstrap `35789673574`, Project State Gate `35789676276`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35789678905` on x86_64/ARM64, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35789681346`. Native `dnlogin` now adds standard Session Control access-data options `-u USER`, `-p PASSWORD`, and `-a ACCOUNT`, encoded through `SO_CONACCESS` before connecting to CTERM object 42. The independent PyDECnet CTERM harness validates all three fields on the live interactive session before accepting it, while the probe remains credential-free. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then extend CTERM control handling and terminal characteristics.


Fast acceptance for access-data candidate `26971adfc9368f1727498fb82a1f660cebfb7d24` passed Repository Policy `35790707667`, Build Bootstrap `35790754254`, and Project State Gate `35790756444`; x64 PyDECnet interoperability job `106958397196` in run `35790760961` then exposed a reference-observation mistake in the new test, not a candidate wire failure. Pinned PyDECnet deliberately exposes inbound username/account fields to an API object only when that object enables authentication; object 42 is bound with authentication off, so its API Connect event correctly contains empty access fields even when Session Control access data was present on the wire. The live CTERM harness therefore no longer asserts fields PyDECnet intentionally hides. `dnlogin -u/-p/-a` remains implemented through the already-proven native `SO_CONACCESS` path, while interactive CTERM behavior is still independently exercised. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then extend CTERM control handling and terminal characteristics.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for the access-data/reference-observation correction `29ad2d1b69f51a66c8e5e5ed92dbdb7a24aeae75` is green: Repository Policy `35791165994`, Build Bootstrap `35791198107`, Project State Gate `35791199993`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35791201684` on x86_64/ARM64, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35791203343`. CTERM interactive control coverage now adds CHECK INPUT/INPUT COUNT and explicit WRITE COMPLETE. Native `dnlogin` answers CHECK INPUT from the local terminal/pipe byte count via FIONREAD and already-supported WRITE completion is now independently exercised. The PyDECnet object-42 harness requests write completion, validates the exact completion record, queries queued input and requires the expected seven bytes before issuing START READ. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add CTERM terminal-characteristics handling and additional control messages.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for CTERM control candidate `97f7ab4597a54f395d976e00fcdbc1b1bcacb988` is green: Repository Policy `35791997034`, Build Bootstrap `35792035306`, Project State Gate `35792037556`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35792039554`, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35792041561`. CTERM now adds READ CHARACTERISTICS/CHARACTERISTICS for a first useful terminal subset: physical character size, logical line width/page length, and handler normal-echo state. Native `dnlogin` derives geometry from TIOCGWINSZ when available and echo from termios, with conventional 80x24/8-bit defaults for non-TTY pipes. The independent PyDECnet object-42 harness requests and validates all four selectors before START READ. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add CLEAR INPUT/UNREAD/OOB and broader characteristic selectors.


Build Bootstrap `35793464970` for CTERM terminal-characteristics candidate `af57567ff9d3587619189720ebb0cce9151a4feb` failed before protocol execution because the new READ CHARACTERISTICS/CHARACTERISTICS handlers referenced message constants that were added to the PyDECnet harness but omitted from the C client constant block. This follow-up adds only those two protocol constants; behavior is otherwise unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then continue broader CTERM controls.


Fast acceptance for CTERM characteristics build-fix candidate `c680618716c485545c1ad7cae2375ba8f7c05fef` passed Repository Policy `35793631849`, Build Bootstrap `35793664515`, and Project State Gate `35793666201`; x64 PyDECnet interoperability job `106967845923` in run `35793669863` then proved the candidate emitted the requested four characteristics correctly but exposed a harness length mistake. The response is 17 bytes, not 16: two-byte CHARACTERISTICS header plus 4-byte character-size tuple, 4-byte line-width tuple, 4-byte page-length tuple, and 3-byte normal-echo tuple. The harness now validates the full 17-byte body and the echo value byte. Candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then continue broader CTERM controls.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for CTERM characteristics candidate `d161cc85d22c1a117a9d739473cd3182d41a32d0` is green: Repository Policy `35794136759`, Build Bootstrap `35794167365`, Project State Gate `35794169070`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35794170984`, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35794172759`. CTERM control coverage now adds host UNREAD and CLEAR INPUT. Native `dnlogin` answers UNREAD with an empty READ DATA record carrying reason flag 6, and clears pending local input with TCIFLUSH for terminals or bounded FIONREAD draining for pipes. The independent PyDECnet object-42 harness issues CLEAR INPUT after the normal read, then UNREAD and validates the exact empty READ DATA control reply before final output. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then OOB input handling and broader characteristics.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for CTERM UNREAD/CLEAR INPUT candidate `4d52cfb0dcc6dded4fee8680170e9f9bf65df2c1` is green: Repository Policy `35795111428`, Build Bootstrap `35795140483`, Project State Gate `35795142060`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35795143552`, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35795145092`. CTERM READ CHARACTERISTICS coverage now expands from four fields to a practical terminal profile: input/output speed, character size, parity state/type, mode-writing, attributes, VT200 terminal type, input/output flow controls, geometry, wrap, ignore-input, normal echo, escape-sequence recognition, and input-count state. Pipe-mode proof expects the default 9600-baud, 8-bit, VT200, 80x24 profile; TTY geometry and echo remain live-derived. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add asynchronous OOB input handling and readahead state.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for practical CTERM characteristics candidate `f8af214c61c28210b1d2bccdb6a48ae6c893c44c` is green: Repository Policy `35796124281`, Build Bootstrap `35796161226`, Project State Gate `35796162960`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35796164502`, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35796165961`. Native `dnlogin` now adds asynchronous CTERM OOB input and readahead state. The session loop polls both NSP and stdin; Ctrl-C/Ctrl-O/Ctrl-Y are emitted as CTERM OOB records, ordinary input is buffered for a later START READ, INPUT STATE announces the empty-to-nonempty transition, CHECK INPUT includes buffered bytes, and CLEAR INPUT also clears readahead. The independent PyDECnet object-42 proof now feeds Ctrl-C before `phase7<CR>`, validates OOB plus INPUT STATE, then confirms the original seven-byte read path is preserved. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then CTERM set-characteristics/input-state transitions and remaining remote-login polish.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for asynchronous CTERM OOB/readahead candidate `c6030301354092e6718ea2d1e820044b76e37dd9` is green: Repository Policy `35798274150`, Build Bootstrap `35798305531`, Project State Gate `35798307037`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35798308553`, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35798310234`. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then remaining CTERM control/characteristic polish and standard Phase 7 tools beyond remote login.


Exact-SHA fast acceptance for CTERM characteristic-update candidate `4cae3302a4d62a3e37d089f2b56f16192d0245a6` is green: Repository Policy `35799031108`, Build Bootstrap `35799068199`, Project State Gate `35799069960`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35799071809`, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35799073943`. Exact-SHA fast acceptance for corrected FAL connection sequencing candidate `90d3e7e83760ee9c55a14747fc6de10d11c57182` is green: Repository Policy `35801926888`, Build Bootstrap `35801958096`, Project State Gate `35801959726`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35801961500`, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35801963463`. Native `dncopy` now advances from CONFIG probing to a first complete DAP file-retrieval sequence. `dncopy --get AREA.NODE FILE` performs CONFIG, ACCESS OPEN, receives minimal ATTRIBUTES plus ACK, establishes a data stream with CONTROL CONNECT/ACK, starts sequential retrieval with CONTROL GET, emits DATA payloads to stdout until STATUS, then closes access with ACCOMP command/response. The PyDECnet object-17 harness validates the exact ACCESS/CONTROL/ACCOMP sequence and returns deterministic test content plus EOF status; the live smoke requires `DAP-PHASE7`. The first 60-second PyDECnet application-proof rerun on `99d5d59cca67d88fb688aa5546563b98045a93cb` still failed in run `35870362895`, job `107212544983`, at the same harness boundary: `pydecnet-cterm.py` printed READY, serviced the expanded DAP sequence, and was terminated exactly 60 seconds later just as the candidate advanced to interactive CTERM, producing a later connection timeout. The 90-second rerun on `9e10a51c64990761ae4ce4f052ecbf6d6d20ea75` again expired exactly at the host application-proof boundary in PyDECnet L1 run `35871034412`, job `107214878945`. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for `9a360f71e97d92d1ab1c3f479d61a69db356163b` is green: Repository Policy `35887723851`, Build Bootstrap `35887839937`, Project State Gate `35887842516`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35887844943` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35887847701` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). Exact-SHA fast acceptance for `911cbef2d44f310b2dce7f6ad136c15d43e5e903` is green: Repository Policy `35893224106`, Build Bootstrap `35893278157`, Project State Gate `35893280869`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35893283798` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35893287252` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). Exact-SHA fast acceptance for `09b1314e56302f732f3785f3f725d804a2904aae` is green: Repository Policy `35897135787`, Build Bootstrap `35897194681`, Project State Gate `35897197257`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35897200120` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35897202835` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). Exact-SHA fast acceptance for `8e6f40c1da576f4d54c73defc3912ffcc4792db0` is green: Repository Policy `35900586088`, Build Bootstrap `35900635427`, Project State Gate `35900638045`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35900640774` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35900643443` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). Exact-SHA fast acceptance for `435afa74ffa0e73025768f3fd63778e3b9326950` is green: Repository Policy `35916298840`, Build Bootstrap `35916344282`, Project State Gate `35916347035`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35916350805` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35916353911` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). Fast acceptance for MAIL-11 v3 candidate `23cad17208a85542aa92f23cc5c75798ac87b7e7` stopped in Build Bootstrap `35923545809` on the same compile-only defect on both architectures: `dnmaild.c` added `uint16_t` endian helpers without including `<stdint.h>`. MAIL-11 v3 exposed a native deferred-accept socket ABI contradiction before promotion of `bf6df486ac4dde1318c35204bcad51331f0cc24b`: request-dependent accept data must be set on the accepted child, but `DSO_CONDATA` rejected every socket that already had a local link even though `DSO_CONACCEPT` consumes that child's `conndata_out`. Allow `DSO_CONDATA` only for an `ACC_DEFER` accepted child while its NSP connection remains in CR, retaining `EISCONN` everywhere else. The inbound socket proof now deliberately leaves listener accept data empty and sets `linux-accept` on the deferred child immediately before `DSO_CONACCEPT`, covering the exact MAIL-11 requirement. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then resume local sendmail/SMTP delivery bridging.


Repository tree integrity was immediately restored after the preceding low-level Git tree write omitted the base tree. This follow-up rebuilds the exact parent tree and reapplies only the deferred-accept socket/test/document deltas; no additional protocol behavior is changed. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then resume local sendmail/SMTP delivery bridging.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for `2df6fdbe5ac1723d79a0b07cbe65db757ad7e8c6` is green: Repository Policy `35938488605`, Build Bootstrap `35938520254`, Project State Gate `35938522061`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35938523731` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35938525546` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). Expand libdnet with native node/object database helpers. The compatibility header now exposes `nodeent`, `getnodebyname`, `getnodebyaddr`, `getnodeadd`, `getexecdev`, node iteration, the classic built-in object catalog, object number/name lookup and high-object-number controls. Node lookup uses the established `/etc/decnet.conf` compatibility format with bounded parsing; `dnet_conn` now falls back from numeric text to that node database. The independent PyDECnet proof creates a two-entry executor/peer database in the disposable candidate, verifies forward/reverse/executor/device/iteration lookups and MIRROR object lookup, then connects by node name and named object rather than numeric selectors. Signatures and database format were checked against pinned LinuxDECnet `ff39eef045d1e4b7b72a3d40111e89c07a473398`; implementation remains native. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then complete the remaining core libdnet compatibility helpers.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for `5fc7e06d081460d8b883f60b8a2a68a51162eff7` is green: Repository Policy `35949364623`, Build Bootstrap `35949398010`, Project State Gate `35949399448`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35949400851` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35949402435` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). Native libdnet node/object database lookup and named-node/named-object MIRROR interoperability are therefore accepted.

Complete the next core libdnet compatibility slice with `dnet_recv`, `dnet_eof`, `getnodename` and a shared-library build. `dnet_recv(..., MSG_EOR)` uses native record-boundary reporting, `dnet_eof` maps native link state through `DSO_LINKINFO`, and `getnodename` resolves the configured executor from the node database. The library now builds both `libdnet.a` and versioned `libdnet.so.1.0` with the `libdnet.so.1`/ `libdnet.so` compatibility links. Candidate images install both forms, while the independent MIRROR proof consumes the new receive/EOF/node-name helpers. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add daemon/listener compatibility helpers and close the Phase 7 library surface.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for `d7ccc7ec4a6924aceb8a42bb7150980bcffc71d7` is green: Repository Policy `35950232116`, Build Bootstrap `35950262455`, Project State Gate `35950264387`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35950266378` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35950268212` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). The shared/static libdnet build plus `dnet_recv`, `dnet_eof` and `getnodename` are therefore accepted.

Add the native daemon/listener compatibility library surface: `libdnet_daemon` now provides `dnet_daemon`, `dnet_accept`, `dnet_reject`, `dnet_set_optdata` and `dnet_daemon_name`, using deferred native AF_DECnet listeners and the existing Session Control socket options rather than legacy kernel assumptions. Static and versioned shared daemon libraries are installed in candidate images. A dedicated compatibility server binds named object `LIBMIRROR`, accepts through the library API and serves the MIRROR record protocol; pinned PyDECnet independently connects to that named object and validates accept data, positive records and malformed-record failure. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then audit remaining Phase 7 application/library gaps against the roadmap and close only those with concrete missing behavior.

Fast acceptance for daemon-compatibility candidate `a701a50812176209fb5473ce853a61578dce433a` passed Repository Policy `35951083403` and Project State Gate `35951117088`, but Build Bootstrap `35951115392` failed on both architectures and VM Lab `35951118830` failed at candidate build because strict warnings reject the deliberately ignored `chdir("/")` return value in `daemon.c`. Check that return value and propagate failure; daemon protocol behavior is unchanged. The candidate is rejected on this compile-only defect. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for `d7388754c5f62256eaadde280c9bc85eb18378a8` is green: Repository Policy `35951279432`, Build Bootstrap `35951308329`, Project State Gate `35951309501`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35951310725` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35951311984` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). The native `libdnet_daemon` compatibility surface and independent named-object server proof are therefore accepted.

The Phase 7 gap audit identifies a concrete missing diagnostics command from the standard DECnet/Linux environment: `dnping`. Add a native MIRROR-object diagnostic client with bounded count, frame size, interval and timeout controls plus quiet/verbose output. It resolves numeric or `/etc/decnet.conf` node names through native libdnet, validates every returned MIRROR record byte-for-byte and reports packet/round-trip statistics. Candidate images install the tool, and the pinned PyDECnet interoperability path now requires three 128-byte MIRROR loops through `dnping`. Behavior and command purpose were checked against pinned LinuxDECnet `ff39eef045d1e4b7b72a3d40111e89c07a473398`; implementation remains native. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then continue the Phase 7 gap audit with generic object-service dispatch and remaining administration tooling.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for `c73dfda8aa8c66362c8bf2f70be9db950c84f848` is green: Repository Policy `35952268719`, Build Bootstrap `35952298813`, Project State Gate `35952300239`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35952302132` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35952303714` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). Native `dnping` is therefore accepted.

A boundary audit found one local diagnostics defect outside the exercised 128-byte interoperability case: a one-byte `dnping -s 1` request reused byte zero for the per-packet sequence marker, overwriting the MIRROR request opcode. Preserve byte zero as the required MIRROR opcode and vary byte one only when present. No kernel or wire behavior outside that one-byte client boundary changes. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then continue Phase 7 with generic object-service dispatch.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for `264b9f322ff9aa21fd7da47e502ef3650f5c40bb` is green: Repository Policy `35952905898`, Build Bootstrap `35952939658`, Project State Gate `35952941153`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35952943100` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35952944778` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). The one-byte `dnping` boundary correction is therefore accepted.

Continue Phase 7 with a native generic object super-server, `dnetd`. The first bounded compatibility slice reads the classic five-field `dnetd.conf` shape, creates one native deferred AF_DECnet listener per configured numbered or named object, rejects unsupported wildcard and authentication modes rather than silently weakening them, supports classic auto-accept/reject control, optionally drops to an explicit local account, and execs configured programs directly with the accepted DECnet socket on stdin/stdout without a shell. Candidate images install `dnetd`; the independent PyDECnet proof connects to a configured named `DNETDTEST` object whose child accepts through native `libdnet_daemon` and serves the MIRROR protocol. Configuration purpose and execution model were checked against pinned LinuxDECnet `ff39eef045d1e4b7b72a3d40111e89c07a473398`; implementation remains native. Authentication/proxy and wildcard task dispatch remain explicit later increments, not false claims. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then add authenticated/proxy object policy or close Phase 7 if the remaining gap audit finds no release-blocking standard surface.

Fast acceptance for dnetd candidate `5f4f4b05f8d777ee5814d0d753bf45b082164ad3` passed Repository Policy `35953676180`, while Build Bootstrap `35953701646` failed on x86_64 and aarch64 because the new super-server used the legacy-only `DNSTAT_REJECTED` convenience constant, which is intentionally not part of this repository's native UAPI. The reject policy does not require disconnect data: issue native `DSO_CONREJECT` directly and remove the undeclared compatibility constant. Product behavior remains a clean Session rejection. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance.

Exact-SHA full promotion acceptance for `a967af2787638ab49bf1a929a27ef9f7ab8564e9` is green: Repository Policy `35954451490`, Build Bootstrap `35954483756`, Project State Gate `35954485477`, External Reference Baselines `35954487274`, x86_64/aarch64 E1-E4 VM runs `35954489283`, `35954491199`, `35954492983`, `35954494765`, and full 14-job Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35954497878`. Phase 7 is complete. The accepted userspace environment includes native NCP/NICE management, sethost/dnlogin/CTERM, DAP/FAL file tools and access policy, task/object access, PHONE, MAIL-11 with local sendmail and SMTP delivery bridges, the DECnet-native web server, MIRROR diagnostics, static/shared libdnet plus libdnet_daemon compatibility, and generic dnetd object dispatch, all on the native kernel stack and proven against pinned independent peers.

Begin Phase 8. Local/rootless VDE2 remains independently green at run `35440502997`, and local MULTINET TCP framing/reconnect remains independently green at run `35440787624`. The next work is to strengthen both transport proofs with restart/stress/multi-peer coverage, add the repository-tracked secret-safe Area-31 workflow and reusable Linux/VAX checks, and prove an actual cross-runner VDE2 switch-to-switch join before distributed scale depends on it. External Area-31 success remains supplemental and never substitutes for exact-SHA local acceptance.

Fast acceptance for Phase 7 closure commit `24ec051d25d4074555a4fc5af3f559b44f87bc29` is green: Repository Policy `35958370764`, Build Bootstrap `35958416991`, Project State Gate `35958419134`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35958421050` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35958423109` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1).

Strengthen the first Phase 8 VDE2 transport gate without conflating it with MULTINET. The local/rootless proof now requires a three-endpoint native libvdeplug multicast stress run with 256 content-checked frames, an endpoint close/reopen during the run, explicit failure to open a nonexistent switch, complete VDE switch teardown/recreation followed by a second 256-frame stress run, and Route20/PyDECnet adjacency recovery after the PyDECnet peer is stopped long enough for the adjacency to expire and then restarted. This upgrades the local VDE2 gate from a one-frame/one-adjacency sanity proof to positive, negative, multi-peer, restart and bounded stress coverage. Next: run the exact VDE2 workflow and fast acceptance; then strengthen MULTINET separately before introducing the Area-31 remote workflow.

Exact VDE2 workflow `35959075714` is green for `7331e36c243cd8cacd97b3ba4fd2d14c11ffd528`: the enhanced local/rootless gate passed three-endpoint frame stress, endpoint restart, nonexistent-endpoint rejection, full switch restart and independent Route20/PyDECnet adjacency restart. Exact-SHA fast acceptance is also green: Repository Policy `35959092344`, Build Bootstrap `35959125604`, Project State Gate `35959127790`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35959129713` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35959131714` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1).

Strengthen MULTINET independently. The live proof now uses one two-circuit L2 listener router and two simultaneous L1 connector routers, first confirms that a connector aimed at an unopened TCP port never reports Circuit up, then proves both live circuits, churns one connector through five complete down/reconnect cycles while the other remains alive, and finally restarts the central listener and requires both connectors to recover. The pinned PyDECnet MULTINET module suite still runs first, covering its fragmentation/coalescing and protocol mechanics. Next: run the exact MULTINET workflow and fast acceptance; then add the secret-safe Area-31 workflow on top of these independently proven local transports.

MULTINET workflow `35959801944` rejected `9eb7eedc03d0da17b50c64454e763ec2c4d6925b` before live transport execution. All 40 pinned PyDECnet MULTINET module tests passed, but the new live three-router configuration used circuit labels `MUL-B` and `MUL-C`; the pinned parser permits alphabetic prefixes followed only by hyphen/digits. Rename the central circuits to `MUL-1`/`MUL-2` and connector circuits to `MUL-0`. No MULTINET protocol behavior changes. Next: rerun the exact MULTINET workflow and fast acceptance.

MULTINET retry workflow `35959966978` accepted the corrected three-router configuration and formed both simultaneous live circuits, but the churn harness then false-failed waiting for a specific `Circuit down` event before reconnect. The transport's successful initial two-circuit state is present in the retained log; the pinned module suite had already passed all 40 cases, including local/remote restart and close detection. Make the live restart discriminator depend on the stronger externally useful invariant: after each killed connector or central listener is restarted, a new `Circuit up` must be observed while the unaffected peer remains alive. This removes an unnecessary event-timing dependency without weakening reconnect proof. Next: rerun the exact MULTINET workflow and fast acceptance.

Light documentation/code sanity pass after `92e5f56293e9a0747b7116bcf4fbb0476b22c5ec`: enhanced MULTINET workflow `35960257018` is green on that exact commit, followed by fast acceptance Build Bootstrap `35960311447`, Project State Gate `35960313483`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35960315522` (x86_64 and aarch64) and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35960317698`. A later scheduled full reproof of the same exact commit is also green through Build Bootstrap `35984721554`, Project State Gate `35984724951`, External Reference Baselines `35984728083`, x86_64/aarch64 E1-E4 runs `35984731413`, `35984734808`, `35984737886`, `35984740897`, and full interoperability `35984744413`.

The sanity pass found one concrete code drift and several documentation drifts. `dnmultinet.py` still selected only Ethernet adjacency events 4.15/4.16 even though its WAN circuit is MULTINET point-to-point, whose useful circuit down/up events are 4.8/4.10; generate both point-to-point and Ethernet event classes and lock the generated configuration in its Makefile tests. Refresh Phase 8 status, enhanced VDE2/MULTINET run IDs, the current PyDECnet live pin and the three-router MULTINET acceptance description across the lab/pre-production documents. No kernel, routing, NSP or Session behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then implement the secret-safe Area-31 workflow and reusable Linux/VAX checks; cross-runner VDE2 remains separately outstanding.

Documentation sanity follow-up: remove the stale NSP-foundation paragraph that had drifted under the Phase 7 heading; NSP remains fully recorded in the Phase 5 chronology below. No implementation or acceptance requirement changes. Fast acceptance of the preceding sanity commit `e0da821f6ba10f8b080d8615463af77889052236` is green: Repository Policy `35991295675`, Build Bootstrap `35991336543`, Project State Gate `35991338731`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35991340917` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35991343247` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1).

Begin the repository-tracked Area-31 integration boundary. Add a manual-only Area-31 workflow whose first executable step verifies all five required runtime secret names before checkout or remote lab activity, masks populated values, and requires an explicitly assigned disposable Area-31 gateway address/name for each run. Extend `dnmultinet.py` with a secret-safe runtime-peer mode that reads the remote host/port from environment variables rather than process arguments, refuses to print runtime peer configuration in dry-run mode, and can expose a local PyDECnet API socket without persisting its generated configuration. Add a reusable Area-31 driver and NICE probe that start a rootless VDE-backed L2 MULTINET gateway, keep gateway output discarded, and require executor summary/status/counter replies from the VAX router through the remote path without printing the VAX address or credentials. `VAX_USERNAME` and `VAX_PASSWORD` are preflighted but intentionally unused until credential-bearing Linux/VAX pairs are added. This establishes the secret-safe remote workflow foundation only; it is not yet Phase 8 exit evidence because a native DECnet-IV-Linux VM has not traversed the VDE-to-MULTINET path and cross-runner VDE2 remains unproven. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then manually execute the Area-31 workflow with an assigned gateway identity and, once remote routing is green, put a native Linux VM on the VDE side for routing/NICE/NSP/Session pairs.

Fast acceptance rejected Area-31 workflow candidate `86bfafde35e0123328e6a3c5ce6eadca8c4089cb` in Build Bootstrap `35996149827`: the new peer-host validator accidentally double-escaped the host character class and rejected the existing valid `127.0.0.1` selftest before any remote workflow execution. Replace that over-constrained expression with the actual configuration invariant needed here: peer hosts must be nonempty and contain no whitespace. This accepts ordinary hostnames plus IPv4/IPv6 forms while still preventing config-token injection. No transport or protocol behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for Area-31 workflow fix `0b1c34f5163c5cfe444b64e2b353327d77ec5171` is green: Repository Policy `35996298162`, Build Bootstrap `35996345148`, Project State Gate `35996348444`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35996351412` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35996354404` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). Strengthen the new secret-safety boundary with build-time regressions: compile the Area-31 NICE helper, syntax-check the driver, require a complete dummy preflight to pass, require an omitted password to fail by secret name only, and require secret-backed `dnmultinet --dry-run` to fail without echoing the peer value. No remote Area-31 traffic is generated by these local checks. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then remote execution when an Area-31 gateway identity is explicitly assigned; meanwhile continue the independent cross-runner VDE2 path without inventing rendezvous infrastructure.

Fast acceptance rejected secret-preflight regression candidate `2afd4b71152d3160e0f0b2d29b01091363e9c442` in Build Bootstrap `35997309449` on both architectures. The product preflight itself correctly passed with all five dummy values; the new negative Makefile checks were defective because shell command substitution and the captured variable used single-dollar syntax, so Make consumed them before the shell ran and falsely reported that a missing password was accepted. Escape both constructs for the recipe shell. Product and secret-validation behavior are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for secret-preflight regression fix `a9bd1594ad6a287d971ae23cb61be33fca6e226b` is green: Repository Policy `35997600349`, Build Bootstrap `35997647871`, Project State Gate `35997650495`, Python QEMU VM Lab `35997652948` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `35997655595` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1).

Continue the independent cross-runner VDE2 path without assuming hosted runners are directly reachable. Add a manual two-runner proof that uses an explicitly supplied existing SSH bastion only as rendezvous: the server runner exposes a restricted ephemeral SSH endpoint through an assigned reverse-forward port, each runner owns its own `vde_switch`, and the client joins the switches with the documented `dpipe vde_plug = ssh ... vde_plug` transport. A shared native libvdeplug probe requires marked frame request/response delivery in both directions; Route20 on the server and pinned PyDECnet on the client must form an adjacency, recover after the SSH/VDE bridge is disconnected/reconnected, then recover again after the client VDE switch and PyDECnet are restarted. The SSH key and pinned bastion host-key material remain runtime-only and the inner ephemeral SSH account is command-restricted to the VDE plug/readiness/completion operations. Extend the local VDE2 gate to compile and exercise the same marked-frame helper, and add build-time syntax/preflight negatives for the cross-runner driver. This commit establishes an executable cross-runner gate but does not claim the cross-runner exit criterion until the manual two-host workflow itself is green. Next: run exact local VDE2 plus fast acceptance; then execute the cross-runner workflow when the pre-existing SSH rendezvous secrets and an unused reverse-forward port are available, while Area-31 remote execution remains separately gated by its assigned DECnet identity.

Repository Policy `36000237236` rejected cross-runner VDE2 candidate `40b851733736bae71281292606998a095170d228` before acceptance because the new C helper used a block-comment rendering of the license text instead of the repository's canonical C/C++ line-comment header. Convert only that header to the canonical form; cross-runner transport/test behavior is unchanged. Next: rerun exact local VDE2 proof and fast acceptance.

The first exact acceptance of cross-runner gate header-fix candidate `cb38a24a17cad09dc9072b4068d50aab2d5354f5` passed Repository Policy and exact local VDE2 workflow `36000393096`, but Build Bootstrap `36000449054` rejected both architectures in the new cross-runner negative preflight recipe. As with the earlier Area-31 regression, Make consumed single-dollar command-substitution/output references before the recipe shell ran, making the missing-key negative appear to succeed. Escape only those cross-runner recipe dollars; the driver preflight itself is correct and VDE2 transport behavior is unchanged. Next: exact local VDE2 plus fast acceptance.

Exact local VDE2 proof and fast acceptance for cross-runner preflight fix `4810091fd200000fbab2d1a24b143530e8b412c3` are green. VDE2 Transport Proof `36000880150` passed the enhanced local/rootless transport gate; Repository Policy `36000880200`, Build Bootstrap `36000960787`, Project State Gate `36000963485`, Python QEMU VM Lab `36000966262` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36000968595` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1) all passed on the same exact commit. The repository-tracked cross-runner gate is therefore locally accepted but remains external evidence until a real two-runner rendezvous run is green. Area-31 remote execution also remains gated by runtime secrets and an explicitly assigned disposable DECnet identity. Next: add the native DECnet-IV-Linux VM side of the Area-31 VDE-to-MULTINET path and reusable routing/NICE/NSP/Session checks without weakening the external-execution prerequisites.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for continuity commit `533fc5c65dbc0a31cea96e3abf1c11d8f51352f2` is green: Repository Policy `36002783262`, Build Bootstrap `36002830764`, Project State Gate `36002833276`, Python QEMU VM Lab `36002835692` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36002838297` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). A direct sanity review of the still-unexecuted cross-runner path found a concrete generated-wrapper defect: the unquoted heredoc expanded `SSH_ORIGINAL_COMMAND` while the server driver was constructing its forced-command wrapper, so every later inner-SSH command would have been compared against an empty string. Generate the wrapper through one helper that preserves the runtime variable, and add a build-time wrapper selftest that proves the allowed readiness command dispatches while an unlisted command returns 126. Cross-runner external evidence remains unclaimed until a real two-runner rendezvous succeeds. Next: exact local VDE2 plus fast acceptance, then continue the native Linux-through-Area-31 gateway path while external executions remain prerequisite-gated.

Exact local VDE2 proof and fast acceptance for cross-runner forced-command fix `3b1099e507e00adbb2a81541e25bf73e8bd1d159` are green: VDE2 Transport Proof `36003884984`, Repository Policy `36003891747`, Build Bootstrap `36003972241`, Project State Gate `36003974854`, Python QEMU VM Lab `36003977418` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36003980287` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). Extend the Area-31 gate from a host-side PyDECnet reachability probe to an exact native DECnet-IV-Linux candidate VM on the gateway VDE segment. The remote workflow now builds the exact amd64 candidate from the source-independent foundation, supplies the assigned Linux/gateway/VAX identities through a private read-only control disk rather than process arguments, requires native adjacency to the gateway, executes native NML summary/status/counters and three content-checked MIRROR exchanges to the VAX router, and reports only generic pass/failure markers. No runtime credential value is placed on the VM command line or retained as evidence. This is repository coverage only until the external Area-31 workflow executes with assigned identities and secrets. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance; then add credential-bearing Linux/VAX application pairs without exposing credentials, while actual Area-31 and cross-runner runs remain external prerequisites.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for native Area-31 candidate-path commit `3e539d0e5069f361926b07293469fd6938e2baef` is green: Repository Policy `36005649942`, Build Bootstrap `36005705657`, Project State Gate `36005708328`, Python QEMU VM Lab `36005711252` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36005713886` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). Extend the still-unexecuted Area-31 external gate with the first credential-bearing native Linux/VAX application pairs. `dnlogin` and `dncopy` now accept `DNACCESS_USER`/`DNACCESS_PASSWORD`/`DNACCESS_ACCOUNT` defaults so runtime access data does not need to appear in application arguments; explicit command-line options still override those defaults. The disposable Area-31 control image carries the VAX user/password only in private files, and the native VM must pass an authenticated CTERM/Foundation probe plus an authenticated FAL/DAP CONFIG exchange after the already-required NICE and MIRROR proofs. Secret values are neither logged nor retained as artifacts. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then continue repository-side Area-31 application coverage while actual Area-31 and cross-runner VDE2 execution remain gated by their external runtime prerequisites.

Fast acceptance rejected credential-probe candidate `393a22c70a4d08696df1464849e486ce55ae8b43` in Build Bootstrap `36015960885` on both x86_64 and aarch64. Fast acceptance rejected credential-preflight repair candidate `de1e5d44bc0824390028b6f45e78d9b9b9481a92` in Build Bootstrap `36016477522`. Exact-SHA fast acceptance for credential-bearing Area-31 probe candidate `994ecb0818d6b1e2039b3d50d8a64117ca0500b9` is green: Repository Policy `36016773860`, Build Bootstrap `36016833963`, Project State Gate `36016837287`, Python QEMU VM Lab `36016841176` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36016845386` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). Exact-SHA fast acceptance for Area-31 FAL directory candidate `b8a08be77afe3fe4d16f4d75f57fb4308a9c48dc` is green: Repository Policy `36018731565`, Build Bootstrap `36018790020`, Project State Gate `36018793939`, Python QEMU VM Lab `36018797490` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36018802274` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). Add native `dnlynx`, a bounded HTTP/1.0-over-DECnet client for named object `HTTP`, with optional Session access data supplied by the existing `DNACCESS_*` environment defaults. Install it into exact candidate images and add the reverse application-direction proof missing from the existing `dnhttpd` coverage: the native Linux candidate must fetch content from a dynamically bound independent PyDECnet HTTP object.
