Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full x86_64/aarch64 independent HTTP-client proof at the next consolidated/full gate while Area-31 and cross-runner executions remain external prerequisites.

Exact current-head full acceptance for native HTTP client candidate `3e7928335807cb0ff63f9dce1078b638c4ab798d` is green through Build Bootstrap `36021867668`, Project State Gate `36021871497`, External Reference Baselines `36021875243`, the full x86_64/aarch64 Python VM matrix `36021878906`/`36021882933`/`36021887119`/`36021890820`, and full independent interoperability `36021894313`. Add the real-VAX QCOCAL HTTP experiment without guessing its node address: the manual Area-31 workflow accepts an optional explicit `qcocal_node`; when present, the exact native Linux VM uses the existing runtime VAX credentials to upload a temporary OpenVMS zero-object `HTTP.COM` through FAL, fetches it with native `dnlynx`, requires the fixed response marker, and deletes the remote procedure. The OpenVMS command procedure uses the documented named zero-object/SYS$NET activation model. This remains repository-prepared coverage, not claimed external evidence, until the Area-31 workflow actually runs with a current QCOCAL address and succeeds. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then execute Area-31 when its explicit runtime identities are available; cross-runner VDE2 remains independently external-gated.

Repository Policy `36024926392` rejected QCOCAL candidate `9d6267e260390a6d9e3a902906ac322a6cb13a85` before acceptance because the checked-in OpenVMS `.COM` procedure necessarily used DCL `$!` comments while LICENSE-MONKEY classifies generic text suffixes under the canonical hash-comment header. Do not weaken policy or inject non-DCL header lines into a remote command procedure. Replace the checked-in `HTTP.COM` with a canonical Python generator that creates the ephemeral DCL procedure only inside the disposable control image. QCOCAL protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for runtime-generated QCOCAL object candidate `bfa7eb73dbe94f5eab51006fa3fffd0a8a3d5682` is green: Repository Policy `36025276435`, Build Bootstrap `36025337871`, Project State Gate `36025341706`, Python QEMU VM Lab `36025345852` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36025349071` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). Generalize the still-manual Area-31 external gate so the exact native proof runs sequentially on both amd64 and arm64 hosted runners using the corresponding immutable foundation and native QEMU machine. The QCOCAL deployment remains serialized, so its temporary `HTTP.COM` cannot race between architectures. Add build-time selftest of the DCL generator plus amd64/arm64 Area-31 preflight checks. No external Area-31 success is claimed until the manual workflow executes with assigned gateway/Linux identities and, for QCOCAL, its current explicit Area-31 address. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance; then execute the dual-architecture Area-31 gate when runtime identities are available.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for dual-architecture Area-31 candidate `e49e28bb8a748eb9645eddad418cec9ecaaa6099` is green: Repository Policy `36026736544`, Build Bootstrap `36026788201`, Project State Gate `36026791357`, Python QEMU VM Lab `36026794470` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36026797274` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). Remove the need to know QCOCAL's numeric node address in advance: after MULTINET routing and the configured VAX NML endpoint are live, query its NICE known-node summary, resolve the exact node name `QCOCAL`, and feed the discovered Area-31 address into the existing temporary DCL/FAL/`dnlynx` proof. An explicit `qcocal_node` remains a validated override. Discovery failure leaves the optional QCOCAL subtest disabled rather than weakening the required VAX router proof. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance; then execute the manual dual-architecture Area-31 workflow when disposable gateway/Linux node identities are available.

Add a local syntax regression for the new NICE QCOCAL discovery helper so build acceptance cannot leave that manual Area-31 path unparsed until the first external run. This is test-only; live discovery behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then manual dual-architecture Area-31 execution when disposable identities are available.

Harden QCOCAL NICE discovery: first query the volatile SHOW-known-node view, then fall back to the permanent LIST-known-node database when the live table does not carry the requested name. This avoids guessing an address and preserves the explicit override. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then manual dual-architecture Area-31 execution when disposable identities are available.

Make the QCOCAL experiment non-destructive. Native `dnlynx` now accepts a bounded `-o OBJECT` named-object override while retaining `HTTP` as the default. The Area-31 VAX proof uses the project-specific zero-object name `DNIVHT` and temporary `DNIVHT.COM` instead of overwriting/deleting a potentially pre-existing user `HTTP.COM`. Object names are restricted to 1..16 alphanumeric characters beginning with a letter and covered by the existing native selftest. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then manual dual-architecture Area-31 execution when disposable identities are available.

Prove the new `dnlynx -o OBJECT` path before relying on QCOCAL. Extend the pinned PyDECnet HTTP server oracle to bind a caller-selected named object and response marker, and add a second independent client-direction exchange using object `DNIVHT` after the existing default `HTTP` exchange. This exercises the exact object override used by the non-destructive VAX experiment under normal x86_64/aarch64 independent interoperability. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then manual dual-architecture Area-31 execution when disposable identities are available.

Fail closed around the temporary QCOCAL object file. Before installing `DNIVHT.COM`, the native VM performs an authenticated FAL directory query for `DNIVHT.COM;*`; any query failure aborts, and any pre-existing version aborts without modification. Only a proven-empty name is uploaded, exercised through `dnlynx -o DNIVHT`, and removed. This prevents the external experiment from overwriting an existing VAX account file. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then manual dual-architecture Area-31 execution when disposable identities are available.

Extend the optional QCOCAL pair with generic task/object access. `dntask` now consumes `DNACCESS_USER`/`DNACCESS_PASSWORD`/`DNACCESS_ACCOUNT` defaults when access fields are not embedded in the task specification, keeping credentials off command lines while preserving explicit transparent syntax precedence. The Area-31 control image generates a second temporary VMS zero-object procedure, `DNIVTK.COM`; QCOCAL preflight fails closed if either project-specific object file already exists, then the native VM uploads `DNIVTK.COM`, invokes `QCOCAL::DNIVTK` through `dntask`, requires `QCOCAL-DECNET-TASK-PASS`, and removes the procedure. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then manual dual-architecture Area-31 execution when disposable identities are available.

Fail closed on oversized `dntask` environment-supplied access data. `DNACCESS_USER`, `DNACCESS_PASSWORD`, and `DNACCESS_ACCOUNT` now produce a bounded validation error rather than silently falling back to an unauthenticated task connection when an environment field exceeds the classic DECnet access-data limit. Explicit task-spec access fields remain authoritative. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then manual dual-architecture Area-31 execution when disposable identities are available.

Bind an explicit QCOCAL override back to live NICE information before any remote file mutation. The Area-31 driver now always attempts the VAX manager's QCOCAL name lookup; when both an explicit `qcocal_node` and a live NICE mapping exist, they must match exactly or the proof stops. A manager with no QCOCAL mapping may still use the explicit override, preserving the escape hatch for sparse VMS databases. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then manual dual-architecture Area-31 execution when disposable identities are available.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for QCOCAL NICE cross-check candidate `8e25ef0ac72993f02efbd38cf34aba499864efc8` is green: Repository Policy `36032464953`, Build Bootstrap `36032524591`, Project State Gate `36032527137`, Python QEMU VM Lab `36032529528` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36032532035` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). Repository-side Phase 8 preparation is now at the external-execution boundary. Remaining Phase 8 exit evidence requires (1) a real dual-architecture Area-31 workflow run using explicitly assigned disposable gateway/Linux node identities and the configured runtime secrets, and (2) a real two-runner VDE2 workflow run using an existing SSH rendezvous and assigned reverse-forward port. Do not fabricate either result or substitute local acceptance. Next: execute and record those external gates when their runtime inputs are available; close Phase 8 only if both are green.

Fast acceptance of continuity commit `56e80b84a7b2266dc3b98cedd2064225b62d1d9c` passed Repository Policy `36034862042`, Build Bootstrap `36034917777`, Project State Gate `36034920798`, x86_64 E1 and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36034926627`, but Python QEMU VM Lab `36034923551` false-failed the arm64 E1 controller bound. The guest emitted `DNIV-E1-PASS` at uptime 378.213 seconds, just after the configured 375-second arm64 controller budget; protocol recovery had already completed successfully. Raise only the bounded arm64 VM-lab controller allowance to 420 seconds, preserving all protocol and convergence assertions. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then return to the Phase 8 external-execution boundary.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for arm64 E1 controller-bound candidate `c36dcea810dd10d82bac26e64421b9638410cd9e` is green: Repository Policy `36036073070`, Build Bootstrap `36036128169`, Project State Gate `36036131759`, Python QEMU VM Lab `36036134865` (x86_64 and aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36036138366` (Route20 and PyDECnet L1). The bounded arm64 allowance change therefore clears its intended false-timeout without weakening protocol assertions. Phase 8 is back at the external-execution boundary: execute and record the real dual-architecture Area-31 workflow and the real two-runner VDE2-over-SSH workflow when their external runtime inputs are available; close Phase 8 only if both are green.

Automate Phase 8 execution. Consolidated/full acceptance dispatches local VDE2 and MULTINET proofs; full acceptance dispatches cross-runner VDE2-over-SSH and dual-architecture Area-31/MULTINET interoperability. No human-entered DECnet identity or reverse port is required. The cross-runner port is exact-SHA-derived. Area-31 uses only Area 31: it establishes the secret-backed MULTINET path, asks PYRTR at 31.3 for its known nodes through NML, chooses two high-number Area-31 addresses absent from that list, then restarts the gateway on the selected identity before the native Linux/VAX proof. Secrets remain GitHub Actions secrets and are never logged. Child workflows bind checkout to the dispatcher-supplied exact SHA. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance to execute autonomous Phase 8 external gates.

Repository Policy `36041058603` rejected `dd51159bb8e441efb84c1034d0d35a4d6bd1053c` solely because new `tests/lab/area31-list-known.py` lacked the canonical blank line after the project license header. Restore the canonical Python header formatting; workflow/protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance.

Full Area-31 run `36043104934` exposed a host packaging defect on x86_64: Ubuntu's hosted QEMU reports no built-in `-netdev vde`, so the external proof stopped before any Area-31 traffic. Preserve VDE2 as the lab fabric but add a host TAP fallback: when QEMU lacks its optional VDE backend, create a disposable TAP owned by the runner, bridge it to the VDE switch with `vde_plug2tap`, attach QEMU through its standard TAP backend, and clean both bridge and TAP on exit. No DECnet protocol behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then rerun full acceptance and continue through the real Area-31 and cross-runner gates.

Full external run `36045020013` reached the autonomous Area-31 preflight but exposed a generated-name defect before network activity: high-number nodes such as 31.1021 produced seven-character names (`DNG1021`), exceeding the Phase IV six-character node-name limit. Keep the exact-SHA high-number allocation but use fixed six-character `DG%04d`/`DL%04d` names for gateway/Linux identities. No wire behavior changes. The same full run confirmed local VDE2 `36045011635`, local MULTINET `36045014203`, full VM coverage, references, and independent interoperability are green. Cross-runner VDE2 `36045016850` remains blocked because all configured `VDE_SSH_*` Actions secrets resolve empty; do not fabricate rendezvous credentials. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then rerun full Area-31; keep cross-runner fail-closed until a real SSH rendezvous secret set exists.

Full Area-31 run `36051102753` now passes autonomous identity preflight but both architectures lose the user-space VDE/MULTINET gateway before VAX NML becomes reachable. The driver previously discarded the gateway stderr, hiding the concrete PyDECnet failure. Retain the gateway log only in the disposable job workspace and emit it on early gateway/NICE failure through explicit masking of every secret-backed host/port/VAX/access value. No protocol behavior changes. Cross-runner run `36051099600` still fails closed because all `VDE_SSH_*` rendezvous secrets are absent. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then rerun full acceptance to capture and fix the concrete Area-31 gateway failure.

Masked diagnostics from x86_64 Area-31 run `36054313471` exposed the gateway failure: pinned PyDECnet rejects a single top-level config file containing both `routing` and `api` (`Exactly one of routing, bridge, or http and/or api required`). Fix `dnmultinet` to emit routing and API as separate PyDECnet top-level config files and pass both files to `decnet.main`; memory-backed configs remain the default and secret-backed peer data is still never printed or persisted. This preserves the local API socket strictly as control plumbing while PYRTR is queried through real NML over DECnet. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then rerun full Area-31.

Tighten the `dnmultinet` split-config regression test: exercise `build_config()` and `build_api_config()` directly and require the routing config to contain no API component while the separate API config contains the requested socket. The earlier validate-only check returned before config generation and therefore did not cover the defect. Runtime behavior is unchanged.

Full Area-31 x86_64 run `36056177189` proved the MULTINET circuit itself is healthy: the gateway reached `Circuit up` with adjacent PYRTR at `31.3`, but the driver incorrectly waited for the separate VAX NICE endpoint before asking PYRTR for topology, creating a false ordering dependency and repeated local API disconnect noise. Reorder the proof around the actual network contract: wait for the real MULTINET adjacency to PYRTR, issue the standard NCP/NML equivalent of `TELL 31.3 SHOW KNOWN NODES`, choose the final disposable Area-31 gateway/Linux identities from addresses absent in that returned view, restart on the chosen identity, then require the VAX NICE proof. Remove the now-unneeded custom known-node API helper. Also make the split routing/API config regression test exercise generated configs directly and refresh stale HECnet lab documentation. No area other than 31 is used. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then rerun full acceptance.

Fast Build Bootstrap `36058353726` rejected the new split-config regression on both architectures because the Makefile passed `\\n` to Python as a literal backslash-plus-n instead of a newline escape; the implementation itself already generates separate routing and API configs. Correct only the test literal escaping. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance.

Full Area-31 run `36059574567` confirmed the new PYRTR NML query works on both architectures, but the allocator searched only 31.900-31.1023 and found no two addresses absent from PYRTR's known-node view. That high-number restriction was artificial. Scan the complete legal Area-31 node range 1..1023 in a deterministic SHA-seeded permutation, excluding PYRTR 31.3 and the configured VAX address, and choose the first two addresses not returned by `TELL 31.3 SHOW KNOWN NODES`. No other area is ever considered. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then rerun full acceptance.

Full acceptance at exact main `259abc71d3c20f075b5032efd4719d47641b07af` completed Python QEMU VM Lab `36062371887` green on x86_64/aarch64 and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36062374633` green across all 14 jobs. Cross-runner VDE2 run `36062382395` remains correctly fail-closed because the required `VDE_SSH_*` rendezvous secrets are absent. Area-31 run `36062384963` reached the real PYRTR NML query on both architectures but the allocator still rejected the entire legal Area-31 range. The concrete defect is whole-file address matching against NCP `SHOW KNOWN NODES`: anonymous `Unreachable` routing-table rows are placeholders rather than allocated/configured identities, yet their addresses were treated as occupied. Parse only anchored Area-31 node rows; treat named rows and rows whose state is not `Unreachable` as occupied, ignore anonymous `Unreachable` placeholders, require a structured PYRTR 31.3 row, and retain explicit PYRTR/VAX exclusions. Keep the standard `TELL 31.3 SHOW KNOWN NODES` NML query; do not substitute `LIST KNOWN NODES`, whose permanent flag does not by itself remove routing-layer rows in the pinned PyDECnet implementation. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance.

Full Area-31 x86_64 run `36066609444` finally exposed the exact PYRTR NCP rendering. `TELL 31.3 SHOW KNOWN NODES` returns an executor banner `Executor node = 31.3 (PYRTR), State = On,` and omits 31.3 from the following node table. The Area-31 table itself contains named reachable/unreachable entries plus anonymous `Unreachable` routing slots throughout the legal range, confirming that whole-file address matching was the allocation defect. The first structured parser correctly distinguishes named/non-Unreachable rows from anonymous placeholders but incorrectly required PYRTR 31.3 to appear as a table row. Parse the executor banner as an occupied structured identity as well; the existing explicit 31.3 exclusion remains. No query or protocol behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance.

Full Area-31 run `36080601382` on exact candidate `fdc7a32b5c38dbdd104cbbfbba276d6c4a730974` proves the structured allocator fix: x86_64 selected 31.285 as absent from PYRTR's occupied-node view, restarted as DG0285, and re-established the real MULTINET adjacency to PYRTR 31.3. The next failure is later: VAX NICE did not converge. The retry loop discarded every helper diagnostic, so the run cannot distinguish missing routing from an NML Session rejection. Preserve the last `area31-nice.py` diagnostic and, on timeout, print only the configured VAX row from the local gateway's real NCP `SHOW KNOWN NODES` view before the already-masked gateway log. This is diagnostic-only and does not change DECnet protocol behavior, identities, credentials or queries. Cross-runner VDE2 remains separately fail-closed on the absent SSH rendezvous secrets. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then rerun full acceptance and fix only the newly exposed concrete Area-31 cause.

Full Area-31 run `36082276468` on exact candidate `97aaa18718cfeb99e377b74fb2c6deeb3f031173` exposes the next concrete x86_64 failure. After allocating and restarting, the local gateway's own NCP known-node view reports the configured VAX reachable through `WAN-0` via PYRTR 31.3, so routing convergence is not the blocker. The NML Session connect also succeeds and returns a data message; `area31-nice.py` then raises `NICE summary failed` because its hard-coded positive-reply signature rejects the real VAX response. Preserve the response type and a bounded 64-byte hex prefix on failure so the real VMS NICE framing can be implemented exactly rather than guessed. Tighten the route diagnostic to match the VAX address as the first table field so a short address such as 31.1 does not also print 31.10..31.199. No protocol behavior changes yet. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then rerun full acceptance to capture the real VAX NICE response bytes and fix only that parser defect.

Full Area-31 x86_64 run `36083581481` on exact candidate `0213583bc30f2cfd139f1ba3cf3031d426b60ba5` captured the real VMS NICE framing: the first executor reply is `02ffff00`, the standard retcode-2 multiple-response header. The prior helper incorrectly required its first frame to be a retcode-1 item and therefore rejected a valid VAX NML session. Mirror the pinned PyDECnet NCP response iterator: consume retcode 2 as list start, validate positive node-item replies, require retcode -128 list termination, and reject negative/error or malformed sequences. Build the executor requests with the pinned NICE classes rather than raw literals; this also corrects the previously mislabeled summary request from information selector 2 (characteristics) to selector 0 (summary), while retaining status 1 and counters 3. Add syntax coverage for the helper. Candidate kernel and wire protocol behavior are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and continue only from concrete external results.

Full Area-31 x86_64 run `36084834122` on exact candidate `3581114467ddf8dd2cba531bb5d095efce792aef` proves the VMS NICE framing correction: executor summary, status and counters all pass, and QCOCAL is discovered through NICE. The run then exposes two host-lab defects before the native VM can execute. The DECnet MAC arithmetic at `prove-area31.sh` is missing the final arithmetic-expansion parenthesis, so Bash interprets `<< 2` as a here-document at runtime and corrupts the generated MAC. Separately, the QEMU-without-VDE fallback gives the same TAP device to both `vde_plug2tap` and QEMU; the first process owns the TAP queue and QEMU fails with `Device or resource busy`. Correct the MAC expression and use two TAP devices joined by a disposable Linux bridge: one TAP is owned by `vde_plug2tap`, the other by QEMU. Extend cleanup for both TAPs/bridge and emit a secret-masked native VM log on later failure. No DECnet candidate protocol behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and continue from the next concrete Area-31 result.

Full Area-31 x86_64 job `107918164560` in run `36086078999` on exact candidate `12b5313f142b49786da6a5d1a07ac70a3e237bbb` proves the host TAP fallback and Python/VMS NICE path are now healthy: disposable identities are allocated, the gateway reaches PYRTR 31.3, executor summary/status/counters pass, and QCOCAL is discovered through NICE. The native Linux VM then boots and fails specifically at `area31-native: NICE proof failed`. Its C probe still assumes a single retcode-1 NICE reply, while the same real VMS endpoint has already proven the standard retcode-2 multiple-response header followed by positive node item(s) and retcode -128 termination. Make the native probe consume that exact sequence, validate each positive executor node reply with the repository NICE parser and require the configured target address. Add fast syntax coverage for `area31-native.c`. Candidate kernel and wire behavior are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and continue from the next concrete Area-31 result.

Full Area-31 x86_64 job `107924875358` in run `36088269646` on exact candidate `861e0f671618692da899fe0eaa7b851e421e3578` proves the native VMS NICE correction: native executor summary/status/counters now pass. The next concrete failure is MIRROR. The Area-31 C probe requests the string object name `MIRROR`, while the repository's independent-reference helper identifies the standard DECnet MIRROR endpoint as numbered object 25 and uses numeric object 25 by default. Use explicit `#25` for the real VMS peer so Session Control selects the standard numbered object rather than requiring a named-object registration. MIRROR payload semantics are unchanged. Cross-runner VDE2 remains fail-closed solely on the absent `VDE_SSH_*` rendezvous secrets. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and continue from the next concrete Area-31 result.

Full Area-31 x86_64 job `107928474130` in run `36089449271` on exact candidate `762ad596b27e3e00be4999eb0726ee0566228f59` again reaches PYRTR, passes the host-side VAX NICE checks and discovers QCOCAL, but the freshly booted native node intermittently fails its first NICE operation. This is a readiness race in the Area-31 smoke harness: it waits only for the local adjacency to the disposable gateway, then immediately opens a Session connection to the remote VAX. Adjacency-up does not prove that the VAX L1 route has arrived. Require the native kernel route table to contain the exact VAX destination via the selected gateway before starting NICE/MIRROR. This changes only lab convergence gating, not candidate routing or Session behavior. The numeric MIRROR object-25 correction remains in place and will be exercised once route convergence is explicit. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance.

Full Area-31 x86_64 job `107932753210` in run `36090844216` on exact candidate `8819b622cf4c01f93d1003ba7cb7accd560d85b1` proves that explicit VAX route convergence alone does not remove the intermittent native NICE failure: the native route is present before the probe, yet `area31-native` still reports only the aggregate NICE failure. Earlier exact-head runs have shown the same native NICE code succeeding, so changing protocol behavior without the failing wire/stage evidence would be guesswork. Add bounded failure-only diagnostics to the native probe: identify the NICE information selector and socket stage, report errno, and dump at most the first 32 reply bytes when framing/retcode/entity validation fails. No credentials, endpoints or successful-session payloads are logged, and candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance to capture the concrete failing native NICE stage/frame.

Full Area-31 x86_64 job `107935998822` in run `36091932114` on exact candidate `dcdaadedece5e447b42c3286bf2b9259ba53fd37` reaches the newly instrumented native probe without emitting a NICE failure diagnostic and proceeds to the same later failure already seen on arm64: `DNIV-AREA31-NATIVE-FAIL reason=qcocal-http-preflight`. This proves the current blocker is QCOCAL preflight, not native NICE. The preflight asks FAL to list only `DNIV*.COM;*`; a clean QCOCAL with no matching temporary objects can legitimately return a no-match status, which the generic directory client reports as failure. Gate on the exact QCOCAL route, prove FAL Session/access independently with `dncopy --probe`, then list the normal broad `*.*;*` directory and reject only if DNIVHT/DNIVTK already exist. Also reduce failure-only NICE hex evidence from 32 bytes to the four-byte NICE reply header so endpoint-derived entity bytes cannot leak through logs. No candidate protocol behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance.

Full Area-31 x86_64 job `107950032334` in run `36096590771` on exact candidate `7cc9ca1f35239795c1c43a2e9b49f68077b2fb94` passes PYRTR allocation, VAX NICE, QCOCAL NICE discovery, native NICE/MIRROR, VAX CTERM/FAL, QCOCAL route/FAL access and QCOCAL DCL upload, then fails specifically at `DNIV-AREA31-NATIVE-FAIL reason=qcocal-http-client`. The current smoke discards all `dnlynx` diagnostics, so the evidence does not yet distinguish Session/object rejection, HTTP parse failure or a non-2xx response. Preserve only failure-safe evidence: capture `dnlynx -i` output, emit its first generic stderr line and/or the HTTP status line on failure, never emit response body or credentials, and retain cleanup of the ephemeral DNIVHT.COM object. No candidate protocol behavior changes. Cross-runner VDE2 run `36096589135` remains correctly fail-closed because the five `VDE_SSH_*` rendezvous secrets are absent. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance to expose the concrete QCOCAL HTTP cause.

Full acceptance on exact candidate `5f463888d6201dbef68f15d799859ec4aafba9f5` exposed two independent non-candidate failures. Independent Ethernet Interoperability run `36122701896` failed only three arm64 jobs while all corresponding x86_64 jobs and eleven other matrix jobs were green; each failed before any interoperability execution because Ubuntu's arm64 mirror returned transient 404s for libpcap packages immediately after apt metadata refresh. Make the host dependency step retry mirror acquisition once after clearing stale lists, with apt Acquire::Retries enabled. Area-31 run `36122710927` reaches QCOCAL on both architectures and still fails only at the HTTP client; Session connect itself succeeds, but the prior failure-safe logging still emits only the aggregate `dnlynx: HTTP exchange failed`. Add stage-only HTTP diagnostics (request send, response recv, header framing/status, output/flush) without logging response bytes, endpoints or credentials. No DECnet candidate protocol behavior changes. Cross-runner VDE2 remains correctly fail-closed on the absent `VDE_SSH_*` rendezvous secrets. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and fix only the concrete QCOCAL HTTP stage exposed there.

Full Area-31 x86_64 job `108056565374` in run `36130547028` on exact candidate `f9c57291b5e99ae9b7d5638f6698abadb973e456` exposes the native NICE intermittency precisely: after local adjacency and the exact VAX L1 route are present, the first executor-summary Session connect can still time out with `errno=110`. This is external convergence/readiness, not a NICE framing failure; previous exact-head runs have reached and passed native NICE/MIRROR over the same path. Make the Area-31 smoke retry the complete native NICE/MIRROR proof for up to 60 seconds after route appearance, retaining only the final bounded diagnostic if convergence never occurs. Candidate routing, NSP and Session behavior are unchanged. The same full run also exercises the arm64 apt mirror retry hardening independently. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance.

Full Area-31 run `36131929932` on exact candidate `b1d7f0ab35ba003469680231ce72faf930691df5` gets through PYRTR allocation, VAX NICE and QCOCAL discovery, then both architectures fail only at the ephemeral QCOCAL HTTP object. The failure-safe client diagnostic is now concrete: `dnlynx: HTTP stage=no-header`, meaning the named Session object accepts and closes without delivering a parseable HTTP header. The generated DCL object is the only Phase-8 HTTP component using multi-line DCL expression continuation; `SET NOON` then masks any command-procedure parse error and yields exactly this empty-close symptom. Replace the continued response expression with short independent `WRITE SYS$NET` records, each carrying explicit CR/LF bytes; `dnlynx` already concatenates DECnet records while framing HTTP. Add a selftest guard forbidding the fragile continuation form. This changes only the ephemeral reference-side DCL test object, not candidate protocol behavior. Cross-runner VDE2 remains separately fail-closed because the five `VDE_SSH_*` rendezvous secrets are absent. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and continue from the next concrete Area-31 result.

Full Area-31 x86_64 job `108072650188` in run `36135552061` on exact candidate `67fa2aebf37bea26cfc6827a949701c7ab571f0b` still reaches the QCOCAL HTTP object but returns `dnlynx: HTTP stage=no-header` after the prior DCL response split. VSI's DCL WRITE definition is explicit: `WRITE logical-name expression[,...]`, and comma-separated expressions are concatenated into one output record. The generated object incorrectly used shell-like `+` operators directly in WRITE commands; with `SET NOON`, those command errors are suppressed and the object closes with no response, exactly matching the wire symptom. Emit each HTTP record using canonical comma-separated DCL WRITE operands and add a generator selftest guard rejecting `+` in WRITE lines. This changes only the ephemeral QCOCAL reference object; candidate DECnet and HTTP client behavior are unchanged. Fast acceptance for `67fa2aebf37bea26cfc6827a949701c7ab571f0b` was fully green. Cross-runner VDE2 remains fail-closed on missing `VDE_SSH_*` secrets. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance.

Full Area-31 x86_64 job `108078584179` in run `36137373745` on exact candidate `e906b900d88fe0e2c43b1d1bbf271ccb8cc3dc88` still reaches PYRTR allocation, VAX NICE, QCOCAL discovery and the native QCOCAL Session object, but the object closes with `dnlynx: HTTP stage=no-header`. The remaining generated DCL defect is concrete: OpenVMS DCL has no `F$CHAR` lexical function. The procedure used `F$CHAR(13)` and `F$CHAR(10)` under `SET NOON`, so those assignment errors were suppressed before the HTTP WRITE commands. Construct CR and LF using DCL's documented binary-overlay assignment (`cr[0,8] = 13`, `lf[0,8] = 10`) and add a generator selftest guard forbidding `F$CHAR`. This changes only the ephemeral QCOCAL reference-side DCL object; candidate DECnet/HTTP behavior is unchanged. Cross-runner VDE2 remains fail-closed because the required `VDE_SSH_*` rendezvous secrets are absent. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and continue from the next concrete Area-31 result.

Exact candidate `5cf52c584e077d841a8a240fc7cf326b1d0b3f6b` is the first dual-architecture real Area-31 green state. Fast acceptance is fully green: Repository Policy `36138401401`, Build Bootstrap `36138441856`, Project State Gate `36138444274`, Python QEMU VM Lab `36138447794` (x86_64/aarch64), and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36138450607`. Full acceptance is green through Repository Policy `36139253300`, Build Bootstrap `36139306038`, Project State Gate `36139308529`, External Reference Baselines `36139310791`, all four x86_64/aarch64 VM runs `36139313854`/`36139316663`/`36139319254`/`36139321679`, all 14 independent interoperability jobs in `36139324159`, local VDE2 `36139326492`, local MULTINET `36139328804`, and dual-architecture Area-31 `36139333328`. The DCL binary-overlay correction therefore closes the Area-31 external evidence gap, including the routed native Linux, VAX NICE/MIRROR/login/FAL, and discovered-QCOCAL HTTP/task checks. The only non-green full gate is cross-runner VDE2 `36139331212`, which fails correctly before network activity because all five required `VDE_SSH_*` rendezvous secrets are absent. Phase 8 remains open solely on that external dependency; do not enter Phase 9 or fabricate/manualize the rendezvous. Next action is the automatically dispatched two-runner VDE2 proof after the real SSH rendezvous secrets are configured.

Area-31 expansion starts while Phase 8 is otherwise blocked on the external VDE2 rendezvous secrets. PYRTR's real NML `SHOW KNOWN NODES` output is now retained as a structured occupied-node manifest rather than serving allocation only. The native candidate consumes that manifest after the strict VAX/QCOCAL proof and performs a bounded capability survey of every currently reachable Area-31 identity: NICE executor summary first, then MIRROR object 25 when NICE succeeds. Object absence/unavailability is recorded, not promoted to a candidate failure; malformed required VAX behavior remains strict. The required VAX MIRROR proof is broadened from three 128-byte records to a 1/127/128/255/256/511/512-byte matrix and NICE counters are read again after the traffic. This directly exercises heterogeneous real Area-31 peers through the candidate kernel/NSP/Session path without credentials or destructive operations. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance to prove the expanded dual-architecture Area-31 corpus. Cross-runner VDE2 remains fail-closed until the five real `VDE_SSH_*` secrets exist; Phase 9 remains blocked.

Cross-runner VDE2 is no longer blocked on absent secrets. Full run `36142699326` on exact candidate `e2d330016224c6f1083e5a889037412ddc8ebad0` passed SSH prerequisite validation on both GitHub runners and entered the real rendezvous. The server's reverse-SSH process stayed alive but no completion marker arrived; it failed after its 900-second marker wait. The client remained inside `Join remote VDE switch and prove recovery` until GitHub cancelled the 25-minute job, leaving two SSH processes. This is a harness observability/bounding defect: readiness SSH calls have no command-level timeout, inner SSH has no ConnectTimeout, and merely observing a live background reverse-SSH PID does not prove the reverse endpoint is usable. Bound outer and inner SSH connects, wrap remote readiness/completion commands in `timeout`, shorten the server marker wait to 600 seconds, and print only the final bounded readiness error. No endpoint/credential value is logged and no VDE/DECnet protocol behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance; fix only the concrete bounded rendezvous error exposed next. Phase 9 remains blocked until cross-runner VDE2 is green.

Cross-runner VDE2 full run `36147917346` on exact candidate `90679867da8fd3426b812d90db845eeda013a19f` converts the previous hang into a bounded concrete failure: the client reports `Connection timed out during banner exchange` and then `server rendezvous did not become ready`. Both runners passed secret/key preflight and the server kept its reverse-SSH session alive, so the remaining uncertainty is between the server's ephemeral inner sshd and the bastion reverse-forward endpoint. Add a server-local authenticated inner-SSH selfcheck before publishing the reverse tunnel, then make the client require a raw `SSH-2.0-` banner through the bastion before attempting inner authentication. Probe-only proxy commands now have kill-after bounds; the long-lived VDE bridge keeps its unbounded proxy after the banner is proven. No DECnet, VDE2 or candidate protocol behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and fix only the newly isolated rendezvous stage. Phase 9 remains blocked until the real cross-runner VDE2 gate is green.


Cross-runner VDE2 full run `36151198844` on exact candidate `5d1f1a6e40961d86b540eabde9bcaf6559e863bd` isolates the failure to the server runner itself: the new authenticated loopback selfcheck of the ephemeral inner sshd fails before the reverse tunnel is published, while secret/key preflight succeeds. GitHub-hosted Ubuntu uses a locked password entry for the `runner` account; OpenSSH with `UsePAM no` rejects that account before public-key authentication. Run the private inner sshd with `UsePAM yes` while retaining public-key-only authentication, the forced-command `authorized_keys` restriction, disabled TTY/agent/X11/TCP forwarding, and the loopback-only listener. Preserve only bounded local selfcheck diagnostics on failure. This changes only the disposable two-runner test harness, not VDE2 or DECnet protocol behavior. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and continue only from the next concrete cross-runner result. Phase 9 remains blocked until the real two-runner gate is green.

Fast Build Bootstrap run `36152059542` on exact candidate `46c1340433ee085294c5e0995026de4283ff726e` failed identically on x86_64 and arm64 before any protocol test because `tests/lab/prove-vde2-cross-runner.sh` lost its executable mode in the preceding tree update; the test invocation returned `Permission denied`. Restore only the repository mode to `100755`; script contents and protocol behavior are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance.

Full cross-runner run `36153674513` on exact candidate `775b23c2e36803c0af2fd6c03a174dbfb5c85382` proves the reverse path is not yet relevant: the server fails its authenticated loopback inner-SSH selfcheck after the executable-mode repair, even with PAM enabled. The bounded client receives no useful stderr, so the remaining defect is inside the disposable inner sshd/auth/forced-command path. Enable verbose logging only for that loopback sshd and print only the final bounded client/server diagnostic tails on selfcheck failure. The log contains no rendezvous endpoint or credential values. No candidate, VDE2, routing or Session behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and fix only the concrete local-SSH cause exposed there.

Full acceptance on exact candidate `db6c0c978a3ccd231539446840efbfcdaad3990c` exposed scheduler starvation rather than a transport defect. Cross-runner VDE2 run `36155492527` started its client at 15:38:52 UTC, but the server job did not receive a hosted runner until 15:43:43 UTC, 13 seconds after the client had already exhausted its bounded reverse-banner wait and exited. The server then advanced beyond the previously failing loopback inner-SSH selfcheck, proving the PAM correction. Dispatch the two-runner VDE2 workflow before the VM/interoperability fan-out in full acceptance so both halves enter the Actions queue ahead of the large matrix. No manual dispatch, protocol behavior, credentials, or rendezvous semantics change. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance; inspect the real cross-runner result before any further change.

Acceptance dispatcher observability is now self-contained: every automatically dispatched child workflow is discovered immediately by exact workflow, event, branch and candidate SHA, excluding the pre-dispatch run-ID set, and its numeric run ID is persisted in the dispatcher scratch artifact. Dispatcher run-ID evidence is now also posted back to the owner-created `DNIV acceptance gates` issue after dispatch completes. FAST dispatcher run `36158748964` proved child run-ID discovery works, but the final issue-comment publish failed with `Resource not accessible by integration` because the dispatcher job had `actions: write` and `contents: read` only. Grant `issues: write` to that job so the already-owner-gated `DNIV acceptance gates` event can publish non-secret child run IDs. Child workflows themselves dispatched successfully; candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance.

Full cross-runner run `36160103171` on exact candidate `40c18823de61f168080e7dd4d98963f8e5430fca` removes scheduler starvation from the failure: server and client received runners at 16:20:21/16:20:22 UTC and entered the proof together. The server passed the authenticated loopback inner-SSH selfcheck and kept its reverse SSH process alive; the client nevertheless received no `SSH-2.0-` banner through the bastion reverse endpoint and failed at 16:24:59 UTC. Add a bounded server-side self-probe through the actual bastion reverse listener before publishing readiness, keep verbose reverse-SSH evidence private, and emit only a sanitized classification distinguishing bastion direct-forward denial, listener refusal, a delivered `forwarded-tcpip` channel with no inner banner, or no delivered forwarding channel. No DECnet/VDE2 protocol behavior or credentials change. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and fix only the classified rendezvous defect.

Cross-runner run `36162729374` on exact candidate `4c1646cb2e1f4fcab1c07464d9b7558beec911bb` proves the server can traverse the published reverse listener and read the inner `SSH-2.0-` banner, while the client still exhausts every eight-second banner probe with no stderr. The generated bastion SSH config used a ten-second `ConnectTimeout`, so the outer `timeout -k 2 8` killed client probes before OpenSSH could report an outer-connect failure. Reduce only the outer bastion connect bound to five seconds, below every eight-second probe wrapper, so the next run either succeeds or emits the concrete SSH failure instead of a silent timeout. Candidate, VDE2 and credential behavior are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance.

Full cross-runner run `36165678044` on exact candidate `29e4ce7fc7a88652c7bf17da79ee5a5f084280c3` makes the remaining failure concrete: the server runner publishes and self-probes the reverse endpoint successfully, but the separate x86_64 client runner cannot establish even the outer SSH connection to the bastion (`Connection timed out`) across repeated bounded attempts. This is runner-egress reachability, not the reverse tunnel or inner sshd. Move only the client half to GitHub's native `ubuntu-24.04-arm` pool, retaining the x86_64 server half, so the gate both diversifies hosted-runner egress and strengthens the proof into a real mixed-architecture two-host VDE2 join. No credentials, rendezvous endpoint, candidate protocol behavior or manual dispatch changes. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and inspect the real mixed-architecture cross-runner result.

Mixed-architecture full cross-runner run `36167545491` on exact candidate `6b518e1f3eb2ff3d2874d2770ab2d9f9efb21ee2` reached the real reverse endpoint from the arm64 client, proving the runner-egress problem is avoided. The next concrete failure is local to the arm64 client bridge launch: `setsid dpipe ...` exits immediately with `Err: cannot create pgrp`. `dpipe` creates its own process group; wrapping it in `setsid` makes that group creation fail on this runner. Remove only the redundant outer `setsid`; existing cleanup still targets the dpipe-created process group. No rendezvous, VDE2, DECnet or credential behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance.

Full cross-runner run `36169051957` on exact candidate `8d4e99b6fdfdac28933c23965b066b89b5b0097a` advances past the arm64 process-group failure: the mixed-architecture client reaches/authenticates through the rendezvous and starts the bridge, but the first raw Ethernet echo times out. The forced-command whitelist is updated to the exact same URI command. No DECnet protocol behavior, credentials, endpoint, or manual dispatch changes. Full cross-runner run `36170889385` on exact candidate `a4926cb4a2b6c92253315ccbbd0bebba317d8491` advances past outer reachability and raw reverse-banner checks, then fails the authenticated ready probe with `Connection timed out during banner exchange`. Reuse the normal bounded SSH proxy there and keep the existing outer `timeout -k 2 8 ssh ...` around the complete ready/completion probe. No protocol, credential, endpoint, or manual-dispatch behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance.

Full cross-runner run `36180046635` on exact candidate `39887ef5560354db9d1f06f5a5e536643aed488b` repeated the arm64 outer bastion timeout, but the automatic fallback did not start because Actions masks both the host and port as `***`; the classifier incorrectly required a numeric port and therefore returned the normal hard-failure status. Repair the timeout-classifier edit itself and lock it down with a permanent unit regression. The previous text replacement malformed the shell line before acceptance could validate it. The repaired script factors classification into `is_outer_connect_timeout`; its selftest accepts GitHub's masked outer-connect diagnostic and rejects an inner banner-exchange timeout, and the root unit gate runs that test on every build. Full cross-runner run `36188286348` on exact candidate `f960b2226d00628fc43ca0c6cdf3d6661456441b` reached the reverse endpoint from the arm64 client but then exhausted the authenticated ready probe with `Connection timed out during banner exchange`; the automatic alternate-runner fallback correctly stayed disabled because this was no longer the raw outer-connect signature. The persistent outer forward is killed during normal cleanup; an explicit outer-connect timeout while creating it remains eligible for the automatic alternate-runner fallback, while inner SSH/VDE/frame/adjacency failures remain hard failures. Full cross-runner run `36189654229` on exact candidate `aeb9ef081a956a8a1670637c49d86e706d80cea5` reaches the server through the persistent client-side bastion forward and passes the authenticated ready probe, so repeated bastion connections are no longer the blocker. The native vdeplug4 bridge then exits immediately with `vde_plug: invalid option -- 'F'`: GNU option permutation makes the inner SSH `-F /dev/null` argument look like a `vde_plug` option. Terminate vde_plug option parsing explicitly with `--` before the local VDE URL, preserving the documented `vde_plug URL = command [args]` stream form and all SSH arguments unchanged. No endpoint, credential or DECnet/VDE2 protocol behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and inspect the first frame result. Full cross-runner run `36192721038` on exact candidate `a6c461cf474d35cb348a50d4ffae3dfe7d29dfdc` is the first run to carry real Ethernet and DECnet traffic through the native vdeplug4/SSH bridge: the initial marked frame succeeds and pinned PyDECnet forms adjacency to Route20. The remaining failure is the bridge-reconnect assertion: after the first adjacency-up event, the harness disconnects the bridge for only eight seconds and then waits for a second adjacency-up event, but the Ethernet circuit itself remains locally up and the remote adjacency has not yet aged out, so no down/up transition occurs. Make the fault proof wait explicitly for PyDECnet event 4.16 (`Adjacency down`) with a bounded 120-second expiry window before restarting the bridge, then require the next 4.15 adjacency-up event as before. This strengthens the disconnect/reconnect proof rather than weakening it; frame, switch-restart and independent-peer requirements remain unchanged. No candidate protocol behavior, credentials or endpoint values change. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and continue from the next concrete cross-runner result. Phase 9 remains blocked until cross-runner VDE2 is green.

Full cross-runner run `36196720805` on exact candidate `a1cb8c532d1efd2eee44b72d758002c81503f1c0` still never observed PyDECnet adjacency-down after the bridge was stopped. The client log retained the initial successful adjacency for the entire new 120-second expiry window, proving the stop operation killed only the native `vde_plug` parent while its spawned SSH stream remained alive and continued carrying Route20 hellos. Start the native bridge in its own session/process group with `setsid` and stop the entire bridge group, including the spawned SSH command, before waiting for adjacency expiry. This is the native-vdeplug equivalent of the earlier dpipe process-group cleanup and is deliberately applied only after dpipe has been removed; the prior dpipe/setsid incompatibility is therefore not reintroduced. Frame delivery, peer behavior, credentials and endpoint values are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and require bridge disconnect, adjacency expiry, reconnect, client-switch restart and all three marked frame exchanges to pass. Phase 9 remains blocked until cross-runner VDE2 is green.

Full cross-runner run `36198514542` on exact candidate `3036cddba1d5a2b26e276442b5261f0f9513ed13` again carried the first marked Ethernet frame and formed the independent PyDECnet/Route20 adjacency, but the bridge fault still did not age the adjacency down within the bounded 120-second window. The process-group stop is therefore not sufficient for vdeplug4's spawned command path. Record the complete descendant PID tree before teardown, terminate both the bridge group and every recorded descendant (including any SSH stream in its own process group), and require a marked raw-frame negative immediately after stop before waiting for adjacency expiry. A root unit selftest creates a child in a separate session and proves the tree-stop helper kills it, preventing this cleanup regression from escaping build acceptance. No candidate protocol, credentials or endpoint values change. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance; the cross-runner gate must show frame-path loss, adjacency expiry, bridge reconnect, switch restart and all three marked frame exchanges before Phase 8 can close.

Full cross-runner run `36199792553` on exact candidate `a80823bdd3f428601a96b66b074365f9fb795a21` again carried the first marked Ethernet frame and formed the pinned PyDECnet/Route20 adjacency. This guarantees that no one-way or detached inner SSH stream can continue carrying Route20 hellos after the bridge process tree is stopped. Full cross-runner run `36200905966` on exact candidate `6ef9a9114a4ff7adcf56acd738a92d163ebc6ce6` failed before the bridge stage at the new reusable client-side bastion forward. The helper treated a still-running outer SSH process as ready, but every authenticated inner probe then received `ssh: connect to host 127.0.0.1 ...: Connection refused`, so the local listener/full reverse path had never become usable. Make forward readiness application-backed: after each `ssh -NT -L` launch, require a bounded raw `SSH-2.0-` banner through the localhost forward before accepting it; otherwise terminate that attempt and retry. Only a classified outer bastion timeout remains eligible for alternate-runner fallback. No credentials, endpoint values or DECnet/VDE2 candidate behavior change. Full cross-runner run `36202093416` on exact candidate `a2da9dd54616345870ef01ab1f2af5a0a19b3bf4` failed at the new application-backed persistent client forward, but the failure was self-inflicted by the readiness loop: each `ssh -NT -L` process was killed after a fixed one-second sleep if the local banner was not already available, shorter than the configured five-second outer SSH connect bound. The repeated localhost `ConnectionRefusedError` diagnostics therefore did not prove the forward was unusable; they proved it had not been allowed to finish connecting. Keep each forward attempt alive for a bounded eight-second readiness window, probe every 0.5 seconds, suppress transient localhost probe tracebacks, and only then terminate/retry the attempt. The raw outer-connect classifier and all inner SSH/VDE/frame/adjacency requirements remain unchanged. Full cross-runner run `36202993942` on exact candidate `b4b8324c6c5c73d1de5be5d5d006bdbf27cf7bf1` reached the native bridge, carried the initial marked Ethernet frame and formed the pinned PyDECnet/Route20 adjacency. The transport cut then made the marked frame path fail, but the harness waited for a log string that it had filtered out: pinned PyDECnet defines routing event 4.15 as Adjacency up, 4.16 as Adjacency rejected, and 4.18 as Adjacency down. The test configuration subscribed to `4.15,4.16`, so a genuine listener-timeout down event could never satisfy `wait_downs`. Subscribe to `4.15,4.18` and keep the existing strict transport-cut, expiry, reconnect, switch-restart and marked-frame requirements unchanged. This fixes only independent-reference harness observability; no candidate protocol, credentials or endpoint values change. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and require the complete real cross-runner fault/recovery sequence to pass before Phase 8 closes.

No inner SSH, VDE frame, adjacency or recovery failure becomes fallback-eligible, and no endpoint or credential value is emitted.
