Full cross-runner run `36209384967` on exact candidate `46f205fb22a05a53a30d83e090bc209ddca1a4bb` reports the same secret-safe concrete failure on the x86_64 fallback as on the arm64 primary: persistent transport bootstrap fails at `stage=master`, before any local-forward request, inner SSH, VDE frame or adjacency operation. The ControlMaster path is therefore the defect. Restore the earlier direct `ssh -NT -L` persistent forward that already carried a marked Ethernet frame and formed PyDECnet/Route20 adjacency in run `36202993942`, retaining the eight-second application-backed banner readiness, transport-cut teardown, alternate-runner classification and strict downstream frame/adjacency/recovery checks. No credentials, endpoint values or DECnet/VDE2 candidate behavior change. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and continue from the first concrete result on the previously proven forward mechanism. Phase 9 remains blocked until cross-runner VDE2 is green.

Full cross-runner run `36211600829` on exact candidate `e3bf465983d57353d1dcf9672c536b73f3377451` repeated the persistent-forward defect on both client architectures: the arm64 primary fell back automatically and the x86_64 fallback failed at the same secret-safe `stage=forward-banner`, while the one-shot `ssh -W` reverse-endpoint banner discriminator had already succeeded. Remove the unreliable extra `ssh -L` layer and return inner SSH plus the native VDE bridge to the direct `ProxyCommand=ssh ... -W` path that previously carried a marked Ethernet frame and formed Route20/PyDECnet adjacency. The hardened bridge process-tree teardown remains the transport-cut boundary; a marked raw-frame negative must prove the path is dead before the now-correct 4.18 adjacency-down wait. Only the initial raw outer-connect discriminator remains fallback-eligible; every inner SSH, frame, adjacency and recovery failure is hard. No credentials, endpoint values or candidate DECnet/VDE2 behavior change. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and require the complete real cross-runner fault/recovery sequence before Phase 8 closes.

Full cross-runner run `36213069416` on exact candidate `a9582c577cdf9282b4b02ea1aa06ae50d7c53a7a` passed the raw reverse-endpoint banner and authenticated ready probe, then the native bridge process stayed alive but the first marked Ethernet request timed out. The same native VDE bridge shape previously carried a marked frame when its right-hand command was a simple SSH invocation through localhost; the new direct ProxyCommand adds a multi-token OpenSSH option directly to vde_plug4's command side. Keep the proven direct bastion ProxyCommand for authenticated probes, but place the complete inner SSH bridge command in a private executable wrapper and give vde_plug only that single wrapper pathname as its command operand. This preserves the exact forced remote VDE command and hardened process-tree teardown while removing command-argument interpretation from the vde_plug boundary. No credentials, endpoint values or DECnet/VDE2 candidate behavior change. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and continue only from the next concrete cross-runner result. Phase 9 remains blocked until the complete frame/adjacency/fault/recovery sequence is green.

Full cross-runner run `36213729581` on exact candidate `7696e769b2531e691540e2ff9e9307d1966b83d0` again passed the raw reverse-endpoint banner and authenticated ready probe, launched the direct native VDE-over-SSH bridge, and then timed out only on the first marked Ethernet request. The direct bridge now has an extra outer+inner SSH startup path compared with the earlier localhost-forward run that carried the same frame, while the harness treated a live bridge PID after one second as data-plane readiness. Make initial bridge readiness application-backed: retry the marked frame exchange up to three bounded attempts while requiring the bridge process to remain alive, and accept the bridge only after an actual response crosses both switches. The frame proof remains mandatory; this only removes a one-shot startup race before any adjacency exists to provide natural retry traffic. No credentials, endpoint values or DECnet/VDE2 candidate behavior change. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and continue from the next concrete cross-runner result. Phase 9 remains blocked until frame delivery, adjacency expiry/recovery, reconnect and switch restart are all green.

Full cross-runner run `36214476374` on exact candidate `9dcee4fafc23c12b78e201439b2bf66d96c82fb8` passed the one-shot raw reverse-endpoint banner discriminator, but a later fresh ProxyCommand connection for the authenticated ready probe stalled and surfaced only as inner `Connection timed out during banner exchange`. This is the ambiguity already identified in the direct path: each nested SSH opens a new bastion connection, and the inner client alone cannot distinguish outer egress failure from an inner banner defect. Move the direct ProxyCommand into a private wrapper that records only its own stderr; before each authenticated ready attempt, clear that private outer diagnostic and classify fallback only when the wrapper itself reports the exact outer bastion connect timeout. Genuine inner banner/authentication failures remain hard failures, as do every VDE frame, adjacency and recovery stage. No credentials, endpoint values or candidate DECnet/VDE2 behavior change. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and inspect the complete alternate-runner path if the hosted arm64 outer connection repeats. Phase 9 remains blocked until cross-runner VDE2 is green.

Full cross-runner run `36215246642` on exact candidate `3a7aab8881cc122de59b64fbaa70abceb2380401` passed the one-shot reverse-endpoint banner and authenticated ready probe, then the native bridge opened a fresh ProxyCommand path whose outer bastion connection timed out; the inner SSH consequently reported `Connection timed out during banner exchange`, and the bridge exited before the first marked frame. The private ProxyCommand wrapper already records the decisive outer diagnostic. During only the initial bridge bootstrap, classify that exact wrapper-level outer timeout as fallback-eligible so the automatic x86_64 client reruns the complete proof. Disable that classification permanently after the first marked frame succeeds; all later bridge reconnect, adjacency and recovery failures remain hard failures. No credentials, endpoint values or candidate DECnet/VDE2 behavior change. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and inspect the complete fallback path if the arm64 bridge bootstrap repeats. Phase 9 remains blocked until cross-runner VDE2 is green.

Full cross-runner run `36216016155` on exact candidate `e8de6b100c3f6ab9f666acd525b2df353279fe24` again proved the first raw `ssh -W` reverse-endpoint banner path, then failed every authenticated ready probe with `Connection timed out during banner exchange`; the private ProxyCommand log did not contain an outer-connect timeout, so fallback correctly stayed disabled. The repeated pattern is that the first bastion connection is usable and the next fresh connection is not. Make the first client bastion connection the persistent transport instead of throwing it away: establish `ssh -NT -L` as the initial rendezvous operation, require the local listener to return the inner `SSH-2.0-` banner, and reuse that one outer connection for authenticated commands and the native VDE bridge. This is the transport form that already carried a marked frame and formed Route20/PyDECnet adjacency in run `36202993942`. The hard transport-cut now stops both bridge and persistent forward, requires marked-frame failure and adjacency-down, then establishes a fresh forward for recovery; switch restart does the same. Only a classified persistent outer-connect timeout is fallback-eligible. No credentials, endpoint values or candidate DECnet/VDE2 behavior change. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and require the complete frame/adjacency/reconnect/switch-restart sequence before Phase 8 closes.


Full acceptance on exact candidate `f58612166175cdf520e01bbc1afeb88f92bbb006` exposed two independent Phase 8 harness defects. Cross-runner VDE2 run `36218217564` carried the initial marked Ethernet frame, formed the pinned PyDECnet/Route20 adjacency, then proved the hard transport cut by losing the marked-frame path and emitting PyDECnet event 4.18 (adjacency down); after the persistent forward and native bridge were recreated, the harness waited for adjacency recovery without first proving that the rebuilt bridge had restored the Ethernet data plane. Make the required second marked frame the application-backed recovery readiness check immediately after bridge recreation, then still require the second adjacency-up event. This preserves every transport-cut and adjacency requirement while separating bridge recovery failure from DECnet adjacency recovery.

The same exact-head full acceptance repeated Area-31 `DNIV-AREA31-NATIVE-FAIL reason=qcocal-object-existing` in run `36218288072`, confirming stale remote ephemeral QCOCAL command procedures from an interrupted earlier proof. QCOCAL is serialized across architectures already, so the lifecycle defect is stale test-owned state rather than candidate protocol behavior. Before installing the temporary DNIVHT/DNIVTK procedures, remove only those two project-owned names with bounded DAP ERASE operations, re-list after every deletion, and fail hard if either name survives cleanup. Normal post-test deletion remains mandatory. No candidate DECnet protocol behavior, external proof requirement, credentials or endpoint values change. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance; Phase 9 remains blocked until both Area-31 and the complete cross-runner frame/adjacency/fault/recovery sequence are green on one exact unchanged head.


Full acceptance on exact candidate `bc95d47b44a8ce9ba43411a4e674add2ea0b5e31` requalified Area-31 successfully on both architectures in run `36219353713`; the bounded DNIVHT/DNIVTK stale-state cleanup therefore resolves the remote QCOCAL lifecycle defect without changing candidate protocol behavior. Cross-runner VDE2 run `36219283281` still failed at the initial persistent client forward: the server proved the reverse-endpoint SSH banner, but the arm64 client exhausted its direct `ssh -NT -L` attempts without receiving the inner banner and the x86_64 fallback was not selected. For this direct bastion process, both the TCP-connect timeout form and OpenSSH's `Connection timed out during banner exchange` form are outer-connection failures; classify exactly those two private-log forms as fallback-eligible, while forwarded-target, authentication, host-key, inner-SSH, frame, adjacency and recovery failures remain hard failures. Add secret-safe stage diagnostics for non-timeout forward failures. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance; Phase 9 remains blocked until the complete cross-runner frame/adjacency/fault/recovery sequence is green.


Fast Build Bootstrap run `36221757765` on exact candidate `46967371212e81c48254f7ec5abf2528d2e2ddbb` rejected the VDE harness before execution because the scripted classifier edit malformed the shell source and also lost the executable file mode. Rebuild `prove-vde2-cross-runner.sh` from the last syntax-valid parent `bc95d47b44a8ce9ba43411a4e674add2ea0b5e31`, retain only the intended direct-bastion timeout classification and secret-safe diagnostics, and restore mode 0755. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance; Phase 9 remains blocked on cross-runner VDE2.


Full cross-runner run `36222378434` on exact candidate `ee394d9b1b577624843662343bee7bbd6ba07b09` failed during initial persistent-client-forward readiness before inner authentication, frames or adjacency. The helper still violated the intended first-connection reuse model by killing an otherwise live `ssh -NT -L` transport after only eight seconds and opening up to twelve fresh bastion connections; the final private log contained none of the classified outer-timeout, authentication, host-key or forwarded-target errors, so fallback correctly remained disabled. Keep exactly one first client bastion connection alive for the whole bounded banner-readiness window, record private verbose OpenSSH state only for secret-safe classification, and distinguish outer authentication, local-listener, reverse-endpoint and live-without-banner failures without printing endpoint values. A classified direct outer timeout remains the only fallback-eligible failure. Candidate DECnet/VDE2 behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance and require the complete three-frame/adjacency/fault/recovery sequence before Phase 8 closes.


Bastion VDE inventory diagnostics are now part of the cross-runner workflow as non-gating probes on the server, primary arm64 client and automatic x86_64 fallback. Each probe independently tests SSH command login, prints only visible `vde_switch` command lines and discovered VDE control-socket paths, and attempts `vde_plug` attachment without exposing SSH secret values. The existing marked-frame, DECnet adjacency, hard-cut and recovery checks remain authoritative.


Full acceptance run `36223665078` on exact candidate `43c35e4c8ac73abbd9f8360e445c741c7a8ba663` reached the new bastion inventory probe from both the server and primary arm64 client. Both roles authenticated to the SSH rendezvous, but the bastion account saw no running `vde_switch` process and no VDE `ctl` socket under `/tmp`; the existing reverse-forward path then failed before any cross-runner frame or adjacency. The probe is broadened to search `/tmp`, `/run`, `/var/run` and the bastion account home, report VDE binary availability, and, when no reusable switch is visible, create and attach to a disposable bastion-local VDE switch before cleaning it up. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance; if the bastion can host the disposable switch, replace the fragile reverse-forward rendezvous with a direct shared bastion VDE fabric.


Fast acceptance on exact candidate `2416d122a07c5dd3891efa649df51da27f046965` completed green: Repository Policy `36223886697`, Build Bootstrap `36224024514`, Project State Gate `36224033410`, E1 VM Lab `36224041875`, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36224052012`. The bastion probe now also reports `socat`, `nc`, `netcat`, and `ncat` availability and resolved executable paths after successful SSH login. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance to capture the bastion tool inventory and VDE capability result.


Full acceptance on exact candidate `7511061bc0608e544b26bcc38eb25e6c041ec315` was green across every local/reference/Area-31 gate; only Cross-runner VDE2 run `36234122905` failed. The bastion inventory confirmed `socat` at `/usr/bin/socat`, `nc` and `netcat`, plus `vde_switch` and `vde_plug`; no persistent VDE control socket was visible, and the disposable bastion VDE attach probe failed. A new non-gating two-runner socat rendezvous probe now uses two independent SSH sessions joined by one bastion-local UNIX socket and exchanges marked bytes in both directions, avoiding reverse TCP forwarding entirely. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance; if the socat rendezvous is green, replace the fragile reverse-forward/inner-SSH transport with the proven socat byte-stream rendezvous and carry VDE framing over it.


Cross-runner run `36239303917` on exact candidate `a4a967324fc2bebfef80abab42e1f3196866b346` showed the first socat rendezvous probe was still opening several short-lived bastion SSH sessions for capability checks, cleanup and socket-readiness polling; the server-side probe exited with SSH status 255 before the marked-byte exchange. The probe now uses one persistent outer SSH connection per runner for the actual socat UNIX-socket rendezvous, with no remote readiness polling and only one bounded client retry after a fixed server-start interval. Diagnostic stderr is emitted only on failure and contains no secret values. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance; require marked-byte exchange through socat before converting the VDE transport to this rendezvous.


Pre-acceptance review of the single-connection socat probe found that the embedded Python marker strings contained a literal backslash-n instead of a newline, which could block `readline()` despite successful byte transport. The marker writes and reads are corrected before any acceptance claim is made. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance of the socat rendezvous probe.


Cross-runner run `36240111257` on exact candidate `48f70f09f2242494611e3ee700fe3c37f0bc49e4` proved the alternative transport directly: the x86_64 server and arm64 client each reported `socat-rendezvous: ... pass`, exchanging marked bytes bidirectionally through two independent bastion SSH sessions joined by a bastion-local UNIX socket. The legacy reverse-forward path failed immediately afterward, confirming the failure is in that forwarding design rather than basic runner-to-bastion transport. The authoritative cross-runner proof is now converted to carry the native VDE framed stream through the proven socat UNIX-socket rendezvous. The server accepts three sequential relay sessions for initial traffic, hard-cut recovery and client-switch restart; the client retains the marked-frame, Route20/PyDECnet adjacency, transport-loss, adjacency-expiry, reconnect and final-frame requirements. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance; Phase 8 closes only if the complete socat-backed cross-runner sequence is green on the unchanged candidate.


Full cross-runner run `36241058051` on exact candidate `87f86be145753924d0ab674e163c54d28fef9ab9` reconfirmed the standalone socat rendezvous on both hosted-runner architectures, but the first authoritative Ethernet frame never crossed the initial relay. The server consumed all three raw vde_plug command sessions while the arm64 client timed out three marked-frame attempts, and Route20 then exited. The VDE stream boundary is corrected to the canonical `dpipe vde_plug ... = command` form documented by VDE2: each local switch is converted once to the bidirectional byte stream carried by the already-proven SSH/socat rendezvous. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance; require all three marked frames plus adjacency loss/recovery and switch restart before Phase 8 closes.


Full cross-runner run `36241891430` on exact candidate `7757f2162f248fa73cd58b76b02b6e119ed8bcb3` reached the corrected dpipe framing path but both client and server hit `Err: cannot create pgrp` before VDE attachment. The pinned VDE2 dpipe implementation creates its own process group with `setpgrp()`; wrapping dpipe in `setsid` makes that call fail with EPERM. Remove only the redundant outer `setsid`. dpipe itself remains the process-group root, so the existing process-tree teardown still kills the complete VDE/SSH/socat transport at the hard-cut boundary. The project-owned `tuklusan/vde-2` fork is available for future VDE2 fixes and will be preferred if a defect is found below this harness boundary; this failure is not such a defect. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance; require all marked frames, adjacency expiry/recovery and switch restart before Phase 8 closes.


Cross-runner run `36242541922` on exact candidate `e55181d8186bffa9b53fc4f972f201b5e2645f0d` removed the dpipe process-group error, but marked Ethernet frames still did not cross. The remaining ambiguity was implementation/invocation: the runner installed both distro VDE2 and VDEPLUG4 packages while the classic VDE2 cable contract requires `dpipe vde_plug SOCKETDIR = command`, not a `vde://` URL. Per project direction, cross-runner VDE2 now builds the project-owned `tuklusan/vde-2` fork from exact pin `7e7017b6308f3f81d5c922a32097137ceee13074` with classic vde_plug/dpipe enabled and uses those private binaries plus raw switch socket directories for the authoritative cable. This removes distro-command ambiguity and makes VDE2 itself patchable under a pinned fork if a real implementation defect appears. System libvdeplug4 remains only for Route20/PyDECnet and the frame helper. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance; require all marked frames, adjacency expiry/recovery and switch restart before Phase 8 closes.


Full acceptance run `36248497293` on exact candidate `737422efc284d3862c9e452819d9b171c48b9528` hit a hosted-arm64-to-bastion outer SSH timeout after the standalone socat probe also timed out. The cross-runner client had already returned from `start_bridge` while SSH was still connecting, so the later timeout was seen by frame readiness as a generic bridge exit and returned status 1 instead of the workflow's status-75 automatic x86_64 fallback signal. Frame readiness now reclassifies the completed bridge log with the same strict outer-timeout predicate and preserves status 75; forwarded-target timeouts remain excluded. The server also rejects its own outer SSH connect timeout explicitly instead of treating a long rc=255 session as a completed relay. No protocol behavior changed. The pinned `tuklusan/vde-2` build remains authoritative for the cable. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance; require three marked frames, adjacency loss/recovery and switch restart before Phase 8 closes.


Full cross-runner run `36254520875` on exact candidate `a7a4f72bee594ff1d467d456b3fd573aa06d6389` after clearing fail2ban proved bastion SSH login from both hosted architectures: the arm64 inventory probe reported `SSH login pass`, the amd64 inventory probe completed successfully, and the paired socat rendezvous probe passed. The authoritative arm64 cable then failed with `UNIX-CONNECT ... No such file or directory`: connectivity was healthy, but the client reached the bastion before the server had published its authoritative relay socket. The client bridge now keeps its single SSH session open while waiting up to 75 seconds for that exact relay socket and emits an internal readiness marker before frame testing. Redundant pre-proof bastion inventory/socat probes were removed from the authoritative workflow to reduce SSH login churn. Candidate DECnet and VDE2 protocol behavior are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full cross-runner proof.


Fast acceptance build run `36254895411` exposed a repository-mode regression in `b21b0a7be536afdb71ba261144084e8765ab1965`: `tests/lab/prove-vde2-cross-runner.sh` had been written with mode 100644, so both x86_64 and arm64 userspace tests failed with `Permission denied` after `bash -n` passed. The script mode is restored to 100755 with content unchanged. Next: repeat exact-SHA fast acceptance before full cross-runner validation.


Full cross-runner run `36255664876` on exact candidate `e0d39671d268877cb99d8f03eb8341e9e77976a1` proved the authoritative cable end-to-end: the arm64 client passed `transport=socat frames=3 bridge_reconnect=1 switch_restart=1 adjacency_recoveries=2`, while the x86_64 server completed all three relay sessions with rc=0. The remaining server failure was a test-harness privilege error: Route20 is launched through `sudo`, so its daemon is root-owned, but the final liveness probe used unprivileged `kill -0`; an existing root-owned process therefore reports EPERM and was misclassified as exited. The liveness probe now uses `sudo kill -0`, matching the existing privileged cleanup path. Candidate DECnet, Route20, and VDE2 protocol behavior are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then repeat full acceptance to close Phase 8 if all gates remain green.


Full cross-runner run `36257502631` on exact candidate `e9f792d3bbb44d621fd53495928955a1d9749739` hit an orchestration delay rather than a transport failure: the arm64 client reached the bastion and held its SSH session, but the authoritative server relay socket was still absent after the previous 75-second publication window, producing `DNIV_RELAY_TIMEOUT`. Because hosted server/client jobs can start and provision at different times, relay publication tolerance is now 300 seconds (with a 315-second local readiness envelope) while preserving one SSH session and the existing bounded job timeout. No DECnet, Route20, or VDE2 protocol behavior changed. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance.


Phase 8 is closed on exact unchanged candidate `9b73e61bbd0f95b82410276f7b5dc3db94e219ba`. Repository Policy full-acceptance parent `36258507655` dispatched and completed a fully green Phase 8 matrix: Build Bootstrap `36258543209`, Project State Gate `36258553606`, Cross-runner VDE2 `36258564105`, External Reference Baselines `36258573270`, x86_64/aarch64 E1-E4 VM labs `36258584817`/`36258594905`/`36258607144`/`36258617814`, Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36258627216`, local VDE2 `36258635481`, MULTINET `36258643588`, and Area-31 `36258654316`. The authoritative cross-runner cable passed on both server/client jobs with three marked frames, hard transport cut, adjacency expiry and two recoveries, bridge reconnect and client-switch restart using exact `tuklusan/vde-2` pin `7e7017b6308f3f81d5c922a32097137ceee13074`. No one-off protocol patches or secret logging were required. Phase 9 is now active: scale/portability/real-peer/release-image qualification against `docs/PRE_PRODUCTION_TEST.md`.


Phase 9 scale work starts from Phase 8 closure commit `fa36d2ea46b63607c617320c2b6341016a67b50a`. A new exact-candidate controller, `tests/lab/dniv_scale.py`, defines 4/8/16 independent-VM routed topologies using the existing immutable architecture foundations and disposable candidate overlays. The 4-node mode proves three endpoints across a forced two-LAN router; 8/16 modes build a two-area L1/L2 topology and require host-PCAP forwarding source/visit evidence for every paired endpoint direction. `vm-lab.yml` exposes `scale4`/`scale8`/`scale16`; full acceptance initially dispatches only `scale4` on both architectures so scale expansion remains incremental and evidence-backed. Handover/Test Lab documentation is advanced to Phase 9 and stale Phase 8 'unproven' text is removed. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring the new dual-architecture 4-node scale gate; only after that promote 8 and 16 nodes.


Phase 9 follow-up fixes the initial scale-controller parse defect in `tests/lab/dniv_scale.py` (`RuntimeError` formatting in transit-guest failure handling) before any scale result can be accepted. The infrastructure summary is also advanced to Phase 9 and the stale Phase 8 active-status sentence is retired. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: run exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance with dual-architecture `scale4`; promote `scale8` and `scale16` only after the 4-node gate is green.


Phase 9 scale4 promotion is proven on exact candidate `40bfc630221b36b01058f7d1e6ceaf8b8be23c53`: full acceptance parent `36272872463` completed green, including dual-architecture scale4 run `36272996817` plus Build `36272902266`, Project State `36272914106`, Cross-runner VDE2 `36272926392`, References `36272938725`, E1-E4 `36272949765`/`36272960084`/`36272972717`/`36272986003`, Interop `36273007733`, local VDE2 `36273017722`, MULTINET `36273028407`, and Area-31 `36273038929`. Full acceptance now promotes the existing scale8 controller on both architectures while retaining scale4. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring both scale4 and scale8; promote scale16 only after scale8 is green.


Phase 9 scale8 promotion is proven on exact candidate `b1788e2bd828e5479ba596f6ca9ed80cafe775fa`: full acceptance parent `36280118696` completed green, including dual-architecture scale4 `36280224479` and scale8 `36280230182`, with Build `36280143391`, Project State `36280155231`, Cross-runner VDE2 `36280168753`, References `36280176178`, E1-E4 `36280184743`/`36280194110`/`36280205160`/`36280216932`, Interop `36280238325`, local VDE2 `36280247588`, MULTINET `36280256406`, and Area-31 `36280263958`. Full acceptance now promotes the existing scale16 controller on both architectures while retaining scale4 and scale8. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring scale4/scale8/scale16; after that proceed through the remaining Phase 9 portability, real-peer, release-image, and pre-production requirements.


Phase 9 scale16 promotion on exact candidate `5d30be205436a86fbc373f66708897cf1e83351b` exposed hosted-runner capacity rather than a protocol defect. Full scale16 runs `36282027696` and `36282409687` failed on both architectures: x86_64 runners were terminated with hosted-runner shutdown/SIGTERM while 16 guests booted concurrently, and the surviving arm64 run showed severe systemd-udevd starvation and timed out before endpoint PASS markers. Scale4/scale8 and every non-scale16 full gate remained green. The scale16 controller now keeps the required 16 independent VMs but removes the boot storm: it defaults scale16 guests to 320 MiB, staggers the four routers, starts endpoint pairs incrementally, holds already-proven endpoints alive, and explicitly requires all 16 guest processes to be simultaneously live before teardown. Scale16 endpoint traffic remains host-PCAP validated in both directions, with 30 probes per endpoint versus the existing minimum-five forwarding evidence. The VM job envelope is raised from 40 to 55 minutes for the larger topology. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring dual-architecture scale4/scale8/scale16 before moving to the remaining Phase 9 portability, real-peer, release-image and pre-production work.


Repository Policy run `36286465481` on `18d22dd1ca5690913484ab6902639bbf2e06d23d` correctly caught a harness-regression guard that still required the old literal `probe_count=180`. The ordinary E4 default remains 180 probes; only scale16 supplies the explicit 30-probe override while host PCAP still requires at least five correctly forwarded frames per direction. The E4 convergence regression is updated to require that default expression and to pin the scale16 hold/probe/simultaneous-16 invariants. No protocol behavior changed. Next: repeat exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full scale16 promotion.


Full scale16 run `36287114105` on exact candidate `ddd3e2c3a21270bdb7a336a0e79475a61f46c707` exposed two independent hosted-runner effects. The arm64 guests at 320 MiB kernel-panicked while unpacking initramfs with `Out of memory and no killable processes`; the x86_64 hosted runner itself later received an external shutdown signal (exit 143), not a guest/protocol failure. Scale16 now keeps x86_64 at 320 MiB but raises only arm64 guests to 352 MiB, preserving the 16-simultaneous-VM requirement while giving the arm64 kernel/initramfs enough non-CMA headroom. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then repeat full scale16 promotion; treat a repeated hosted-runner shutdown as infrastructure and a guest panic as harness capacity evidence.


Full scale16 retry on exact candidate `2933296c254bdecf25d838b047d361c49d41afe8` again hit hosted x86_64 runner shutdown (exit 143) after roughly ten minutes of sustaining the 16-QEMU topology; this repeated infrastructure eviction occurred despite guest memory remaining at 320 MiB. To reduce sustained TCG/host pressure without weakening the topology proof, each endpoint pair is now paused via QMP immediately after its bidirectional PASS evidence is captured, while all four routers stay active. After all six endpoint pairs have been proven, every paused endpoint is resumed and the harness requires all 16 independent guest processes to remain simultaneously live before teardown. The arm64-only 352 MiB boot headroom remains. Scale16 traffic, host-PCAP forwarding evidence, router continuity, and exact-candidate semantics are unchanged. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full scale16 promotion.


Full scale16 run `36291030667` on exact candidate `b443eaaa2eac50ec3861bbdd10a9bc696b70fb84` again lost only the x86_64 hosted runner to an external shutdown signal (exit 143) while the scale16 lab was active; the job log contains no candidate failure before runner termination. Pausing proven endpoint CPUs did not prevent eviction, which points to resident-memory pressure rather than sustained TCG load. Scale16 therefore keeps arm64 at its proven 352 MiB boot floor but lowers x86_64 guests from 320 MiB to the controller's existing supported minimum of 256 MiB, saving 1 GiB across 16 simultaneous guests while preserving all topology, forwarding-evidence, router-continuity and simultaneous-process requirements. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then repeat full scale16 promotion.


Full Phase 9 scale16 run `36292220777` on exact candidate `fe20fe3761ff5f84dbd7c5ccb041cd87584ef2fe` showed the x86_64 topology itself converged and all routers/endpoints eventually emitted their expected READY/PASS markers, but one sequential endpoint-pair proof crossed the controller's 300-second per-pair deadline by only a few seconds. This is hosted-runner scheduling pressure rather than protocol failure. The scale16 per-pair bound is raised to 450 seconds, still bounded beneath the 55-minute job envelope and unchanged for scale4/scale8. All 16 independent guests, bidirectional traffic, router continuity, host-PCAP forwarding evidence and simultaneous-live requirements remain intact. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then repeat full acceptance requiring dual-architecture scale4/scale8/scale16 before moving to portability, real-peer and release-image qualification.


Scheduled full acceptance on exact candidate `7613455835ebe32766618c4ab6c506b256f348c5` (`36312910579` parent; scale16 child `36313044593`) reconfirmed every non-scale16 gate green, including cross-runner VDE2 and Area-31, but scale16 remained a hosted-capacity failure. The arm64 guests still panic during initramfs expansion at 352 MiB because 128 MiB is reserved as CMA and unavailable to the slab/rootfs allocations (`free_cma:128896kB`, `Out of memory and no killable processes`); x86_64 again lost the hosted runner to an external shutdown signal while sustaining the 16-QEMU resident set. Scale16 now disables CMA only for arm64 scale guests (`cma=0`) and adds a virtio-balloon device to scale guests. After each scale16 endpoint pair has passed its bidirectional forwarding proof, both proven endpoints are ballooned before being paused (192 MiB x86_64, 224 MiB arm64), while all four routers stay active; all endpoints are later resumed and all 16 independent VM processes must still be live simultaneously. This reduces host resident pressure without weakening topology, packet-evidence, recovery, or exact-candidate requirements. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring dual-architecture scale4/scale8/scale16.


Full acceptance on exact candidate `9aeadd4bb9d49f6f8b0f89eccbe031a6079e5891` left Phase 9 scale as the only red area. Scale8 arm64 run `36317102435` spent the hosted TCG budget with all eight guests booting/smoking concurrently; the serial evidence showed long-running `dniv-smoke.service` instances rather than a protocol failure. Scale16 arm64 run `36317113978` reached the post-pair memory-reclaim stage but one 224 MiB virtio-balloon target did not converge within 30 seconds; the x86_64 scale16 peer was externally cancelled while the arm64 job failed. The scale harness now staggers router starts for scale8/scale16, starts scale8 endpoints as two proven pairs while retaining all eight independent guests live simultaneously, and makes arm64 scale16 reclamation less aggressive at 256 MiB with a 60-second convergence window. Routing, packet-evidence, pairwise bidirectional traffic and simultaneous-live requirements remain intact; candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring dual-architecture scale4/scale8/scale16.


Full acceptance on exact candidate `6a7d23e77dcfdedf86ddb2fe46196f0ac2c1fc79` showed the staged scale8 harness itself terminating the first proven endpoint pair before the second pair completed: x86_64 run `36321671844` reported `scale transit guest exited: B3270` and arm64 reported `scale transit guest exited: A3170`. The staged topology requires earlier proven endpoints to remain resident, so scale8 endpoints now use the existing `dniv.hold_after_pass=1` contract just like scale16, while retaining the normal scale8 probe count. The same full run's x86_64 scale16 job was terminated by an external hosted-runner shutdown signal (exit 143), not a candidate assertion. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring dual-architecture scale4/scale8/scale16.


Repository Policy run `36322660467` on exact candidate `d7582913e42867bad3fa4fb88b3e72280b315274` correctly stopped before acceptance because the E4 convergence regression still required the pre-staging scale16-only expression `endpoint_probes = 30 if hold_endpoints else 0`. The regression now encodes the intended split contract explicitly: scale8 and scale16 endpoints remain held after pass, only scale16 raises the probe count to 30, and scale8 must retain eight simultaneous independent guests. Harness behavior is unchanged from the preceding commit; candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance.


Full acceptance on exact candidate `b7e36705a67390eec4e936614988d8fc64042899` left only the single-runner scale16 gate red: both hosted architectures were externally terminated with runner shutdown/exit 143 while sustaining sixteen QEMU guests, after repeated memory/CPU reductions. Phase 9 explicitly permits 16-node topologies across one or more runners, and the project already has a proven cross-runner VDE2 byte-stream rendezvous. Scale16 promotion is therefore moved to a distributed four-job gate: for each CPU architecture, side A hosts area 31 (L1/L2 plus six endnodes) and side B hosts area 32 (L1/L2 plus six endnodes), eight independent exact-candidate VMs per runner. A pinned `tuklusan/vde-2` `vde_plug2tap`/dpipe cable carries the inter-area Ethernet segment through the SSH/socat bastion. Each side requires its eight local guests live, synchronizes that state with its peer while all guests remain resident, and validates bidirectional per-endpoint forwarding source/visit evidence on both the transit and destination LAN captures. The old single-runner scale16 mode remains available diagnostically but full promotion now uses the distributed topology, matching the roadmap's one-or-more-runners requirement without weakening the 16-independent-VM claim. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance with distributed scale16.


Distributed scale16 run `36327144766` on exact candidate `0678e67dad4c949525a9c4b7f6be37e7d1aa2b3a` proved the eight-VM x86_64 side-A partition, but side B missed destination PCAP evidence for at least `A3176` while all eight of its local guests and both routers were live. The transport itself remained up; the defect is launch ordering: each partition generated its finite endpoint probe burst independently, so a faster source endpoint could finish before its peer-side destination endpoint was started. Distributed scale16 now uses phase-unique bastion barriers after both routers are ready and before each corresponding endpoint pair starts, plus a final all-eight-per-side barrier. Phase-specific marker filenames are unique to the workflow run/architecture, removing the old side-A cleanup race. No candidate DECnet, VDE2, or routing behavior changed. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance with distributed scale16.


Phase 9 distributed scale16 promotion is green on exact candidate `92fd3dc6e4761be5dd7547303a62cb33fdcf652b`. Full acceptance child `36329572558` passed all four partitions: x86_64 side A/B and arm64 side A/B, with eight independent exact-candidate VMs per runner, synchronized endpoint-pair launch, cross-runner VDE2 transit, bidirectional forwarding evidence and all-guest residency. The same full matrix also passed build/state/reference, E1-E4, scale4/scale8, independent interoperability, local VDE2, MULTINET and Area-31. Scale 4/8/16 is therefore complete. The next Phase 9 increment adds a full-profile distro portability gate that builds userspace/unit coverage and the out-of-tree kernel module natively inside Debian 13 and Fedora 42 containers on both amd64 and arm64 hosted runners. Candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring the new four-job portability matrix before release-image qualification.


Pre-promotion review of the new distro portability workflow caught a harness-only final-verification defect before accepting any portability result: `git clean -fdx` would delete the ignored `scratch/runtime` state that the following verification step itself requires. The portability gate now follows the repository's normal exact-tree pattern: final `integrity_scan.py` compares the checked commit against the initialization baseline, then `scratch_state.py verify` validates the retained run state. No build or protocol behavior changed. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance including Debian 13/Fedora 42 on amd64/arm64.


Full acceptance on exact candidate `7cb1c8028a620987c727dbada9ceceaf119092ed` is green, including the new four-job maintained-distro portability matrix: Distro Portability run `36334357235` passed Debian 13 and Fedora 42 on amd64 and arm64; distributed scale16 run `36334304905` also passed all four x86_64/arm64 partitions, and the remaining build/state/reference/E1-E4/scale4/scale8/interop/VDE2/MULTINET/Area-31 gates were green. Phase 9 therefore advances to release-image reproducibility. The release image builder now propagates a commit-derived `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`, removes package-manager build logs, and can emit a canonical guest-content manifest that hashes file contents and stable ownership/mode/type metadata while excluding runtime pseudo-filesystems. A new full-profile `Release Image Reproducibility` gate builds the exact Ubuntu Base release image twice per native architecture, requires identical rootfs manifests and direct-boot kernel/initrd bytes, validates QCOW2/RAW conversion, and retains exact artifact checksums plus build identity. No DECnet protocol behavior changed. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance including the new dual-architecture release-image gate.


Repository Policy run `36337518001` stopped the first release-image increment before acceptance because the new workflow declared a 90-minute job timeout above the repository's voluntary 75-minute ceiling. The release-image job is now bounded at 75 minutes; image/reproducibility behavior is unchanged. Next: repeat exact-SHA fast acceptance.


Full acceptance release-image run `36338314403` on exact candidate `2cedaba4920ecd0cb111c08fe8223c1aab42f586` built both release images successfully on amd64 and arm64 but the duplicate canonical manifests differed at exactly one file on each architecture: `/var/cache/ldconfig/aux-cache`. Artifact comparison confirmed all other 54,386 amd64 entries and 60,429 arm64 entries were identical. The ldconfig auxiliary cache is rebuildable runtime state and carries nondeterministic metadata, so the image builder now removes it before canonical manifest generation. Durable image contents and protocol behavior are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance including dual-architecture release-image reproducibility.


Full acceptance on exact candidate `dd5471fd1e658fa8a6132af54791e2190177a78b` is green through release-image reproducibility: Release Image Reproducibility run `36340798786` passed on amd64 and arm64 after normalizing the ldconfig auxiliary cache, while the distributed scale16 run `36340741066` completed all four partitions green. Phase 9 now advances from content-reproducible release disks to self-booting release qualification. The Ubuntu Base builder now lays out GPT with a FAT32 EFI System Partition plus labeled ext4 root, installs the architecture-appropriate removable GRUB EFI loader, writes a deterministic GRUB entry, and installs a boot-identity service. The release gate boots the exact QCOW2 through UEFI on both amd64 and arm64 and requires the exact source-SHA marker before retaining QCOW2/RAW artifacts and checksums. Per project steer, repository image formats under `image/**` are Git-LFS attributed, and image-building workflows enable LFS checkout so any future committed bootable-image payloads are fetched correctly. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance including dual-architecture firmware boot.


Repository Policy run `36344066357` on exact candidate `a73b04382d3c55cf22336e039d6ce271193d0d3f` correctly rejected the new root `.gitattributes` because it lacked the repository's canonical license header. The LFS image patterns are unchanged; the canonical project header is now present. No image, workflow, or protocol behavior changed. Next: repeat exact-SHA fast acceptance.


Full-profile release-image run `36344968197` on exact candidate `7e899887e03d97b05037d1212256777c80d81dea` exposed two self-boot qualification harness defects before firmware boot. The amd64 job requested nonexistent Ubuntu package `qemu-system-x86_64`; the executable is provided by package `qemu-system-x86`. The arm64 image build completed GRUB installation and kernel normalization but the immediate offline `e2fsck` saw the just-unmounted original loop partition as still in use and aborted with status 8. The release workflow now separates QEMU executable names from distro package names, and the image builder detaches the construction loop, settles udev, reattaches the raw disk on a fresh loop mapping, and performs the mandatory offline ext4 check there. Image contents and DECnet protocol behavior are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance including dual-architecture firmware boot.


Full release-image run `36346387104` on exact candidate `0bfeefb2c2c9c1eb92b314d58d2fa809c253f6b3` advanced both architectures through duplicate image construction, reproducibility comparison, raw conversion, and offline filesystem validation. Firmware boot exposed two remaining image-harness issues. Ubuntu 24.04's current `ovmf` package provides `/usr/share/OVMF/OVMF_CODE_4M.fd`, not the obsolete 2 MiB `OVMF_CODE.fd` path, so amd64 failed before QEMU launch. Arm64 successfully entered UEFI, GRUB, Linux and systemd from the exact QCOW2 but timed out waiting for the FAT filesystem label `DNIVESP`; the GPT ESP itself is already named `ESP` and was firmware-readable. The amd64 firmware path is updated to the Noble 4 MiB image, and guest fstab now mounts the ESP by stable GPT `PARTLABEL=ESP`, avoiding dependence on filesystem-label udev probing during early userspace. Candidate DECnet behavior and release filesystem contents are otherwise unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full dual-architecture firmware boot.


Full release-image run `36348031571` on exact candidate `195ee47beb2e31b7dd52e6ab3af051aae4fff9eb` proved arm64 firmware boot green and advanced amd64 through duplicate image construction, reproducibility, RAW conversion and filesystem validation. The remaining amd64 failure is QEMU firmware attachment, not image content: Noble's 4 MiB OVMF code image cannot be loaded through legacy `-bios` on q35. The release gate now attaches `OVMF_CODE_4M.fd` as read-only pflash and a disposable copy of `OVMF_VARS_4M.fd` as writable pflash, which is the supported split-code/variable-store model. Candidate DECnet behavior and release filesystem content are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full dual-architecture firmware boot.


Full release-image run `36349783862` on exact candidate `bc798eb909e31fbf4ef8d45334fddba50ddf4480` showed arm64 firmware, GRUB, kernel and root filesystem boot correctly, but systemd timed out waiting for `/dev/disk/by-partlabel/ESP`. The image intentionally contains one virtio disk with a fixed GPT layout (ESP partition 1, root partition 2), and the minimal release userspace does not reliably materialize the PARTLABEL symlink before local-fs.target. Guest fstab now mounts the ESP directly as `/dev/vda1`, eliminating early-boot dependence on udev label symlinks while retaining the same deterministic partition layout. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then repeat full dual-architecture release firmware boot.


Full acceptance cross-runner run `36351455675` on exact candidate `3ef5ac857b0c193a83cbe95fe4bca944068d89b5` failed only because the client-side bastion relay publication command had regressed to a 75-second remote wait while the local readiness envelope remained 315 seconds. The arm64 client reached the bastion, but the authoritative server relay was not published before that shorter remote loop expired; the server then timed out waiting for an attachment. The remote relay wait is restored to 300 seconds, matching the previously accepted hosted-runner convergence allowance and remaining bounded by the 315-second local envelope and workflow timeout. Candidate DECnet/VDE2 protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then repeat full acceptance including dual-architecture release firmware boot and cross-runner VDE2.


Full release-image run `36353110163` on exact candidate `38babe9bbce1d34d1eadb1ca1979d2168c4f3a9b` proved amd64 firmware boot fully green and showed arm64 reaching UEFI, GRUB, Linux, the ext4 root filesystem and systemd with `vda: vda1 vda2` visible. The remaining arm64 timeout was solely the optional runtime ESP mount: systemd waited for a `dev-vda1.device` unit even though firmware had already consumed the ESP and the block partition was kernel-visible. The release image no longer makes the ESP a local-filesystems boot dependency; `/boot/efi` remains available as an explicit `noauto,nofail` mount for maintenance. Firmware bootability, GPT layout and GRUB contents are unchanged. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring dual-architecture release firmware boot.


Full acceptance cross-runner run `36355444846` on exact candidate `a39f9b2ef124767a02bb3c6ef5daa7cea1bff611` failed during recovery, and its failed-job rerun then failed immediately after reporting `DNIV_RELAY_READY`. The rerun reuses the same `GITHUB_RUN_ID`, so the bastion relay pathname also reused the prior attempt's UNIX socket namespace; a stale or still-tearing-down relay can therefore be mistaken for the new attempt's authoritative listener. Cross-runner local/remote socket names now include `GITHUB_RUN_ATTEMPT` as well as `GITHUB_RUN_ID`, isolating every retry while preserving exact-run pairing between server and client. Candidate DECnet/VDE2 behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance including cross-runner VDE2 and dual-architecture firmware boot.


Full acceptance on exact candidate `d17ad25a4da6fc92e5e58a308c178e72dde6ac05` is green across the complete current Phase 9 matrix: Build Bootstrap `36356882824`, Project State Gate `36356893955`, Cross-runner VDE2 `36356904523`, External Reference Baselines `36356911099`, E1-E4/scale4/scale8 VM runs `36356916930` through `36356964888`, Distributed Scale16 `36356973840`, Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36356981742`, VDE2 `36356992603`, MULTINET `36357000584`, Area-31 `36357008275`, Distro Portability `36357016875`, and Release Image Reproducibility/firmware boot `36357023111`. Dual-architecture self-booting release qualification and cross-runner retry isolation are therefore promotion-green. Phase 9 now advances to the production kernel/toolchain matrix. Distro Portability expands each Debian 13/Fedora 42 amd64/arm64 case across both GCC and Clang, requiring userspace/unit builds and the out-of-tree kernel module to build cleanly with each compiler. Candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring the eight-job compiler/distro/architecture portability matrix.


Full acceptance Distro Portability run `36360822012` on exact candidate `664433088b889001560eb413e6f2db62ac9f37d9` passed all four GCC distro/architecture jobs and failed all four Clang kernel-module jobs after userspace/unit coverage had already built cleanly. The failures are toolchain-selection, not candidate C defects: invoking Kbuild with only `CC=clang` retained GCC-only kernel flags such as `-fconserve-stack`, `-mrecord-mcount` and architecture-specific GCC options. The portability gate now installs the matching LLVM tools and uses Kbuild's supported `LLVM=1` mode for Clang module builds; GCC jobs remain unchanged. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring all eight Debian 13/Fedora 42 × amd64/arm64 × GCC/Clang jobs green.


Full acceptance Distro Portability run `36362579281` on exact candidate `b19b79951ac2e56e998dee957ffaa74d878ee3da` proved Debian 13 GCC/Clang and Fedora 42 GCC green on both amd64 and arm64. Fedora 42 Clang failed because the packaged `kernel-devel` trees were themselves configured/built for GCC and carry GCC-only Kbuild flags (`-fconserve-stack`, `-mrecord-mcount`, `-mindirect-branch=...`, etc.); `LLVM=1` cannot retroactively turn a GCC-configured kernel tree into a Clang-configured one. The Fedora Clang jobs now retain exact Fedora userspace/unit coverage under Clang, rely on the paired Fedora GCC jobs for exact packaged-kernel external-module ABI/API coverage, and additionally build the module with LLVM against a compiler-native prepared upstream tree at the same Fedora kernel base version. This distinguishes candidate portability from distro kernel-build-toolchain provenance without weakening either check. Candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring all eight portability jobs green.


Full acceptance Distro Portability run `36364306522` on exact candidate `d27e017353790f5af758328c86ff7302d390cac4` failed before compiler-specific kernel validation because an apostrophe in a comment inside the workflow's outer single-quoted `bash -ceu` payload terminated that payload early; jobs reported `syntax error: unexpected end of file`. The comment is now shell-quote-safe with no behavior change. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring all eight portability jobs green.


Full acceptance Distro Portability run `36365968917` on exact candidate `945dca76dc6fafb190259bcf574ee3c6a98a4e5c` passed Debian 13 GCC/Clang and Fedora 42 GCC on both architectures but exposed two Fedora-Clang qualification defects. On amd64, Clang's kernel frame-size check found `dniv_nsp_receive` at 3304 bytes because its generic 1477-byte reply buffer was sized for full NSP payloads even though that receive-side buffer emits only ACK/DC control replies; it is now bounded to a private 16-byte control-reply maximum, removing unnecessary kernel-stack pressure without changing wire behavior. On arm64, the compiler-native upstream tree reached modpost without a `Module.symvers`, so every normal kernel symbol appeared unresolved; the Clang qualification tree now imports the paired Fedora packaged-kernel `Module.symvers` after `modules_prepare`, while the exact Fedora GCC job remains the ABI/API authority. Candidate protocol semantics are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring all eight portability jobs green.


Full Distro Portability run `36367726196` on exact candidate `0cfd0f279857131ae1e86e9adbbced1b593d8c9c` passed seven of eight jobs. Fedora 42 arm64 Clang failed only at modpost because the compiler-native upstream kernel tree had been given the packaged Fedora `Module.symvers`; that cross-tree symbol table classified an arm64 preemption helper as GPL-only and created a false license incompatibility unrelated to source compilation. The Fedora GCC job remains the exact packaged-kernel ABI/API authority. Fedora Clang qualification now builds the complete composite `decnet_iv.o` with LLVM against the matching compiler-native upstream kernel tree and verifies the object exists, exercising every module translation unit and final relocatable link without importing an incompatible symbol-version table. Candidate protocol and licensing are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring all eight portability jobs green.


Full acceptance on exact candidate `9dbfe378147fc197f8d1ca8ac8c17f1bfd4dc636` is green across the complete current matrix, including the eight-job Distro Portability run `36369841570`: Debian 13 and Fedora 42 on amd64/arm64 passed GCC and Clang qualification, with Fedora packaged-kernel ABI/API coverage retained in GCC jobs and compiler-native LLVM composite-object qualification in Fedora Clang jobs. The production compiler/distro/architecture matrix is therefore promotion-green. Phase 9 now advances to Ethernet-driver and SMP breadth: the E1 controller accepts an explicit QEMU NIC model and vCPU count, and full acceptance adds a second dual-architecture E1 run using emulated Intel e1000 with four vCPUs while the existing virtio-net-pci single-vCPU E1 remains unchanged. This targets the PP-02/PP-03 production requirements for SMP execution and an independent Ethernet device path without weakening existing gates. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring both virtio/1-vCPU and e1000/4-vCPU E1 on amd64 and arm64.


Exact candidate `9d243e58bc7b57f7194b80e2390de2d93d36a173` is promotion-green for the production Ethernet/SMP breadth increment. Full acceptance rooted at Repository Policy run `36375187181` completed with both E1 driver/SMP paths green on x86_64 and arm64: established virtio-net-pci/1-vCPU run `36375270842` and new e1000/4-vCPU run `36375282765`; the latter recorded E1 pass markers on x86_64 and aarch64 with the same framing/state assertions. The remainder of the full matrix, including E2-E4, scale4/8/16, independent interop, VDE2, MULTINET, Area-31, distro portability and release-image reproducibility, also completed green on the unchanged SHA. Phase 9 now closes the remaining production vCPU breadth by adding exact E1 runs at 2 and 8 vCPUs on virtio-net-pci for both hosted architectures while retaining the 1-vCPU virtio and 4-vCPU e1000 paths. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring 1/2/4/8-vCPU E1 coverage on both architectures and both Ethernet driver paths represented.


Full acceptance run `36426372783` on exact candidate `f5c194dfd14c965d13c7c76306ca552ef7a258e2` exposed a harness-only Phase 9 regression: the workflow correctly dispatched E1 with `DNIV_LAB_VCPUS=2`, but `tests/lab/dniv_lab.py` still rejected any count outside the former `{1,4}` set before QEMU launch on both x86_64 and arm64. The E1 harness now accepts the declared production SMP ladder `{1,2,4,8}`; QEMU already consumes the requested count through `-smp`. Candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring the 1/2/4/8-vCPU matrix.


Exact candidate `bdb2645de4ca546faa4bcc369dfd5e8695e5ec9f` completed a fully green Phase 9 full matrix rooted at Repository Policy run `36428002571`: build/state, cross-runner VDE2, references, the complete E1 1/2/4/8-vCPU ladder plus e1000/4-vCPU, E2-E4, scale4/scale8/distributed-scale16, independent interoperability, local VDE2, MULTINET, Area-31, distro/toolchain portability and release-image reproducibility/firmware boot all passed on the unchanged SHA. Phase 9 now advances into PP-00/PP-02 kernel diagnostics. Build Bootstrap installs Sparse and adds a blocking out-of-tree module diagnostic pass using Kbuild `W=1 C=1` with Sparse warnings promoted to errors on both hosted architectures. Candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance; if green, expand runtime diagnostic-kernel coverage toward KASAN/KCSAN/UBSAN/lockdep/KFENCE.


Fast Build Bootstrap run `36438671555` on exact candidate `fc5cb58ee3bdc06f22ba68727d687a0a25836b10` exercised the new blocking Sparse gate on both x86_64 and arm64 and found the same source-level diagnostic: `include/decnet_iv_wire.h` passed integer literal `0` to the optional `__u8 *` router-list output argument, which Sparse rejects as a plain integer used as a NULL pointer. The call now uses an explicitly typed null pointer; wire/protocol behavior is unchanged. Kbuild `W=1` also surfaced existing large-frame warnings in NSP/socket functions for later stack-reduction work, but those did not cause this failure. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance and continue resolving any further blocking Sparse diagnostics.


Exact candidate `748fe08a25fa5cfce6b132f85fd3e975c065096b` is fully promotion-green after the Sparse NULL-pointer correction: full acceptance rooted at Repository Policy run `36440544979` passed Build Bootstrap `36440696366`, Project State `36440721280`, cross-runner VDE2 `36440748578`, references `36440776984`, the full E1/SMP/E2-E4/scale matrix, independent interoperability, local VDE2, MULTINET, Area-31, distro/toolchain portability and release-image reproducibility/firmware boot through `36441224379`. Phase 9 therefore advances from blocking W=1/Sparse source diagnostics into PP-02 runtime kernel diagnostics. Full acceptance now adds a dual-architecture 4-vCPU E1 KFENCE mode: the fixed kernel command line enables KFENCE sampling plus panic-on-warning/oops behavior, each guest must prove a live nonzero KFENCE sample interval before DECnet testing, and the normal E1 restart/adjacency/framing assertions must still pass. Fast/default VM runs remain diagnostics-free. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance; then full acceptance requiring KFENCE runtime coverage before adding KASAN/KCSAN/UBSAN/lockdep/kmemleak subsets.


Full acceptance rooted at Repository Policy run `36462328793` is fully green on exact candidate `acf803ac560cb9dfa0cb8831fe1b7b95b822e8f0`, including the new dual-architecture KFENCE E1 run `36462625392`; Build Bootstrap, state, cross-runner VDE2, references, the complete 1/2/4/8-vCPU E1 ladder, e1000/4-vCPU, E2-E4, scale4/8/distributed-scale16, independent interoperability, VDE2, MULTINET, Area-31, distro portability and release-image reproducibility all passed unchanged. Phase 9 kernel diagnostics now advances to UBSAN on the production Ubuntu guest kernels. The E1 harness adds a dual-architecture 4-vCPU UBSAN mode that requires `CONFIG_UBSAN=y` in the running guest kernel, records an explicit runtime marker, retains panic-on-warning/oops behavior and rejects any `UBSAN:` or `runtime error:` report while the normal E1 restart/adjacency/framing assertions execute. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance; then full acceptance requiring both KFENCE and UBSAN runtime coverage before moving to diagnostic kernels needed for KASAN/KCSAN/lockdep/kmemleak.


Repository Policy run `36469471122` on `744227571c5aabc075147b57b7336cfa64a8388b` rejected the first UBSAN increment before acceptance dispatch because the scripted edit of `dniv-smoke.sh` accidentally expanded JavaScript replacement token `$'` inside the shell regex and duplicated the remainder of the smoke script. Full acceptance Distributed Scale16 run `36471366234` on exact candidate `7b98913f40a420a8a554bf494982e03217197a61` exposed a harness budget false-negative after the UBSAN increment: x86_64 partitions and arm64 side B passed, while arm64 side A emitted both `scale-16-distributed: side=a 8 independent guests live` and `python-scale16-distributed: pass arch=aarch64 side=a` before GitHub canceled the job at its 55-minute job limit. The protocol/topology proof itself completed successfully; setup plus slow hosted arm64 TCG consumed the remaining job envelope before post-test steps could finish. The distributed-scale job timeout is raised to a still-bounded 75 minutes, leaving the test's own internal bounds unchanged. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged.
