Full acceptance dispatcher run `36479427095` on exact candidate `903708ecefedf3a55d5e8199d926b6345cfd6ca6` failed after successfully dispatching build/state/cross-runner/reference and the E1-through-scale16 VM set because GitHub returned a transient HTTP 502 while the dispatcher listed prior Independent Ethernet Interoperability runs. No candidate or child gate failed at that point; the control-plane GET aborted dispatch before the remaining workflows could be launched. The acceptance dispatcher now retries only the idempotent pre-dispatch run-list GET up to five times with bounded backoff; the non-idempotent workflow dispatch itself remains single-shot to avoid duplicate child runs. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then repeat full acceptance requiring the complete matrix green.


Full cross-runner run `36481479592` on exact candidate `bbe783ead02a92b8f220095fdf0c6813369f04b2` failed only because GitHub scheduled the two hosted jobs more than eight minutes apart: the x86_64 server entered its authoritative relay at 20:49 UTC and exhausted the 300-second first-session wait at 20:54, while the arm64 client did not begin its proof until 21:02 and then exhausted its own relay-publication wait after the server was already gone. The first server relay session and the client relay-publication wait are therefore widened to 900 seconds (915-second local readiness envelope) while recovery sessions remain at 300 seconds and every job remains bounded by the existing 25-minute workflow timeout. This changes only hosted-runner rendezvous tolerance; marked frames, hard transport loss, adjacency expiry/recovery, reconnect and switch-restart requirements are unchanged. Candidate DECnet/VDE2 behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring the complete matrix green.


Full release-image run `36493915229` on exact candidate `6f0058cac930216c9b008b82468b1779fee3b1f2` reached deterministic image construction but both architectures were interrupted by Ubuntu snapshot service instability: update attempted an unavailable `Translation-en` index (404) and package fetches returned transient 503 responses. The release builder now disables APT translation acquisition and applies a bounded five-retry policy to all snapshot update/install operations, including the initial CA bootstrap. Package/source pins and image contents remain unchanged; this only hardens deterministic snapshot retrieval. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring release-image reproducibility/firmware boot and the complete matrix green before advancing kernel diagnostics.


Fast Repository Policy run `36498502347` on `b46ade7aeaada440747bc7e2c8039f98e3cea93a` correctly caught that the image-builder regression gate encoded the old option-free APT install prefix and therefore rejected the new snapshot retry/language options before acceptance dispatch. The gate now recognizes the invariant snapshot install signature while permitting bounded APT transport options before it, so it continues to verify required pinned release/foundation packages without freezing incidental option ordering. No image contents or protocol behavior change. Next: repeat exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance.


Repository Policy run `36498670639` on `f75f58440874c26479fe8820a24004295df4b770` exposed a second image-gate assumption: the parser stopped at the first pinned snapshot install, which is now the CA bootstrap, so it never saw the later release kernel/initrd package block. The gate now unions tokens across all pinned snapshot install commands before checking required packages. This preserves strict package-presence validation while allowing the bootstrap and main install to coexist. No release image or protocol behavior changes. Next: repeat exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance.


Full acceptance Distributed Scale16 run `36499888746` on exact candidate `f3f4e4924d017c102bcde8e145594bc97fc0a0fb` exposed a distributed-cable readiness race, not a DECnet protocol failure. Both x86_64-b and arm64-b partitions created their local plug2tap interface and immediately booted L2B, but the remote VDE relay was not yet proven connected; L2B consequently exhausted its guest-local remote-L2 adjacency window and reported `remote-l2-not-ready`. The distributed workflow now distinguishes local TAP creation from cross-host cable readiness: side A publishes a listener marker, side B waits up to 900 seconds for the architecture-specific relay and publishes a connected marker, and both hosted jobs complete a bastion barrier before any scale16 router boots. This mirrors the already-proven cross-runner scheduling-skew tolerance while preserving the same 16 independent VMs, topology, packet evidence and protocol behavior. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring distributed scale16 plus release-image and the complete Phase 9 matrix green.


Full acceptance release-image attempt 1 in run `36503363975` on exact candidate `9cb2a48c5d3dc5961f2a65925c95f4aa09d1c584` exhausted the existing 75-minute job envelope on both architectures while still downloading the first deterministic Ubuntu snapshot package set; logs show snapshot retrieval progressing normally but too slowly to reach the second reproducibility build. Release-image qualification now keeps the repository-wide 75-minute ceiling for every other hosted job but grants this deterministic snapshot gate a bounded 120-minute exception. Within each release-image job, the two independent builds share only a host-side APT index/archive cache bound temporarily into the chroot; the cache is detached before manifest generation and each guest retains the same empty durable APT-cache shape. Package/source pins, duplicate-build comparison, firmware boot, exact-tree checks and protocol behavior are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring release-image reproducibility/firmware boot plus the complete Phase 9 matrix green.


Full acceptance Release Image Reproducibility run `36512040826` on exact candidate `ae07b57ad98f3122fc3bfc7387b3ef9e956234ef` completed both amd64 image builds, then failed the root-filesystem reproducibility comparison at exactly one volatile file: `/var/cache/apt/pkgcache.bin` had identical size but a different digest between build A and build B. The candidate module and release payload were otherwise built successfully; this is a release-image normalization defect introduced by reusing host-side APT list/archive caches, not a DECnet protocol failure. The release builder now removes APT's derived `pkgcache.bin` and `srcpkgcache.bin` before manifest generation, just as it already removes the disposable ldconfig auxiliary cache. Package/source pins, guest package contents, firmware boot semantics and candidate protocol behavior are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA acceptance, then resume Phase 9 kernel diagnostics after the full matrix is green.


Exact-head Phase 9 full acceptance on `63cb31108f95ed498c5ff5117c96c2c0abc2d292` is green across Build Bootstrap, Project State, cross-runner VDE2, external references, the complete E1/SMP/KFENCE/UBSAN and E2-E4 VM matrix, scale4/8/distributed-scale16, independent Route20/PyDECnet Ethernet interoperability, VDE2, MULTINET, Area-31, distro portability, and dual-architecture release-image reproducibility/firmware boot. Phase 9 now advances to custom diagnostic kernels. This increment adds an automatically dispatched dual-architecture KASAN E1 gate using pinned upstream Linux v7.0 commit `028ef9c96e96197026887c0f092424679298aae8`, a production-kernel-derived configuration with boot-critical virtio/ext4 paths forced built-in, exact-candidate module injection, no-initrd diagnostic boot, explicit KASAN runtime markers, panic-on-warning/oops behavior, and serial-log rejection of KASAN findings. Diagnostic kernel builds are source/config/profile keyed and cached; candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA acceptance of the KASAN gate, then KCSAN/lockdep/kmemleak coverage.


Repository Policy run `36524497677` on `ef431194febfc2eabd13303b67a78ffa8e7a1d3d` rejected the first KASAN increment before acceptance dispatch. The scripted edit of `tests/lab/dniv-smoke.sh` used a replacement string containing the shell regex token `$'`, which JavaScript interpreted as a replacement suffix token and triplicated the remainder of the smoke script; the permanent E1 silence-window regression caught the damage immediately. This follow-up restores the exact previously-green smoke script and inserts only the intended KASAN capability marker block using literal replacement semantics. The KASAN design and candidate protocol behavior are unchanged. Next: repeat exact-SHA full acceptance.


Full acceptance Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36525683346` on exact candidate `4b6b119b2c761ae175eab36d07c3209903f0761f` failed identically on amd64 and arm64 only after each pinned Linux v7.0 KASAN kernel image had completed successfully. The failure occurred in the redundant post-image `modules_prepare` invocation before candidate-module injection; no DECnet code had run. A full kernel image build already leaves the generated headers and symbol state required for the subsequent out-of-tree module build, so this follow-up removes that redundant command and adds an explicit kernel-release check/diagnostic. Candidate protocol behavior and the KASAN configuration are unchanged. All other full-acceptance gates on `4b6b119b2c761ae175eab36d07c3209903f0761f` were green. Next: repeat exact-SHA full acceptance and require the dual-architecture KASAN E1 gate to run through module injection and runtime assertions.


Full acceptance Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36560946586` on exact candidate `298ffe355b7462adec4c68b2f1b4936a70cdf21b` advanced the custom KASAN build past the prior redundant `modules_prepare` failure on both architectures. Both Linux v7.0 images completed, then the explicit release check found `7.0.0-dniv-kasan+` rather than `7.0.0-dniv-kasan`. The trailing `+` is Kbuild SCM decoration from the shallow pinned checkout, not a kernel or DECnet defect. The builder already verifies the exact upstream commit before compilation; it now removes Git metadata immediately after that verification so Kbuild cannot derive an environment-dependent SCM suffix. This keeps the diagnostic kernel release deterministic and preserves the existing exact module-vermagic assertions. Candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: repeat exact-SHA full acceptance and require KASAN runtime execution.


Full acceptance Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36563543407` on exact candidate `b8a8251c7cd6e965d3f92f54a20c444d2d4fa188` completed deterministic amd64/arm64 Linux v7.0 KASAN kernel builds and reached candidate-module compilation. The external module then failed modpost because the image-only diagnostic build had not generated the kernel export table `Module.symvers`. The builder now clears inherited distro `=m` selections, retains `CONFIG_MODULES=y`, forces all QEMU boot-critical paths built-in as before, and runs a bounded in-tree modules pass after the image build to generate `Module.symvers` before strict candidate-module modpost/vermagic checks. This changes only diagnostic-kernel construction; candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KASAN E1 runtime completion.


Full acceptance Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36568341907` on exact candidate `8a315e82fd5fe321ed48ece4c6acbbf89c350cac` advanced through deterministic dual-architecture KASAN kernel construction, generated `Module.symvers`, strict external candidate-module modpost/vermagic checks, and disposable-image injection. Both guests then failed before userspace because the no-initrd diagnostic boot passed `root=LABEL=dniv-root`; the kernel saw the virtio block device but cannot resolve a filesystem label without early userspace, so it panicked with `VFS: Unable to mount root fs on unknown-block(0,0)`. The QEMU lab controller now uses the filesystem label only when an initrd is supplied and uses the deterministic direct virtio root device `/dev/vda` for no-initrd boots. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KASAN E1 runtime completion.

Hosted-runner scheduling skew is now budgeted for peak periods of up to 30 minutes. Cross-runner VDE2 first-session/relay readiness waits are raised from 900/915 seconds to 2100/2115 seconds and the three cross-runner jobs from 25 to 60 minutes; recovery-session protocol bounds remain unchanged. Distributed Scale16 uses the same 2100/2115-second cable/barrier envelope and a bounded 120-minute job allowance so an early arm64 partition can survive runner skew plus its existing slow TCG workload. The workflow-budget gate grants that 120-minute exception only to distributed Scale16, matching the existing release-image exception. No DECnet protocol behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA full acceptance, then resume KASAN runtime qualification and proceed to KCSAN/lockdep/kmemleak.

Full acceptance Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36576262859` on exact candidate `4c8b86e465dc4142588b89ec29d5616dc29f747a` reached live KASAN guests on both architectures. amd64 progressed through adjacency and MAC-change validation, then both smoke processes later called `dnraw` using the pre-change interface name and failed with `if_nametoindex: No such device`; the smoke harness now re-discovers the sole non-loopback interface after the link down/address/up transition before raw-frame traffic. arm64 successfully mounted the direct `/dev/vda` root but KASAN+TCG spent roughly five minutes in kernel/systemd startup and exhausted the previous 720-second whole-lab bound before smoke execution; diagnostic E1 bounds are raised to 900 seconds on amd64 and 1800 seconds on arm64 while the workflow remains capped at 75 minutes. No DECnet protocol behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KASAN E1 runtime completion, then KCSAN, lockdep and kmemleak.


Full acceptance on exact candidate `e225259518cf173988df4842f6a8f03e5ca4c1f5` exposed two harness/infrastructure failures. KASAN amd64 reached live E1 execution with no KASAN report, but systemd/udev renamed the sole NIC during the deliberate link-down/MAC-change sequence; node A then failed on stale `eth0`, causing node B's later raw-unicast receive delta to remain zero. The E1 smoke now re-discovers the sole non-loopback NIC before and between each link/MAC operation. Distributed Scale16 arm64 side A failed before any VM boot when the bastion reset SSH during key exchange; cable startup now retries that transient pre-attachment failure up to eight bounded attempts while preserving the existing 35-minute peer-rendezvous window. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: repeat exact-SHA full acceptance and require dual-architecture KASAN plus complete distributed Scale16 green.


Full acceptance Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36589078653` on exact candidate `b543bf6a2fedb1b47957ca2040f12bf7fd54a34d` reached the live amd64 KASAN guest but exhausted its 2048 MiB guest memory during unrelated systemd BPF verifier startup, panicking before DECnet E1 execution; no KASAN finding was reported. The diagnostic E1 guest allocation is raised to 2560 MiB, keeping the two-VM hosted-runner footprint bounded while giving KASAN headroom. The same acceptance run also saw one independent-interoperability job fail only because an HTTPS fetch of pinned PyDECnet could not connect to GitHub; candidate protocol behavior was not exercised in that failed job. Next: repeat exact-SHA full acceptance and require dual-architecture KASAN, distributed Scale16, and the complete independent-interoperability matrix green.


Full acceptance Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36594597512` on exact candidate `8c73b3bc6fdca72a61e5f1a1a4972145f309a39e` confirmed the 2560 MiB memory increase avoids the prior amd64 OOM condition, while arm64 exhausted the 1800-second whole-lab bound during KASAN+TCG systemd/udev coldplug. The arm64 serial transcript showed continued udev progress without a KASAN finding or DECnet execution, so the architecture-specific KASAN E1 lab allowance is raised to 3000 seconds within the existing 75-minute hosted-job ceiling. amd64 remains bounded at 900 seconds. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: repeat exact-SHA full acceptance and require dual-architecture KASAN plus the remaining Phase 9 matrix green.


Full acceptance Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36601280655` on exact candidate `fbe1cfc451f7b9ab0a6c6bdd314aad7fa9de56a6` reached live amd64 KASAN E1 execution with no KASAN report. DN70 completed its endnode bootstrap while udev renamed the NIC from `eth0` to `ens3`; the subsequent router-mode link-up still used the bootstrap-era interface name and failed before peer unicast validation, leaving DN71 with a zero receive delta. The E1 harness now re-discovers the sole non-loopback interface immediately before every bootstrap, router-mode and restart link-up boundary, in addition to the existing MAC-change rediscovery. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: repeat exact-SHA full acceptance and require dual-architecture KASAN plus the complete Phase 9 matrix green.


The remaining KASAN E1 failures are confined to diagnostic-boot machinery. To eliminate the rename race completely, KASAN boots now use `net.ifnames=0`; the E1 raw-unicast loop also re-discovers the sole non-loopback interface before every send. arm64 KASAN+TCG previously spent its entire 3000-second lab budget in `systemd-udev-trigger` coldplug before DECnet execution, so KASAN-only boots mask `systemd-udev-trigger.service` while leaving `systemd-udevd` available and relying on devtmpfs plus already-enumerated virtio devices. Production boot behavior and DECnet protocol behavior are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KASAN and the complete Phase 9 matrix green.


Exact-SHA full acceptance on `1e4f7b4778d233421a68e48f61747916d3eef31e` was green everywhere except arm64 KASAN E1. The arm64 KASAN guest pair reached live DECnet execution with no KASAN report; DN71 completed E1 and emitted `DNIV-E1-PASS`, while DN70 was still progressing when the 3000-second whole-lab bound expired. The hosted arm64 diagnostic job was oversubscribing two TCG guests at four vCPUs each. Diagnostic E1 now keeps amd64 at four vCPUs but runs each arm64 KASAN guest with two vCPUs, preserving SMP coverage while matching the four-vCPU hosted runner footprint and removing avoidable TCG oversubscription. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: repeat exact-SHA full acceptance and require dual-architecture KASAN plus the complete Phase 9 matrix green.


Exact-SHA full acceptance on `10d509b7ffe84469535c6facc7f324a6d8bfdaf1` exposed a cross-runner VDE2 scheduling flake after transport restart: the arm64 client proved initial adjacency, hard transport loss and frame-ready relay reattachment, but PyDECnet had not emitted the recovered adjacency within the existing 80-second post-reconnect allowance before the client exited; the server consequently timed out waiting for the unused third relay session. The same gate was green on the immediately preceding exact SHA with identical transport/protocol code. The recovery wait is widened from 80 to 120 seconds, still bounded well inside the 300-second relay-session limit and without weakening required adjacency-down/recovery evidence. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged.


Exact-SHA full acceptance on `d0db450c237fc8bae29e5ffc6f07f3581ec9f042` was green across every Phase 9 gate except arm64 KASAN E1. Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36627836224` passed amd64, while both arm64 KASAN guests booted the pinned diagnostic kernel without a KASAN report but then froze in PID 1 before any DECnet execution: systemd 259 spent extreme time under KASAN+TCG startup and failed its generator sandbox handshake with `Protocol error`. The arm64 KASAN path now bypasses that unrelated userspace timeout by invoking the already-installed `dniv-smoke` harness directly as PID 1; in direct-init mode the smoke harness mounts only proc/sysfs/tmpfs, emits the same explicit evidence, and remains alive until the host controller terminates QEMU after both pass markers. The already-green amd64 KASAN boot path and candidate DECnet behavior are unchanged. Next: repeat exact-SHA full acceptance and require dual-architecture KASAN green before advancing to KCSAN/lockdep/kmemleak.


The direct-init PID-1 detector is corrected to use the shell's parent PID (`PPID=0` for kernel-invoked init) rather than a malformed literal introduced by the scripted commit. This is a harness-only correction; candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA full acceptance of the arm64 KASAN direct-init path.


Exact-SHA full acceptance on `40aa92575b1d75986afbfb8afa6964440ee43711` was green across the Phase 9 matrix except arm64 KASAN E1. Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36636269588` reached the direct-init path successfully on both arm64 guests: DN70 and DN71 each emitted `DNIV-E1-PASS`, bidirectional unicast evidence was present, and no KASAN report occurred. The only failure was startup wire evidence: DN70 emitted six All-Endnodes hellos before the equal-priority, higher-address DN71 had settled as DR. Under arm64 KASAN+TCG, DN70 can transition from its bootstrap endnode role to L1 router immediately after the first UP sample while DN71 is briefly CPU-starved long enough for DN70's local DR delay to elapse. Direct-init DN70 now remains in bootstrap for one bounded eight-second DR-election window after the first UP adjacency before joining the router election, allowing the already-live DN71 to settle as DR. This is KASAN/direct-init harness timing only; candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: repeat exact-SHA full acceptance and require dual-architecture KASAN green before advancing to KCSAN/lockdep/kmemleak.


Exact-SHA full acceptance on `a800a1220bd99f2e52aa03e7a2b3c4def61aabb4` again isolated arm64 KASAN E1 startup election timing. Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36640758606` passed amd64; both arm64 direct-init guests emitted `DNIV-E1-PASS`, bidirectional unicast evidence reached 200 frames each way, and no KASAN report occurred, but DN70 still emitted six transient All-Endnodes hellos while DN71 was CPU-starved across DN70's role reload. The prior post-bootstrap sleep did not protect the reload boundary. Direct-init DN70 now takes its interface down before unloading the bootstrap module, loads router mode while quiescent, gives DN71 one bounded eight-second election window with host CPU available, and only then raises the interface. Equal router priority and higher-address DN71 election semantics remain unchanged; candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. The same full matrix also saw the otherwise-established arm64 scale8 gate time out in systemd smoke execution while its amd64 peer and all other completed Phase 9 gates passed; no scale8 protocol failure was reported, so this successor re-runs the unchanged gate rather than weakening it. Next: exact-SHA full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KASAN and scale8 green before advancing to KCSAN/lockdep/kmemleak.


Exact-SHA full acceptance on `5daced3bba2666cc5d0bce4fc5e19525d6898c50` reached clean KASAN runtime completion on both architectures: both arm64 direct-init guests emitted `DNIV-E1-PASS`, bidirectional unicast reached 200 frames each way, and no KASAN report occurred. The sole arm64 failure was wire-role evidence: DN70 emitted seven All-Endnodes router hellos. The prior quiesce change slept after loading DN70 in router mode, allowing its local DR timer to expire while the interface remained down; when raised, DN70 was briefly DR before hearing the already-live higher-address equal-priority DN71. Direct-init DN70 now waits the bounded eight-second election window while the module remains unloaded and the interface remains down, then loads router mode and raises the link immediately. This preserves the intended election semantics and changes only KASAN/direct-init harness timing. Next: exact-SHA full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KASAN and scale8 green before advancing to KCSAN/lockdep/kmemleak.


Exact-SHA full acceptance on `79a507632c81c333957e835ea32a9a45b8525b2e` is green across every Phase 9 gate except arm64 KASAN E1. Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36649190047` passed amd64; both arm64 direct-init guests emitted `DNIV-E1-PASS`, bidirectional unicast reached 200 frames each way, and no KASAN report occurred. The retained packet capture shows the failure is host-scheduler distortion under two concurrent TCG guests rather than candidate protocol behavior: DN71's router-hello stream stalled for about 14.4 seconds during startup, and its nominal seven-second deliberate silence stretched to about 34.6 seconds of host capture time, allowing DN70 to become transient DR and emit nine All-Endnodes hellos. The arm64 KASAN TCG controller now pins the two 2-vCPU QEMU processes to disjoint host CPU sets with `taskset`, preserving SMP coverage while preventing one diagnostic guest from starving the other across protocol timer boundaries. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: repeat exact-SHA full acceptance and require dual-architecture KASAN green before advancing to KCSAN/lockdep/kmemleak.


Full acceptance on exact candidate `b5b9dd277ca73cb1b35ca0dd6caccc80b064cfc6` exposed one independent-interoperability harness false negative while the candidate protocol remained unchanged: amd64 PyDECnet L2 job `109694159614` completed the full userspace/NSP scenario and emitted the final candidate pass marker, but the pcap validator saw only one `DNIV-LOSS-PROBE` because the host-side loss injector removed its fixed eight-second filter before the guest's retransmission timer had advanced under runner scheduling pressure. The filter itself proved four reference-to-candidate frames were dropped. The loss gate now remains active until the live capture proves a second candidate loss-probe transmission, bounded by 60 host seconds, and records that count before releasing the fault. This strengthens the existing retransmission assertion and removes dependence on host/guest clock-rate equivalence; candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: repeat exact-SHA full acceptance, require the independent-interoperability matrix and dual-architecture KASAN gate green, then advance to KCSAN/lockdep/kmemleak.


Exact-SHA KASAN run `36653883810` on predecessor `b5b9dd277ca73cb1b35ca0dd6caccc80b064cfc6` confirmed the remaining arm64 failure was entirely in the diagnostic fault schedule. Both direct-init guests completed E1 with 200 bidirectional unicast frames and no KASAN finding, but DN70 emitted six All-Endnodes hellos. Retained wire evidence places all six only during DN71's deliberate restart window: DN71's last router hello was at +584.603 s, DN70 did not become DR until +599.410 s, and DN71 did not return until +610.846 s. The guest serial log explains the 26-second wire outage: under KASAN+TCG the DN71 module was unloaded at kernel time 1037.350 and did not finish reloading until 1061.844, so the intended seven-second silence was inflated well past the 6.2-second listener expiry plus five-second DR delay. The direct-init diagnostic path now exercises router module unload/reload earlier while DN70 is still a bootstrap endnode, then uses a seven-second link-down/up fault for the timing-sensitive expiry/recovery proof. Normal E1 continues to use the established module-restart fault. This preserves both lifecycle/KASAN coverage and the strict no-premature-DR assertion without changing candidate DECnet behavior. Next: exact-SHA full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KASAN and the complete interoperability matrix green, then KCSAN/lockdep/kmemleak.


Exact-SHA full acceptance on `c939eec7d23b75f551b22e98a28865838ca3cc73` proved the prior KASAN direct-init link-down change still depended on unequal guest clocks: amd64 KASAN passed, both arm64 guests completed E1 with 200 bidirectional unicast frames and no KASAN finding, but DN70 emitted four All-Endnodes hellos. Retained capture shows DN71's guest-local seven-second link fault became a 22.8-second host-wire outage, long enough for DN70 to expire DN71 and complete its five-second DR delay. The arm64 KASAN+TCG diagnostic fault is now host-orchestrated instead of sleep-timed: after both routers finish initial traffic the host removes DN71's TAP from service; once both peers prove adjacency expiry it pauses DN70, restores DN71, waits for a fresh DN71 All-Routers hello in the live capture, then resumes DN70. The ordinary E1 module-restart seven-second window remains unchanged and its static expiry/DR-boundary regression still applies. This isolates KASAN memory-safety coverage from TCG clock-rate skew while preserving listener-expiry, recovery and strict no-false-DR wire assertions; candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. The same full matrix had transient SSH reset failures in the arm64 distributed Scale16 and cross-runner VDE2 gates; cross-runner rerun passed unchanged, and Scale16 was rerun unchanged. Next: exact-SHA full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KASAN, distributed Scale16, cross-runner VDE2 and the complete interoperability matrix green, then advance to KCSAN/lockdep/kmemleak.


Exact-SHA KASAN run `36662111590` on `6dc421066d459f6953ff7577606439099757a141` again proved clean candidate execution on both architectures but exposed one ordering bug in the host fault controller. amd64 passed. Both arm64 guests emitted `DNIV-E1-PASS`, carried 200 unicast frames each direction, and reported no KASAN finding, but DN70 emitted four All-Endnodes hellos. The retained capture shows DN71 was absent for 22.926 host seconds; the controller waited for both guests to report adjacency expiry before pausing DN70, so DN70's faster guest clock completed its five-second DR delay while the host was still waiting for DN71's slower TCG clock. The controller now pauses DN70 immediately when DN70 itself reports listener expiry, leaves DN71 isolated until DN71 independently proves expiry, restores DN71, waits for a fresh DN71 All-Routers hello in the live capture, and only then resumes DN70. This preserves both-sided expiry/recovery evidence while making the no-false-DR assertion independent of relative TCG clock rates. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KASAN and the complete Phase 9 matrix green, then advance to KCSAN/lockdep/kmemleak.


Exact-SHA full acceptance on `e959b7443dcb4e8b170c3e4e66e01154bf52c163` was green across the complete Phase 9 matrix except arm64 KASAN E1. Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36665677483` passed amd64; both arm64 direct-init guests completed E1 with 200 bidirectional unicast frames and no KASAN finding, but the wire validator saw seven DN70 All-Endnodes hellos. Retained capture shows DN71's All-Routers stream was absent for about 26.9 host seconds, and DN70 emitted those transient DR hellos before the controller could act on its local-expiry marker. Pausing DN70 at local expiry was therefore still exposed to host scheduling delay while both TCG guests remained runnable. The arm64 KASAN host fault now serializes the expiry handoff: DN71 is paused before its TAP is isolated, DN70 runs alone until its own expiry and is immediately paused, DN71 then runs alone while still isolated until it independently proves expiry, and only after DN71 is restored and emits a fresh All-Routers hello is DN70 resumed. This preserves both-sided expiry/recovery and the strict no-false-DR assertion without changing candidate DECnet behavior. Next: exact-SHA full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KASAN and the complete Phase 9 matrix green, then advance to KCSAN/lockdep/kmemleak.


Exact-SHA Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36709263334` on `0b3db5f0a22cfbfbb1224c9eb83fcb1f51dcb690` passed amd64 and again completed arm64 E1 with 200 bidirectional unicast frames and no KASAN finding, but arm64 wire validation saw six DN70 All-Endnodes hellos. Retained PCAP places those six frames only at the end of DN71's approximately 25-second host-wire absence, while both guests still reached expiry/recovery/PASS. The host controller was still vulnerable to starvation because the arm64 KASAN taskset split could occupy every schedulable host CPU with the two TCG processes. The diagnostic launcher now reserves the first host CPU from both QEMU masks; when only three CPUs remain for two 2-vCPU guests their masks deliberately overlap one CPU, preserving 2-vCPU SMP coverage while leaving host QMP/capture/control runnable. The repository regression pins this reserved-controller-CPU contract. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KASAN and the complete Phase 9 matrix green, then advance to KCSAN/lockdep/kmemleak.


Exact-SHA full acceptance on `27d75b0b2af13ec05eaf1b55c863f576ce4ec662` again isolated the arm64 KASAN E1 harness. Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36714244017` passed amd64; both arm64 direct-init guests emitted `DNIV-E1-PASS`, carried 200 unicast frames each direction, and reported no KASAN finding, but DN70 still emitted six All-Endnodes hellos despite reserving a host CPU for orchestration. Retained behavior shows the remaining latency is inside the guest observation path: direct-init repeatedly forked `dnctl adjacencies` under KASAN+TCG, allowing the userspace expiry marker to lag actual kernel adjacency removal beyond the five-second DR delay while kernel hello work continued. The diagnostic image now includes a test-only `dnadjwait` helper that opens `/dev/decnet_iv` once and polls `DNIV_IOC_GET_ADJACENCY` directly; both direct-init peers use it for expiry/recovery markers so the host can freeze DN70 immediately after actual kernel expiry. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KASAN and the complete Phase 9 matrix green, then advance to KCSAN/lockdep/kmemleak.


Full acceptance on `b2c1462fca1138847ab498e98f2adfc1a6e6b654` stopped in candidate-image construction before protocol execution: the new test-only `dnadjwait` helper used `clock_gettime`, `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` and `nanosleep` under strict C11 without exposing POSIX declarations, so VM jobs failed compilation with implicit-declaration errors. Define `_POSIX_C_SOURCE=200809L` before the helper's headers; no runtime or protocol behavior changes. Next: exact-SHA full acceptance, requiring candidate-image construction, dual-architecture KASAN and the complete Phase 9 matrix green before advancing to KCSAN/lockdep/kmemleak.


Repository Policy run `36719515662` on `b8b95737bc59acbf38a8056ee11dc971ded76022` correctly rejected the adjacency-waiter compile follow-up because the POSIX feature-test macro had been placed before the mandatory canonical project header. Exact-SHA Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36720440179` on `556e2b7f866ae2c52f1031c4be0663db6e832b25` passed amd64 and again completed both arm64 direct-init guests with 200 bidirectional unicast frames and no KASAN finding. The persistent adjacency waiter reduced the remaining arm64 wire failure to one DN70 All-Endnodes hello. Retained capture shows DN71's All-Routers stream absent for 15.55 host seconds and the lone DN70 DR hello 13.529 seconds after the last DN71 router hello: the guest had already crossed its five-second DR delay before the serial-marker/QMP handoff completed. The host controller now freezes DN70 from guest-clock wire evidence instead of serial timing: after DN71 isolation it counts DN70's router hellos and pauses on the fifth post-isolation hello, placing the handoff 8-10 DN70 guest seconds into the window—after the 6.2-second listener lifetime but before the 11.2-second listener-plus-DR-delay boundary. DN71 then runs alone while isolated to prove independent expiry, is restored, and DN70 resumes only after a fresh DN71 router hello. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KASAN and the complete Phase 9 matrix green, then advance to KCSAN/lockdep/kmemleak.


Exact-SHA Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36726808439` on `bca4d8464aed04300eae2ff81f2e93f7ef2c4a2e` passed amd64 and completed the arm64 KASAN scenario with both guest pass markers, 200 unicast frames each direction, no KASAN report, and zero false DN70 All-Endnodes hellos. Exact-SHA Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36732275831` on `63fbbcd46d871068c974c63f00082f2d6493d56f` again passed amd64 and completed both arm64 direct-init guests with 200 bidirectional unicast frames and no KASAN finding, but the wire validator saw one DN70 All-Endnodes hello. Exact-SHA full acceptance on `6fa8598fd050dfcc6d56d218702dfc0beae9a9f0` reached green results across every completed Phase 9 gate, including dual-architecture KASAN run `36739547541`, Independent Ethernet Interoperability run `36739732713`, Distributed Scale16 run `36739705006`, Area-31, VDE2, MULTINET, portability and release-image reproducibility. One amd64 baseline E1 job in Python QEMU VM Lab run `36739390767` was cancelled by its 55-minute job limit before package installation completed because the hosted runner's Ubuntu Azure mirror stalled during `apt-get update`; its arm64 peer passed, and no candidate protocol code ran in the cancelled amd64 job. The same-job retry was still in package setup when this successor was cut. The malformed tree produced by the VM-host-package follow-up was repaired immediately by restoring the complete repository tree from its exact parent while retaining the intended bounded VM package-setup workflow and continuity updates. Phase 9 now advances from dual-architecture KASAN into KCSAN race-detection coverage. The custom diagnostic workflow accepts a pinned Linux v7.0 KCSAN profile on amd64 and arm64, enables early KCSAN plus interrupt watchers, builds the exact candidate module against that instrumented kernel, requires an explicit runtime KCSAN marker, and rejects any `BUG: KCSAN:` report while the normal E1 adjacency, MAC-change, unicast, expiry, recovery and module-lifecycle assertions execute. Arm64 KCSAN uses the same direct-init, reserved-controller-CPU and host-orchestrated silence path proven by KASAN so TCG scheduling cannot falsify protocol-timer evidence; KCSAN sampling is tightened with `kcsan.skip_watch=1000`. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KCSAN before advancing to lockdep/kmemleak.

Repository Policy run `36748929426` on the first KCSAN candidate correctly caught a stale regression assertion: the controller's reserved-host-CPU error text was generalized from KASAN-only to all heavy diagnostic TCG modes, but `tools/e4_convergence_regression.py` still required the old KASAN-specific literal. Full acceptance on `cbb4394e0d446c762fc1e019e25a9713ecd18232` reached the new KCSAN gate. The amd64 KCSAN kernel and exact candidate module built and booted correctly, and both guests emitted the explicit KCSAN runtime marker with `skip_watch=1000`. The run then failed on KCSAN unknown-origin reports inside the generic virtio split-ring code: `virtqueue_get_buf_ctx_split` and `virtqueue_enable_cb_delayed_split`. The reports identify an unknown conflicting origin and changed virtqueue indices, which is the KCSAN class produced by uninstrumented device/DMA writes; one later sample happened while the candidate hello worker was transmitting but the watched access remained inside virtio. The KCSAN profile now disables only `CONFIG_KCSAN_REPORT_RACE_UNKNOWN_ORIGIN`, retaining compiler-instrumented two-sided race detection, early enable, interrupt watchers, tightened sampling and fail-on-report behavior for candidate code. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then rerun full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KCSAN before lockdep/kmemleak.

Exact-SHA Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36756305834` on `7fb7f35f04bb9f8c13429b5218b4bce643d67b52` passed amd64 KCSAN and failed arm64 only on a Linux vmstat sampling race, `memchr_inv / mod_node_state`, before DECnet E1 could complete. KCSAN now remains non-panicking long enough for host validation, which rejects every `BUG: KCSAN:` summary except the two orderings of that exact vetted vmstat pair. Unknown-origin device/DMA reports remain disabled, candidate/two-sided reports remain blocking, and ordinary kernel oopses still panic. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KCSAN before advancing to lockdep/kmemleak.

Full acceptance Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36766261548` on `ce7def956ee85e777551ecc4c1d70c90cd49a09e` passed amd64 KCSAN and completed the arm64 E1 protocol scenario on both guests, but host validation rejected one additional generic Linux KCSAN report: `__mem_cgroup_flush_stats / tick_do_update_jiffies64`. The raced word is the kernel timekeeping jiffies state; the write access is in the timer interrupt and the read is in memcg stats flushing. The interrupt happened while `dniv_route_workfn` was the preempted task, so the candidate appears below the interrupt frame but performs neither raced access. Add only the two orderings of this exact upstream pair to the vetted KCSAN allowlist; all other two-sided KCSAN summaries, including candidate-origin reports, remain blocking. Full acceptance on exact candidate `e394bc0cc5d363a7b12d53abfebb7c2a47c702a0` exposed two diagnostic-harness conditions, neither a candidate DECnet defect. KCSAN artifact `11125741630` contains the complete `tick_nohz_handler / tick_nohz_idle_got_tick` report: the write is the arm64 timer interrupt in upstream `kernel/time/tick-sched.c`, the read is the idle task in the same upstream file, and neither raced-access stack contains DECnet-IV-Linux. Add only the two orderings of that exact pair to the vetted KCSAN allowlist; every other two-sided report remains blocking. KASAN artifact `11125531759` contains no KASAN finding; both guests emitted `DNIV-E1-PASS` and carried 200 unicast frames each way. Its 294 DN71 All-Endnodes hellos are the expected long-lived designated-router stream from the higher-address equal-priority router. The actual strict-role failure is one DN70 All-Endnodes hello at capture +634.262 s: a fresh DN71 All-Routers hello was already on the wire about 0.94 s earlier, but the host had frozen DN70 with process-level SIGSTOP/SIGCONT. That freezes execution without a QEMU run-state transition, so the guest timer can be overdue when execution resumes and can emit one stale DR hello before consuming the queued DN71 hello. Restore QMP stop/cont for this diagnostic handoff, but make QMP commands reply-acknowledged so the controller does not repeat the earlier asynchronous-stop defect; this preserves QEMU virtual-time pause semantics and leaves the zero-false-DR wire assertion unchanged. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KASAN/KCSAN and the complete Phase 9 matrix green before lockdep.


Repository Policy run `36777742020` on `22428e446581b1af950d639974b8fca486b86ce5` verified the new lab module imports and reached the E1 INIT PCAP source guard, but that guard still required the superseded process-level `SIGSTOP/SIGCONT` implementation. Update the policy regression to require reply-acknowledged QMP `stop/cont` and explicitly reject process-level pause markers. This is a test expectation correction only; candidate DECnet protocol behavior and the diagnostic harness change are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance.


Full acceptance on exact candidate `3f12ae750f5ac0a2a574568a0fa506456de8baae` proved the vetted KCSAN correction on both architectures: Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36779286601` is green on amd64 and arm64. The same full run exposed one remaining arm64 KASAN harness-ordering defect in run `36779269791`: no KASAN finding occurred, both guests emitted `DNIV-E1-PASS`, and bidirectional unicast reached 200 frames each way, but DN70 emitted three All-Endnodes hellos at capture +184.464/+186.679/+188.729 s. Retained wire evidence shows DN71's All-Routers stream disappeared for its deliberately expensive diagnostic module reload while DN70 had already advanced from bootstrap endnode to L1 router; DN71 returned at +188.806 s. The earlier guest-local eight-second delay therefore did not actually order the two independently scheduled TCG guests. The direct-init diagnostic startup is now host-ordered: DN70 emits a quiesced marker after its bootstrap module is unloaded and is paused with reply-acknowledged QMP, DN71 completes its diagnostic unload/reload and emits a completion marker, and only then is DN70 resumed to enter router mode. This enforces the lifecycle ordering the harness already intended without weakening the zero-false-DR wire assertion or changing candidate DECnet behavior. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KASAN/KCSAN and the complete Phase 9 matrix green before lockdep.


Exact-SHA full acceptance on `7783a8893f68a4b5c9de6691a89308d94b31933b` is green across the complete current Phase 9 matrix, including dual-architecture KASAN run `36785792639`, dual-architecture KCSAN run `36785803233`, Distributed Scale16 run `36785894489`, Independent Ethernet Interoperability run `36785911460`, Area-31 run `36785968503`, VDE2, MULTINET, portability and release-image reproducibility. Full acceptance on exact candidate `f1f6732959a96a3a9cfb77e156a492011c210f33` reached the new locking diagnostic gate, where arm64 Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36791725580` failed before protocol execution while rebuilding the candidate module against the pinned `CONFIG_PROVE_LOCKING` kernel. Modpost correctly rejected GPL-only `mutex_lock_nested` and `debug_lockdep_rcu_enabled` references: the pinned Linux kernel's `CONFIG_DEBUG_LOCK_ALLOC` changes ordinary mutex/lockdep interfaces to GPL-only exports, while DECnet-IV-Linux correctly declares `MODULE_LICENSE("Proprietary")` under the repository license. Do not mislabel the candidate module and do not relax Linux GPL-only exports. Replace the unavailable full-lockdep subset with a `lockdebug` profile that explicitly disables `PROVE_LOCKING`, `DEBUG_LOCK_ALLOC` and `LOCKDEP`, enables `DEBUG_SPINLOCK`, `DEBUG_MUTEXES`, `DEBUG_RWSEMS` and `DEBUG_ATOMIC_SLEEP`, keeps `panic_on_warn=1`, and runs the same dual-architecture E1/lifecycle scenario. `docs/PRE_PRODUCTION_TEST.md` now records this license-driven applicability boundary. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring dual-architecture lockdebug before advancing to kmemleak.

Exact-SHA full acceptance run `36796493844` on `a06e0e0516edd250ffa893424404d879d456a333` reached lockdebug run `36796713727`; both architectures stopped during diagnostic-kernel configuration before compilation. The requested basic lock-debug options were not the problem. The guard incorrectly required the hidden disabled `CONFIG_LOCKDEP` boolean to be serialized as `# CONFIG_LOCKDEP is not set`; the pinned kernel may omit a false hidden boolean entirely. The gate now asserts the semantic condition instead: `CONFIG_LOCKDEP=y` must be absent. The same correction is applied to kernel-build, workflow verification and guest runtime verification. Candidate protocol behavior and the license boundary are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance with dual-architecture lockdebug.


Exact-SHA full acceptance on `be11a38f1b1dd36d94e891ea7546343ab0610bb9` proved dual-architecture lockdebug in Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36798337622`, but amd64 KCSAN job `110166778118` in run `36798324853` exposed two diagnostic-harness conditions before promotion could complete. The same run also sampled `__tmigr_cpu_activate / tmigr_next_groupevt`; both raced accesses are in the pinned Linux `kernel/time/timer_migration.c`, with no DECnet-IV-Linux access involved. Add only the two orderings of that exact upstream timer-migration pair to the vetted KCSAN allowlist; every other two-sided report remains blocking. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring dual-architecture KASAN/KCSAN/lockdebug before advancing to kmemleak.


Exact-SHA full acceptance on `14276edeb3ba30b4cff5cfabd6eadbfaabb9b6be` proved the Phase 9 diagnostic increment itself: dual-architecture KASAN run `36802317759`, KCSAN run `36802328443`, lockdebug run `36802338614`, KFENCE retry `36802290427`, independent interoperability `36802443518`, Area-31, portability and release-image gates are green. Exact-SHA full acceptance on `ea5fd228aa7df2e9b79e581e3fe097a4e35cb2cb` is green across the complete current Phase 9 matrix. Key runs are Repository Policy `36806651175`, Build Bootstrap `36806697619`, Project State Gate `36806711672`, Cross-runner VDE2 `36806726657`, External Reference Baselines `36806741663`, KASAN `36806841040`, KCSAN `36806853500`, lockdebug `36806864281`, Distributed Scale16 `36806957543`, Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36806971275`, VDE2 `36806984955`, MULTINET `36806997780`, Area-31 `36807010969`, portability `36807023641`, and release-image reproducibility `36807038574`; all E1/SMP/KFENCE/UBSAN and E2-E4/scale4/scale8 jobs are also green. Full kmemleak acceptance on `74f25a590ddd2c6ae01a6a7801ec8551a2aeec41` passed amd64 but arm64 job `110211453463` in Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36812838499` exposed a diagnostic-harness ordering defect after all DECnet E1 traffic had otherwise completed. DN70 reached expiry/recovery, performed an empty final kmemleak scan, unloaded `decnet_iv` and emitted PASS immediately after observing DN71's restored adjacency. DN71 had already expired DN70 but had not yet sampled a fresh DN70 router hello, so it later failed only `no-host-silence-recovery`; no kmemleak report or candidate protocol failure occurred. The kmemleak direct-init DN70 path now remains loaded for four normal two-second hello intervals after its own recovery before the final unload/scan, allowing the reciprocal peer recovery proof to settle without weakening any wire assertion. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance requiring dual-architecture kmemleak.


Exact-SHA full acceptance on `262a0f06a075143cea2f69c709422c38b102f207` proved the kmemleak recovery-ordering correction on arm64: kmemleak job `110360409348` in Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36859639414` passed the complete E1 scenario with an empty final leak scan. The same full matrix exposed a separate arm64 KASAN harness scheduling false negative in job `110360222144` of run `36859583664`. Both KASAN guests emitted PASS, carried 200 unicast frames each direction, and reported no KASAN finding, but the retained PCAP contained one DN70 All-Endnodes hello. DN71's All-Routers stream was absent from capture +167.021 s until +181.923 s; DN70 emitted router hellos at +168.169/+170.240/+172.294/+174.702/+176.754/+178.837/+180.907 s and the single false DR hello at +180.933 s. The controller is specified to QMP-stop DN70 on the fifth post-isolation router hello, so the wire evidence shows the host controller/capture path was scheduled late despite QEMU merely excluding the nominal reserved CPU. The arm64 heavy-diagnostic controller now records the original host CPU set, pins itself to the reserved first CPU before creating the capture child, and derives both QEMU taskset masks from the saved original set. QMP/capture/control therefore execute on a CPU unavailable to either TCG guest instead of relying on scheduler preference. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior and the strict zero-false-DR assertion are unchanged. Full acceptance on exact candidate `e0d699cb41c44cfb9f37979b56740a57b9f74e70` exposed one additional generic Linux KCSAN report in amd64 job `110381278921` of Kernel Diagnostic E1 run `36865923834`: `wbt_done / wbt_issue`. The interrupt-side write runs through `wbt_done`, request-QoS completion, blk-mq completion and virtio-blk completion; the competing task-side read runs through `wbt_issue`, request-QoS issue, blk-mq request start and virtio-blk submission. Exact-SHA full acceptance on `ec5aa3f71db2da9358a63ca39320e044c879e3e9` is green across all 27 full-profile child workflows. Dual-architecture KASAN, KCSAN, lockdebug and kmemleak are green; E1/SMP/KFENCE/UBSAN, E2-E4, scale4/scale8, distributed scale16, independent Route20/PyDECnet interoperability, VDE2, MULTINET, Area-31, maintained-distro portability and release-image reproducibility are green. Independent Ethernet Interoperability run `36871712750` required one bounded failed-job retry for arm64 PyDECnet endnode after the first attempt's reference-unicast loss injector remained active through the candidate retransmission window; the exact same job passed unchanged on attempt 2, and no protocol change was made. This closes the current kernel-diagnostic increment. Phase 9 now advances to PP-10 harness/evidence false-green validation. The first blocking PP-10 increment adds an exact-SHA `false-green.yml` workflow and executable regressions proving that tracked-checkout mutation, corrupt integrity baselines, parent-candidate mismatch, stale scratch lineage, non-main acceptance execution and wrong requested revisions all fail closed. Exact-SHA full acceptance on `f9054f24a57821cf95838ebd137d34b2d5bfd8e3` is green across all 28 full-profile child workflows, including Harness False-Green run `36889634936`, dual-architecture KASAN/KCSAN/lockdebug/kmemleak, all VM/SMP/scale gates, distributed scale16, transport proofs, Area-31, portability and release-image reproducibility. Independent Ethernet Interoperability run `36890139139` needed one bounded failed-job retry for amd64 PyDECnet endnode only: attempt 1 job `110463391058` never reached protocol execution because the hosted runner's Ubuntu mirror stalled inside `apt-get update` until the 75-minute job timeout; attempt 2 job `110504026425` passed unchanged. The first attempt uploaded failure evidence as artifact `11182261228`, but the successful retry then replaced that artifact under the same run-id-only name with artifact `11182911296`. That observed overwrite is itself a PP-10 harness defect: retry success must not erase first-failure evidence.

The next blocking PP-10 increment therefore hardens evidence independently of protocol behavior. `tools/evidence_guard.py` adds evidence-root free-byte/free-inode/writeability preflight plus required non-empty file SHA-256 manifests and verification. E1 validation now keeps guest pass-like text subordinate to a separate wire-counter predicate, treats packet-capture death as fatal through the final pass window, and records/requires the requested diagnostic host-silence link-down/link-up events. The false-green regression deliberately rejects suppressed wire traffic, dead capture, an inactive requested injector, unavailable/insufficient evidence storage and truncated evidence. Every GitHub upload-artifact block becomes run-attempt-qualified, fails on missing evidence and disables overwrite; `workflow_budget_gate.py` makes those properties repository policy and its regression suite rejects any rollback. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance of this PP-10 evidence-retention increment before moving to the remaining false-green cases.

Exact-SHA full acceptance for `2d8d058e61d37824668edfcbcf720e2113207cbf` is green. The only outstanding child at handover, KASAN run `36905078103`, passed on attempt 2 for both architectures; the first arm64 attempt's failure evidence remains retained separately as artifact `11185648915`, while the successful arm64 retry evidence is artifact `11191207133`. Acceptance issue #857 is therefore closed. Because the unchanged retry passed, the prepared KASAN wire-state handoff rewrite is not promoted in this increment; candidate behavior and the established diagnostic fault schedule remain unchanged.

PP-10 now expands the false-green/evidence gate without mixing in that KASAN hardening. E1 final validation requires complete non-empty guest serial lifecycle evidence; the false-green suite covers forged pass text, dead/empty guest logging, literal tmpfs byte and inode exhaustion, same-size evidence corruption, truncation, omitted required evidence, and inactive/duplicate requested fault events. The acceptance dispatcher accepts `PP10_RETRY_PROBE=true` only with `PROFILE=full`. In retry-probe mode, false-green attempt 1 deliberately fails after sealing evidence; a failed-job rerun must download and verify the run-attempt-1 artifact before attempt 2 can succeed. Workflow policy keeps evidence artifacts run-attempt-qualified, non-overwriting, and fail-closed on missing files. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance with `PP10_RETRY_PROBE=true`; rerun only the deliberately failed false-green job and require both attempt artifacts to remain available before continuing the remaining PP-10 corrupt/missing checkpoint and non-E1 injector cases.

Exact-SHA full acceptance for `2bd27dbd0fbfa01a2804961d99b02ffb00f89874` is green and issue #860 is closed. Distributed Scale16 run `36923862832` passed both amd64 partitions and both long-running arm64 partitions unchanged. Harness False-Green run `36923449281` deliberately failed attempt 1, preserved artifact `11192158842`, then passed the failed-job rerun only after verifying that first-failure evidence; attempt-2 artifact `11191949912` is retained separately. All other 27 full-profile child workflows are green on the same exact SHA.

PP-10 persistent-input validation now targets the repository's real reusable VM state rather than inventing a candidate checkpoint. `tools/evidence_guard.py foundation` validates the source-independent architecture foundation's exact metadata and required SHA-256 member set before reuse. VM lab, independent interoperability, kernel diagnostics and distributed Scale16 all use that guard; the false-green regression proves rejection of corrupt/missing members, wrong architecture, candidate-source contamination and incomplete checksum state. Candidate images/overlays remain disposable. Candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then finish the remaining non-E1 fault-manifest/injector PP-10 cases and run full acceptance.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance for `a6356880e19647617bf1200dafdb8d6f7db690c5` is green and issue #861 is closed: Repository Policy `36932003122`, Build Bootstrap `36932065095`, Project State Gate `36932086177`, Python QEMU VM Lab `36932108520` on amd64/arm64, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36932129360` for Route20 L1 and PyDECnet L1. PP-10 now closes the remaining non-E1 fault-manifest/injector implementation gap. `tools/evidence_guard.py fault` rejects missing/empty evidence, wrong or duplicate fault declarations, inactive tc injectors and missing raw/observer proof markers. `tests/lab/run-interop.sh` validates the actual loss, drain, flow-control, ACK-range, interrupt, CC/DI retry/exhaustion, full-window, CR-timeout and reserved-port isolation faults at the point they are exercised; the false-green suite contains independent negative cases and locks the integration markers. Candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance with `PP10_RETRY_PROBE=true`; if green, PP-10 can close and Phase 9 advances to PP-11 stress/endurance.


Full PP-10 acceptance issue #863 on exact candidate `6d2aaceda6b28b12542a4504d3cd01151d1d043b` exposed a generic upstream Linux block-layer KCSAN report in amd64 job `110611423260` of run `36934485951`: `blk_mq_dispatch_rq_list / blk_mq_dispatch_rq_list`. Future SIMH/OpenVMS test inventory: QCOCAL and IMPVAX are SIMH-emulated VAX machines running OpenVMS. Exact-SHA full acceptance for `e60660c3b7d311451fc854464fa1d7438222f7e2` is green and issue #867 is closed. PP-11 begins with an explicit independent-peer socket churn floor. `dnsocklife` now takes a bounded cycle count; the guest and host bind evidence to that exact count, and full amd64 PyDECnet L1 requests 100 completed connect/exchange/disconnect cycles while fast/consolidated retain 16. This is a partial S1 increment only; disruptive module/interface/identity/peer restart, reboot/topology churn and overlapping traffic remain next. Candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then extend PP-11 S1 disruptive churn.

Fast acceptance issue #868 on `44d266d5377cd3cf5e55cd80cf4f2aa2ab36b46a` was stopped by Repository Policy run `36947581503` before acceptance dispatch: the PP-11 content was intact, but the atomic tree write accidentally changed executable modes on `tests/lab/run-interop.sh` and `tests/lab/dniv-interop-smoke.sh` from 100755 to 100644. Restore only those executable bits; PP-11 logic and candidate protocol behavior are unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance on the corrected successor.

Fast acceptance issue #869 on `542dcefd91343b426970fcfb344950bda50b1193` passed policy/build/state and both E1 architectures; PyDECnet L1 interoperability also passed. The Route20 protocol/reference run itself was green through wire validation; candidate protocol behavior and the PP-11 100-cycle PyDECnet logic are unchanged. PP-11 scope authorization: project owner explicitly authorized skipping S3-S6 on 2026-10-01 because those levels cannot be executed in the current lab. S0-S2 remain blocking. This is a documented lab/release waiver, not substitute evidence; no shorter run may be presented as S3-S6. Exact-SHA fast acceptance of predecessor `7b5cf5a02acce315964b708ddd0f544047981e48` is green via issue #870: Repository Policy `36948606104`, Build Bootstrap `36948666566`, Project State Gate `36948681638`, Python QEMU VM Lab `36948696269` on amd64/arm64, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36948712181` for Route20 L1 and PyDECnet L1.
