PP-12 remote-safety authorization: project owner explicitly forbids any power-cycle, shutdown, reboot, pause/resume, hard-stop or other running-state change for machines reached beyond PYRTR (31.3) over the MULTINET/HECnet uplink. Those remote systems are observation/traffic peers only. Disruptive PP-12 restart/power tests must remain on project-controlled local/SIMH systems, including QCOCAL/IMPVAX where applicable. This boundary is mandatory and supersedes any generic PP-12 text that might otherwise imply remote peer reboot/hard-stop testing.

PP-13 first-release authorization: project owner explicitly authorized skipping PP-13 completely on 2026-10-01. No production version has yet been released, so there is no real N-1 version or mixed-version population to test. PP-13 is therefore not applicable and non-blocking for the first production release. Do not invent synthetic downgrade/rollback/mixed-version evidence; re-enable PP-13 once a real N-1 production release exists.

Full acceptance issue #875 on predecessor `081f2ef9cb219a143ad15e18a34ad1a4ea376951` is not promotable. KCSAN run `36954406524` amd64 job `110674076971` reported `tmigr_cpu_deactivate / tmigr_next_groupevt`; retained artifact `11206077517` shows the race in the pinned Linux timer-migration path and the candidate E1 traffic itself continued to PASS. Upstream syzkaller has independently reported the same timer-migration `cpuevt.ignore` access family in `__tmigr_cpu_deactivate` versus `tmigr_next_groupevt`; this successor filters only the exact observed summary and its reverse ordering. Portability run `36954591057` also had an unrelated Debian 13 amd64 GCC infrastructure failure before compilation because the Debian mirror was mid-sync (`libpipeline1` size/hash mismatch); artifact `11205828708` retains that evidence. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged.

Owner constraints are now consolidated and durable: `docs/PP_EVIDENCE.md` is the canonical PP proof index; PP-11 S3-S6 remain skipped; PP-13 remains skipped for the first release; from `## Timing/scheduler tests` onward only practically executable lab work is attempted, remote active tests are limited to user-mode C/C++, and impossible requirements are recorded exactly as `Not tested in lab environment`. MIM `1.13`, reachable through PYRTR `31.3`, is a stricter read-only DECnet resource source only: no remote writes, execution, load/stress/fault testing or state changes. Local lab hosts may build multiple pinned-SIMH RSX-11M-PLUS V4.6 instances from resources copied read-only from MIM, with every acquisition/hash/install/configuration step retained. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance on this successor, then resume PP-11 S1/S2 only.

Exact-SHA fast acceptance of `ec25ff975628886bdfc410fb9b73813c3f6c6c4c` is green via issue #876: Repository Policy `36956593473`, Build Bootstrap `36956656716` on amd64/arm64, Project State Gate `36956671877`, Python QEMU VM Lab `36956687408` on amd64/arm64, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `36956703038` for Route20 L1 and PyDECnet L1. This validates the canonical PP evidence ledger, consolidated owner constraints and the narrowly vetted Linux timer-migration KCSAN summary without changing candidate DECnet protocol behavior.

PP-11 S1 disruptive churn now has a dedicated exact-candidate two-VM gate. Full acceptance dispatches `vm-lab.yml` in `pp11s1` mode on amd64 and arm64 with two vCPUs. Per architecture the gate executes exactly 100 counted cycles: 10 module reloads, 20 interface cycles, 20 runtime identity cycles, 20 peer-restart cycles observed by the opposite VM, 20 host topology detach/reattach cycles and 10 QMP reset/reboot cycles. Raw DECnet and MIRROR traffic overlap the churn; every cycle must recover MIRROR service. Retained evidence includes cycle/fault timeline, PCAP, complete serial logs, host RSS/thread/CPU samples, guest link/adjacency/route/memory/slab snapshots, p50/p95/p99 latency and payload throughput with predeclared bounds. This is the disruptive-cycle increment only; S1 remains open for repeated sequence-wrap/table/queue/malformed-control pressure. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then execute the new S1 full-only gate and continue the remaining S1 pressure coverage.

Full acceptance issue #878 on exact candidate `73e96f38f73cff067769353fa2bb998cb78b563d` left every existing full-profile child green except the new PP-11 S1 disruptive-churn gate. Python QEMU VM Lab run `36959665140` failed on both architectures only in the new harness: amd64 job `110690334636` (artifact `11208856214`) recovered adjacency after the first module reload and continued recording successful background MIRROR calls, but a simultaneous single-shot foreground MIRROR probe raced that traffic and emitted `pp11-post-cycle-mirror`; arm64 job `110690334421` (artifact `11208986490`) passed two complete module cycles and failed the third the same way. The successor removes that self-contention by making each cycle wait for a fresh success from the continuously offered MIRROR stream after restoration, and the host controller now aborts immediately on a guest `DNIV-LAB-FAIL` marker instead of consuming the whole S1 timeout. This is a PP-11 harness correction only; candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then repeat full acceptance and require both amd64 and arm64 PP-11 S1 100-cycle evidence green before adding the remaining sequence/table/queue/malformed pressure.


Full issue #880 and scheduled PP-11 S1 revalidation of `a0a20d942a15f7cb51044489d6eedf93f8823602` establish a protocol-side NSP link-slot leak. Run `36997320356` failed amd64 after DN71 accumulated 205 links and arm64 after DN70 interface churn reached 41 links; scheduled run `36999699012` repeated the class with arm64 at 43 links and amd64 DN71 at 138 links. All four retained PCAPs continue router hellos, peer unicast and PP-11 raw payloads through the terminal interval, excluding a dead adjacency/wire path.

Root cause is successful active close: `dniv_sock_release()` sends DI from RUN and drops its socket reference, but the NSP entry remains through DI/CLOSED with no socket owner left to release it. The correction marks successful closing links detached, recycles detached links on DI/DC terminal completion or retransmission timeout, and immediately recycles the close/DC race when detach finds CLOSED. Attached sockets retain terminal EOF/disconnect-data semantics. PP-11 S1 also gains a peak-link bound of 32 in addition to the existing final-link bound of 8.

Scheduled KCSAN run `36999503334` arm64 reported `memchr_inv / refresh_cpu_vm_stats`; artifact `11225005387` shows both raced accesses entirely in generic Linux vmstat workers. Pinned upstream `torvalds/linux` `ce1e0223d8ad4211275c82a17ed6d43ab81e13d9` contains the same `need_update()` memchr scan racing the per-CPU vmstat flush and explicitly states that non-precise vmstat users tolerate imprecision because regular flushing may occur at arbitrary time. Only the two exact orderings are added to the vetted KCSAN filter. Scheduled Scale16 run `36999675158` completed green on all four amd64/arm64 partitions. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then full acceptance; PP-11 S1 remains blocking until green evidence closes it.


Full acceptance issue #882 on exact candidate `b01b50fd1f33250fa7c18cae57e24317967d4559` completed with three failing children and all remaining full-profile children green. PP-11 S1 run `37017940457` proves the detached-link protocol correction itself: during all completed guest-local churn, amd64 peak NSP links stayed at 5/5 and arm64 at 5/6, with every guest completing all 35 module/interface/identity/peer-restart cycles and adjacency restored. This is the intended bounded behavior and removes the prior 41-205-link monotonic growth. The run still failed for two harness reasons. amd64 job `110873367775` (artifact `11234426233`, SHA-256 `e8ec857e3e0cf9ba412fd33ddbcdc4e46987c1a72a1cffdc2e8f3614f6a75dea`) let role B begin its reciprocal actor three seconds after observing role A's 35th UP transition, before role A had entered its observer; DN70 therefore saw only 34 of DN71's 35 disruptions. The handoff now leaves a 25-second bounded phase gap after role B's observer, covering the final recovery/probe/resource/tail path without lowering the required 35-transition proof. arm64 job `110873367262` (artifact `11234516392`, SHA-256 `32b5ba6be7fa07d04a28bfabbc70f7aebf4a5341b57a7756b8cc6cf1a93ed95a`) reached HOST-READY on both guests and passed all 20 topology cycles, then DN70 exited on the first QMP `system_reset`. The shared QEMU command still had `-no-reboot`; that flag is now omitted only for `pp11s1`, whose declared test explicitly requires ten QMP reset/reboot cycles. Other lab modes retain `-no-reboot`.

KCSAN run `37017706629` failed only amd64 job `110872585255` with exact summary `mem_cgroup_wb_stats / tick_do_update_jiffies64`; artifact `11232847830`, SHA-256 `3015063da09ba917d845e1e3ca966a65b03d0f478160d70b86ba218f7e36704a`. Pinned upstream Linux `ce1e0223d8ad4211275c82a17ed6d43ab81e13d9` has `mem_cgroup_wb_stats() -> mem_cgroup_flush_stats_ratelimited()`, which reads `jiffies_64` directly, while the reported writer is the generic tick path. Upstream commit `ca1f4a5ecab084af7f405baa902edbed171b57e6` documents the same `tick_do_update_jiffies64` writer racing another direct `jiffies_64` reader and states that `jiffies` shares the address but its volatile type prevents the warning. The race is independent of DECnet-IV-Linux; add only the exact observed summary and reverse ordering to the vetted filter and regression marker set.

KASAN run `37017683435` passed amd64 and produced no KASAN finding on arm64, but arm64 job `110872505018` failed strict E1 wire validation because `endnodesA=1`; artifact `11234056167`, SHA-256 `7f0d6e6dd69109bc56045653c1f78f5d0f1f95410e9048e3be0baf022af8e168`. The retained PCAP identifies a harness timing miss, not a candidate role error: after DN71 isolation, DN70's first All-Routers hello that omitted DN71 appeared at 14:34:39.257 UTC, directly proving listener expiry, while the lone DN70 All-Endnodes DR hello did not appear until 14:34:45.704 UTC. The old controller waited for a fixed count of five post-isolation router hellos and acted too late under instrumented TCG. It now freezes DN70 on the first fresh peer-unlisted router hello, a semantic wire transition that preserves the full DR-delay margin; the strict final `endnodesA == 0` assertion remains unchanged.

These are harness/diagnostic-filter corrections around the executed detached-link protocol fix; no additional candidate protocol behavior is changed. PP-11 S1 remains open even after this disruptive gate is green because `docs/PRE_PRODUCTION_TEST.md` still requires repeated sequence-wrap, table/queue-limit and malformed/control-flood pressure under valid traffic before S1 closure. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, fresh full acceptance including dual-architecture PP-11 S1/KASAN/KCSAN, then implement and execute the remaining S1 pressure coverage before PP-11 S2.


Full acceptance issue #884 on exact candidate `28073997370bd62f4e15a56d3ab4adadbb4c5d72` completed with three failing child workflows; every other full-profile child passed, including distributed Scale16, independent interoperability, Area-31, portability and release-image reproducibility. PP-11 S1 run `37029470552` no longer shows the predecessor protocol leak. amd64 job `110912380527` (artifact `11240550142`, SHA-256 `17207193432513db431718b9b2712aa73e672e387a58f728a8ce3ec57d20450a`) completed all 100 disruptive cycles: both serial logs retain all 35 guest-local cycles, `cycles.tsv` retains all 20 topology and 10 reboot PASS records, and each guest reached boot 6. Final validation alone failed because its three evidence regexes were anchored at column zero while the serial backend prefixes guest messages with kernel/syslog text. Those parsers now search for the exact marker inside each line while retaining exact session/node/kind/index and line-end matching. arm64 job `110912380983` (artifact `11239900490`, SHA-256 `2db03a530dd49043d3fe0d8a6ede8b9dca0a5f22b30312f7e1cb095df0f5fd2f`) completed both local actors/observers and all 20 topology cycles; the first DN70 `system_reset` visibly began a fresh kernel boot, but QMP did not return its reply before reset side effects and the controller aborted. Reboot execution now records the reply status but treats the required incremented BOOT-READY, HOST-READY and post-reset application markers as the fail-closed proof that the reset actually happened; a command that did not take effect still times out and fails.

KCSAN run `37029238762` failed only amd64 job `110911600985` with `__d_lookup_rcu / d_lru_add`, artifact `11237009025`, SHA-256 `be4e3b9595d3812c6d23ef2d3e668752d48e0cd4be8e6d25bdc4168592199e13`. The retained report says the 32-bit dentry flags word changed from `0x00100000` to `0x00140000`, exactly the `DCACHE_LRU_LIST` bit-18 transition performed by generic VFS `d_lru_add()`; `__d_lookup_rcu()` tests `DCACHE_OP_COMPARE` bit 1 in the same word. Pinned Linux `028ef9c96e96197026887c0f092424679298aae8` and current upstream `ce1e0223d8ad4211275c82a17ed6d43ab81e13d9` retain this store-free/racy RCU lookup structure. Upstream syzkaller has repeatedly closed the same `d_lru_add / lookup_fast` d_flags race family as invalid, and a 2026 kernel-test-robot report likewise notes recurring generic `__d_lookup_rcu` KCSAN noise. No DECnet-IV-Linux function participates. Vet only the exact observed pair and reverse ordering; all other KCSAN findings remain blocking.

KASAN run `37029209298` failed only arm64 job `110911501618`, artifact `11238033525`, SHA-256 `65ab93e551d7bef24e15a799549ac61896ce1f12a977289a29b4e36ada3cc8a9`, with no KASAN report. Strict E1 wire validation saw one DN70 All-Endnodes hello. The retained PCAP places the first post-isolation DN70 All-Routers hello omitting DN71 at 16:13:51.755663 UTC and that All-Endnodes hello at 16:13:57.946674 UTC, a 6.191-second DR-delay window. The host already detected the correct semantic transition, but `QmpClient.execute()` waited for a capabilities reply before it even sent `stop`; under instrumented TCG that round trip consumed the remaining window. QMP capabilities and the requested command are now queued together on the ordered QMP stream and both replies are still required. This changes only host harness timing, not candidate election behavior. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance and then fresh full acceptance. PP-11 S1 remains blocking after the disruptive gate until repeated sequence-wrap, table/queue-limit and malformed/control-flood pressure is implemented and retained green; do not advance to S2 before S1 closes.

Full acceptance issue #886 on exact candidate `19227f2eecdaf725c6a7bc77ce45158bef854cdf` exposed one more diagnostic-harness false failure. KASAN run `37039942791` passed amd64; arm64 job `110947314461` retained artifact `11244046288` (SHA-256 `dcc557e66baa73636483d88dfc3311e690a8feb22464f013937b9c7a5412466e`) with no KASAN report and both E1 guest PASS markers, but final PCAP validation rejected `endnodesA=1`. The retained frame is a complete type-`0x0b` Router Hello from DN70 to All-Endnodes, not an Endnode Hello. During the deliberate DN71 isolation DN70 becomes designated router, and the kernel intentionally emits Router Hellos to All-Endnodes while `dniv_local_is_dr()` is true. The validator is corrected to preserve `endnodesA == 0` for ordinary E1 while allowing the diagnostic DR-transition only when every DN70 All-Endnodes frame is independently parsed as a complete Router Hello. The E1 PCAP regression now proves both the allowed DR case and rejection of a non-router All-Endnodes frame. Candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then fresh full acceptance; PP-11 S1 remains blocking until its disruptive gate is green and the repeated sequence/table/queue/malformed-control pressure gate is added and passes.

Fast acceptance issue #887 is green on `c5e59c4a8373b5616340eaacc9a54e8874663c6d`: Repository Policy `37045940236`, Build Bootstrap `37046021065`, Project State Gate `37046048326`, Python QEMU VM Lab `37046072957` on amd64/arm64, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `37046097228` for Route20 L1 and PyDECnet L1. This validates the diagnostic DR-router-hello evidence correction.

Full issue #886 PP-11 S1 run `37040170774` produced the first complete green 100-cycle disruptive result on amd64. Job `110948059304`, artifact `11244945035`, SHA-256 `58eb64b2697acd52067a7fee1f2d731bc4661da8ce5bd17d64ed6c7f81530531`, completed in 2200.222 seconds with exact cycle counts 10 module, 20 interface, 20 identity, 20 peer-restart, 20 topology and 10 reboot. DN70/DN71 p99 MIRROR latencies were 16118/16429 ms, successful MIRROR counts 358/364, raw markers 4442/4361, peak NSP links 2/3, final links 1/0, both final adjacencies UP, both vCPUs exercised and aggregate payload throughput 1719.499 B/s; slab stayed within the declared 64 MiB envelope. arm64 job `110948059604` did not fail the candidate: GitHub canceled it at the workflow's 55-minute job ceiling while the PP-11 test step was still running, retaining artifact `11245387982` SHA-256 `a390851baf78d4d51154c38bd2e64a9df038f7042986542eb99032ee1405f3f0` without a completed PP-11 result. Raise the bounded VM-lab job ceiling to the repository-policy maximum of 75 minutes and lock that budget in the PP-11 regression. Candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Next: exact-SHA fast acceptance, then fresh full acceptance; require arm64 PP-11 S1 green before implementing/closing the remaining repeated sequence/table/queue/malformed-control pressure coverage.


Fast acceptance issue #888 is green on exact candidate `b41684079f3866b1cb82039c1dbc299d9c9c2098`: Repository Policy `37049209882`, Build `37049279087`, Project State `37049303227`, E1 `37049330940` on amd64/arm64 and Independent Interop `37049356107` for Route20 L1 and PyDECnet L1.

Full acceptance issue #889 is closed green on the same exact SHA, parent `37052529554`; every dispatched full-profile child passed. PP-11 S1 disruptive run `37053101042` is now genuinely green on both architectures. amd64 job `110991061230`, artifact `11250032411`, SHA-256 `e3551fc24207a5339b9f5b1b5c23b60369cad31dbd441e83b028bead42dbc56e`, completed exactly 100 cycles in 2295.182 s with 10/20/20/20/20/10 module/interface/identity/peer-restart/topology/reboot cycles, p99 16417/16562 ms, MIRROR 373/377, raw 4574/4741, peak/final links 3/3 and 2/0, final adjacency UP, both vCPUs exercised, 1712.282 B/s and slab inside 64 MiB. arm64 job `110991061492`, artifact `11251345505`, SHA-256 `99072edd33efa49295140e06537d526f2f85d5c2a77991035b18be2b9da1776d`, completed exactly 100 cycles in 3468.682 s with the same split, p99 16824/16840 ms, MIRROR 294/326, raw 4436/4347, peak/final links 5/6 and 0/0, final adjacency UP, both vCPUs exercised, 936.609 B/s and slab inside 64 MiB. Issue #890 was an accidental duplicate full trigger and was closed without use.

The next S1 increment is the retained sustained-pressure gate, restricted to full-profile PyDECnet L1 on both amd64 and arm64. It executes three rounds while a long-running valid MIRROR/sequence-wrap stream remains active, independently checks repeated modulo-4096 wraps from PCAP, reaches the 256-link table boundary with DC No Resources overflow, the 32-entry receive queue, 64-entry listener backlog plus one OBJECT_BUSY, the 20-segment data window and the 64-entry retransmit queue, and repeats 96 already-vetted malformed/control probes per round. Each round requires final adjacency UP, at most eight links, zero tx-data/tx-other/rx counters after quiescence and slab within the declared 64 MiB envelope. `pp11-pressure.env` starts at FAIL and can become PASS only after host phases, guest recovery and PCAP validation all pass. No S1 closure is claimed until this new exact candidate fast-accepts and then passes fresh full acceptance on both architectures.


Pressure candidate `85c5f2f11adec28578868446663257df8402bf5b` failed fast acceptance issue #891 before any VM protocol execution. Parent `37069438232`; Build `37069496159` and Project State `37069513797` passed, while E1 `37069526585` and Independent Interop `37069544516` failed during disposable-image preparation because `tests/lab/dnseqwrap.c` contained literal `\\n` escape text outside a C string at the pressure-start insertion. The failure is a harness source-generation defect only; no PP-11 pressure result is claimed from this SHA. The successor fixes only that C source syntax plus continuity/evidence records and must fast-accept before a fresh full run.


Full acceptance issue #893 on pressure successor `b19fe138bc78ef75dc3371ea7a789d346d5e4ef9` reached the new sustained-pressure gate and exposed a host-filter offset defect, not a candidate protocol failure. Independent Ethernet Interoperability run `37071188500`, amd64 PyDECnet L1 job `111050769639`, artifact `11254284128` SHA-256 `5601c08f2f8b33e3c94283a683bb5aca0df84741d1d94fa22c3b4e8864d68714`, completed the normal interoperability suite and began PP-11 pressure round 1. The guest reached exactly 256 links and proved simultaneous sequence/MIRROR progress, while the retained PCAP contains candidate Disconnect Confirm No Resources responses to the synthetic overflow CIs. The host then failed only because its tc u32 table-isolation filter matched zero packets: the pressure filter assumed short-routing NSP offset 8, but the captured L1-router replies use Phase IV long-data routing, placing the NSP flag at DECnet offset 23 and destination link at offsets 24/25. The successor moves the table, window and retransmit selective filters to those observed long-routing offsets and locks them in the PP-11 regression. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. PP-11 S1 remains open pending fresh exact-SHA pressure execution on both architectures.


Full acceptance issue #895 on exact candidate `e1a1c25eecfb06507c1c37da2c72d0046324ed9c` reached the corrected long-routing PP-11 S1 pressure path on arm64 and failed only at fail-closed evidence sealing. Independent Ethernet Interoperability run `37073805818`, arm64 PyDECnet L1 job `111059080632`, artifact `11256736602` SHA-256 `444c50b5568c5c759f2428d5c2ca94074be979df5344b3805ca7e580fa021c7a`: round 1 reached exactly 256 links, simultaneous sequence/MIRROR traffic remained live, and the long-routing table-isolation tc filter matched packets. `evidence_guard.py` then rejected the log because it declared `fault=pp11-control-reply-isolation` while the verification name was `pp11-table-1`. Review found the same declaration/name mismatch in the RX, window and retransmit pressure evidence paths, with RX/retransmit logs also lacking a predeclared unique `fault=` line. The successor gives every pressure phase exactly one declaration matching its `--name`, pre-seeds RX/retransmit logs before appending injector output, and locks all four declarations in the PP-11 regression. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. PP-11 S1 remains open pending exact-SHA dual-architecture pressure execution.


Full acceptance issue #898 on `aea85b8e6649702fce7d0ce89479de45d731a9c5` drained with exactly two failures, both full PyDECnet L1 PP-11 pressure jobs in interop run `37077497018`; every other full-profile child passed. arm64 job `111070546096` retained artifact `11257563098` (SHA-256 `c1651221bfb30aca26c3bc9f132e6878c5049b3e54d171e7db26f8afbc7353fd`); amd64 job `111070546359` retained artifact `11257419389` (SHA-256 `0341e9758e3c0fd14bd98517c884267df7eeae5b89a31e615c9a3ea84c46ddca`). Both reached the exact 256-link table limit under live MIRROR/sequence traffic and passed the corrected long-routing tc/evidence guard, then never reached RX pressure because the table did not drain. Both PCAPs show 252 synthetic rejected inbound links receiving valid PyDECnet DC replies after isolation was removed. The protocol lifecycle defect is local: internal Session-Control reject paths had no socket owner but did not detach the NSP connection, while DC completion recycles only detached terminal entries. The successor marks only these unowned internal rejects detached, preserving user-owned DSO_CONREJECT semantics; terminal DC or retransmission timeout can then recycle the slot. PP-11 S1 remains blocking until the exact successor pressure gate is green on both architectures.


Acceptance runner policy is now bounded for development iteration. Full acceptance is no longer the default follow-up to every fix: the automatic full regression moves from daily to weekly, and a new `PROFILE=targeted SCOPE=pp11-pressure` path runs only Build Bootstrap, Project State, dual-architecture E1, and the two amd64/arm64 PyDECnet L1 pressure jobs. This preserves exact-SHA dual-architecture proof for the blocking PP-11 pressure work while avoiding the unrelated diagnostic, scale, transport, Area-31, portability, release-image and 14-way interop fan-out on each correction. Full acceptance remains mandatory when closing a canonical PP increment and for final pre-production promotion. Existing full issue #900 may finish as predecessor evidence but is not a reason to dispatch another full matrix after this workflow-policy commit. Next: validate the bounded targeted profile itself, then use targeted PP-11 pressure runs until S1 pressure is genuinely green; run one fresh full matrix only at the S1 closure checkpoint.


Production delivery contract pivot: disk images are no longer project deliverables. The release object is a reproducible versioned source tarball for existing x86_64/aarch64 Linux systems, built against the selected target kernel's matching headers. The repository now carries end-user `build.sh`, manifested `install.sh`/`uninstall.sh`, `INSTALL.md`, complete `docs/FEATURES.md` and `docs/COMPONENTS.md`, and canonical `docs/DELIVERY.md`. Full acceptance dispatches `source-release.yml` instead of the retired release-image workflow; portability builds from an extracted source archive rather than trusting a checkout. Ubuntu/QEMU/QCOW2 machinery remains lab-only disposable infrastructure and is never a release artifact. Initial production compatibility floor is Linux 6.8+, GCC 12+ or Clang 16+, Python 3.10+ where required, with maintained forward-kernel compatibility treated as a blocking source-maintenance obligation. Next PP work remains PP-11 S1 pressure; once green, source-release acceptance is part of the canonical full checkpoint.


Source-delivery audit correction: targeted source-release issue #902 stopped in Repository Policy run `37087847503` before any child dispatch because the four new executable shell entry points omitted the blank line required by the repository's canonical license-header format. Add only that canonical separator to `build.sh`, `install.sh`, `uninstall.sh` and `tools/build-source-release.sh`; delivery semantics are unchanged. Audit clean-scan count resets to zero after this correction.


Targeted source-release issue #903: Repository Policy, Build Bootstrap and Project State were green on `c2b3a35f35a8e12df45b0ff27d57a2c7866b6e2a`; Source Release run `37088010676` failed before archive completion because GNU tar rejected positional option ordering in the normalized archive command. The same run exposed that Ubuntu 24.04's maintained 6.8 kernel headers sit below the initially documented 6.12 floor. The source contract now uses Linux 6.8+ as the maintained-LTS floor and retains newer Debian/Fedora/current-kernel coverage for forward compatibility. The archive command now places all tar options before the file list. Audit clean-scan count resets to zero after this correction.


Targeted source-release issue #904 on `1333cfce76793da58297e8a428118aee6287ac6a` is mechanically green: Build Bootstrap `37088686781`, Project State `37088696379`, Source Release `37088703343`; source artifact `11261526493` contains `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-1333cfce7679.tar.xz` SHA-256 `e8f70f1d0243483d9db8551f8a5a026b9061342fedfc0b193ac5dee3c4413997`, and both amd64/arm64 build-install-uninstall jobs passed. The required fresh-disk manual audit then found new defects, so this candidate is superseded and the clean-scan count returns to zero: relative output directories break `tools/build-source-release.sh` after its work-directory `cd`; portability accepted a missing/mismatched `SOURCE-METADATA` because its SHA check was fail-open and the expected SHA was not passed into the container; `docs/HANDOVER.md` still described only three acceptance depths; and the end-user build/install path lacked target-kernel-release cross-checking, documented compiler-floor enforcement and Secure-Boot/module-unload guidance. The successor fixes all of these and strengthens the source-release policy guard before restarting the three-clean-scan requirement.


Source-delivery audit/acceptance follow-up on `1e7336e24c8dbc72af6180859d73ee64263d18aa`: targeted issue #905 had Build Bootstrap `37089334210` and Project State `37089343070` green, but Source Release run `37089349635` failed on amd64 before packaging acceptance because Ubuntu's header Makefile reports base `kernelrelease=6.8.12` while `include/config/kernel.release` carries the installed module ABI release `6.8.0-146-generic`. The end-user target-release guard now prefers `include/config/kernel.release` and falls back to the Makefile target only when that file is absent. The same fresh-disk audit also found that a failed/interrupted install could leave copied files without a durable manifest and that unmanaged existing targets could be overwritten. The successor makes the install manifest progressive and collision-protecting, hardens uninstall manifest/DESTDIR validation, adds missing-artifact and unmanaged-target negative source-release tests, exercises relative source-release output paths, and tightens portability source-SHA/checksum provenance. Audit clean-scan count resets to zero after these corrections.


Source-delivery audit follow-up after targeted issue #906: source release run `37089750994` is mechanically green on amd64/arm64 and artifact `11261772408` contains `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-586372b33470.tar.xz` SHA-256 `f07e3d22cf4d36ebe53385d5e0008b54965b4f00f6a5327d6ede56c4b8fd25ce`. The required fresh-artifact scan nevertheless found release gaps, so this candidate is superseded and the clean-scan count returns to zero: full acceptance still dispatched a separate portability workflow that rebuilt an equivalent archive instead of consuming the exact packaged bytes; one current lab comment still described a release-image builder; and installer replacement paths needed explicit symlink/type safety on reinstall. The successor makes source-release portability part of `source-release.yml`, using only two native architecture runners that each exercise Debian 13/Fedora 42 with GCC/Clang serially against the exact downloaded tarball/checksum, removes the duplicate full-profile standalone portability dispatch, updates the lab wording, and hardens managed-target replacement rules. This also cuts six runner jobs from full release portability coverage while strengthening provenance.


Targeted source-release issue #907 on `48fddc41fa58adee4f7b4edcb7f485025d21039e` proved package-amd64, validate-arm64, Build Bootstrap and Project State green, but both exact-artifact portability jobs failed in Debian 13 Clang cases. The Debian kernel development trees are GCC-configured and export GCC-only module flags; forcing Clang against them is not a supported kernel/toolchain pairing. The successor makes this compatibility rule explicit in `build.sh` and `INSTALL.md`, fails early on common GCC/Clang KDIR mismatches, and changes portability Clang coverage on both distros/architectures to compile userspace with Clang and compile the kernel module object against the same kernel base in a fresh LLVM-configured upstream tree. GCC cases continue to build/install/uninstall the exact release archive against each distro's native kernel development tree. Audit clean-scan count resets to zero after this correction.


Targeted source-release issue #908 on `f6eb58566506765cd0096eef30a3b33514261647` again proved package-amd64, validate-arm64, Build Bootstrap and Project State green. Exact-artifact portability advanced through Debian 13 Clang userspace, then both native architecture jobs failed while preparing the matching upstream LLVM kernel tree because the Debian container lacked kernel Kconfig/build prerequisites (`flex` was the first missing tool). The successor adds the complete required Debian kernel-source preparation set (`bc`, `bison`, `flex`, `libelf-dev`, `libssl-dev`) to both source-release and standalone portability workflows and locks that requirement in the source-release policy gate. Audit clean-scan count resets to zero after this correction.


Targeted source-release issue #909 on `a16171cdaad50bb293f218dd0f370c639e65c7ed` again proved package-amd64, validate-arm64, Build Bootstrap and Project State green. Both exact-artifact portability jobs progressed through Debian 13 Clang userspace but failed because Debian's kernel package version exposed a distro ABI kernelversion whose kernel.org tarball URL does not exist. The successor stops deriving external Clang source URLs from distro ABI versions and instead reuses the project's canonical pinned current-upstream Linux source from `tests/lab/build-diagnostic-kernel.sh`, fetching and verifying that exact commit before LLVM configuration and candidate module compilation. Git is added to the portability container prerequisites and the policy gate locks the exact-source verification. Audit clean-scan count resets to zero after this correction.


Targeted source-release issue #910 on exact `29df4b36e25b90f97ca6f23f4aad1ce0550ba710` is mechanically green: Repository Policy `37092119939`, Build Bootstrap `37092154461`, Project State `37092163925`, Source Release `37092171792`; exact source artifact `11263555544` contains `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-29df4b36e25b.tar.xz` SHA-256 `76b9d64991c44f2cdb976ef66eb5d8bbfab0c5230450a05d1f0883df9537d8ba`, with amd64/arm64 package validation and exact-artifact portability green. Required fresh-artifact audit scan then found new delivery defects, so the clean-scan counter resets to zero and #910 is not final audit evidence: end-user `build.sh` still inherited lab-only Area-31/VDE2 selftests from the `dnmultinet` default Make target; Clang portability compiled only `decnet_iv.o` instead of exercising the documented full `build.sh` plus install/uninstall path; installation did not derive/validate the target kernel release from module vermagic and accepted unsafe root dot-components/managed regular-file type replacement; the build prerequisite check omitted `ar`; the source packager did not reject tracked bytecode/unexpected ELF payloads; and the delivery inventory silently omitted standing-project-goal DDCMP, which has no current implementation and therefore must be explicitly pending rather than falsely implied complete. The successor separates product build from lab tests while retaining lab coverage under `make unit`, validates full compiler-compatible Clang builds/installs, hardens install/uninstall and source-only packaging, expands feature/component documentation, and makes DDCMP a release-completeness blocker unless owner-de-scoped. After this correction, restart the required three successive fresh-disk zero-defect scans from scan 1.


Targeted source-release issue #911 on exact `e5a797a64a438d3d5250b7b981ebf2e8c76d512d` is non-promotable. Repository Policy `37093555493` and Project State `37093594201` passed. Build Bootstrap `37093583443` failed identically on x64 job `111118860033` and arm64 job `111118860221` because `test_source_release_gate.py` still required the superseded installer text `DESTDIR must be empty or absolute` after the installer had intentionally strengthened that contract to normalized absolute non-root paths. Source Release `37093604023` proved package-amd64 `111118915813` and validate-arm64 `111119069247` green, but both exact-artifact portability jobs failed in the synthetic upstream Clang compatibility case: amd64 `111119069160` and arm64 `111119069174` reached a complete candidate compile then modpost rejected the intentionally source-only LLVM tree because it has no built-kernel `Module.symvers`. The successor fixes the stale policy marker and limits `KBUILD_MODPOST_WARN=1` to that explicitly non-loadable current-upstream forward-compile/staged-install proof; real target-kernel builds continue to require normal modpost success. Audit clean-scan count remains zero.

Targeted source-release issue #912 on exact `b1d5cd80ec0ef9e235fdaee9884c564f52a55d07` is mechanically green: Repository Policy `37094018057`, Build Bootstrap `37094053965`, Project State `37094064255`, and Source Release `37094073276` all passed. Source artifact `11263039524` contains `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-b1d5cd80ec0e.tar.xz` SHA-256 `27e2afb01888c4c57e1cfa740788cc7158551d9742570cf0b4afa01019c78e79`; package validation and exact-artifact amd64/arm64 portability are green. The required fresh-artifact audit nevertheless found new delivery defects, so this candidate is superseded and the clean-scan count remains zero: the current Project State goal still advertised reproducible images, an obsolete manually dispatchable `portability.yml` could rebuild from checkout instead of consuming the exact packaged bytes, staged install/uninstall could traverse a symlinked parent outside `DESTDIR`, and the installation manual omitted Fedora `binutils` and several standard command prerequisites while ambiguously describing SIMH/Route20 as administrator dependencies. The successor removes the obsolete portability workflow, locks exact-artifact portability to `source-release.yml`, hardens staged-path ancestry with install/uninstall regression tests, synchronizes the current goal/manual, and restarts the required fresh-disk audit from scan 1.

Source-delivery audit follow-up on `9032c386946dc57b59af6142a71771697d9c3ac4`: before restarting the clean-scan counter, continued source review found two further release-contract gaps, so this candidate is superseded and the count remains zero. `docs/COMPONENTS.md` did not enumerate the internal kernel/protocol headers and release-support tools even though `docs/DELIVERY.md` requires every delivered source component to be documented. The single install manifest also rejected a previously managed standard `/lib/modules/<release>/extra/decnet_iv.ko` entry when building for a newer target kernel, contradicting the documented kernel-upgrade path. The successor inventories those source components, makes exact standard module paths from older kernel releases valid manifest-owned entries, preflights them before removal, refreshes all affected live `depmod` caches, and adds a source-release regression proving a staged multi-kernel manifest survives reinstall and is completely removed by uninstall. Custom `MODULE_ROOT` remains explicitly bound to the supplied root.

Source-delivery audit follow-up on `ddd8ebdbf65c9d6b52cf0924685989c2b0e7807d`: continued pre-scan review found two more contract gaps, so targeted issue #914 is superseded and the clean-scan count remains zero. The multi-kernel manifest allowance accepted canonical `/lib/modules/<release>/extra/decnet_iv.ko` entries even when an administrator selected a custom `MODULE_ROOT`, contradicting the documented requirement that custom module roots remain bound to that explicit root. The component manual also still summarized lab/reference/workflow/governance material by category despite the delivery contract requiring every installed/source component to be documented. The successor limits cross-release standard-module manifest ownership to default-module-root installations, adds a custom-root foreign-manifest negative regression, and explicitly inventories all shipped workflow, test-suite, lab-image, reference, governance and repository-control source components.

Source-delivery audit scan 1 on exact artifact candidate `31c883f76953fc561f0d66fb412d5c21d869223f` found a new path-contract defect before a clean scan could be completed, so targeted issue #915 is superseded and the clean-scan count stays zero. `install.sh`/`uninstall.sh` described `DESTDIR`, `PREFIX` and `MODULE_ROOT` as normalized absolute paths but still accepted repeated `//` separators, and manifest-path validation likewise accepted repeated separators under managed roots. The successor rejects repeated separators in root and manifest paths, adds a source-release negative regression, and makes the uninstall instructions explicit that non-default `PREFIX`/`MODULE_ROOT` values must be reused.

Continued source-delivery audit of superseded exact artifact `31c883f76953fc561f0d66fb412d5c21d869223f` found an additional documentation-completeness defect after the non-normalized-path defect was recorded: `kernel/decnet/README.md` still described disconnect data, deferred accept controls and stream mode as future Phase 5 work even though `DSO_DISDATA`, `DSO_ACCEPTMODE`/`DSO_CONACCEPT`/`DSO_CONREJECT` and native `SOCK_STREAM` are accepted and present in the current kernel socket implementation. The top-level README and feature/component catalogues also omitted portions of that delivered socket surface. The clean-scan count remains zero. The successor synchronizes those delivered-surface documents and adds source-release policy regressions against stale socket feature descriptions.

Source-delivery audit of the current portability contract found that the exact-artifact matrix still used Fedora 42 even though Fedora 42 reached end of life on 2026-05-27; this contradicts `docs/DELIVERY.md`'s requirement to test maintained distro kernels. The clean-scan count remains zero. The successor advances the exact-artifact GCC/Clang portability cases to Fedora 44, the current stable Fedora line, and adds a policy rejection for the EOL Fedora 42 image.

Source-delivery audit after Fedora-matrix refresh found that the synthetic forward-compatibility kernel was still pinned to Linux 7.0 (`028ef9c96e96197026887c0f092424679298aae8`, 2026-04-12), while kernel.org now lists mainline 7.3-rc5 (2026-09-27) and stable 7.2.8. This no longer satisfies the delivery contract's current validated upstream-kernel-line requirement. The clean-scan count remains zero. The successor pins the forward/diagnostic upstream source to Torvalds v7.3-rc5 commit `72d3fcf802c45d00b300f25b848a93c3a2bd7c7e` and locks that exact baseline in the source-release policy gate.

Fresh-artifact pre-scan of candidate `2b1c157e04174a7284596f46c8ca9e206e585b34` found that refreshing the upstream source SHA alone left the diagnostic-kernel contract internally inconsistent: the builder, module injector and kernel-diagnostics workflow still required `7.0.0-dniv-<profile>`, the deterministic Kbuild timestamp/comment still described the April Linux 7.0 source, and `docs/TEST_LAB.md` still named the old v7.0 pin. A v7.3-rc5 diagnostic build would therefore fail its own release assertion before use. The candidate is superseded and the clean-scan count remains zero. The successor synchronizes the expected release to `7.3.0-rc5-dniv-<profile>`, the exact upstream commit timestamp, active lab documentation, and policy regressions.

Fresh-artifact audit scan 1 on exact source artifact `11265545071` for candidate `838730c7b8a5e95f272f581398b94aac83c6c48c` found a new delivery-documentation defect, so the candidate is superseded for audit purposes and the clean-scan count remains zero. The exact-artifact portability workflow had already advanced to maintained Fedora 44, but `docs/TEST_LAB.md` and `docs/PRE_PRODUCTION_TEST.md` still described the active `SCOPE=source-release` matrix as Fedora 42. The successor synchronizes both canonical acceptance documents to Fedora 44 and adds a source-release policy regression that rejects stale Fedora 42 matrix text in either document. After targeted source-release acceptance, restart the required fresh-disk audit from scan 1.

Fresh-artifact audit scan 1 on exact source artifact `11265038469` for candidate `ce50f736d4b459596e672ff98400db8b19076ca3` found a new acceptance-documentation defect, so the candidate is superseded for audit purposes and the clean-scan count remains zero. `docs/TEST_LAB.md` still said full acceptance initially dispatched only `scale4` and that `scale8`/`scale16` were not promotion-green, while the current full dispatcher requires dual-architecture `scale4`, dual-architecture `scale8`, and the distributed 16-node scale workflow. The successor synchronizes the active lab contract and adds a source-release policy regression against the obsolete scale-promotion text. After targeted source-release acceptance, restart the required fresh-disk audit from scan 1.

Fresh-artifact audit scan 1 on exact source artifact `11265392416` for candidate `88cda75cbada0f0e337723b52c75e101201e502b` found a new pre-production documentation defect, so the candidate is superseded for audit purposes and the clean-scan count remains zero. `docs/PRE_PRODUCTION_TEST.md` still named removed `run-two-node.sh` as a current harness even though the delivered tree uses `tests/lab/dniv_lab.py`, `tests/lab/dniv-smoke.sh` and repository-tracked workflows. The successor removes the nonexistent harness reference and adds a source-release policy regression tying the documentation to the active two-node harnesses. After targeted source-release acceptance, restart the required fresh-disk audit from scan 1.

Continued fresh-artifact audit of candidate `88cda75cbada0f0e337723b52c75e101201e502b` also found that `docs/PRE_PRODUCTION_TEST.md` still excluded serial/synchronous DECnet media from the project transport claim in two places, contradicting the standing native DDCMP project goal and the release-completeness blocker already documented elsewhere. The clean-scan count remains zero. The successor states DDCMP serial/synchronous media as pending rather than out of scope, makes their applicable reference/device tests blocking once implemented, and adds a source-release policy regression against the obsolete exclusion wording. Restart the required fresh-disk audit from scan 1 after targeted source-release acceptance.

Continued fresh-artifact audit of the delivery candidate found additional stale canonical acceptance status, so the clean-scan count remains zero. `docs/PRE_PRODUCTION_TEST.md` still described PP-10 as open even though exact full candidate `e60660c3b7d311451fc854464fa1d7438222f7e2` closed it, and still treated delivered Phase 7 Linux/VAX tool coverage as future work. `docs/HECNET_LAB.md` still described cross-runner VDE2 as the sole remaining Phase 8 exit dependency despite Phase 8 having closed on `9b73e61bbd0f95b82410276f7b5dc3db94e219ba`, and its workflow/progression text still used pre-delivery future-tense wording. `docs/TEST_LAB.md` and `docs/ROADMAP.md` retained the same obsolete Phase 7 maturity language. The successor synchronizes those canonical documents to the completed PP-10/Phase 8/Phase 7 state and adds source-release policy regressions against the stale wording. Restart the required fresh-disk audit from scan 1 after targeted source-release acceptance.

Continued fresh-artifact delivery audit found that the declared Linux 6.8 compatibility floor was not actually compiled by the exact-artifact source-release workflow: the matrix covered maintained distro kernels and the current upstream line, but no pinned 6.8 tree. The same scan found `build.sh` invoked external `dirname` before its prerequisite validator even though the installation manual lists that command as required. The clean-scan count remains zero. The successor pins Torvalds v6.8 commit `e8f897f4afef0031fe618a8e94127a0934896aba`, prepares it on both amd64 and arm64 in the exact-artifact portability path, requires `build.sh` plus staged install/uninstall at kernel release `6.8.0`, documents the executed floor gate, and preflights `dirname` before resolving the source root. Source-release policy now rejects loss of either safeguard. Restart the required fresh-disk audit from scan 1 after targeted source-release acceptance.

Continued fresh-artifact audit found that shipped `scratch/README.md` still described the removed candidate-VM checkpoint artifact model: it claimed the two-node workflow uploaded a sealed QCOW2 checkpoint, retained/pruned checkpoint artifacts, and said persistence existed only through uploaded artifacts. Current workflows instead cache only the verified source-independent architecture foundation and deliberately keep exact-candidate QCOW2 state disposable. The clean-scan count remains zero. The successor synchronizes the scratch persistence contract to the verified Actions-cache foundation model, states that candidate images/overlays are never resume input, and adds source-release policy regressions against the obsolete checkpoint wording. Restart the required fresh-disk audit from scan 1 after targeted source-release acceptance.

Continued fresh-artifact audit found a PP-00 release-gate coverage gap. `docs/PRE_PRODUCTION_TEST.md` requires negatives for bad source/checksum/archive metadata, generated payload entry, missing/wrong/unsupported kernel inputs, below-floor compiler/Python, non-root live install and unavailable/read-only output, but the source-release workflow exercised only the happy archive path plus installer collision/safety cases. The clean-scan count remains zero. The successor makes the amd64 packaging job reject checksum corruption, truncated archives, missing/wrong `SOURCE-METADATA`, injected generated payloads, wrong source pins, read-only output directories, missing/mismatched/Linux-6.7 kernel trees, Python 3.9, GCC 11, Clang 15 and non-root live installation, while retaining the exact packaged artifact for arm64 and portability consumers. The source-release policy locks every negative marker. Restart the required fresh-disk audit from scan 1 after targeted source-release acceptance.


Owner scope decision synchronized: DDCMP and IPv6 are explicitly out of scope effective immediately. Current delivery, feature/component, roadmap and pre-production contracts must not treat either as a product feature, release blocker or source of PP/reference/device acceptance requirements; MULTINET project acceptance is IPv4-only. Historical chronology that recorded earlier DDCMP pending/blocking decisions remains historical. Source-delivery audit clean-scan count is zero. Next: run bounded targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance for the exact synchronized commit, obtain its exact packaged source artifact, and restart the mandatory fresh-disk audit at scan 1/3; any defect resets the count to zero. After three successive fresh zero-defect scans, resume blocking PP-11 S1 pressure work before S2.


The first scope-synchronized candidate `08668314dbf06d0a129f5941ef1e00a8729c9aae` exposed an inherited source-release workflow defect from predecessor `c317c6ff6d107c55bed234ab9540b5abf4659c4a`: GitHub rejected `.github/workflows/source-release.yml` at workflow-parse/startup time in run `37101198817` before any job existed. The PP-00 negative insertion had accidentally spliced duplicate workflow bodies into the `SOURCE-METADATA` version/date validators. Targeted issue #929 is therefore superseded and the clean-scan count remains zero. The successor removes those duplicate splices, restores the intended metadata validators, preserves the required source-input negatives, and leaves the DDCMP/IPv6 owner de-scope unchanged. Next: run bounded targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance on the exact repair commit, obtain its exact packaged source artifact, and restart fresh-disk audit scan 1/3 only if the gate is green.


Workflow-parse repair follow-up: exact candidate `89568fe2f97e1a1d46ae6a96a8996381fa1cbb86` still produced immediate parse/startup failure in source-release run `37101532647` with no jobs. The remaining defect was YAML block-scalar indentation: the fake GCC 11 and Clang 15 helper heredoc bodies were unindented and therefore escaped the `run: |` scalar. Targeted issue #930 is superseded and the clean-scan count remains zero. The successor emits both fake compiler helpers with indented `printf` commands, preserving the same negative semantics and every scope decision. Next: dispatch bounded targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance on the exact successor; only a green exact-artifact run may restart audit scan 1/3.


Targeted source-release issue #931 on exact `49bace5e1742fb6f77c81888a1e5b0793fbb1134` reached executable acceptance and exposed two delivery-gate defects, so the clean-scan count remains zero. Build Bootstrap `37101639751` failed because the source-release policy treated the new PP-00 `.qcow2` generated-payload negative itself as forbidden disk-image release behavior. Source Release Reproducibility `37101664918` built the archive and passed the new invalid-input step, then failed extracted-tree execution because tracked `build.sh` had lost its executable bit; earlier green delivery candidates carried mode 100755, while the current tree carried 100644. The successor narrows the qcow2 policy exception strictly to the negative validator/injected `audit.qcow2` paths, requires that negative to remain present, restores `build.sh` mode 100755 without changing its bytes, and adds an executable-mode regression. Issue #931 is superseded. Next: dispatch bounded targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance on the exact successor and restart audit scan 1/3 only after exact-artifact green.


Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11266991181` from candidate `4edabae80b2ff56fe30a667baa6db8bc9c43c788` (tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-4edabae80b2f.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `95ab77a784c6d456ca3160b471e781c34c6c3988b2a8f1b99b0238de6d272336`) found a scope defect, so the clean-scan count remains zero and targeted issue #932 is superseded despite green Repository Policy `37101800149`, Build Bootstrap `37101832345`, Project State Gate `37101842296` and Source Release Reproducibility `37101849730`. The pinned PyDECnet in-scope list still selected the whole `tests.test_multinet` module, whose pinned test suite contains UDP plus IPv6-specific connect/listen classes. That made out-of-scope IPv6 (and non-claimed UDP) behavior a blocking reference baseline despite the owner scope decision. The successor selects only the IPv4 TCP `TestMultinetTCPconnect`, `TestMultinetTCPconnectLate` and `TestMultinetTCPlisten` classes, documents that PP-08 boundary, and adds a release-policy regression rejecting the broad module or any UDP/IPv6 MULTINET class. Restart the mandatory fresh-disk audit at scan 1/3 after exact targeted source-release acceptance of the successor.


Continued fresh-disk audit of superseded exact artifact `11266991181` also found a live-install dependency-cache defect. `INSTALL.md` declares `depmod` required for live module installation/loading, but `install.sh` and `uninstall.sh` silently skipped dependency-cache refresh when `depmod` was absent and could still report success after changing module files. The clean-scan count remains zero. The successor fail-closes live install/uninstall on missing `depmod` before manifest mutation/removal, runs the required refresh unconditionally once preflight passes, documents that behavior, and locks both preflights in the source-release policy gate. The IPv4-only MULTINET reference correction from `2d57bdc0112680dc3bec8334e9dd1baa384e8aeb` is preserved, and DDCMP/IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance and then fresh-disk audit scan 1/3 on the resulting exact artifact.

Continued fresh-artifact audit of superseded exact artifact `11273540604` found a remote-safety contradiction in the canonical PP-09 contract. `docs/PRE_PRODUCTION_TEST.md` required “peer and gateway restart, link interruption” inside the Area-31/HECnet stage without limiting those disruptions to project-controlled systems, while `docs/HECNET_LAB.md` and the owner boundary prohibit changing or disrupting any remote HECnet machine beyond PYRTR `31.3`. Fresh-artifact audit scan 1 on exact source artifact `11274085501` for candidate `4d2d19443ab9f56b86a5d53f81b3f80b01234e58` (tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-4d2d19443ab9.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `b0287fc0e6e4d82190748324b68016e46a15d9582ef5a17c5c7420982d45e8cb`) found a live-install dependency-cache contract defect, so the candidate is superseded for audit purposes and the clean-scan count remains zero. `INSTALL.md` allowed arbitrary live `MODULE_ROOT`, while `install.sh`/`uninstall.sh` always refreshed dependency metadata with `depmod -a <release>` in the standard `/lib/modules` tree. A live custom module root could therefore change/remove a module outside the refreshed dependency tree while still reporting success. The successor restricts custom `MODULE_ROOT` to non-empty `DESTDIR` staged/package operations, keeps live install/uninstall on `/lib/modules/<kernel-release>`, documents the boundary, and locks it in the source-release policy gate. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance and then fresh-disk audit scan 1/3 on the resulting exact artifact.

Pre-audit of exact source artifact `11275142036` for candidate `9a315e1fbb523ff159c34206e9bad17278567b2f` (Source Release Reproducibility `37126088069`, tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-9a315e1fbb52.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `64ee9dce26925a9fd7cdd8ffce70635e7c46048ecb55d2c19e0cdacf14f58e9b`) found that two documented installer surfaces still lacked behavioral release acceptance: staged custom `PREFIX=/opt/decnet`, and automatic kernel-release selection from module vermagic when `KERNEL_RELEASE` is omitted. Continued audit of superseded exact source artifact `11275142036` for candidate `9a315e1fbb523ff159c34206e9bad17278567b2f` found an additional end-user MULTINET configuration-safety defect; the clean-scan count remains zero. `dnmultinet` rejected whitespace in runtime peer hosts but accepted newline/whitespace in `--vde` and `--local-address`, so a single argument could inject another PyDECnet configuration line. It also allowed `--runtime-peer-env` with `--config-out`, persisting the runtime remote host/port even though the secret-backed mode already refuses dry-run output, and it accepted IPv6-style peer literals despite the owner scope boundary. The successor validates generated-config values as single tokens, requires an IPv4 local address, rejects IPv6 peer literals, refuses `--config-out` in runtime-peer mode, adds normal-build negative regressions for each case, and documents the memory-backed runtime-peer contract. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance and then restart mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3 on the resulting exact artifact.

Pre-audit of candidate `b799c8c283bcd26740f4d7be647845f96a846310` found one remaining MULTINET generated-config lexer gap before the candidate could qualify for a clean scan. The first hardening rejected whitespace and leading-option tokens, but PyDECnet parses each generated line with POSIX `shlex.split()`: unmatched quotes or a trailing backslash could therefore be accepted by `dnmultinet` validation yet change tokenization or raise inside the downstream parser. The successor now validates each generated config value with the same POSIX shlex tokenization rule and requires an exact single-token round trip; permanent selftests cover newline, unmatched-quote and trailing-backslash VDE values. The clean-scan count remains zero. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance and then mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3 on the resulting exact artifact.

Pre-audit of exact green source-release candidate `2d1f4a562f77174e10e17ce17ec63d7d2c4a2267` (Repository Policy `37129075959`, Build Bootstrap `37129105289`, Project State Gate `37129116680`, Source Release Reproducibility `37129126076`, artifact `11276306784`) found two remaining delivery-boundary defects before a clean scan could be recorded, so the candidate is superseded for audit purposes and the clean-scan count remains zero. First, `dnmultinet` copied `MULTINET_REMOTE_HOST` and `MULTINET_REMOTE_PORT` into the environment inherited by the PyDECnet child even though the runtime-peer contract said the endpoint remained only in memory-backed configuration. Second, `install.sh`/`uninstall.sh` accepted CR/LF in `DESTDIR`, `PREFIX` and `MODULE_ROOT` despite using a line-oriented ownership manifest, permitting one configured root/path to be encoded as multiple manifest records. The successor scrubs both runtime endpoint variables before `execve`, adds a normal-build child-environment regression, rejects CR/LF in installer/uninstaller roots and managed paths with extracted-source negatives, documents the line-oriented encoding boundary, and adds the previously omitted `basename` command prerequisite used by `install.sh`. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance and then restart mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3 on the resulting exact artifact.

Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11276408792` for candidate `634a7eabafb5fabbad1b3aec8c8cbcd1e0e79b67` (Source Release Reproducibility `37130515627`, tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-634a7eabafb5.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `4dfb17294fcbf764e8561b895d5ff280061da1656b1a0337dfd3aa48c29ebf9e`) found a source-tree portability defect before a clean scan could be recorded, so the clean-scan count remains zero. The shipped `make unit` bridge-stop selftest failed on a modern Linux environment where `/proc/<pid>/task/<pid>/children` is absent: `stop_process_tree` silently collected no separately-sessioned descendants and left the detached child alive. The successor enumerates direct children from the universally available Linux `/proc/*/status` `PPid:` field instead, retaining recursive descendant-first termination; the exact failing selftest then passes repeatedly and the full shipped `make unit` target is green in that environment. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance and then restart mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3 on the resulting exact artifact.

Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11276529556` for candidate `9c96d7e99d61f3e9cf99a45cb4bef119c86a652a` (Repository Policy `37132476001`, Build Bootstrap `37132511831`, Project State Gate `37132522737`, Source Release Reproducibility `37132530840`, tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-9c96d7e99d61.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `5bdc71876206b3c780707e504621317b3f086b02468acaf86934507e3464e496`) found another installer safety defect before a clean scan could be recorded, so the clean-scan count remains zero. `install.sh` rewrote its line-oriented ownership manifest through predictable path `$manifest.tmp.$$`; a pre-existing symlink at that exact name could redirect the `sort` rewrite, overwrite an arbitrary writable target, and then be renamed into place as the install manifest. The audit reproduced this deterministically by pre-creating the symlink from the same PID and `exec`ing the installer: installation returned success, the victim was replaced by manifest contents, and the installed manifest became the attacker-supplied symlink. The successor creates the rewrite file atomically with `mktemp` in the manifest directory, verifies that temporary object is a regular non-symlink, documents the new prerequisite/contract, and adds an extracted-source regression plus policy locks proving a planted old predictable-name symlink cannot redirect the manifest write. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance and then restart mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3 on the resulting exact artifact.

No acceptance run or release artifact from `f8bb8364f93835aa707401819bcda620232c888f` qualifies for audit. The successor uses Bash `BASHPID` to identify the regression process without generator-sensitive doubled-dollar syntax and locks that exact path construction in policy. Clean-scan count remains zero. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance only from the repaired successor.

Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11277389552` for candidate `6afa9031eadf46f8dcba953159822ea4a4881c93` (Repository Policy `37133646730`, Build Bootstrap `37133681420`, Project State Gate `37133691950`, Source Release Reproducibility `37133702220`, tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-6afa9031eadf.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `ecf06e6717fbe4f5b97a8d439c60cc70e969970c54e29ad01f5500ad5fc50f1d`) found another installer ownership-manifest defect before a clean scan could be recorded, so the clean-scan count remains zero. A pre-existing manifest that was a hard link to another writable regular file passed the regular/non-symlink check; `record()` then appended newly managed paths through that shared inode before the final atomic rewrite, corrupting the external hard-linked file. The audit reproduced this deterministically with a valid one-line manifest hard-linked to a victim: installation returned success and appended the complete unsorted ownership set to the victim while replacing only the manifest pathname at final rewrite. The successor requires `stat`, accepts both the atomically created manifest temporary and any existing manifest only when their link count is exactly one, documents the single-link contract, and adds an extracted-source hard-link regression plus policy locks. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance only from the repaired successor, then restart mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3 on its exact packaged bytes.

Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11278773170` for candidate `e96fe72637db03f47698e657ceea0db15738f7a7` (Repository Policy `37136215323`, Build Bootstrap `37136258907`, Project State Gate `37136270478`, Source Release Reproducibility `37136281827`, tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-e96fe72637db.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `6f811ca0b349f4214e1db0670fe3116a752ea26cd026a1634ff8984fb0c6a34f`) found another shipped source-tree defect before a clean scan could be recorded, so the clean-scan count remains zero. `tests/lab/dniv-interop-smoke.sh` contained five generator-damaged scratch paths with a literal `.$` suffix and several additional PID-derived/static `/tmp` paths, including a fixed `/tmp/dnetd.conf` written by the root candidate guest. The successor creates every candidate-guest DAP/config/root scratch object atomically with `mktemp`/`mktemp -d`, reuses the securely created files instead of unlinking them before writes, cleans the dnetd config after use, and adds a repository-policy regression that locks the atomic scratch-path contract and rejects the obsolete predictable forms. Pre-audit source review of candidate `b297c2249db7825257a4d8488b5360b7e2d77a70` found another release defect while its targeted source-release acceptance was still running, so that candidate and any artifact it produces are superseded and the clean-scan count remains zero. `docs/FEATURES.md` promises `dnhttpd` “safe root-level GET handling”, but `dnhttpd` validated only the URI text and then used `fopen(root/name)`; a root-level symbolic link therefore followed its target outside the configured document root and could expose an arbitrary readable host file over DECnet. The successor opens the configured root as a directory descriptor, opens the single validated leaf with `openat(..., O_NOFOLLOW | O_CLOEXEC)`, requires a regular file with `fstat`, adds a selftest that proves a symlink escape is rejected while a regular root file remains readable, and locks those primitives in the source-release policy gate. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance only from the repaired successor, then restart mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3 on its exact packaged bytes.

Pre-audit source review of candidate `3892c91ce2d5c6131056f59c0983a7025bb027a1` found another installer safety defect while its targeted source-release acceptance was running, so that candidate and any artifact it produces are superseded and the clean-scan count remains zero. Reinstallation trusted any manifest-owned regular-file target that was not a symbolic link. If an installed managed file was replaced with a hard link to another writable regular file, the next `install -D` write could overwrite the shared inode and corrupt the external hard-linked file. The successor requires every pre-existing managed regular-file target to have link count exactly one before replacement, documents that contract, and adds an extracted-source regression that hard-links the first managed module target to a victim and proves reinstall rejects it without changing the victim, plus policy locks for the implementation and regression. Pre-audit source review of candidate `df47c73bcad988196d2a38a078ecd8b67fa37333` found another release security defect while its targeted source-release acceptance was running, so that candidate and any artifact it produces are superseded and the clean-scan count remains zero. `dnfald --root` validated DAP file names but then used pathname-based `fopen()`, `getxattr()` and `setxattr()`; a symbolic-link leaf could therefore expose or overwrite a file outside the configured FAL root, and a hard-linked leaf could expose or truncate the shared external inode. The successor pins the configured root with a directory descriptor, performs file opens with `openat(..., O_NOFOLLOW | O_CLOEXEC)`, requires single-link regular files before reads or truncation, uses fd-based extended attributes, confines directory/rename/delete operations to the pinned root with `fdopendir`/`renameat`/`unlinkat`, extends the selftest with symbolic-link and hard-link escape negatives that preserve the victim, documents the root-confinement behavior, and locks the primitives in source-release policy. The successor opens the configured root and then `mailbox.log` with `openat(..., O_NOFOLLOW | O_CLOEXEC | O_NONBLOCK)`, verifies a single-link regular file with `fstat` before any write, converts the descriptor with `fdopen`, adds selftest negatives for both symlink and hard-link victims plus a positive regular spool, documents the confinement contract, and locks those primitives in source-release policy. Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11283014755` for candidate `bdd85301a890ad368d943b0e3fb95c5b48f33756` (Repository Policy `37148991747`, Build Bootstrap `37149023768`, Project State Gate `37149036904`, Source Release Reproducibility `37149048333`, tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-bdd85301a890.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `ede91b5a99a020d0b6e04451837194320417ae338ba90e8839209f7d2bf29a41`) found a multi-segment socket-send correctness defect before a clean scan could be recorded, so the clean-scan count remains zero. `dniv_sock_sendmsg()` may legitimately return a short positive count after queuing one or more NSP segments when flow/window pressure, timeout or interruption stops the remainder; a retry of the unsent bytes then marked the first retry segment as a new BOM, causing the peer reassembler to see a second BOM inside the incomplete record and fail it with `EPROTO`. The same path also treated an immediate Ethernet transmit failure as if a queued NSP segment had not been accepted, even though that segment remained in the retransmit queue with its sequence number consumed; retrying the reported-unsent bytes could therefore duplicate user data under a new NSP sequence. The successor tracks an in-progress normal-data record across short sends so retries continue without a second BOM, and treats queued normal/interrupt segments as accepted once retransmit ownership is established while leaving the immediate lower-layer attempt best-effort. Source-release policy locks both invariants and feature documentation records the socket contract. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance only from the synchronized successor.

Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11285873181` for candidate `2d663e6d8bcb5c556e2d1f0c05eaba4916daf3e3` (Repository Policy `37156643929`, Build Bootstrap `37156674068`, Project State Gate `37156684115`, Source Release Reproducibility `37156693555`, tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-2d663e6d8bcb.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `dc4128bf9e9b635bd5555cb5940eb32984fd80199f2289c2412eb7bb68ed3ce2`) found a classic `SOCK_SEQPACKET` record-framing defect before a clean scan could be recorded, so the clean-scan count remains zero. `dniv_sock_sendmsg()` accepted `MSG_EOR` but ignored it when choosing NSP EOM: every successful send syscall ended the record. The pinned LinuxDECnet comparison (`ff39eef045d1e4b7b72a3d40111e89c07a473398`) ends a sequenced-packet record only when the final segment is sent with `MSG_EOR`, allowing one DECnet record to span multiple writes; the candidate therefore broke classic applications that deliberately assemble a record across calls. The successor makes EOM conditional on `MSG_EOR` for `SOCK_SEQPACKET` while retaining implicit EOM for `SOCK_STREAM`, preserves BOM/EOM state across short sends, and tracks aggregate open-record length so a multi-write record cannot exceed `DNBUFSIZE`. Source-release policy and feature documentation lock the restored ABI. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance only from the repaired successor, obtain its new exact source artifact, and restart mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3 on those packaged bytes.

Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11285544306` for candidate `590e2664e1241e19d8980fd83f95f4220320cf3a` (Repository Policy `37157848575`, Build Bootstrap `37157883287`, Project State Gate `37157895973`, Source Release Reproducibility `37157906494`, tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-590e2664e124.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `b30e930e1ffdba8b70ab52aea8c3024079cfa74b7bf7575000b904f3bc7b904e`) found a kernel lifecycle exposure defect before a clean scan could be recorded, so the clean-scan count remains zero. `dniv_init()` registered `/dev/decnet_iv` before routing, NSP, Ethernet and AF_DECnet socket initialization completed. A process able to open the mode-0600 management device during module load could therefore issue ioctls into only partly initialized subsystems; for example `SET_IDENTITY` can call Ethernet address/workqueue logic before `dniv_eth_init()` has established the Ethernet subsystem. The symmetric exit path also withdrew the misc device only after subsystem teardown had begun. The successor initializes route/NSP/Ethernet/socket state first, publishes the management endpoint last, withdraws it first on exit, and unwinds the fully initialized data plane if the final misc registration fails. Source-release policy and feature documentation lock the endpoint lifecycle ordering. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance only from the repaired successor, obtain its new exact source artifact, and restart mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3 on those packaged bytes.

Pre-acceptance source review of candidate `5863cbcff3ad4ec404a3dd9ac904cbdf8e0cadfe` found another production parser-boundary defect while its targeted source-release acceptance was running, so that candidate and any artifact it produces are superseded and the clean-scan count remains zero. `userspace/libdnet/dnetdb.c` read `/etc/decnet.conf` through a fixed 512-byte `fgets()` buffer without rejecting a physical line that filled the buffer. One overlong physical line could therefore be split across successive `read_node_record()` calls; with a valid first record followed by padding and another valid-looking fragment, libdnet could treat data after the buffer boundary as a second node/executor entry even though no newline separated it. Embedded NUL bytes could similarly terminate the C-string view before the physical line ended. The successor uses an explicit bounded physical-line reader that consumes through newline/EOF, rejects embedded NULs, fails `E2BIG` before a line can span the buffer, documents the strict node-database parsing contract and locks it in source-release policy. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance only from the repaired successor.

Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11287814795` for candidate `0bcb2c296f37491cf23ea045086d62a7b52aaf3e` (Repository Policy `37163355153`, Build Bootstrap `37163376052`, Project State Gate `37163387587`, Source Release Reproducibility `37163396716`, tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-0bcb2c296f37.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `99ef40847af7326c29fc73420787fd5220bbfe7178f4d79774255ab0b60882d7`) found a residual libdnet node-database record-boundary defect before a clean scan could be recorded, so the clean-scan count remains zero. The newly bounded `read_node_line()` returned immediately on an embedded NUL or when its 512-byte buffer filled, despite the documented contract that a rejected physical line is consumed through newline/EOF. One-shot lookups close the file after that error, but the public `dnet_getnode()` / `dnet_nextnode()` iterator keeps its FILE open; a caller that continues after the error could therefore resume in the unconsumed suffix of the same malformed physical line and parse that suffix as a new node record. The successor records the first line error while continuing to drain the physical line, then returns the preserved error only after newline/EOF, documents the iterator no-resynchronization guarantee, and locks the drain behavior in source-release policy. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance only from the repaired successor, obtain its new exact source artifact, and restart mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3 on those packaged bytes.

Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11289527933` for candidate `b89d2d53cdbaa71e8a347507dd6956544d79d79f` (Repository Policy `37165919811`, Build Bootstrap `37165975959`, Project State Gate `37165984335`, Source Release Reproducibility `37165991355`, tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-b89d2d53cdba.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `2b4feea46772708c4031db9d23e94e94640a3d8847558e59a5d5b5520611c163`) found a DAP CREATE failure-cleanup defect before a clean scan could be recorded, so the clean-scan count remains zero. `dnfald` correctly unlinked an incomplete CREATE target on ordinary protocol/write failures, but returned directly when the final `fclose()` failed. A delayed writeback error such as ENOSPC could therefore leave a partially written or truncated target even though CREATE failed and no successful ACCOMP response was delivered. The successor routes final stream close/flush failure through the same target-unlink cleanup while marking the stream closed to avoid a second `fclose()`, documents the failed-CREATE cleanup contract and locks the close-error path in source-release policy. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance only from the repaired successor, obtain its new exact source artifact, and restart mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3 on those packaged bytes.
