Project-instruction synchronization: the current repository instructions now make the completion order explicit. The immediate gate is three successive zero-defect fresh-disk scans (3/3) of one exact accepted source-release artifact; any defect resets/keeps the count at 0/3 and requires a repaired exact-SHA artifact before scanning restarts. After 3/3, canonical pre-production testing resumes with blocking PP-11 S1 pressure before S2, followed by the remaining applicable PP/release gates and final full acceptance. The final production deliverable is explicitly the reproducible versioned source tarball plus checksum/release notes, complete source, build/install/uninstall scripts and detailed step-by-step `INSTALL.md`; disk images, prebuilt kernels/initrds and prebuilt modules are lab/intermediate artifacts only. This instruction-only change supersedes any still-running acceptance for the prior exact tree and requires fresh targeted/source-release acceptance before the audit resumes. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope.

Targeted source-release acceptance of candidate `e7ccf176173ef4cd2ccaf9d07257b130e5dd2323` exposed a continuity/policy marker mismatch before the candidate could qualify for audit. Repository Policy run `37168791366` and Project State Gate run `37168828559` passed, but Build Bootstrap run `37168818868` failed both architectures only at `tests/policy/test_source_release_gate.py` after product/component builds and selftests passed. The gate requires the literal current-goal phrase `portable source release`; the Goal correctly described source-tarball delivery but had been reworded without that exact marker, producing the misleading diagnostic `current project goal still advertises disk-image delivery`. Source Release Reproducibility #89 / run `37168833811` is therefore superseded regardless of its eventual conclusion and cannot qualify an artifact for audit. The successor restores the required marker without changing delivery policy or product behavior. Clean-scan count remains 0/3. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance only from the synchronized successor.

Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11290499370` for candidate `38259627c134c5fac0f557c59b406109fd7474fd` (Repository Policy `37169287991`, Build Bootstrap `37169316383`, Project State Gate `37169327105`, Source Release Reproducibility `37169335617`, tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-38259627c134.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `c91cd08a18e287658b69b249567c86a6bf134a412c2090774af03d15f9eea39d`) found a `dnmaild` final spool-close error-path defect before a clean scan could be recorded, so the clean-scan count remains 0/3. After successfully writing the terminal spool separator, a failing final `fclose(out)` transferred control to common cleanup while `out` still pointed at the already-invalidated `FILE`; cleanup could therefore call `fclose(out)` a second time, invoking undefined behavior on a delayed flush/writeback error such as ENOSPC. The successor separates the final marker write from the stream close, clears `out` immediately when `fclose()` reports failure before entering common cleanup, documents the invariant and locks the old/new forms in the source-release policy gate. The repaired packaged source must receive new exact-SHA targeted/source-release acceptance and a fresh download before mandatory audit scan 1/3 restarts. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope.

Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11291380640` for candidate `bc61fdee9081640b677426a015d50aa4ab3d1411` (Repository Policy `37170169037`, Build Bootstrap `37170191816`, Project State Gate `37170199719`, Source Release Reproducibility `37170206888`, tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-bc61fdee9081.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `28af4529635278490929bae3b95960c0da2b65c148f0eb67b166b0af5378bec1`) found additional source-tree cleanup defects before a clean scan could be recorded, so the clean-scan count remains 0/3. Clang static analysis confirmed that `dncopy` opened a named local STORE input before connecting to FAL and returned directly if `open_fal()` failed, leaking the local stream until process exit. The same source review found test-harness cleanup defects: the `dnetd` overlong-config selftest could call `close(fd)` twice when the first close reported failure and checked `errno` only after `unlink()` could overwrite it, while the `dnmaild` and `dnhttpd` filesystem selftests collapsed final write/close handling in a way that could either discard a still-open stream on write failure or obscure close-state ownership. The successor closes the STORE input while preserving the FAL error, makes the dnetd selftest single-close and preserves the parser errno across cleanup, splits write/close state in both filesystem selftests, and locks each invariant in the source-release policy gate. The repaired packaged source requires new exact-SHA targeted/source-release acceptance and a fresh download before mandatory audit scan 1/3 restarts. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope.

Targeted source-release acceptance of candidate `96a02f8e9bec74dda6738a3bfe4276d58ff33528` passed Repository Policy `37171345866` and Project State Gate `37171384242`, but Build Bootstrap `37171373028` failed both architectures at compile time because the dncopy cleanup edit was applied to the first matching `open_fal()` block in `retrieve_file()` instead of the intended STORE path, introducing an undeclared `in` reference while leaving the STORE leak unchanged. Source Release Reproducibility `37171392129` and any artifact it produces are superseded and cannot qualify for audit. The successor restores the retrieval path unchanged, applies the close/error-preservation logic only inside `store_file()`, and scopes the source-release policy assertion to the STORE function so the same misplacement cannot satisfy the gate. Product protocol behavior is otherwise unchanged; clean-scan count remains 0/3. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Restart targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance only from the corrected successor.

Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11291103542` for candidate `8ee870df66092ca3756989fc09c5479604257faf` (Repository Policy `37171477049`, Build Bootstrap `37171502011`, Project State Gate `37171510632`, Source Release Reproducibility `37171518559`, tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-8ee870df6609.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `f25f534cda1d107e729e496b02eac24f78edc1e77e6f5667e3104671cb6e0dc2`) found a `dnmaild` sendmail-child lifecycle defect before a clean scan could be recorded, so the clean-scan count remains 0/3. `finish_sendmail()` returned immediately if closing the parent write end of the sendmail pipe failed; its caller then discarded both descriptor and child PID state. The spawned sendmail process was therefore not waited or killed on that path and could remain running or become a zombie. The successor records the pipe-close errno but still completes bounded child wait/reap (including the existing timeout kill/reap), returns the retained close error only after child ownership is resolved, adds a deterministic selftest using an invalid parent descriptor plus a real child to prove the child is reaped, documents the invariant and locks it in source-release policy. Requalify the repaired successor, freshly download its exact packaged source artifact, record tarball name/SHA-256, and restart mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope.

Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11292365567` for candidate `ee9bd542d17193d36a6f42ee92f46f1bcf26503b` (Repository Policy `37172622484`, Build Bootstrap `37172657769`, Project State Gate `37172665846`, Source Release Reproducibility `37172674752`, tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-ee9bd542d171.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `18a320b32ad7fee253e559f3efb50b430323049724a463633334a839cb3b41a5`) found another cleanup defect before a clean scan could be recorded, so the clean-scan count remains 0/3. The shipped user-mode SMTP lab helper `tests/lab/dnsmtpfake.c` routed a failing final `fclose(out)` into common cleanup while leaving `out` non-NULL; cleanup could therefore call `fclose(out)` a second time on an already-invalidated `FILE`, invoking undefined behavior exactly on the delayed write/flush error path the helper is meant to exercise. The successor clears `out` immediately when the close reports failure and locks the ownership transition in the source-release policy gate. Requalify the repaired successor, freshly download its exact packaged source artifact, record tarball name/SHA-256, and restart mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope.

Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of accepted artifact `11292177393` for exact candidate `b8c151fd711b4d8d3de17f45dde5f05456cc43cd` (Repository Policy `37173444038`, Build Bootstrap `37173467063`, Project State Gate `37173475310`, Source Release Reproducibility `37173484368`; tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-b8c151fd711b.tar.xz`; SHA-256 `ead3daf227449ae20f3e742fbb9ce8fc7ed6c87997f5bac128aaed9d8074975a`) found a `dnfald` directory-enumeration false-success defect, so the clean-scan count remains 0/3. `serve_directory()` treated every `readdir()` NULL result as end-of-directory without checking `errno` and ignored the final `closedir()` result; a filesystem enumeration error could therefore produce an ACCOMP success after an incomplete listing. The successor distinguishes clean EOF from `readdir()` failure, preserves the enumeration errno across cleanup, rejects final directory-stream close failure, documents the behavior and policy-locks the error handling. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Requalify the successor with targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release`, download its exact artifact fresh, record tarball name/SHA-256, and restart mandatory audit scan 1/3.

The same fresh-disk scan 1 of accepted artifact `11292177393` for exact candidate `b8c151fd711b4d8d3de17f45dde5f05456cc43cd` also found an errno-integrity defect in `tests/lab/dnflow.c::send_record()`. It collapsed every non-full `send()` result to -1 without assigning errno; a rare nonnegative short send would therefore make the XOFF acceptance path inspect stale/indeterminate errno and could false-fail or misdiagnose flow-control acceptance. The successor preserves real send errno, assigns `EMSGSIZE` for an oversized local record and `EIO` for a nonnegative short send, and policy-locks the invariant. Clean-scan count remains 0/3. Requalify this combined successor, freshly download its exact packaged source artifact, record tarball name/SHA-256, and restart mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3.

Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11293623873` for candidate `030119717cefc542f847c657c14123a811d7854a` (Repository Policy `37177427552`, Build Bootstrap `37177456701`, Project State Gate `37177466420`, Source Release Reproducibility `37177474887`; tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-030119717cef.tar.xz`; SHA-256 `1a6d51b4b4c096e60ca9f61cb92676c62c0347d0150dc88a19250d23e5f0d074`) found an NSP parser defect before a clean scan could be recorded, so the clean-scan count remains 0/3. The Phase IV NSP Connect Acknowledgment wire format is exactly MSGFLG plus DSTADDR (3 bytes), but `dniv_nsp_parse()` accepted any length >=3 and silently ignored trailing bytes. The successor requires exactly 3 bytes, adds a deterministic trailing-byte negative unit test, and source-release policy-locks the exact-length invariant. Supplementary full unit execution passed with the repair. Requalify the successor, freshly download its exact packaged source artifact, record tarball name/SHA-256, and restart mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope.

Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11293623873` for candidate `030119717cefc542f847c657c14123a811d7854a` also found the same short-send errno defect class in `tests/lab/dnwindow.c::send_tag()`: a nonnegative short `send()` returned -1 without assigning errno, while the window-full acceptance path immediately inspects errno for `EAGAIN`/`EWOULDBLOCK`. The successor preserves real send errors and assigns `EIO` to a nonnegative short send, with source-release policy coverage. Clean-scan count remains 0/3. The in-flight acceptance for `f141b40e93233c52982b7764e17991c0894308ad` is superseded by this successor; requalify the new exact SHA, freshly download its accepted artifact, record tarball name/SHA-256, and restart mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3.

Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11294725667` for candidate `ef47246a3fc67ac2cf7107eb89a1114bf0771760` (Repository Policy `37178989069`, Build Bootstrap `37179013484`, Project State Gate `37179021444`, Source Release Reproducibility `37179028634`; tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-ef47246a3fc6.tar.xz`; SHA-256 `66ffc5b96f4fe167c866a662c51c92a3cf6286ee2fc83635b1216ac8648bca68`) found a numeric-address boundary defect before a clean scan could be recorded, so the clean-scan count remains 0/3. The canonical PP contract requires DECnet area 0 and node 0 to be rejected at parsing/configuration boundaries, and the kernel wire validator already enforces area 1..63 and node 1..1023. However `dnmail`, `dntask`, `dnlogin`, `dncopy`, `phone`, and `dnlynx` accepted area 0; `libdnet` numeric conversion accepted both area 0 and node 0. The successor aligns every affected user-facing parser with the project address contract, extends component selftests with zero-area/zero-node negatives, and source-release policy-locks the range checks. This is input validation only; valid DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Requalify the successor, freshly download its exact packaged source artifact, record tarball name/SHA-256, and restart mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope.

Fresh-disk audit scan 1 of exact accepted source artifact `11295166972` for candidate `43c6bb392e74f1c10e71621550554a082e72e7f1` (Repository Policy `37180040006`, Build Bootstrap `37180061924`, Project State Gate `37180072941`, Source Release Reproducibility `37180082375`; tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-43c6bb392e74.tar.xz`; SHA-256 `d4581bd069e52dfbd276d04de3a649e191dcf38c20c68357c3381abd1fb998de`) found a delivered userspace short-send defect before a clean scan could be recorded, so the clean-scan count remains 0/3. The native `AF_DECnet` socket ABI deliberately permits a short positive `SOCK_SEQPACKET` send when one or more NSP segments were accepted before flow/window pressure, timeout, or interruption stopped the remainder; callers must retry the unsent suffix to finish the open record. Delivered clients/servers instead treated any non-full `send()` as terminal failure, so pressure could leave a partially transmitted record and abort otherwise recoverable application operations. The successor adds a shared sequenced-record send helper that retries short positive sends and `EINTR` while preserving final `MSG_EOR`, converts every delivered record sender to it, gives zero-progress write failures deterministic `EIO`, documents the contract, and policy-locks the complete sender set. Supplementary packaged-source builds, selftests, ASan/UBSan and GCC analyzer checks were green apart from the previously understood daemon stdio `dup2` analyzer false-positive. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope. Requalify the successor, freshly download its exact packaged source artifact, record tarball name/SHA-256, and restart mandatory fresh-disk audit scan 1/3.

Pre-acceptance documentation reconciliation after `ea102c2360e4c76a71f32f12e00b842108ac18cc`: the implementation and feature contract already describe reliable short-record send retry, but `docs/COMPONENTS.md` still described `userspace/common/record_io.h` as receive-only. The successor synchronizes the delivered component catalogue to the actual bidirectional record-I/O helper. This documentation-only exact-tree change supersedes targeted/source-release acceptance and any artifact for `ea102c2360e...`; clean-scan count remains 0/3. Requalify the successor before restarting audit scan 1/3.

Source-delivery audit completed **3/3 successive zero-defect fresh-disk scans** against the exact accepted packaged source artifact `11302732224` from Source Release Reproducibility `37200464677`, candidate `b0db43e8f8d5519c01f863282e7e6348adb619b1`. Each scan used a newly downloaded copy of artifact `decnet-iv-linux-source-37200464677-1`, independently recorded tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-b0db43e8f8d5.tar.xz` SHA-256 `808ff287aece4fb514760aed677cd5e6e56b9685e08d234683401c0cda90c437`, and inspected the extracted packaged source rather than a checkout. The three scans supplemented source-by-source review with GCC/Clang builds, full unit/source-release policy execution, Python compilation, GCC/Clang static analysis, ASan/UBSan builds, parser fuzzing, archive/path metadata validation and targeted error-path review; no new defect/gap was found in any of the three scans. The mandatory source-delivery audit gate is therefore closed at 3/3. Resume canonical Pre-Production Testing immediately at blocking PP-11 S1 sustained pressure with targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp11-pressure`; S1 must close before S2. DDCMP and IPv6 remain out of scope.

Targeted PP-11 S1 pressure issue #1005 on exact candidate `4082f6585bde0db68abb39bfe1c5da1dfffe150a` reached pressure execution and exposed a host-harness serial parsing defect, not a candidate protocol failure. Independent Ethernet Interoperability run `37206164824`, amd64 job `111447680106`, artifact `11304634284` (artifact ZIP SHA-256 `f8d8271d0bf458914d9c056d08c307f18fd55cd0e9913f67bfd7b44f02ee4965`) completed ordinary interoperability, PP-11 table=256 with fail-closed evidence, malformed/RX=96/32 with evidence, and backlog=64+busy recovery. The guest then emitted `DNIV-INTEROP-PP11-WINDOW-LINK ... link=8483`; because serial lines retain CRLF, the host extracted `8483\r` and rejected it with the numeric-only regex before applying window pressure. The same latent defect exists in interrupt-link extraction. The successor strips exactly one trailing carriage return from both parsed serial link values before validation; policy locks both normalizations. Candidate DECnet behavior is unchanged. The remaining #1005 jobs and any predecessor result are superseded for promotion. PP-11 S1 pressure remains blocking; rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp11-pressure` on the successor.

Repository Policy run `37206832257` for `725026004496cc109b546914c2c50283c1ed360a` rejected the newly added PP-11 CRLF regression guard before acceptance dispatch: the policy test embedded the shell `$'\\r'` marker with incorrect Python escaping, producing an unterminated string literal at `tests/policy/test_pp11_s1_harness.py:112`. The runtime `run-interop.sh` normalization itself is unchanged; this successor repairs only the policy-test string literals so the intended trailing-CR normalization is testable. PP-11 S1 remains blocking; rerun targeted `pp11-pressure` acceptance from the successor.

Pre-rerun validation of `f3b6fbe8140c8d17764cafec2cab349b697f112f` found the repaired Python policy strings still over-escaped the shell CR marker: the source literal contained four backslashes before `r`, which would evaluate to two literal backslashes and fail to match the single-backslash `$'\\r'` text in `run-interop.sh`. The successor reduces each policy literal to the correct Python `\\r` source escape (one literal backslash plus `r` at comparison time). Runtime PP-11 harness behavior remains unchanged. PP-11 S1 remains blocking.

Pre-rerun validation found commit `33f96e3864be323f99056f4ee31a82aa1eebd857` corrupted `tests/policy/test_pp11_s1_harness.py` while attempting to reduce CR-marker escaping: the replacement duplicated large portions of the file and left unterminated marker strings. No PP-11 pressure acceptance from that exact tree can qualify. The successor restores the complete policy harness from its syntactically valid parent `f3b6fbe8140c8d17764cafec2cab349b697f112f` and changes only the two CR normalization markers from four source backslashes to two, so Python compares the intended single-backslash shell text `$'\\r'`. Runtime `tests/lab/run-interop.sh` remains unchanged. PP-11 S1 remains blocking; rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp11-pressure` on the successor.

Repository Policy `37211423624` on `c5357aa512ff6f6047f57e390cbe8cb11f6ffeab` confirmed the previous restoration still inherited a corrupt duplicated tail from parent `f3b6fbe8140c8d17764cafec2cab349b697f112f`, failing Python syntax at line 165 before acceptance dispatch. The successor restores `tests/policy/test_pp11_s1_harness.py` from the last known clean PP-11 policy baseline `4082f6585bde0db68abb39bfe1c5da1dfffe150a` and adds only the two CR-normalization assertions, each encoded with exactly two Python source backslashes so the runtime comparison contains one literal backslash. Runtime `run-interop.sh` remains unchanged. PP-11 S1 remains blocking; rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp11-pressure` on the successor.

Repository Policy `37211538731` on `38397bf56c62327883ad9d21815c41e5e4a56bd4` proved the policy file was syntactically clean but also exposed that `tests/lab/run-interop.sh` itself had been corrupted by the earlier replacement: both CR-normalization assignments were truncated and consumed the following shell code, so the intended marker could not be found. The successor rebuilds both `run-interop.sh` and `test_pp11_s1_harness.py` from the last known clean pressure baseline `4082f6585bde0db68abb39bfe1c5da1dfffe150a`, then applies only the two intended runtime trailing-CR strips and their two correctly escaped policy assertions. No candidate protocol code changes. PP-11 S1 remains blocking; rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp11-pressure` on the successor.

Targeted PP-11 S1 pressure rerun on exact candidate `9ddca20220e0481234d7112593988af4ced65cc5` progressed through the repaired CRLF link parsing on both amd64 and arm64, then failed at the intended 65th interrupt queue-bound check. Independent Ethernet Interoperability run `37211728289` showed the candidate correctly returned `ENOSPC` (errno 28) when the bounded 64-entry retransmit queue was full; `dnintflow` incorrectly accepted only EAGAIN/EWOULDBLOCK/ENOBUFS and therefore reported `pp11-retransmit-client` despite the kernel's existing socket path explicitly treating `-ENOSPC` as the retryable bounded-queue condition. The successor changes only the lab pressure client to accept ENOSPC for this resource-bound result and policy-locks the expected errno. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. PP-11 S1 remains blocking; rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp11-pressure`.

Repository Policy `37213211920` on `c0384365be4e8436018c74189c87713504ca21fc` rejected the new ENOSPC regression guard before acceptance dispatch because the policy checked the Python interrupt injector text instead of the C pressure client that interprets the 65th send result. The runtime `dnintflow.c` fix was correct; the successor adds a separate policy input for `tests/lab/dnintflow.c` and applies the ENOSPC/ENOBUFS assertion there while retaining the injector's credit/count assertion on `inject-nsp-intflow.py`. Candidate DECnet protocol and PP-11 runtime behavior are unchanged. PP-11 S1 remains blocking; rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp11-pressure`.

Targeted PP-11 S1 pressure acceptance of `a958b5f2cc0d6033b63d9bbdc553f416146f9eba` passed Repository Policy `37213367132`, Build Bootstrap `37213399882`, Project State Gate `37213411787` and dual-architecture E1 `37213422042`. Independent Ethernet Interoperability `37213433344` passed amd64 but arm64 failed round-1 RX pressure at the guest's exact aggregate `rx=32` assertion after the host injector successfully sent all 96 malformed controls and 32 future Link Service records. This is a harness accounting defect: the exported `rx` field is the sum of two independently bounded 32-entry NSP receive subchannels, so concurrently ready normal-data state may legitimately make the aggregate exceed 32 even while the injected other-data channel is exactly at its 32-entry limit. The successor accepts the only valid aggregate envelope for this proof, 32..64 inclusive, records the observed aggregate while retaining `channel-limit=32`, and regression-locks the range. The amd64 exact-pressure PASS is retained as useful evidence but does not close S1 without arm64. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. PP-11 S1 remains blocking; rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp11-pressure`.

Repository Policy `37214496123` on `0557ae5d22f87e0e650654b63cf9bf7d295c7970` rejected the RX-accounting successor before acceptance dispatch because the older PP-11 smoke-marker tuple still required literal `count=32`; the guest marker was intentionally changed to the accurate `channel-limit=32 total=<aggregate>` form. The successor updates that stale policy marker only. Runtime harness and candidate protocol behavior are unchanged; PP-11 S1 remains blocking.

Targeted PP-11 S1 pressure acceptance of exact candidate `173c99d6ebdac304aae8313f29c4875b417cdb37` is fully green: Repository Policy `37214607234`, Build Bootstrap `37214639109`, Project State Gate `37214648363`, dual-architecture E1 `37214658135`, and Independent Ethernet Interoperability `37214669342`. amd64 job `111472644586` retained artifact `11308695528` (SHA-256 `50efa874992dee957fcb9520aec392baf909dcbcd0a26226ad5903a6e860b416`); arm64 job `111472644893` retained artifact `11308925377` (SHA-256 `7b7d29cffc4cb87a08a7735d965236bed6d9db5dc19a28ce72705bfbb7acf6da`). Both architectures passed all three sustained S1 pressure rounds with table=256, malformed-control=96, future-other-data=32, backlog=64, data-window=20, retransmit-queue=64, sequence-wrap traffic, fail-closed fault evidence and final PCAP validation. Combined with the previously green exact 100-cycle disruptive churn on `b41684079f3866b1cb82039c1dbc299d9c9c2098`, PP-11 S1 is closed. Proceed to blocking PP-11 S2: 4-8 VMs, at least one hour, at least 10,000 deterministic injected fault events, sustained valid traffic and bounded resource/recovery evidence. S3-S6 remain owner-authorized skips.

PP-11 S2 implementation is now explicit and fail-closed. Targeted scope `pp11-s2` dispatches a dual-architecture four-VM routed lab. Three endnodes sustain valid routed traffic for 1,850 probes each while a router remains live; the host injects exactly 10,000 individually logged malformed DECnet EtherType frames over at least 3,600 seconds across both LANs. Pass requires retained capture proof of all injected events, at least 1,000 correctly forwarded valid frames per traffic source, at least 50 guest and host resource samples, final adjacency, bounded live links, slab growth within 64 MiB, no fatal kernel markers, and exact one-hour/count evidence. The gate stays within the hosted 75-minute job ceiling and runs independently on amd64 and arm64. Next: targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp11-s2` exact-SHA execution; PP-11 S2 remains blocking until both architectures retain PASS evidence.

Targeted PP-11 S2 execution on exact candidate `f5708bc69ff5bf8d590e8d609e8e1161eae5944f` (issue #1015) passed Repository Policy `37217231810`, Build Bootstrap `37217266507`, Project State Gate `37217277949`, dual-architecture E1 `37217288913`, and the amd64 S2 job in run `37217297860`. amd64 job `111480306299` retained artifact `11310945056` (SHA-256 `b027694756964eb67ce282ea1b5d132cefc9b874bb0c4e2c1471a9d856605e9a`) and proved four VMs, exactly 10,000 captured deterministic faults over 3,600 seconds, all three valid routed streams above 1,000 forwarded frames, >=50 resource samples and bounded final state. arm64 job `111480306171` was cancelled only when the fixed 75-minute workflow ceiling expired; artifact `11310860851` SHA-256 `55b5169ea9e5f8320904a59d0ee8274df25df6d248a9df59ec2dbb7770a308e1`. The first S2 traffic loop used 1,850 probes per endpoint with a fixed 2-second post-send sleep, so per-probe process-launch cost accumulated beyond the one-hour fault interval on arm64. The successor preserves all production floors while using 1,200 probes at a 3-second cadence: 3,600 seconds nominal valid traffic, 1,200 forwarding opportunities against the unchanged >=1,000 proof floor, and 48 periodic plus initial/final endpoint resource samples. Candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. PP-11 S2 remains blocking; rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp11-s2` on the successor.

Targeted PP-11 S2 retry on exact candidate `2b007322f0b2d12125a70672d8c383ae9a51fdbb` (issue #1016) reconfirmed the arm64 runtime problem. Build Bootstrap `37223047361`, Project State Gate `37223060118` and dual-architecture E1 `37223073537` were green; S2 run `37223087381` passed amd64 job `111497178314` (artifact `11312760236`, SHA-256 `cfafd4589a728c96549a2b173e99b70442d1c6b53c649d16180c60e6b3c71ee8`) but arm64 job `111497178466` again reached the fixed 75-minute ceiling while still in the S2 controller (artifact `11312731083`, SHA-256 `62ad2719b1922bc9d995b3cd23d46a652b55a5a5c7f2effa62125bdf271693a8`). The 1,200-probe three-second guest-clock loop still stretched beyond the host job budget under arm64 TCG. The successor decouples traffic lifetime from the threshold marker: endpoints reach 1,200 probes at a two-second cadence, emit PASS, and then continue the same routed traffic until host teardown; endpoint sampling moves to every 20 probes and router sampling to 45 seconds. The host now additionally proves each forwarded-valid stream spans at least 3,590 host seconds with no gap above 30 seconds, so early PASS cannot mask a traffic lapse. The one-hour host-timed injector, exact 10,000 faults, four candidate VMs, >=1,000 forwarded-valid frames, resource envelopes and final adjacency remain unchanged. Any still-running duplicate retry of `2b007322...` is superseded by this successor regardless of outcome. PP-11 S2 remains blocking; run targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp11-s2` on the successor.

Pre-acceptance review of `32def090507e24384479f21543e3c037e959ed3e` found a controller-only PCAP parser defect before its S2 result could qualify: the newly added magic constants were written as byte strings containing literal backslash-x text, so valid captures would be rejected after the one-hour run. The successor compares the four-byte magic through `data[:4].hex()` for both microsecond and nanosecond PCAP byte orders and policy-locks those exact forms. S2 traffic, fault, duration, topology and resource requirements are unchanged. Acceptance issue #1018 is superseded; rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp11-s2` from the corrected successor.

Pre-acceptance review of `0244b2edc65a83dc4f06546e0a2bd8149c8a043a` found one remaining escaped-byte mistake in the same new PCAP temporal parser: its EtherType comparison used a byte string containing literal backslash-x text, so forwarded-frame recognition would be empty despite the corrected file magic. The successor compares `frame[12:14].hex()` with `6003` and policy-locks that exact check. No S2 production requirement changes. Acceptance issue #1019 and its in-flight child runs are superseded; rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp11-s2` from the corrected successor.

PP-11 S2 is closed on exact candidate `35042437a2bb4314b344b2143fc953326d93c8ec` via targeted issue #1020. Repository Policy `37229490036`, Build Bootstrap `37229521352`, Project State Gate `37229533293`, dual-architecture E1 `37229546317`, and S2 run `37229557472` are green. amd64 job `111516158458` retained artifact `11313754063` (SHA-256 `a660e2740fdbd3629174bffe3bf6eb7489ae37c1cba0fe15d5808eb9351638b7`) with exactly 10,000 captured faults over 3,600 seconds and three 1,734-frame valid streams spanning more than 3,602 seconds. arm64 job `111516158610` retained artifact `11315345439` (SHA-256 `92aeb21a4e42451bc3eb219196f44a5ca90d1713f6b41b8523dca37cf33be91b`) with exactly 10,000 captured faults over 3,600 seconds and 1,554-1,556 valid frames per stream spanning more than 3,604 seconds. Resource and final-adjacency gates passed on both architectures. S0-S2 are complete; S3-S6 remain owner-authorized skips.

Next blocking canonical stage is PP-12. The successor adds a targeted `SCOPE=pp12` source-release mode. It uses only the exact packaged source artifact after ordinary source-release validation, performs production build/install/reinstall/uninstall plus module reload and warm-reboot recovery in disposable x86_64/aarch64 guests, and then runs the recovered exact-source image through the non-disruptive Area-31 OpenVMS proof. Full acceptance also enables the same PP-12 source-artifact mode. Run targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp12`; PP-12 remains open until both architectures retain PASS evidence.

Push validation of `30cce034ab1e0f1ae860777607fd9574ff543096` failed before any PP-12 execution because the newly appended `pp12-real-peers` workflow job lost its required two-space indentation under `jobs:`, so GitHub rejected `.github/workflows/source-release.yml` as invalid YAML (run `37236073439`, no jobs created). The successor changes only that workflow indentation; PP-11 S2 closure evidence and all PP-12 requirements/behavior are unchanged. Run targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp12` only from the corrected successor.

Targeted PP-12 acceptance issue #1021 on `1bc208151f4e7550d7263cc720be2b6cbea91712` passed Repository Policy `37236181537` and Project State Gate `37236229193`, but Build Bootstrap `37236217725` failed both architectures only at `tests/policy/test_source_release_gate.py`: the historical source-release policy globally forbade any `qemu-system` or non-negative QCOW2 reference, while the new PP-12 job intentionally creates a disposable lab VM from the exact tarball. Production delivery remains source-only and the PP-12 QCOW2 stays under runner-temporary storage, outside uploaded evidence/release artifacts. The successor scopes the disk-image prohibition to the production packaging/portability portion of the workflow and separately policy-locks the PP-12 image as lab-only/non-uploaded. Source Release run `37236240912` and any artifact it produces are superseded regardless of outcome. Rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp12`.

Pre-acceptance safety review of `09aecece4c6072e8e6cf75fbd3c6c289b7a2f43f` found that the inherited Area-31 QCOCAL path still uploaded/deleted temporary DCL files and executed a temporary task, and the new PP-12 extension added remote rename operations. That conflicts with the repository's canonical PYRTR `31.3` boundary: systems reached beyond it are read/traffic-only and must not be state-changed. Targeted PP-12 issue #1022 and Source Release run `37236485139` are therefore superseded regardless of eventual result. The successor removes all remote file upload/rename/delete/task execution from the Area-31 proof, removes generation of the temporary QCOCAL programs, keeps only read-only FAL directory plus NICE/MIRROR/Session probes, documents the filesystem-mutation prohibition explicitly, and policy-locks the remote read-only contract. Candidate DECnet protocol behavior is unchanged. Rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp12` from the successor.

Targeted PP-12 issue #1023 on `aee3482e4d963bdab5f5719a5d4e23e90c6a5262` passed Repository Policy `37237033654` and Project State Gate `37237085979`, but Build Bootstrap `37237073829` failed both architectures only at the source-release policy gate. The read-only Area-31 hardening intentionally removed the QCOCAL HTTP path, yet the policy test still required its former `http_out="$scratch/qcocal-http.out"` and `http_err="$scratch/qcocal-http.err"` scratch variables. The successor removes only those stale requirements while retaining the private `mktemp` scratch directory, native probe scratch file, cleanup trap, and all new no-remote-state-change guards. Source Release run `37237098891` and any artifact it produces are superseded. PP-12 remains blocking; rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp12` from the successor.

Targeted PP-12 issue #1024 on `53a724ecdedf0371c0b1478d153af80ed415bfea` passed Repository Policy `37238664634`, Build Bootstrap `37238697597`, Project State Gate `37238708121`, package-amd64, validate-arm64 and both source portability jobs. In Source Release run `37238719140`, amd64 PP-12 job `111544412516` also completed the exact packaged-source live lifecycle (build/install/reinstall/uninstall, module reload and warm reboot), then the read-only Area-31 phase failed only at the one-shot CTERM `dnlogin --probe` after native routing/NICE/NSP reachability had already converged. Retained artifact `11317570156` has SHA-256 `d048fdbf98a7ad6e58330851dbfa29ff618651ddc5af0781992bffb0263ba008`. The successor keeps CTERM mandatory, retries that non-state-changing remote probe up to 10 times at one-second intervals, and emits only the final bounded diagnostic if it still fails; no credentials are printed and no remote state is changed. The arm64 job from the superseded run cannot qualify PP-12 regardless of outcome. Rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp12`.

Targeted PP-12 issue #1025 on `8d7b4e7687386bbc79c8e45084b03b2c7572794b` passed Repository Policy `37240021132`, Build Bootstrap `37240057728`, Project State Gate `37240067884`, package-amd64, validate-arm64 and both source portability jobs. amd64 PP-12 job `111548368365` completed the exact packaged-source lifecycle and warm reboot, then failed only because the read-only Area-31 PyDECnet gateway remained alive but did not establish its PYRTR `31.3` MULTINET adjacency inside the fixed 180-second convergence window; artifact `11316908834` SHA-256 `c4ed4dac68004be5d64e0425015969b43a9ecf9271972a59cb32db565650e907`. This is a bounded remote-connectivity convergence failure after local lifecycle success, not a candidate protocol failure. The successor extends only `wait_gateway_wan` from 180 to 360 seconds and policy-locks that bound; no remote state-changing action is added and the strict read-only boundary remains unchanged. The still-running arm64 job from #1025 is superseded regardless of outcome. PP-12 remains blocking; rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp12` from the successor.

Targeted PP-12 issue #1026 on `4afd2e2e5a212bf50cae8fcb76401058f22072a2` passed Repository Policy `37241409442`, Build Bootstrap `37241452229`, Project State Gate `37241465680`, package-amd64, validate-arm64 and both source portability jobs in Source Release run `37241477702`. amd64 PP-12 job `111552342893` completed the exact packaged-source lifecycle and reached the native strictly read-only Area-31 guest, then failed only because `dniv-area31-smoke.sh` invoked `/usr/local/sbin/dnlogin` even though the accepted production installer correctly installs `dnlogin` at `/usr/local/bin/dnlogin`; retained artifact `11317981684` has SHA-256 `ad6475fd498156e2a47898697d59f4afde134aaae677e58a255735a1a4f4aa1d`. The successor corrects only that PP-12 harness path and policy-locks the installed client location. Candidate protocol and production install behavior are unchanged. The arm64 job from this superseded exact tree cannot qualify PP-12 regardless of outcome. PP-12 remains blocking; rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp12` from the successor.

Targeted PP-12 issue #1028 on `2d964758620d20e909a9b37dcb69d8ef0a68c5e4` passed Repository Policy `37243075158`, Build Bootstrap `37243105936`, Project State Gate `37243118096`, package-amd64, validate-arm64 and both source portability jobs in Source Release run `37243129463`. The exact packaged source was `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-2d964758620d.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `8cc3e34219927a8fc5ade165118d88e2932a38b2acf1cbc8804c828935f46109`. Both PP-12 jobs completed the exact packaged-source lifecycle and reached the strictly read-only Area-31 guest, then failed at the delivered `ncp tell ... show executor summary` check: amd64 job `111557094097`, artifact `11318168472`, SHA-256 `0b7eb9036c4f48866d1bffe091055f07afb9ac87ffcd6b5a2d24b100a15a3899`; arm64 job `111557094101`, artifact `11318619475`, SHA-256 `31a4ac417b9eac539eae9e42c2324cffe9c57336cfc1a81ded2c46961d5890e7`. Source inspection found a concrete delivered-client defect: `ncp` still used `/usr/local/sbin/dnnice` as its absolute helper path even though the production installer places `dnnice` at `/usr/local/bin/dnnice`. The successor corrects that product path, policy-locks it, and preserves the first six stderr lines on any future read-only NCP failure. No remote state-changing action is added. PP-12 remains blocking; rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp12` from the successor.

Targeted PP-12 issue #1029 on `a1b1e3d325e11fe2d3a5f13033b9df342f2ded07` passed Repository Policy `37247515993`, Build Bootstrap `37247545410`, Project State Gate `37247557508`, package-amd64, validate-arm64 and both source portability jobs in Source Release run `37247569153`. Exact packaged source artifact `11318994438` contains `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-a1b1e3d325e1.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `e45793ca95372c0fef38a42701741165aea1fd2e087fa10e7b372c96da5dfc2c`. amd64 PP-12 job `111570084231` completed exact-source build/install/reinstall/uninstall, module reload, warm reboot and the strictly read-only Area-31 CTERM/FAL path, then the delivered NCP/NICE check failed with `dnnice: malformed READ NODE reply`; retained artifact `11320570063` has SHA-256 `ccad472583c85aeb0de296e266b5a5d647b9d59e3d9491dd95bf1e3c266b7ea5`. This exposed a real client interoperability defect rather than a remote failure: Phase IV Network Management Functional Specification section 6.11 defines successful NICE responses with ENTITY ID immediately after RETURN CODE, while ERROR DETAIL and ERROR MESSAGE are optional error-only fields. `dnnice` had learned only the extended four-byte success header emitted by pinned PyDECnet and the candidate NML server. The successor centralizes success-entity offset parsing, accepts both canonical OpenVMS and existing extended forms, adds a canonical reply unit regression, and policy-locks the shared parser. No reference defect is compensated for and no remote state-changing action is added. The still-running arm64 job from #1029 is superseded regardless of result. PP-12 remains blocking; rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp12` from the successor.

Targeted PP-12 issue #1030 on `054cda319f7c65e7ae7d46ec1821f42e86079e74` passed Repository Policy `37250184891`, Build Bootstrap `37250227380`, Project State Gate `37250239470`, package-amd64, validate-arm64 and both source portability jobs in Source Release run `37250252106`. Exact packaged source artifact `11320403415` contains `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-054cda319f7c.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `12bf4479f1d9d277f086bdeb3af617015548e1bcb5803a49a02358fb197198d4`. amd64 PP-12 job `111577899620` completed exact-source build/install/reinstall/uninstall, module reload, warm reboot and the strictly read-only Area-31 CTERM/FAL path, then again failed at the delivered NCP/NICE executor-summary check with `dnnice: malformed READ NODE reply`; retained artifact `11320863518` has SHA-256 `518681f6e95d92d434b5909f9547aea4c8706dbb6341049e2c43b889119aae0b`. The canonical-success entity-offset correction was therefore necessary but did not cover the complete OpenVMS flow. Pinned LinuxDECnet `ncp/show.c` provides the missing independent behavior: SHOW/LIST may first receive the standard `NICE_RET_ACCEPTED` control record and then read the following data response sequence. `dnnice` already supported that framing for plural reads but singular executor/node/circuit reads consumed only one record. The successor adds exactly one bounded ACCEPTED prelude for singular reads, centralizes strict compact/extended control-record validation, and adds unit/policy regression markers. No remote state-changing action is added and candidate routing/NSP behavior is unchanged. The still-running arm64 job from #1030 is superseded regardless of result. PP-12 remains blocking; rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp12` from the successor.

Targeted PP-12 issue #1031 on `f0d21f8545b5a243d17a18172589facca741d98d` passed Repository Policy `37251621622`, Build Bootstrap `37251653129`, Project State Gate `37251668120`, package-amd64, validate-arm64 and both source portability jobs in Source Release run `37251681917`. Exact packaged source artifact `11321121744` contains `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-f0d21f8545b5.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `fafb5bd0ca27bd46e3b8a04e8cfc9009efb2302467c8d60ad88aab633978ce37`. amd64 PP-12 job `111582026949` completed the exact-source build/install/reinstall/uninstall, module reload, warm reboot, strictly read-only CTERM/FAL path, and the repaired NCP/NICE executor-summary check, then failed at the delivered `dnping` probe with `DNIV-AREA31-NATIVE-FAIL reason=dnping-remote`; retained artifact `11321207933` has SHA-256 `74d6613db203de790d2770e7ae732afc66f57fc0bb6df77943aad40c0ad05b35`. The same candidate guest had already completed the direct MIRROR matrix through numeric object `#25`, isolating the remaining defect to `dnping` requesting the named object `MIRROR`. The successor uses the standard numeric MIRROR object `#25`, selftests that binding and policy-locks it. No routing/NSP behavior or remote state-changing action is added. The arm64 job from #1031 is superseded regardless of outcome. PP-12 remains blocking; rerun targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=pp12` from the successor.

PP-12 is closed on exact candidate `fdc7abff76cb71b41011b36c44182ce9fee9427d` via targeted issue #1032. Repository Policy `37253144021`, Build Bootstrap `37253184989`, Project State Gate `37253196116`, and Source Release run `37253207303` are green. Exact source artifact `11321976922` contains `DECnet-IV-Linux-0.0.0-ci-fdc7abff76cb.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `3a9232a6a5ab6d8292f5761628ee5b61b4d383be8e32b0405c7b3c9708064691`. amd64 PP-12 job `111586409073` retained artifact `11322430946` SHA-256 `3dda90a7df7626548e89f983cbee7f2c78dbf1478a53184ae0a78d007f653ae0`; arm64 job `111586409022` retained artifact `11322627742` SHA-256 `fff7e2054fd12185241fd85550e13b3661c18db6b33b519bcb1b12d7a14888e9`. Both architectures completed exact-source build/install/reinstall/uninstall, module reload, warm reboot and the strictly read-only Area-31 OpenVMS proof, including delivered CTERM/FAL/NCP/NICE/dnping checks, without remote state changes. PP-13 remains owner-authorized skip for the first release. Next: execute and ledger every remaining applicable requirement from `## Timing/scheduler tests` onward under the owner lab-execution rule, recording impossible items exactly as `Not tested in lab environment`; then run final exact-SHA full release acceptance and publish the production source release.

Post-Timing closure implementation is now explicit. A new targeted `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=post-timing` gate runs dual-architecture 4-vCPU virtio E1 and adds local-only MTU change, promiscuous/all-multicast toggling, GRO/GSO/TSO off/on traffic, veth register/unregister/re-register notifier churn, deterministic management-UAPI/AF_DECnet hostile-call fuzzing, and two-worker CPU saturation overlapping the live listener-expiry/recovery interval. Unit coverage now locks the zero/fallback and maximum 16-bit hello-timer conversion without overflow. The full profile is also corrected to rerun both the post-Timing extension and PP-11 S2, so final exact-SHA promotion cannot inherit either device/timing coverage or the one-hour sustained-fault proof from an earlier candidate. Deterministic nonlinear/cloned/fragmented skb construction, filter-reference coexistence and jiffies-wrap injection remain unavailable in the current lab and will be ledgered exactly as `Not tested in lab environment` after the executable post-Timing gate passes. Next: targeted post-timing acceptance; then ledger every post-Timing requirement and run final exact-SHA full release acceptance.

Production-release hardening: final full preflight issue #1041 started on exact `b97eec08eaa277bde44430b0ee3dab498df47d8a` after two fully green fast runs. Its first amd64 secondary-e1000 attempt failed because the Ubuntu guest kernel panicked in `sched_clock_cpu/ttwu_queue_wakelist` during early boot before `decnet_iv` loaded; the paired arm64 job passed and a job-level retry was queued. While that preflight was running, final delivery review found a separate concrete release-contract gap: `.github/workflows/source-release.yml` still named archives with the CI-only `0.0.0-ci-<sha>` pseudo-version and the repository had no fail-closed path that could publish the exact accepted artifact plus release notes. The preflight candidate therefore cannot be the production deliverable regardless of its remaining child results.

The successor makes root `VERSION` the production version authority, initializes the first production release as `1.0.0`, binds Source Release Reproducibility to that version, requires `VERSION` in the packaged tree, and adds an owner-only production-publication workflow. Publication verifies the exact full-acceptance issue and every required green child run against the unchanged SHA, downloads the exact accepted source-release artifact without rebuilding or repacking it, revalidates checksum/metadata/content, and publishes only `DECnet-IV-Linux-1.0.0.tar.xz`, its checksum, and evidence-bearing release notes under tag `v1.0.0`. Policy regression coverage locks the version and publication contract. Candidate protocol behavior is unchanged. Because these delivery changes alter packaged production bytes, clean-scan status returns to 0/3: next run targeted exact-SHA source-release acceptance on the successor, download that exact artifact freshly and complete three successive zero-defect scans before a new final full acceptance and publication.


Production-publication verification follow-up: pre-acceptance review of the new publisher found two fail-closed gaps before any production publication was attempted. The final integrity pass named a `pass-1.json` baseline that had never been created, and the forbidden-archive-member check streamed `tar` into quiet `grep` under `pipefail`, allowing a matching early exit to be obscured by `tar` SIGPIPE status. The successor creates the exact-source integrity baseline immediately after the initial workflow guard and materializes the archive member list once before required/forbidden checks. Policy coverage requires both safeguards. Targeted issue #1043 for the superseded publisher is non-qualifying; clean-scan count remains 0/3. Next: targeted exact-SHA source-release acceptance of this successor.


Production-publication workflow repair: GitHub workflow validation run `37351928174` caught a generated YAML corruption before any job or publication could run. The prior edit used a replacement string containing the shell regex end-anchor sequence `$'`; JavaScript replacement semantics expanded that sequence and duplicated the workflow suffix into the archive-check block. The corrected blob is rebuilt from the last syntactically accepted publisher, applies the integrity-baseline and materialized archive-list changes without replacement-string interpolation, and verifies that each publication step occurs exactly once. Issue #1044 and commit `a3517f570552a156163e10bba18b76dd5fffd826` are superseded and non-qualifying. Clean-scan count remains 0/3.

Targeted requalification issue #1047 on `2289ce2e8ae003c0e4352ea500de0de1155078a5` is non-qualifying. Repository Policy `37393651425` passed, but Build Bootstrap `37393706063` failed on both architectures before kernel build because the new Makefile policy-test loop reached the repository with a single `$test` instead of the required make-escaped `$$test`; GNU make consumed `$t` and invoked Python on the literal path `est`. This was an edit-generation defect caused by replacement-string dollar semantics, not a product/runtime defect. The successor writes the literal double-dollar shell-variable escape and the existing source-release policy guard requires that exact loop. Clean-scan count remains **0/3** and issue #1047 cannot supply an audit artifact. Next: targeted exact-SHA source-release acceptance of this successor, then restart fresh-artifact scan 1 only if all child gates are green.

Targeted source-release issue #1048 on `1af687db318a322c9b2695187eb4cd942e7bb5ac` is non-qualifying. Repository Policy `37393923919` and Project State Gate `37393991850` passed, but Build Bootstrap `37393972642` failed on both architectures because `tests/policy/test_source_release_gate.py` still required the single-dollar Makefile loop text after the Makefile had been correctly repaired to use the GNU-make escape `$$test`. Source Release Reproducibility `37394008712` is non-qualifying regardless of its eventual result. The successor updates only the policy guard to require the exact make-escaped loop actually needed to execute every shipped policy regression. Product protocol/runtime behavior is unchanged. Final full acceptance issue #1054 on exact candidate `8391dad15e05e26b9ee7d6fab50d50dd6ed3e3c4` is non-qualifying. Every dispatched child except amd64 PP-11 S2 passed; PP-11 S2 run `37617199215` passed arm64 job `112778309727` but amd64 job `112778310100` failed after the full one-hour run because the injector proved exactly 10,000 successful deterministic sends while the retained PCAP contained 9,999 fault markers. This is a capture-finalization race: the controller waited for guest PASS and immediately tore down tcpdump, allowing the final packet still pending in the capture path to miss the materialized PCAP. The successor does not relax any S2 floor; after guest PASS it keeps captures alive for up to 10 seconds until all 10,000 fault markers are visible, fails if a capture process dies, and retains the existing hard `captured_faults < FAULT_EVENTS` rejection after teardown. Policy coverage locks the settle gate. Product protocol/runtime behavior is unchanged. Because the packaged source bytes change, mandatory source-delivery audit status resets to **0/3**. Next: targeted exact-SHA source-release acceptance, fresh accepted artifact, and three successive zero-defect scans before PP-11/full release acceptance resumes.

Final full acceptance issue #1059 on exact candidate `947509d623591f42b879bd60222a9068e0df3c37` is non-qualifying despite every non-KCSAN child completing green. KCSAN run `37926393337` passed arm64 but amd64 job `113806258407` reported exact pair `xas_clear_mark / xas_find_marked`; retained artifact `11615141091`. This is a generic upstream Linux XArray KCSAN false positive, not candidate DECnet code: syzbot bug `57980d45446259bcb330ed78ba02f3b38733d413` is closed invalid for this exact pair, upstream XArray maintainer review confirms the writer is protected by the XArray lock while the marked iterator is intentionally RCU-protected, and pinned Linux `72d3fcf802c45d00b300f25b848a93c3a2bd7c7e` retains that design. The successor adds only this exact summary and reverse ordering to the existing vetted-upstream KCSAN ignore set; all other KCSAN findings remain fail-closed. Candidate protocol/runtime behavior is unchanged. Because the packaged source bytes change, mandatory source-delivery audit status resets to **0/3**. Next: targeted exact-SHA source-release acceptance, fresh accepted artifact, and three successive zero-defect scans before PP-11/PP-12/final full release acceptance resumes.


Fresh production-source audit finding (2026-10-09): exact-SHA targeted source-release acceptance of `dce5826cd1e5e4d0ebf5e6576261e1d1e0f8ae32` was green (Repository Policy `37942533856`, dual-architecture Build Bootstrap `37942616364`, Project State Gate `37942647386`, Source Release Reproducibility `37942673255`). The accepted packaged artifact `11623010682` contained `DECnet-IV-Linux-1.0.0.tar.xz` with SHA-256 `a41070c086d9a41fd3ad418fd898fac9f6d6eb4b8d7e84277d31b2c3c5cad72a`. Three independent fresh downloads passed supplemental full-byte/source-line, syntax, unit, GCC userspace and Clang userspace verification; those automated passes alone did not qualify as the owner's three manual clean scans (issue #1061). Subsequent focused semantic review found a real fail-open KCSAN diagnostic gate: `kcsan_unapproved_findings()` matched each vetted upstream race by substring, so a forged or extended summary containing an allowed XArray pair could hide an unrelated candidate race. The successor compares the complete report summary after the first `BUG: KCSAN:` marker against the exact allowlist, retaining kernel-console prefixes, and adds executable false-green regressions for appended/mixed reports. The new regression failed against the old filter and passed against the repair; the complete packaged-source `make unit` passed locally. This is diagnostic-harness hardening only; production protocol behavior does not change. Mandatory clean-scan count is **0/3**. The superseded artifact cannot qualify. Next: targeted exact-SHA `PROFILE=targeted`, `SCOPE=source-release` acceptance of this successor, fresh artifact download and restart source-by-source manual audit scan 1; only after 3/3 may full release acceptance resume.


Packaged-source semantic audit finding (2026-10-09): supplemental review of the exact accepted `b06096584a213850b9062a64b8dd3d12cca368e4` archive (`11629960534`, `DECnet-IV-Linux-1.0.0.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `7377403001e0f5c1d0fdd3c7436d2169a5f491e2dcaa22a881a5caa9fdc2f732`) found a real FAL CREATE data-loss bug. `open_regular_at(..., 1)` allowed opening and truncating an existing regular file, and `serve_create` unconditionally unlinked the target on protocol/send/flush failure. A small socketpair-backed C regression reproduced that an aborted DAP CREATE deletes a pre-existing file; this is a source defect, so clean-scan count remains **0/3** and the old artifact is superseded. The successor makes DAP CREATE exclusive with `O_EXCL`, preserving existing targets (explicit ERASE/RENAME remain available), retains failed-new-file cleanup, and adds executable existing/new-failure selftest cases and a source-release policy lock. Re-run all local source tests and compilation before committing, then obtain fresh exact-SHA green targeted/source-release acceptance, download a new accepted artifact and restart semantic scan 1. No PP/full acceptance may be promoted from the superseded source.


Packaged-source documentation audit finding (2026-10-09): while exact candidate `6ac86b78bb27a85e62bf15ce69afc710037aea42` was undergoing new targeted/source-release acceptance after the FAL data-loss repair, review found stale current-priority guidance in `docs/HANDOVER.md`, `docs/ROADMAP.md` and the Project State current completion sequence: old PP-11 S1/S2 closures and an earlier 3/3 were described alongside wording that incorrectly made publication (or still-open S1 pressure) the immediate release blocker. The corrected priority makes independent exhaustive 3/3 fresh accepted packaged-source semantic audits the current blocker, resets to 0/3 on any defect, requires requalification of changed source and explicitly makes older PP success historical pending final-SHA reconciliation/reproof. A source-release policy regression now locks the handover ordering. This correction supersedes exact-SHA acceptance of the prior changed tree and requires a new fully green targeted/source-release artifact before scan 1. Formal count remains **0/3**.


Packaged-source semantic audit finding (2026-10-09): the fully green exact source artifact for `6bbc71b14ded2478393038ccaa9de267d834e0d8` was freshly downloaded from Source Release Reproducibility `37965620832`, artifact `11633336302` (`decnet-iv-linux-source-37965620832-1`), archive `DECnet-IV-Linux-1.0.0.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `6ec27535c570d153da02607bbd763565e09704442a604df7224a8ea8e542d37d`, enclosing ZIP SHA-256 `6d674950c8be0306eb1dbb204518cf5cd65e9f7469947747671b3e455486c300`. All four applicable source qualification jobs were successful (package amd64, extracted-source arm64, dual-arch portability; optional PP12 real-peer job skipped in this targeted workflow and **not** counted as proof). Manual review found a second concrete file-deletion concurrency defect: after an in-progress CREATE was renamed away, a concurrent writer could create an unrelated file at the requested name and abort cleanup would `unlinkat` **that replacement**, losing unrelated data. A deterministic socketpair + fork C reproduction confirmed the deletion against exact extracted accepted source. The successor makes DAP CREATE stage with unnamed `O_TMPFILE`, flush/fsync/close before exclusive `linkat(AT_EMPTY_PATH)` publication, and never unlinks a named target during abort. It adds failing-rename/concurrent-replacement and successful-upload executable regressions, source policy checks, and documents the filesystem requirements. Prior artifact is superseded, formal manual clean-scan count remains **0/3**; repair must obtain new green exact-SHA gates and a fresh artifact before restarting semantic scan 1.


Packaged-source semantic review finding (2026-10-09): exact accepted packaged artifact `11633336302` for `6bbc71b14ded2478393038ccaa9de267d834e0d8` (`DECnet-IV-Linux-1.0.0.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `6ec27535c570d153da02607bbd763565e09704442a604df7224a8ea8e542d37d`) exposes a `dncopy` record-mode silent-data-loss bug that remained in successor `43275a2f7b3c561a8a9967bd6afcee46da84bc68`: `fgets()` accepts NUL bytes from local file input but `strlen()` truncates the transmitted DAP record at the first NUL and drops the remainder while reporting success. A standalone host C reproduction demonstrated a six-byte physical input with only two bytes sent and three silently discarded. Repair reads bounded physical text records byte-by-byte, rejects embedded NUL (`EILSEQ`) and overlong records (`E2BIG`) before successful completion, handles CRLF/empty/exact-length EOF, and adds compiled selftests plus source-release policy assertions. Binary files remain supported in explicit `-m block` mode. Local `make -C userspace/dncopy check` and the source-release policy regression passed on the repaired packaged-source-derived tree; the broader local unit suite was interrupted after its policy gate and does not count as full acceptance. Both continuity records updated in the same repair. Formal comprehensive clean-scan count stays **0/3**, pending new exact-SHA green targeted/source-release gates and fresh artifact.


Source-release semantic-review follow-up (2026-10-09): local regression review of `a178ed70a0ff8d09a9a4482f285c5663a9771ff2` found a compatibility boundary error in the new record-mode reader: it stripped a trailing carriage return from an EOF-terminated line without LF, whereas the previous `dncopy` text mode stripped CR only before LF. The repair now tracks whether the record terminated in LF and removes preceding CR **only** in that case. Added a compiled selftest for literal `x\r` at EOF and confirmed the repaired `dncopy` check and source-release policy test pass. This supersedes the initial repair's pending acceptance, requires requalification of a new exact source SHA, and keeps formal fresh-packaged-source comprehensive audit count at **0/3**.


Packaged-source semantic review finding (2026-10-09): while reviewing the `072980940f99dfa7e77107dea4bed0627d433c97` candidate, inspected the MAIL-11 client path and found a concrete silent message-field corruption. The client accepted `dnmail -s ''` and called `dniv_send_record()` with a zero-length subject, but native `AF_DECnet` seqpacket `sendmsg` returns success without emitting any NSP record for a zero-byte send. The following body record is then parsed by `dnmaild` as the subject, potentially yielding a false success with the message contents shifted. This successor rejects empty subject in CLI validation *before* connecting, with executable `dnmail` Makefile regression requiring exit status 2, source-release policy regression, and user documentation. Broader protocol changes to zero-length NSP records are intentionally deferred pending independent correctness proof; nonempty subject preserves existing MAIL-11 framing. The parent targeted source artifact is superseded by this change, and the owner-required independent comprehensive packaged-source manual zero-defect scan count remains **0/3**. Requalify the successor exact SHA through all targeted source-release gates and freshly download its artifact before restarting scan 1.


Targeted acceptance follow-up (2026-10-09): Build Bootstrap run `37970449491` for `52f2df57e4f4bf2a877f833c4f889dfe3a974ed6` failed both amd64 and arm64 at the MAIL client executable negative regression, not at protocol code. The GitHub-committed Makefile recipe accidentally contained a single-dollar `rc=$?; test "$rc" -eq 2`; GNU Make expands `$?` and `$r` itself, causing `/bin/sh: dnmaild: not found` and an invalid test expression. The corrected recipe uses the required `$$?`/`$$rc` Make escaping so the shell, not Make, reads the exit status. Source-release policy now asserts exact escaping as well as the negative input. An independently executed correct recipe and local `make -C userspace/dnmail clean all check` passed. Superseded runs cannot qualify a new exact source SHA. Formal manual 3-scan count remains **0/3**.


Packaged-source semantic audit finding (2026-10-09): fully green exact-SHA source candidate `661bb6013479b75f908fd3c77ef8365b081509b2` had Repository Policy `37970807036`, Build Bootstrap `37970882448` (amd64/arm64), Project State Gate `37970903939` and Source Release Reproducibility `37970933323` (package amd64, extracted-source arm64, amd64/arm64 portability green; optional PP12 real-peer skipped and **not** counted). Freshly downloaded artifact ID `11635242899` (`decnet-iv-linux-source-37970933323-1`), SHA-256 enclosing ZIP `0a494c9ef44a9b0d836cbd1204146ab45076c65143d9800861e4bd3e001d2bea`, archive `DECnet-IV-Linux-1.0.0.tar.xz` SHA-256 `f4e9f32338177a3143b180a2f8673093bde4952e5b947a0368cba82c7f9ec2c4` with checksum and `SOURCE-METADATA` verified. Manual semantic review of packaged `userspace/dnetd/dnetd.c` established two defects: a final configuration line containing an embedded NUL accepted only the valid prefix, discarding hidden trailing bytes; and `parse_options("NOAUTH")` succeeded as `N` rather than rejecting an invalid complete token. Compiled standalone C repros against **extracted accepted source** returned `load_config=0,count=1` for `TEST 0 N,N root /bin/cat\0hidden` and `parse_options("NOAUTH")=0`. The replacement uses a bounded byte-wise physical-line reader that rejects NUL (`EILSEQ`) and oversize (`E2BIG`), preserves valid final non-newline lines, and enforces exact option-token forms. Executable regression coverage now rejects the malformed inputs; all dnetd selftests and policy regression passed locally. Source qualification must restart at the successor exact SHA with a freshly downloaded artifact. **Formal comprehensive clean-scan count remains 0/3**; this is a defect finding, not clean scan 1.


Packaged-source semantic audit finding (2026-10-09): fully green exact-SHA source archive `bdf97a7f7f4ff1ee969d0d7f7b89d7b5d66181b2` (Repository Policy `37972765664`, Build Bootstrap `37972804529`, Project State Gate `37972829619`, Source Release Reproducibility `37972853621`; fresh artifact `11636933153`, tarball `DECnet-IV-Linux-1.0.0.tar.xz`, SHA-256 `8fc29dba9ebe43ea4be7584a1126a37ebaebf339c00649ad89ff3a94b3f8eb68`) exposes a named `dncopy` DAP retrieval data-loss path. Remote DAP OPEN/CONNECT preceding `fopen(..., "wb")` protect against pretransfer failure, but a later failed GET, truncated stream, bad EOF or missing ACCESS_COMPLETE leaves an existing destination truncated or partly overwritten despite reporting failure. The successor stages named local retrieval in the same directory and publishes with `rename` only after complete DAP confirmation and local flush/fsync/close; errors discard staged bytes without changing the original, and existing symlink/special targets fail closed. Executable selftest covers aborted staged output, successful replacement and symlink rejection; source-release policy protects ordering. All source changes require new green exact-SHA targeted/source-release acceptance and a fresh artifact; formal comprehensive manual clean-scan count remains **0/3**.


Packaged-source semantic audit finding (2026-10-09): the next source review of `userspace/dnhttpd/dnhttpd.c` found that an embedded NUL in a DECnet HTTP request is silently treated as an end-of-string by `sscanf`, accepting a forged record's valid prefix while ignoring hidden trailing bytes. A compiled standalone socketpair regression against the previously qualified source received `HTTP/1.0 200 OK` for `GET / HTTP/1.0\0HIDDEN`. The successor checks all bytes received with `memchr(request, '\0', got)` before text parsing and returns HTTP 400 for malformed binary records. An executable daemon selftest covers the complete sequenced-record request and bad-response path; a source-release policy assertion guards both validation and test. No remote test systems were touched. Packaged-source semantic audit finding (2026-10-09): review of the accepted `bdf97a7f7f4ff1ee969d0d7f7b89d7b5d66181b2` DAP client exposed unbounded network waits in `userspace/dncopy/dncopy.c`. Unlike the other interactive DECnet clients, `open_fal` configured no socket send/receive timeouts; a connected silent FAL peer could hang `--probe`, GET, PUT and all other DAP operations indefinitely while awaiting CONFIG/ACK/data/completion. The successor sets bounded 30-second `SO_RCVTIMEO` and `SO_SNDTIMEO` on every FAL socket before any DAP exchange and fails closed if timeout configuration fails. A no-remote UNIX sequenced-socket selftest verifies both timeout settings; source-release policy enforces the client and test. Full local GCC unit/userspace and Clang dncopy selftests are required before qualification. The in-flight `e7324d91263a44d6e134dbf3af4dd9955428d3f3` source-release acceptance will not qualify the successor. Formal owner-required exhaustive manual clean-scan count remains **0/3**; qualify the next exact SHA, freshly download its green source artifact and restart the comprehensive reviews.


Packaged-source semantic audit security finding (2026-10-09): the previously accepted `tools/build-source-release.sh` extracted tracked source and then overwrote `$work/$name/SOURCE-METADATA` without rejecting tracked source symlinks. An isolated local Git source tree containing a tracked `SOURCE-METADATA` symlink to a harmless outside test file caused the packager to overwrite that external file *before* failing its required-member checks, proving an out-of-tree write. The successor fails closed on every tracked symlink immediately after extraction, before writing generated metadata, and rejects a preexisting tracked `SOURCE-METADATA` member. An independent executable Python policy regression creates a temporary `main`-only Git fixture, proves the malicious tracked-symlink build is rejected early and that the victim remains byte-for-byte unchanged, and checks that no release artifact is produced. The source release policy also locks these safeguards. Local complete `make unit` and userspace build must pass before commit; old exact-SHA source-release qualification of `28a9cdfa8099d7c5a022f5b2a7c6fc0869625200` is superseded after this repair. **Formal exhaustive manual clean-scan count remains 0/3** pending new acceptance and a new fresh exact packaged source artifact.

Packaged-source semantic audit finding (2026-10-09): exact predecessor `0f2d1930438e8ad148453af727976d90f63a4228` was fully green for Repository Policy `38002136852`/`38002152194`, Build Bootstrap amd64/arm64 `38002192785`, Project State Gate `38002210845`, and Source Release Reproducibility `38002231131` (package-amd64, validate-arm64 and both architecture portability checks PASS; PP12 skipped, not acceptance). Direct compiled local C socketpair probes of the **unmodified extracted accepted archive** revealed two defects: (1) an interrupted inbound MAIL-11 exchange left **74 bytes** of unacknowledged partial message/header in persistent `mailbox.log` despite reporting session failure; (2) DNFAL sent 2048-byte DAP DATA frames after advertising only 1024-byte CONFIG BUFSIZ, and the dncopy upload path similarly allowed 1027-byte DATA frames with the 1024-byte configured ceiling. The successor stages inbound mail privately and copies only after complete protocol end-of-message; aborted and complete sessions are covered by socketpair regressions. DAP client/server now advertise a complete-frame maximum of 2048, parse the peer's two-byte CONFIG BUFSIZ, fail closed on nonzero less-than-12 values, negotiate the smaller maximum (zero means unlimited), cap every outbound DAP frame and accepted inbound frame, and segment GET/PUT payloads to leave three DATA-header bytes. Client bounded-frame positive/negative socketpair regressions and real DNFAL GET of 4096 bytes through 128-byte negotiated frames were added, plus strict source-release policy coverage. Exact source-repair local `make userspace`, complete `make unit` (exit zero), Python source-policy regression, GCC -Wall/-Wextra/-Werror builds, Clang warnings-as-errors syntax checks, and original-versus-repaired compiled DAP/mail failure probes passed. No HECNET remote systems were touched. Focused manual review of unchanged accepted packaged userspace/dncopy/dncopy.c exposed TWO local file safety defects. (1) Named download staging used mkstemp directly in the destination directory and fchmod applied final mode 0644 before DAP completion; an incomplete named staging file became readable by other local users in a publicly accessible directory. Independent GCC original-source probe demonstrated a public stage pathname with stage_mode=0644. (2) With a destination absent at staging start, publish_staged_output unconditionally called rename and silently overwrote an unrelated file created concurrently during the transfer. Independent compiled original accepted-source reproducer staged TRANSFER, created destination OTHER_WRITER and observed publish_rc=0 dest_bytes=8 dest_value=TRANSFER (12 unrelated bytes lost). Repaired source creates a mode-0700 private staging directory on the same filesystem and refuses to overwrite a newly created destination with atomic exclusive link publication; pre-existing regular destinations still permit intentional atomic rename replacement, and stage directory cleanup follows success/abort. Packaged-source scan-1 defects and local repair in progress (2026-10-10; ZERO qualifying clean scans): immutable fully green source SHA `6f800f115b515b82f782cb602dd35ec27397ac7f`, targeted issue #1092, Repository Policy 38058055524, Build Bootstrap 38058100770 (amd64+arm64 green), Project State Gate 38058112463, Source Release Reproducibility 38058124305 (package-amd64, validate-arm64 and both portability jobs green; optional PP12 skipped). After qualification, fresh artifact 11672315193, ZIP SHA-256 675214eb9955966fe1ea1fd9324bfbff0c10fe10541d86af5acdfd2d23fd5340; DECnet-IV-Linux-1.0.0.tar.xz SHA-256 f0bc6c6c67dd6f324a6fa761ebe2e3e67925a0d65338c53aa7342afb231b2c6e (metadata/checksum verified). Direct compiled AF_UNIX/SOCK_SEQPACKET test of the immutable extracted source proved FAL CREATE writes successive DAP VAR DATA payloads as a raw concatenation, losing record boundaries ("abc" and "def" -> "abcdef", causing a false single record on GET). The same packaged FAL ATTRIBUTES parser failed a valid extensible EX menu `02 00 87 01 01 00 04 20`, while dncopy's own decoder accepted that standard pattern. Local corrective source stages and stores each VAR/VFC record as 16-bit little-endian length+payload, marks an internal framing xattr and reconstructs exact records on FAL GET; fail-closed checks protect malformed/oversized disk records; normal byte-stream behavior is unchanged. An EX menu decoder handles first eight attribute fields with length validation and RAT EX encoding; higher fields remain intentionally rejected. New local end-to-end UNIX sequenced-packet CREATE/GET selftest proves two distinct nonempty records plus empty record, persistent framing/xattr, correct GET frames and EOF, with negative malformed record and ATTR tests. This is only a local candidate, not qualifying acceptance evidence until the eventual exact-SHA main commit qualifies and its new artifact is fresh-downloaded. Formal owner three-scan count remains **0/3**, canonical PP/full acceptance/public release blocked. No HECnet peer touched.

2026-10-10 source semantic follow-up (formal clean count **0/3**): qualified 55b packaged FAL stores logical VAR/VFC DATA records with an xattr framing marker, but `load_record_framing` returned success (`framed=0`) on a local regular file advertising RFM=VAR via `user.decnet.rfm` while lacking `user.decnet.record-framing`. Independent C harness compiled against exact accepted packaged FAL confirmed `load_rc=0, rfm=2, framed=0` on raw `abcdef`; following GET would invent record boundaries by arbitrary chunking, silently changing DAP record-mode semantics. Additional local xattr corruption (`user.decnet.rfm=255` or `user.decnet.rat=255`) was not validated by `load_metadata` and could advertise illegal DAP attributes. Corrective local candidate rejects unmarked VAR/VFC and invalid RFM/RAT metadata with EPROTO before GET, retains raw FIX/stream and correctly framed VAR/VFC, and adds selftests and source-release guards. This must be independently reproduced against the fresh successor b578 packaged source before any new main commit; all tests and new SHA acceptance remain mandatory. No PP or publication, no remote HECnet changes.

2026-10-10 exact packaged-source semantic scan 1 identified a NEW MAIL-11 empty-body protocol defect at fully green candidate ae1975d47a5e38ebc78413792ab224f0aa3019ee, source acceptance issue #1099 / run 38075835267, artifact 11678294693, tarball SHA256 486da8fe86b2bee5ded05af4b1edfb2a5aa8e94e079db3147cc851453f811cc4. The delivered dnmail client accepts an empty MESSAGE and transmits a zero-byte SOCK_SEQPACKET body record; server dnmaild treats recv()==0 as aborted session before the MAIL-11 completion marker, so it rejects a valid empty message. Original packaged-source compiled C AF_UNIX SOCK_SEQPACKET regression: sending zero body then end marker yields EOM_ACK_LEN=-1 and child_status=1; omitting nonexistent body and sending the same EOM yields ACK length=4, code=1, child_status=0. Repair represents an empty message by EOM directly (nonempty bodies unchanged). New client selftest verifies no extra zero record; daemon selftest does completed empty-body session and exact appended mailbox byte comparison (no unexpected newline); source-release policy markers updated, preserving all existing checks, feature documentation synchronized. Local make -C userspace/dnmail clean all and its selftests, source release gate, GCC/Clang C11 -Werror syntax checks green; full make unit attempt timed out, NOT green or evidence. This commit changes packaged bytes, so clean comprehensive scan count RESET 0/3 and all exact-SHA qualifying gates must re-run and NEW accepted artifact be downloaded before new scan. No PP, final full acceptance, public release or HECNET activity claimed.
